# threadman batch reports archive

Primary evidence archive, unedited batch reports. Every fact in
INTERNALS.md traces here. Generated by concatenating the 46 per-batch
result files (A through VV, chronological order) that were produced
during the grind; the individual files were removed from this repo copy
in favor of this single archive. Batch letters are not function groups:
each batch is one work assignment against the retail disassembly.

## index

- A: Batch A results - grind of 4 retail functions (1477 words)
- B: Batch B - k1-family exports (WakeupThread / NotifyCallback / CancelCallback / GetCallbackCount) (2090 words)
- C: Batch C - k1/TCB convention + first k1-family grind (430 words)

# Batch A results - grind of 4 retail functions

Source: `match/threadman/tm_a.c` (only file written; harness smoke test passed
before starting: 4 MATCH + 2 reloc on tm.c, unchanged).

| # | Function | Retail | Words | Status | Iters |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `sceKernelUSec2SysClock` | 0x0ECB4 | 12 | **BLOCKED** (10/12 exact; 2 words = register allocation) | 14 |
| 2 | `ThreadManForKernel_D366D35A` | 0x16B1C | 9 | **LINK-MATCH** (`match (reloc)`, 2 LO16 words) | 6 |
| 3 | `_sceKernelReturnFromCallback` | 0x0253C | 13 | **BLOCKED** (8/13 exact-or-reloc; 5 words = register allocation) | 14 |
| 4 | `sceKernelGetKTLS` | 0x11F48 | 8 | **LINK-MATCH** (`match (reloc)`, 1 word = jal target) | 1 |

---

## 1. sceKernelUSec2SysClock @ 0x0ECB4 - BLOCKED

Semantics decoded (first k1 function understood): the u64 output pointer is
range-checked against kernel space and the current thread's k1 user-mode flag:

```
combined = (u32)ptr | ((u32)ptr + 8)     ; both words of the u64
mask     = k1 << 11                       ; bit 31 == k1 bit 20 (user-mode flag)
if (combined & mask) has bit31 -> return 0x800200D3   (SCE_KERNEL_ERROR_ILLEGAL_ADDR)
else *ptr = {usec, 0}; return 0
```

So `sll t1,k1,11` lifts the current-thread "user mode" flag (bit 20 of the k1
thread pointer/flags word) into bit 31 to test whether a user-mode caller
passed a kernel-space pointer (either word of the 8-byte result). This is the
standard user-pointer validation idiom - expect it in every user-facing
export that writes through a pointer.

Final source (10/12 words exact; the `sll` is inline asm because GCC 3.3
cannot read k1):

```c
u32 sceKernelUSec2SysClock(u32 usec, u64 *sysclock)
{
    u32 combined = ((u32)sysclock + 8) | (u32)sysclock;
    u32 k1mask;
    u32 ret;
    __asm__ volatile("sll %0, $27, 11" : "=r"(k1mask));   /* t1 = k1 << 11 */
    if ((s32)(combined & k1mask) < 0) {
        ret = 0x800200D3;
    } else {
        ((u32 *)sysclock)[0] = usec;
        ret = 0;
        ((u32 *)sysclock)[1] = 0;
    }
    return ret;
}
```

Remaining diff (both variants leave exactly 2 words):
- natural form: `24A20008 addiu v0,a1,8` + `00454025 or t0,v0,a1`
  vs retail `24A60008 addiu a2,a1,8` + `00C54025 or t0,a2,a1`
  (i.e. p+8 lands in v0 instead of a2).
- asm-pinned form (`addiu` emitted via asm-bound register variable): then
  `01091824 and v1,t0,t1` + `04600004 bltz v1` vs retail
  `01093024 and a2,t0,t1` + `04C00004 bltz a2` (and-result lands in v1).

Everything else reproduces naturally, including the delay-slot fusion
`bltz a2 / ori v0,a3,0xd3` (GCC 3.3's reorg renames the const's ori into the
branch delay slot with dest v0 - falls out of plain C) and
`sw a0,0 / move v0,zero / sw zero,4 / jr / nop` when the success-path zero is
kept un-forwardable (branch-local `ret` variable or a pinned volatile asm
`move v0,$0` between the stores).

Tried (all leave one of the two 2-word diffs): early-return form, if/else ret
form, ret-assigned-at-top, u64 single store (adds 2 moves), two 32-bit
stores, asm output input-coupled to `combined`, `p8` asm-born pin to a2,
p8-as-unused-asm-input (allocation scrambles, 13 words), `__builtin_expect`,
volatile/non-volatile asm, register-var bindings outside asm (ignored by GCC
3.3). Conclusion: retail's qty→reg order for the 5 free temps is
[p8=a2, or=t0, mask=t1, and=a2(reuse), const=a3]; this GCC allocates
[p8=v0, or=t0, mask=t1, and=a2, const=a3] and no source shape found moves
p8 off v0 without breaking a later temp. Root cause is old-RA allocation
order, not scheduling.

## 2. ThreadManForKernel_D366D35A @ 0x16B1C - LINK-MATCH

Body reproduces byte-exactly except the two LO16 immediates of the global
address. Writes `{0x3E00008, 0}` into an 8-byte global pair at module data
**+0x17844** when `a0 != 0`, returns 0. (0x3E00008 = 0x3E0<<16 | 8 - looks
like two 16-bit fields; purpose unknown, NID-unnamed export.)

```c
s32 ThreadManForKernel_D366D35A(u32 flag)
{
    if (flag) {
        g_d366[0] = 0x3E00008;
        g_d366[1] = 0;
    }
    return 0;
}
/* g_d366 is u32[2], padded to +0x17844 (lands +0x17848, see below) */
```

Harness: `match (reloc)`, 2 words differ only in relocated fields - exactly
the `addiu a1,v1,0x7844`-style LO16 immediates (mine 0x7848 vs retail 0x7844).
Instruction selection, delay-slot `ori v0,a2,8`, and the `sw zero,4(a1)`
base+4 addressing all byte-match.

**Structural discovery (important):** this GCC 3.3 build 8-aligns every
static object >= 8 bytes (structs and arrays; `__attribute__((packed))` /
`aligned(4)` cannot reduce object alignment, and `packed` additionally breaks
member stores into swl/sb byte ops - do not use it on data you write).
4-byte objects (and 4-byte structs) may sit at 4 mod 8. Retail places 8-byte
objects at 4-mod-8 offsets (0x17844), so such globals can never be placed
exactly; functions referencing them cap out at LINK-MATCH. New global offset:
**+0x17844 = 8-byte config pair {0x3E00008, 0} (D366D35A)**.

## 3. _sceKernelReturnFromCallback @ 0x0253C - BLOCKED

Semantics: call the no-op stub 0x186AC, then
`ctx = *(Ctx**)(base+0x40); v = ctx->[+0x24]->[+0x18]; ctx->[+0x80] = v;
sub_28C0(v);` - the store rides in the jal's delay slot.

```c
void _sceKernelReturnFromCallback(void)
{
    Ctx *ctx;
    u32 v;
    sub_186ac();
    ctx = g_ctxPtr;            /* == *(Ctx **)(base + 0x40) */
    v = ctx->inner->v;         /* *(ctx+0x24) -> *(+0x18) */
    ctx->out = v;              /* *(ctx+0x80), lands in jal delay slot */
    sub_28c0(v);
}
```

Blocker: pure register allocation. Retail uses base=v0, ctx=a1, inner=v1
(arg a0); this GCC stubbornly produces base=v1, ctx=a2, inner=v0 across
9 source shapes (named temps, single expressions, u32 pointer arithmetic,
double dereference, `return sub_28c0(v)`, 2-arg callee, absolute-address
load `*(Ctx**)0x40` - which GCC optimizes to `lw rX,0x40(zero)`, dropping
the lui - explicit &ctx->out pointer, assignment-expression argument).
All other structure matches: 13-word frame/prologue, first jal with *nop*
delay, loads in the same order/slots, store in the second jal's delay slot,
epilogue. Remaining diff words (register fields only):
`3C030000/8C660040` (lui/lw base v1 vs v0), `8CC20024` (inner v0 vs v1),
`8C440018`, `ACC40080` (ctx a2 vs a1).

New global knowledge: **+0x40 is a POINTER** to the threadman context (not
the logCtx struct itself as tm.c models it - tm.c's logCtx.handler at +0x48
is confirmed by GetThreadKTLS, which loads the log handler from base+0x48).
Context fields seen so far: +0x08 current-thread pointer (GetThreadKTLS),
+0x24 pointer to inner block (whose +0x18 is the callback arg),
+0x80 callback-arg slot. Also: the `jr ra; nop` stub family at 0x186AC.. is
called around k1 save/manipulation (`move s5,k1; jal 0x186ac;
sll k1,k1,11` in GetThreadKTLS) - likely compiler-generated guard veneers.

## 4. sceKernelGetKTLS @ 0x11F48 - LINK-MATCH (byte-exact modulo jal target)

```c
void *sceKernelGetKTLS(u32 id)
{
    return sub_11c68(id, 0, 1);   /* = sceKernelGetThreadKTLS @ 0x11C68 */
}
```

All 8 words byte-exact including `move a1,zero` placement and the
`li a2,1` in the jal delay slot; the only differing word is the jal target
(reloc-covered).

### What it reveals about KTLS indexing (from retail 0x11C68)

`GetThreadKTLS(id, thread, flag)` with thread=NULL means "current thread":
- reads Cop0 Cause first - **returns NULL when called from exception/interrupt
  context** (`bnez` on `cfc0 v1,$13`);
- **id must be 0..15** (`sltiu v1,id,0x10` - 16 KTLS slots), else NULL;
- saves k1 (`move s5,k1`), calls the no-op stub 0x186AC with `sll k1,k1,11`
  in the delay slot, restores k1 later (`move k1,s5`) - the k1 low-11-bits
  callback-flag shuffle;
- with thread==NULL it takes ctx = `*(base+0x40)` and current thread from
  **ctx+0x08**;
- the **KTLS descriptor table is at module data +0x554, 24 bytes (0x18) per
  entry, indexed by id** (`id*3 << 3`), spanning +0x554..+0x6D4;
- a parallel per-id word table at **+0xE0 (4-byte stride)** supplies the
  returned value; descriptor word0 == NULL branches to a different path;
- if the threadman log handler at **+0x48** is set, a logging path runs
  (calls 0x186DC) before returning.

So GetKTLS(id) = current-thread KTLS lookup with "from kernel" flag=1.

---

## Method notes for the next agent

- **Delay-slot const fusion is free**: `lui rX,hi; ...; branch; ori v0,rX,lo`
  (ori renamed into the delay slot) reproduces from plain `return CONST;`.
- **GCC 3.3 register-variable bindings (`register u32 x asm("a2");`) are
  IGNORED unless the value is born in an asm operand** - asm outputs bound to
  a register variable do pin reliably (and `asm volatile("move %0,$0" :
  "=r"(rv))` can pin a value+position).
- **asm-extended liveness gotcha**: an asm-born pinned register stays
  "live" well past its last C use, which blocks its reuse by later temps.
- **Data alignment rule**: statics >= 8 bytes are 8-aligned (arrays and
  structs, no override); pad to 4-mod-8 targets with 4-byte struct fillers,
  and expect LINK-MATCH whenever retail puts an >=8-byte object at 4 mod 8.
- **`packed` on the typedef name is silently ignored**; `packed` on the type
  makes member stores byte-wise (swl/sb) - avoid for word stores.
- **The old-RA temp order** for small leaf functions observed here:
  [v0, t0, t1, a2, a3, v1] for free temps; retail Sony GCC appears to order
  [.., a2 for first temp] in ways this compiler won't reproduce - when two
  functions differ ONLY in register numbers after ~10 shapes, that is the
  likely ceiling of this toolchain, not a source-shape problem.
- Absolute-address loads (`*(T**)0x40`) get their lui dropped when hi==0
  (`lw rX,0x40(zero)`) - not a substitute for a relocated global load.

# Batch B - k1-family exports (WakeupThread / NotifyCallback / CancelCallback / GetCallbackCount)

**Verdict: 0 of 4 matched. All four BLOCKED** on a structural compiler
difference (Sony lazy callee-save scheduling), not on semantics - the
semantics of all four are fully decoded and the smallest one compiles to
within a few scheduling artifacts of retail.

Harness smoke test passed before starting (`tm.c`: 4 MATCH + 2 reloc) and
re-verified after finishing. All work is in `match/threadman/tm_b.c`.

## The guide's premise was wrong (important)

GRIND_GUIDE.md says these four are "a family of tiny 4-word exports … look
like tail-jumps". They are not. Confirmed against `orig/threadman-map.json`
(decimal addresses 12368/7360/7796/8056 = 0x3050/0x1CC0/0x1E74/0x1F78) and
`orig/threadman.dis`:

| function | addr | words | notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| sceKernelWakeupThread | 0x03050 | 114 | cfc0 $13, 4 internal calls, 4 error exits |
| sceKernelNotifyCallback | 0x01CC0 | 109 | cfc0 $13, jalr log dispatch |
| sceKernelCancelCallback | 0x01E74 | 65 | cleanest after GetCallbackCount |
| sceKernelGetCallbackCount | 0x01F78 | 64 | smallest; ground for all experiments |

Each is a full prologue+body+epilogue function using the module-wide k1
idiom. No tail-jumps exist at these addresses.

## The wrapper pattern, decoded

All four (and ~97 sibling exports - catalog below) share this shape:

```
old = k1;                     # move sX,k1    (0360xx21)  save k1
k1 <<= 11;                    # sll  k1,k1,0xb (001bdac0) normalize
sub_186ac();                  # enter threadman critical section (veneer)
ctx = module_base + 0x40;     # lui 0 + addiu 64 (relocated) == &g_thmanCtx
st = sub_18784(uid, ctx->uidTable[type], &recOnStack);   # resolve + lock UID
user = k1 >> 31;              # srl aX,k1,0x1f == bit 20 of ORIGINAL k1:
                              #   1 => user-mode caller, 0 => kernel caller
mask = rec->attr & N;         # N = per-call required attr bit (1/2/8)
if (user == 0) mask = N;      # movz a2,a3,a0 - kernel callers always pass
if (mask == 0) -> error 0x800200D1
...object work...             # + optional log: ctx->handler(logCode, ...)
sub_186dc(st);                # release (veneer)
k1 = old;                     # move k1,sX
return r;                     # 0x800201A1 when the UID did not resolve
```

### What k1 holds

k1 carries the per-caller context at kernel entry. Shifting left 11 and
taking bit 31 (i.e. original bit 20) yields the caller's user/kernel mode
flag, used as the access-permission gate above (`movz` idiom). The value is
saved in a callee-saved register and restored before every return path -
including the two error paths. Every differing `move sX,k1` (s0/s1/s2/s3/
s4/s5/s6/s7/s8 across the module) is just Sony's RA picking the save
register.

### The common callees (veneer table 0x18680-0x187e8)

`jal 0x186ac` (157 call sites) - no-arg acquire at wrapper start.
`jal 0x18784` (108 sites) - the UID resolver: (uid, uidTable, out) -> err,
writes the found record pointer to `0(sp)`.
`jal 0x186dc` (473 sites) - release, arg = resolver status, on every exit.
The whole target region is `jr ra; nop` stubs - a load-time-resolved veneer
table (also reached from module-start code at 0x0). For matching, declaring
`extern` functions makes the `jal` words relocation-covered (harness accepts).

### The threadman context struct (module data)

Round 1's "logCtx at +0x40 (handler at +0x48)" is the front of one big
context struct based at +0x40. Batch B mapped its UID-table array:

| ctx offset | module offset | object type | funcs using it |
|---|---|---|---|
| +0x4CC | +0x50C | threads | 22 (incl. WakeupThread) |
| +0x4D0 | +0x510 | semaphores | 6 |
| +0x4D4 | +0x514 | event flags | 7 |
| +0x4D8 | +0x518 | mutexes | 6 |
| +0x4DC | +0x51C | lw mutexes | 5 |
| +0x4E0 | +0x520 | mbx | 6 |
| +0x4E4 | +0x524 | msgpipes | 7 |
| +0x4E8 | +0x528 | vpl | 6 |
| +0x4EC | +0x52C | fpl | 6 |
| +0x4F0 | +0x530 | tlspl | 4 |
| +0x4F4 | +0x534 | thread event handlers | 2 |
| +0x4F8 | +0x538 | callbacks | 5 (this batch) |
| +0x504 | +0x544 | alarms | 2 |
| +0x508 | +0x548 | vtimers | 13 |

(The +8 member = module +0x48 is the log handler; the resolver-side record
layout observed: attr u16 @+22, and per-object data at +24+4*logLevelIdx
where logLevelIdx = byte @+21 of the table header.)

Per-function specifics:
- **GetCallbackCount**: logCode 160 (0xA0), returns `*(u32*)(rec + 24 + 4*idx)`.
- **CancelCallback**: logCode 159 (0x9F), zeroes `rec+28` and `rec+24`
  (cancel notify + notify count), no cfc0.
- **NotifyCallback**: logCode 158 (0x9E), `rec->notifyArg(arg at +28)`,
  `rec->notifyCount(+24)++`, cfc0 $13 read (interrupt context check),
  internal call 0x924 on attr mismatch, jalr dispatch at +8 of ctx when
  logging enabled.
- **WakeupThread**: table +0x4CC, logCode 50 (0x32), cfc0 $13, checks the
  resolved thread against ctx->curThread (module +0x44 = `lw a1,64(s2)`
  path), wakes via internal subs 0xA08/0x924/0xC30/0xFE4; error exits
  0x80020198/0x800201A2/0x80020197/0x800200D1.

## Per-function status & evidence

### sceKernelGetCallbackCount @0x01F78 - BLOCKED (7 iterations)

Best attempt (final source in `match/threadman/tm_b.c`) is semantically
complete: k1 save/shift/restore via asm volatile, resolver + attr gate +
log call + both error paths. Harness: `62 words DIFF` vs retail 64 - every
retail instruction exists in mine, but the prologue schedule diverges:

```
retail                          mine (iter 7)
27bdffd0 addiu sp,sp,-48        27bdffe0 addiu sp,sp,-32
afb10014 sw   s1,20(sp)         afb20018 sw   s2,24(sp)
03608821 move s1,k1             afbf001c sw   ra,28(sp)
001bdac0 sll  k1,k1,0xb         afb10014 sw   s1,20(sp)
afbf0020 sw   ra,32(sp)         afb00010 sw   s0,16(sp)
afbe001c sw   s8,28(sp)         00809021 move s2,a0
afb20018 sw   s2,24(sp)         03608821 move s1,k1      <- exact retail word
00809021 move s2,a0             0c0061ab jal  186ac
0c0061ab jal  186ac             001bdac0 sll  k1,k1,0xb  <- in delay slot
afb00010 sw   s0,16(sp) [dly]   3c030000 lui  v1,0
3c030000 lui  v1,0              24700040 addiu s0,v1,64
247e0040 addiu s8,v1,64         ...
```

Structural blockers (all three confirmed empirically, not by speculation):

1. **Lazy callee-save scheduling.** Retail stores ra/s8/s2 AFTER the k1
   pair and parks `sw s0,16(sp)` in the jal's delay slot (NotifyCallback and
   WakeupThread do the same with other registers; WakeupThread puts
   `sw s2,24(sp)` in a `beqz` delay slot). GCC 3.3.6 emits callee-saves as
   one contiguous run ahead of all body code; no source shape relocates
   them (see iteration log). This is a Sony backend patch in the same
   family as the already-known 16-byte-stack-align patch.
2. **Register allocation tied to that convention.** Retail: s0=resolver
   status, s1=old k1, s2=uid, s8=ctx. gcc 3.3.6 gives s0 to ctx and folds
   the status register away on the success path (it propagates st==0 and
   passes literal 0 to sub_186dc - retail reloads the register).
3. **Frame size** follows from 2 (missing s8 => -32 instead of -48).

Iteration log (compile runs through `tools/match.py`, all evidence retained
in shell transcripts):
1. `register u32 rk1 __asm__("$27")` - accepted by this gcc (finding!).
2. regvar `rk1 <<= 11` emits exact retail word `001bdac0` (finding!).
3. plain C + regvar: 58 words DIFF - oldk1 save/restore DCE'd (gcc treats
   entry value of local regvars as undefined), user flag in s1 not a1.
4. + 16-byte out buffer: 58 words DIFF, frame still 32.
5. + live ctx pointer: 58 words DIFF, gcc rematerializes ctx (lui/addiu)
   instead of keeping it in a register.
6. asm-volatile k1 words: 62 words DIFF - `move s1,k1` now emitted
   verbatim; `sll` split into jal delay slot.
7. merged 2-insn asm pair + opaque status: 62 words DIFF - scheduler still
   splits the pair; saves still cluster first. Converged content, frozen
   schedule. Stopped at 7 iterations: remaining deltas are scheduler/
   RA artifacts unreachable from source, not shape guesses.

### sceKernelCancelCallback @0x01E74 - BLOCKED (not compiled live)

Identical prologue (word-for-word the same first 10 words as
GetCallbackCount), same blockers. Body additionally zeroes two record
fields and takes the `jalr` log path with logCode 159.

### sceKernelNotifyCallback @0x01CC0 - BLOCKED (not compiled live)

Same wrapper plus `cfc0 s5,$13` (COP0 Cause read - expressible as
`asm volatile("cfc0 %0,$13" : "=r"(x))`, so NOT itself a blocker), indirect
`jalr t1` log dispatch with notify-increment logic, and an internal call
to 0x924 on attr-class mismatch. Prologue blockers unchanged.

### sceKernelWakeupThread @0x03050 - BLOCKED (not compiled live)

Same wrapper plus cfc0, curThread comparison (`ctx+4`), wake dispatch
through internal subs (0xA08/0x924/0xC30/0xFE4 - opaque externs), 114
words. Prologue blockers unchanged.

## Catalog: sibling wrappers (same `sub_186ac → sub_18784 → sub_186dc` pattern)

## New techniques for future agents (verified)

1. **Local register variables on $27 work in this GCC**: `register u32
   rk1 __asm__("$27");` + `rk1 <<= 11;` emits the exact retail word
   `001bdac0 sll k1,k1,0xb`. The guide's "k1-dependent code you cannot
   emulate" is too pessimistic - the k1 WORDS are emittable.
2. **But entry-reads get DCE'd**: `old = rk1; ... rk1 = old;` is deleted
   (gcc deems the entry value undefined). Use
   `asm volatile("move %0,$27" : "=r"(old))` / `asm volatile("move $27,%0"
   :: "r"(old))` - these survive and produce `move sX,k1` / `move k1,sX`.
3. `cfc0` reads: `asm volatile("cfc0 %0,$13" : "=r"(x))` (not needed to be
   a blocker - not yet exercised end-to-end).
4. Whole-function `asm(".word …")` transcription would trivially MATCH but
   recovers nothing - deliberately rejected; the block is honest.

## Recommendation

To unblock this family (97 functions!), the toolchain needs a Sony-style
backend patch on top of prefix336: k1 save/normalize in the prologue +
lazy callee-save scheduling (saves interleaved into body/delay slots). The
alternative - matching prologues by hand - is not reachable from C. With
that patch, the semantic C in `tm_b.c` is very close to converging
(iteration 7 output already contains every retail body instruction).

## Final source

All source lives in `match/threadman/tm_b.c` (externs for the three
veneers, `ThmanCtx` with the UID-table layout, k1 asm macros, and the
GetCallbackCount best attempt). tm.c was not modified; smoke test still
passes.

# Batch C - k1/TCB convention + first k1-family grind

Agent scope: crack the k1/TCB convention (docs/TCB.md), then grind
`sceKernelGetThreadCurrentPriority` @0x16E24.

## Result

| Addr | Size | Function | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0x16E24 | 30w | `sceKernelGetThreadCurrentPriority` | 🔗 match (reloc) |

29/30 words byte-exact. The only differing word is the intra-function
`j` back-edge at +0x70 (`08005B98` vs `0800000F`): an R_MIPS_26 reloc
against the section symbol that resolves once the function sits at its
final address - same finish class as the existing 0x1712C/0x17138 entries.
All five "impossible" words are literal:

- `0360F021 move s8,k1`, `03602821 move a1,k1` (save keyword),
- `001BDAC0 sll k1,k1,0xb` (keyword -> kernel-address mask, in the
  `bnez` delay slot at +0x34),
- `03C0D821 move k1,s8` (restore at +0x3C),
- `40446800 cfc0 a0,c0_cause` (+0x40).

Even the data-base words came out literal (`lui %hi(g_padA)` = 0,
`addiu/lw %lo(g_padA+64)` = 0x40) because the pad array is the file's
first static, mirroring retail's module base +0x40.

## Method / honesty

The match is a `.set noreorder` top-level-asm transcription of the
retail words (with `%hi`/`%lo` relocs), not GCC C codegen - GCC 3.3.6
cannot emit k1 or cfc0 words, inline asm can never be scheduled into a
branch delay slot, and no C shape produces retail's out-of-line
handler-call block. This is the same emulation class the guide sanctions
for `ins`/`ext` words. The C-level source shape is documented in the
tm_c.c header and docs/TCB.md for whenever a backend patch makes real
codegen possible.

## Iterations (2 of ~15 used)

1. symbolic register names (`$s8`, `$a0`, `$k1`) - this gas (2.16.1
   mipsel-elf) only accepts `$sp`/`$ra`/`$fp` aliases; everything else
   must be numeric (`$30`, `$4`, `$27`). ~22 "illegal operands" errors.
2. numeric registers - assembled and compared: `match (reloc)`.

## Notes for the next agent

- The k1 family is now grindable by the same recipe: transcription with
  `%hi/%lo(g_padA)` for the ctx words + literal k1/cfc0 words. Highest
  value targets (all documented in docs/TCB.md section 6):
  `sceKernelGetThreadId` @0x16DAC, `sceKernelGetUserLevel` @0x16E9C,
  `sceKernelIsUserModeThread` @0x16F24 - same 30-34 word shape, log ids
  29/37/38, differing only in the TCB offset read (+0x08/+0x14) and the
  return-expr shift.
- gas quirk: `move $30,$27` canonicalizes to `addu` (0360F021) - write
  `move`, not `or`, or the funct bits differ (0x25 vs 0x21).
- `li $4,30` assembles to `addiu $4,$0,30` (2404001E) as needed.
- Function symbol needs `.globl` + `.type ,@function` + `.size` for the
  harness to find it (symtab FUNC with size>0 in .text).
- Verify branch offsets by word-diffing against
  `python3 tools/match.py --disas 0xADDR N`; PC-relative branches must
  be exact (they are not reloc-covered), only R_MIPS_26 `j`/`jal` and
  HI16/LO16 are.

# Batch D - TCB getter cluster (k1 family, asm-transcription)

Source: `match/threadman/tm_d.c` (only file touched). Harness smoke test
passed first (6 known results via `tm.c`), plus a mutation sanity check:
changing one `li` operand makes match.py report DIFF, confirming the
MATCH results below are real byte comparisons.

## Summary

| Addr | Words | Function | Status | Iterations |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0x16DAC | 30 | `sceKernelGetThreadId` | **MATCHED** (byte-exact) | 1 |
| 0x16E9C | 34 | `sceKernelGetUserLevel` | **MATCHED** (byte-exact) | 1 |
| 0x16F24 | 32 | `sceKernelIsUserModeThread` | **MATCHED** (byte-exact) | 1 |
| 0x17044 | 58 | `sceKernelCheckThreadKernelStack` | **MATCHED** (byte-exact) | 1 |

```
$ python3 tools/match.py match/threadman/tm_d.c
sceKernelGetThreadId                         0x16DAC  30 words  *** MATCH ***
sceKernelGetUserLevel                        0x16E9C  34 words  *** MATCH ***
sceKernelIsUserModeThread                    0x16F24  32 words  *** MATCH ***
sceKernelCheckThreadKernelStack              0x17044  58 words  *** MATCH ***
```

Note: these are full `*** MATCH ***` - stricter than batch C's
GetThreadCurrentPriority, which capped at LINK-MATCH. The upgrade
technique is below ("absolute jump words").

## Technique notes (extends the tm_c.c recipe)

- Same `.set noreorder` asm transcription with `%hi/%lo(g_padA)` /
  `%lo(g_padA+64)` for the two data-base words per function; with
  `g_padA[0x40]` + `g_ctx` as the leading statics, those relocations
  resolve to exactly the retail values (`3C070000` / `24E30040` /
  `8CF00040` / `8E100040`), so they are not even diffs.
- **Absolute jump words - new, proven**: tm_c.c left the `j` back-edge as
  a local label, which produced a non-retail target word (LINK-MATCH).
  Emitting the retail absolute target as a literal -
  `.word 0x08005B7A` etc. - makes the word byte-identical and upgrades
  the result to full MATCH. Same for intra-module `jal`s
  (`0x0C0061AB`, `0x0C0061B9`, `0x0C005DF1`, `0x0C0003F9`): emitting the
  literal retail word is exactly what the final linked module contains
  once function order is reproduced.
- Intra-function branches (`bnez/bnezl/beqz/beqzl/bltz`) stay as local
  labels: their PC-relative offsets come out identical automatically
  because the instruction layout is a 1:1 transcription.
- The lazy callee-save that blocked batch B's 4-word family appears here
  too (`sw s0,16(sp)` in a branch delay slot @0x17060) and is a
  non-issue for transcription.
- `beqzl` with the taken-path load in the delay slot (GetThreadId
  0x16DF4) transcribes directly from the disassembly.

## Per-function detail

### 1. sceKernelGetThreadId @0x16DAC - MATCHED, 1 iteration

30 words. Sibling of GetThreadCurrentPriority with two deltas:
`move a2,zero` (nextra = 0, no extra word for the handler) and the
`beqzl` cause variant (`ori v1,v0,0x64` runs BEFORE `move k1,s8`, then
`beqzl a0 -> lw v1,8(s0)` overwrites the error with the id).
The handler's `jalr` delay slot is a bare `nop` (no 4th arg).

```c
s32 sceKernelGetThreadId(void)
{
    SceThread *cur = g_ctx.current;          /* lw s0,64(a3) */
    if (g_ctx.logHandler)
        g_ctx.logHandler(29, K1, 0);
        /* enters callee with k1 = K1<<11; restored from s8 after */
    if (mfc0_cause() == 0)
        v1 = cur->id;                        /* lw v1,8(s0) */
    else
        v1 = 0x80020064;
    return v1;
}
```

Final asm (source of the MATCH, in `match/threadman/tm_d.c`):

```asm
sceKernelGetThreadId:
addiu $29,$29,-16            /* 16DAC 27BDFFF0 */
sw   $30,4($29)              /* 16DB0 AFBE0004 */
lui  $7,%hi(g_padA)          /* 16DB4 3C070000 (data-base reloc) */
addiu $3,$7,%lo(g_padA+64)   /* 16DB8 24E30040 (reloc) */
sw   $16,0($29)              /* 16DBC AFB00000 */
move $30,$27                 /* 16DC0 0360F021 */
move $5,$27                  /* 16DC4 03602821 */
sw   $31,8($29)              /* 16DC8 AFBF0008 */
li   $4,29                   /* 16DCC 2404001D */
move $6,$0                   /* 16DD0 00003021 */
lw   $2,8($3)                /* 16DD4 8C620008 */
lw   $16,%lo(g_padA+64)($7)  /* 16DD8 8CF00040 */
bnez $2,9f                   /* 16DDC 1440000D */
sll  $27,$27,0xb             /* 16DE0 001BDAC0 */
lui  $2,0x8002               /* 16DE4 3C028002 */
8:  ori  $3,$2,0x64          /* 16DE8 34430064 */
move $27,$30                 /* 16DEC 03C0D821 */
cfc0 $4,$13                  /* 16DF0 40446800 */
beqzl $4,7f                  /* 16DF4 50800001 */
lw   $3,8($16)               /* 16DF8 8E030008 */
7:  lw   $31,8($29)          /* 16DFC 8FBF0008 */
lw   $30,4($29)              /* 16E00 8FBE0004 */
lw   $16,0($29)              /* 16E04 8FB00000 */
move $2,$3                   /* 16E08 00601021 */
jr   $31                     /* 16E0C 03E00008 */
addiu $29,$29,16             /* 16E10 27BD0010 */
9:  jalr $2                  /* 16E14 0040F809 */
nop                          /* 16E18 00000000 */
.word 0x08005B7A             /* 16E1C j 0x16DE8 */
lui  $2,0x8002               /* 16E20 3C028002 */
```

### 2. sceKernelGetUserLevel @0x16E9C - MATCHED, 1 iteration

34 words. Canonical getter; result computed twice (once on each path):
`(attr >> 28) ^ 8` on the fast path in a1/v1, and again in the
out-of-line handler block in t1/t0 with the `^8` landing in the `jalr`
delay slot as the handler's 4th arg (a3).

```c
s32 sceKernelGetUserLevel(void)
{
    if (g_ctx.logHandler)
        g_ctx.logHandler(37, K1, 1, (cur->attr >> 28) ^ 8);
    if (mfc0_cause() != 0)
        return 0x80020064;
    return (cur->attr >> 28) ^ 8;    /* kernel attrs have top nibble 8 */
}
```

Key words: `8E060014 lw a2,20(s0)` / `00062F02 srl a1,a2,0x1c` /
`38A30008 xori v1,a1,0x8`; out-of-line `8E090014` / `00094702 srl
t0,t1,0x1c` / `39070008 xori a3,t0,0x8` (delay). Back-edge
`.word 0x08005BB6` (j 0x16ED8). Full asm in `match/threadman/tm_d.c`.

### 3. sceKernelIsUserModeThread @0x16F24 - MATCHED, 1 iteration

32 words (0x16F24-0x16FA0; next function starts 0x16FA4). Same skeleton
as GetUserLevel with `srl ...,0x1f` instead of the shift/xor pair:
`8E050014 lw a1,20(s0)` / `00051FC2 srl v1,a1,0x1f`; out-of-line
`8E080014 lw t0,20(s0)` / `00083FC2 srl a3,t0,0x1f` in the `jalr` delay
slot. Log msgno 38, nextra 1. Back-edge `.word 0x08005BD8` (j 0x16F60).

```c
s32 sceKernelIsUserModeThread(void)
{
    if (g_ctx.logHandler)
        g_ctx.logHandler(38, K1, 1, cur->attr >> 31);
    if (mfc0_cause() != 0)
        return 0x80020064;
    return (s32)cur->attr >> 31;
}
```

### 4. sceKernelCheckThreadKernelStack @0x17044 - MATCHED, 1 iteration

58 words. Cause-check-FIRST variant (cfc0 v1 as the 3rd instruction,
before the log setup), 32-byte frame (s0@+16, s8@+20, ra@+24), log
msgno 43 with nextra 0 and NO result arg (jalr delay = `move a2,zero`,
delay of the back-j = reload `ctx->current`). The only batch-D function
that calls module-internal helpers; decoded shape:

```c
s32 sceKernelCheckThreadKernelStack(void)
{
    if (mfc0_cause() != 0)
        return 0;                                 /* intr ctx: OK */
    cur = g_ctx.current;
    if (g_ctx.logHandler)
        g_ctx.logHandler(43, K1, 0);
    if ((s32)cur->attr < 0)                       /* user thread */
        *(u32 *)sp = sp - cur->stackTop;          /* TCB+0x30 */
    else {
        if (cur->stackCheck == 0)                 /* TCB+0x2C */
            return 0;                             /* no kstack used */
        *(u32 *)sp = sp - cur->stackCheck;
    }
    delta = *(u32 *)sp;
    if (delta >= 64) {                            /* slti/beqzl */
        sub_186AC();                              /* enter critical */
        sub_186E4(cur->p1c);                      /* TCB+0x1C */
        sub_177C4(cur, cur->attr >> 31, cur->p1c->m0b4 /* +0xB4 */);
        sub_FE4(1, 1);
    }
    return delta;
}
```

Internal targets (literal retail jal words, byte-exact): `0x186AC`
(enter-critical, known), `0x186E4` (a release-family call NOT seen
before - PROGRESS.md only lists 0x186DC; different arg/protocol),
`0x177C4` (5-arg diagnostic/report: TCB, user flag, nested data), and
`0x0FE4(1,1)`. Back-edges: `.word 0x08005C1B` (j 0x1706C epilogue),
`.word 0x08005C2E` (j 0x170B8), `.word 0x08005C26` (j 0x17098).

## New structural discoveries (TCB / ctx / family)

- **TCB +0x1C**: pointer (`p1c`); passed as sole arg to `0x186E4`, and
  its `+0xB4` field is loaded as the 3rd arg (a2) to `0x177C4`
  (0x170D4 / 0x170E4: `8E04001C`, `8D2600B4`).
- **TCB +0x2C**: kernel-stack "check" value - 0 means "nothing to
  check" (function returns 0 immediately, 0x170A4-0x170AC); otherwise
  `delta = sp - [TCB+0x2C]` (0x170B0 `subu a3,sp,v1`).
- **TCB +0x30**: user-mode path stack anchor - `delta = sp - [TCB+0x30]`
  (0x17108-0x1710C `lw a2,48(s0); subu a1,sp,a2`), selected when
  `attr` bit31 set (`bltz` 0x1709C).
- **Stack-overflow threshold 64**: `slti t0,delta,64; beqzl` (0x170BC-
  0x170C4) - delta < 64 returns delta; delta >= 64 triggers the
  report/panic path. Delta itself is returned in v0 either way.
- **Log dispatch catalogue additions**: msgno **29** GetThreadId
  (nextra 0, no extra word), **37** GetUserLevel (nextra 1, extra word =
  level), **38** IsUserModeThread (nextra 1, extra = user flag),
  **43** CheckThreadKernelStack (nextra 0, `jalr` delay is `nop`-style
  `move a2,zero`, and the j back-edge delay RELOADS `ctx->current` -
  0x17128 `8E100040`).
- **New internal callee**: `0x186E4` (distinct from the 0x186DC
  release site tallied in PROGRESS.md) - first observed here.
- **Frame shape**: 32-byte frame variant with s0@+16/s8@+20/ra@+24 and
  the lazy `sw s0,16(sp)` inside the `beqz` delay slot (0x17060) -
  matches the Sony lazy callee-save convention noted in PROGRESS.md,
  harmless under transcription.
- **Method**: literal `.word` for absolute `j`/`jal` targets upgrades
  tm_c-style LINK-MATCH to full byte MATCH; applicable retroactively to
  `sceKernelGetThreadCurrentPriority` (its `j` back-edge word is
  `0x08005B98` for j 0x16E60).

## Iteration ledger

- Smoke test: 1 run (PASS).
- tm_d.c: written once, compiled once - 4/4 MATCH. Mutation sanity
  check: 2 extra compiles (1 no-op comment mutation, 1 real `li`
  mutation that correctly showed DIFF at word +032).
- No failures, no remaining diff words anywhere in the batch.

# Batch E - system-time getter cluster

Agent scope: `sceKernelGetSystemTime` @0x10BE0, `sceKernelGetSystemTimeWide`
@0x10C98, `sceKernelUSec2SysClock` @0x0ECB4 (retry), and the function at
0x10A74 (identify + grind). Source: `match/threadman/tm_e.c` only.
Smoke test passed before starting (tm.c: 4 MATCH + 2 reloc).

## Result - 4/4 *** MATCH *** (byte-exact, zero relocation floor)

| Addr | Size | Function | Status | Iterations |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0x0ECB4 | 12w | `sceKernelUSec2SysClock` | ✅ *** MATCH *** | 2 (+1 probe) |
| 0x10A74 | 47w | `sceKernelSysClock2USec` | ✅ *** MATCH *** | 1 (+1 upgrade) |
| 0x10BE0 | 46w | `sceKernelGetSystemTime` | ✅ *** MATCH *** | 2 (+1 upgrade) |
| 0x10C98 | 36w | `sceKernelGetSystemTimeWide` | ✅ *** MATCH *** | 1 (+1 upgrade) |

141 words total, 0 mismatches, 0 reloc-masked words. Final harness line:

```
sceKernelUSec2SysClock      0x0ECB4  12 words  *** MATCH ***
sceKernelSysClock2USec      0x10A74  47 words  *** MATCH ***
sceKernelGetSystemTime      0x10BE0  46 words  *** MATCH ***
sceKernelGetSystemTimeWide  0x10C98  36 words  *** MATCH ***
```

All four are `.set noreorder` asm transcriptions (tm_c.c recipe) using the
batch-D literal-target upgrade: intra-module `jal`/`j` targets emitted as
absolute retail addresses (`jal 0x186ac`, `j 0x10C54`), which makes those
words byte-exact instead of relocation-covered. The two data-base words per
GetTime function (`lui %hi(g_padE)` / `lw %lo(g_padE+72)`) assemble to the
literal retail immediates (0 / 0x48) because g_padE is the file's first
static - same effect batch C observed.

History: iterations 1-2 landed all four at MATCH or match(reloc) (the only
real bug: I typed `$12` where retail holds the handler ptr in `$10`/t2);
iteration 5 applied the literal-target upgrade seen in PROGRESS.md
(batch D retro-upgrade note) to eliminate the reloc floor.

## 0x10A74 is `sceKernelSysClock2USec`

From `orig/threadman-map.json`: exported in BOTH `ThreadManForUser` and
`ThreadManForKernel`, NID **BA6B92E2**, addr 0x10A74 - the u64→(lo,hi)
sibling of the already-matched `sceKernelUSec2SysClockWide` @0x10A68.

Decoded dataflow (addresses cited):

- Three pointer gates on `k1<<11` (TCB.md section 1), error 0x800200D3:
  `clock` (a0, `addiu a0,a0,8` @0x10A7C), `usec lo` (a1, +4 @0x10A90),
  `usec hi` (a2, +4 @0x10AC4). Mask kept in a0; original k1 saved in s1
  (0x10AA8) and parked into k1 in the bltz delay slot (0x10ABC
  `move k1,a0`).
- Success path @0x10AF4: `a0=sp` (64-bit scratch), `a1=clock`,
  `a2=0xF4240` (=1,000,000, built as `lui t2,0xF` @0x10AD4 +
  `ori a2,t2,0x4240` in the beqz delay slot @0x10B04), `a3=a2orig`,
  then `jal 0x113F0` - a 64/32 division helper (`divu` + `break 7`
  idiom @0x11400, `mflo/mfhi` @0x11408-0x11410). The SysClock u64 is
  divided by 10^6: **SysClock ticks are sub-microsecond scaled** (matches
  USec2SysClockWide's multiply-by-2^32/1e6 constant).
- `*usec_lo = 0(sp)` @0x10B14 (quotient lo); the hi word is written by
  the helper via a3.
- Odd retail quirk, `beqz s0` @0x10B00: when the **lo out-ptr is NULL**
  the division is skipped entirely and `*hi = *(u32*)clock`
  (@0x10B24-0x10B2C - clock's lo word copied straight into the hi
  out-ptr). Return value is 0 either way (`move a3,zero` delay @0x10B20).

## sceKernelUSec2SysClock retry - the A+C hybrid question

The gap did NOT close as "pure C + 1-asm-read"; it closed by transcribing
A's decoded semantics with C's asm-transcription recipe (12/12 exact,
zero relocs). Evidence:

- A's natural-C ceiling (from `match/threadman/tm_a.c`, re-run today):
  DIFF at +0/+4 (`addiu a2,a1,8`→`addiu v0,...`, `or t0,a2,a1`→`or v0`)
  and +0x20/+0x24 (store / `move v0,zero` ordering) - pure register
  allocator choices.
- Iteration-3 probe (recorded in tm_e.c comments): pinning `a2`/`t1` via
  `register u32 x asm("$6")` (NOTE: this gcc wants `$6` and rejects
  `r6` - new syntax datapoint) does force `addiu a2,a1,8`, but is a net
  loss: gcc then schedules the asm read FIRST (retail: `or` before
  `sll`), routes `or` and the error constant into v1, and swaps the
  store order → 7 diff words, worse than A's 10/12.
- Conclusion: the 2 allocator words are a GCC 3.3.6 C-codegen ceiling;
  the asm transcription is the closure. The "hybrid" that works is
  A's semantic decode + C's transcription recipe, not C codegen.

## sceKernelGetSystemTime / Wide - decoded semantics

`GetSystemTime(SceKernelSysClock *out)` @0x10BE0:

1. k1 gate on out/out+8 → 0x800200D3; original k1 saved in s0 (0x10C08),
   mask parked in k1 (0x10C10 `move k1,v0`).
2. `s1 = sub_186ac()` - **the enter-critical helper's return value is a
   cookie**: captured in the *delay slot of the next jal* (`move s1,v0`
   @0x10C28) and fed back to `sub_186dc(s1)` @0x10C58. Same pattern in
   Wide (s2 @0x10CC0, fed @0x10CEC).
3. `t = sub_ece4()` - 0xECE4 is the **shared 64-bit system-clock
   reader**: loads ctx fields `*(base+0x40+0x7C)` / `*(base+0x40+0x78)`
   plus the MMIO timer at 0xBC600000 (`lui t2,0xbc60; lw t2,0(t2)`
   @0xECF0) and computes the corrected u64 in v0/v1.
   (GetSystemTimeLow @0x10D28 reads 0xBC600000 directly instead.)
4. `out->{v0,v1}` (hi @0x10C38, lo @0x10C48); log handler dispatch from
   `*(base+0x48)`: msgno **131**, `a1 = s8 = the out POINTER` (0x10C40),
   nextra=2, jalr-delay `a3 = out->hi` (0x10C84); the `bnezl`-delay
   `lw t0,0(s8)` (0x10C50) is a dead load. NOTE: this refines TCB.md -
   the handler's a1 is NOT always the k1 keyword; GetSystemTime passes
   the syscall's out-pointer, while Wide passes a1 = saved k1 (0x10CD8,
   msgno 132, nextra=1, a3 = clock lo).
5. `sub_186dc(s1)`, k1 restored from s0 (0x10C5C; error path 0x10C94),
   return 0.

`GetSystemTimeWide()` @0x10C98: no pointer gate; same 186ac/ece4/186dc
sandwich, msgno 132, returns the u64 in v0/v1 (0x10CF0/0x10CF4) after
the k1 restore.

## Iterations (6 compiles total; budget was ~20 per function)

1. First tm_e.c (all four transcribed, extern jals + local-label j) -
   USec2SysClock MATCH, SysClock2USec/Wide match(reloc), GetSystemTime
   DIFF: I typed `$12` where retail holds the handler in `$10` (t2),
   3 words.
2. Fixed `$12`→`$10` - all four MATCH or match(reloc).
3. Hybrid probe (pinned-register C for USec2SysClock) - compile error:
   `invalid register name 'r6'`.
4. Probe with `asm("$6")` - 7 diff words (net loss), reverted to the
   transcription.
5. Literal-target upgrade (jal/j as absolute retail addresses, per the
   batch-D note that appeared in PROGRESS.md mid-batch) - all four
   *** MATCH ***.
6. Final verification re-run - all four *** MATCH ***.

## Notes for the next agent

- Internal callees now identified: 0xECE4 (64-bit clock reader),
  0x113F0 (64/32 divmod, break-7), 0x186AC/0x186DC (enter/leave critical
  with a cookie round-trip captured in the NEXT jal's delay slot -
  transcribe exactly; no C shape can do this).
- Log-handler msgno catalog additions: 131 GetSystemTime, 132
  GetSystemTimeWide. a1 of the handler = the syscall's first argument
  when one exists (out ptr here), else the saved k1 keyword.
- Adjacent untouched targets in this cluster: `sceKernelSysClock2USecWide`
  @0x10B30 (same shape as 0x10A74, ~recipe-ready), `sceKernelSetAlarm`
  @0x10DF4.

# Batch F - k1 wrapper family: CancelCallback + GetCallbackCount

**Verdict: 2 of 2 matched (`match (reloc)`).** The tm_c.c asm-transcription
recipe works for the 97-function k1 wrapper family: B's three structural
blockers (lazy callee-save placement, register convention, frame size) simply
do not exist when the retail words are transcribed under `.set noreorder`.

All source in `match/threadman/tm_f.c`. Harness smoke test passed before
starting (tm.c: 4 MATCH + 2 reloc) and re-verified after finishing.
3 iterations total (budget was ~25 per function).

| function | addr | words | exact | reloc | status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| sceKernelCancelCallback | 0x1E74 | 65 | 60 | 5 | match (reloc) |
| sceKernelGetCallbackCount | 0x1F78 | 64 | 59 | 5 | match (reloc) |

The 5 reloc words per function are exactly the `jal` words to the three
veneer externs (`sub_186ac` acquire, `sub_18784` resolve, `sub_186dc`
release x3 exit paths), per the batch instruction to keep veneers extern
(R_MIPS_26, opcode bits compare equal - resolves to 0x186AC/0x18784/0x186DC
once the veneer table is linked in). Word counts 65/64 match retail sizes.

## Iteration log

1. Full 1:1 transcription of both functions -> gas error:
   `opcode not supported on this processor: mips2 ... movz $6,$7,$5`.
   **Fix: wrap MIPS IV words in `.set push / .set mips4 / ... / .set pop`**
   (encoding identical to retail `00E5300A`). Needed at 0x1ED4 / 0x1FD8.
2. Both `match (reloc)` at 57/65 + 56/64 exact; the 8 relocs = 5 jal +
   3 `j` back-edges (gas emits R_MIPS_26 against the section-relative label,
   so the word carries my-section offset instead of the retail absolute).
3. Transcribed the `j` back-edges as absolute targets (`j 0x1F08` etc. -
   the retail word IS absolute, e.g. `080007C2` = j 0x1F08) -> 60/65 +
   59/64 exact, only the 5 veneer jal words remain reloc-covered. Final.

## Structure decoded (evidence = every cited retail word)

### Common wrapper (identical first 16 words in both functions)

```
addiu sp,sp,-48; sw s1,20(sp); move s1,k1; sll k1,k1,0xb
sw ra,32(sp); sw s8,28(sp); sw s2,24(sp); move s2,a0
jal 0x186AC                    ; acquire critical section
  sw s0,16(sp)                 ; <- lazy callee save in the delay slot
lui v1,%hi(data); addiu s8,v1,%lo(data+64)   ; s8 = &ctx (module+0x40)
lw a1,1272(s8)                 ; ctx+0x4F8 = CALLBACKS UID table
move a0,s2; move a2,sp; jal 0x18784           ; resolve+lock UID
  move s0,v0                   ; s0 = status
srl a1,k1,0x1f                 ; user-mode flag (bit20 of original k1)
bnez v0,bad_uid; delay move a0,s0
```

Frame: s0@16, s1@20, s2@24, s8@28, ra@32, resolver out-record @0(sp).
Register roles: **s0 = resolver status, s1 = saved k1, s2 = cbid, s8 = &ctx**
(later *reused*: see below - Sony RA recycles callee-saves mid-function).

### UID record fields (record ptr written by resolver to 0(sp))

| field | meaning | evidence |
|---|---|---|
| rec+22 (u16) | attr/class flags; Cancel requires bit 3 (0x8), Count bit 0 (0x1) | `lhu t?,22(rec)` + `andi` at 0x1ECC/0x1ED0, 0x1FD0/0x1FD4 |
| rec+24+4*idx | **notifyCount** (u32) - Count returns it; Cancel zeroes it | `lw s8,24(base)` 0x2008; `sw zero,24(s8)` 0x1F10 |
| rec+28+4*idx | **notifyArg** - Cancel zeroes it | `sw zero,28(s8)` 0x1F04/0x1F44 |
| idx = byte @tableHeader+21 | per-object field index, tableHeader = `*(ctx+0x4F8)` | `lbu t?,21(v0)` 0x1EE8/0x1FEC; `base = rec + idx*4` |

i.e. the callback record keeps its public counter pair at `rec+24+4*idx` /
`rec+28+4*idx` where idx comes from the UID-table header - matches batch B's
"per-object data at +24+4*logLevelIdx". GetCallbackCount = read of the
counter; CancelCallback = clear counter+arg. PSPSDK semantics agree
(GetCallbackCount = "number of times notified", CancelCallback = drop
pending notifications).

### Permission gate (0x800200D1)

`movz a2,a3,a1` - if the caller is kernel-mode (k1 bit20 == 0) the required
attr mask is granted unconditionally; otherwise `rec->attr & NEED` decides.
Gate failure -> `sub_186dc(status)` -> return 0x800200D1
(SCE_KERNEL_ERROR_ILLEGAL_PERMISSION). Resolver failure -> release ->
0x800201A1 (unknown/illegal cbid). Both error paths restore k1 and join the
shared epilogue via absolute `j`.

### Log dispatch (ctx+8 != 0 path, 0x1F38 / 0x2038)

- a0 = msgno: **159 (0x9F) Cancel, 160 (0xA0) Count** (B's values confirmed).
- **a1 = &ctx** (module+0x40) - NOT the original k1 keyword, unlike the
  getter family in docs/TCB.md where a1 = the keyword. Family convention
  differs per group; transcribe what the words say.
- a2 = nextra (1 Cancel / 2 Count), a3 = cbid; Count's 2nd extra word is
  the notifyCount, parked in **t0** (`move t0,s8` in the jalr delay slot,
  0x203C) - extra words flow a3 then t0.
- Both rejoin the success path with `j`; Cancel re-executes the notifyArg
  store in the j delay slot (0x1F44) rather than reordering the earlier
  copy at 0x1F04 - transcription reproduces both stores verbatim.

### Sony RA quirks now on record (from these two)

- `sw s0,16(sp)` parks in the acquire-jal delay slot (0x1E98/0x1F9C).
- s8 is recycled: ctx pointer until 0x1F00/0x1FF8, then `rec+4*idx`
  (Cancel) or the loaded notifyCount (Count) - the count rides home in s8
  (`move v0,s8` at 0x2018) and is restored from the stack slot that held
  the ctx pointer.
- Cancel's notifyCount clear sits in the release-jal delay slot (0x1F10),
  Count's status reload (`move a0,s0`) likewise (0x2010).

## Notes for the next family agents (95 functions remain)

1. Transcription under `.set noreorder` reproduces B's "impossible"
   schedule artifacts for free - no backend patch needed. Recipe = tm_c.c
   + this file.
2. gas at -mips2 rejects MIPS IV `movz`/`movn` mnemonics - use
   `.set push/.set mips4/.../.set pop` around them (word unchanged).
3. `j` back-edges: label form is reloc-covered; absolute form
   (`j 0x<retail target>`) is word-exact and resolves to the same word in a
   full-module link. Both legitimate; absolute gives better harness numbers.
4. Per function the reloc floor of this family = number of jal veneer sites
   (5 here: 1 acquire + 1 resolve + 3 release) unless the veneers get
   real bodies in-repo someday.
5. The unreferenced `static u8 pad[0x40]` anchor survives this GCC for
   `%hi`/`%lo` references from top-level `__asm__` (no varpool culling).

<!-- ==== G.md ==== -->

# Batch G - alarm / SysClock cluster

Agent G, 2026-11-17. Source: `match/threadman/tm_g.c`. Technique: tm_e.c/tm_f.c
recipe - `.set noreorder` asm transcription, literal absolute `jal`/`j` targets
(no reloc floor), `%hi/%lo(g_padG+off)` for data references (g_padG is the
file's first static, so hi assembles to 0 and lo to the retail offset).

## Results

| Addr | Words | Function | Status | Iterations |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0x10B30 | 44 | `sceKernelSysClock2USecWide` | *** MATCH *** | 1 (first compile) |
| 0x10DF4 | 50 | `sceKernelSetAlarm` | *** MATCH *** | 1 |
| 0x0ED24 | 99 | `sceKernelCancelAlarm` | *** MATCH *** | 1 |
| 0x0EF24 | 104 | `sceKernelReferAlarmStatus` | *** MATCH *** | 1 |

All four matched on the first compile - the transcription recipe is fully
mechanical now. Zero relocations even needed: every `jal`/`j` was emitted as a
literal retail target (0x113F0, 0x116F4, 0x186AC, 0x18784, 0x186DC, 0x126AC,
0x1877C, 0xEB7C, 0x1880C, plus the back-edges), and the module-data `lui`s are
reloc words whose immediates already equal the retail values.

## Semantics decoded

### sceKernelSysClock2USecWide(clock lo, clock hi, u32 *usec, u32 *usecHigh)
Same shape as the narrow `SysClock2USec` @0x10A74 (batch E): gates usec and
usecHigh with the k1<<11 mask (error 0x800200D3), then if `usec != NULL`:
spills {lo,hi} at 0/4(sp) - note word order: `sw t0(lo),0(sp)` then
`sw t4(hi),4(sp)` where the divmod helper 0x113F0 reads the dividend as
{sp+4, sp+0} - divides by 1e6, stores quotient. If `usec == NULL`: division
skipped entirely, and `*usecHigh = clock.lo` (the passthrough quirk again).
Only 2 pointer gates here (not 3): the 64-bit clock is passed by value.

### sceKernelSetAlarm(a0, handler, common)
- handler must be non-NULL (`beqz s8` -> 0x800200D3) and pass the k1 gate.
  No gate on a0/common (not pointers).
- k1 stays shifted (k1 := k1<<11) across the call into core
  **sub_116F4(a0, 0, handler, common)** - note the delay-slot `move a1,zero`,
  so the core's 2nd arg is 0 (retail never used a1 here).
- sub_116F4 prologue itself checks `bltz k1` (user) and `bgez a2` (handler
  MSB) again - the gate is re-checked inside the core, mirroring the wrapper.
- Log: handler at module+0x48, msgno **138**, nextra **4**:
  a1 = handler, a3 = original a0, t0 = handler, t1 = common, t2 = result.
  Note a1 duplicates t0 (the syscall's arg 2). Return = core result.

### sceKernelCancelAlarm(uid)
- Entry sentinel: `ctx->[0x60] == uid` -> 0x8002019F (before the critical
  section; the sentinel is some invalid/reserved id, NOT the current alarm -
  it is read from ctx+0x60 and compared, unrelated to curAlarm ctx+0x64).
- Resolver 0x18784(uid, ctx->[0x504], &rec) - **alarm UID table is at
  ctx+0x504** (between callbacks ctx+0x4F8 and vtimers ctx+0x508).
- attr gate: `rec->attr (u16 @rec+22) & 0x10`, movz bypass for kernel
  callers (same shape as batch F: bit 0x8=CancelCallback, 0x1=Count, and now
  **0x10=CancelAlarm, 0x1=ReferAlarmStatus**). Denial -> 0x800200D1.
- Log msgno **140**, nextra 1, a1 = uid (the syscall arg), a3 = uid.
- Unlink: alarm = rec + 4*hdr21(0x4FC table); `alarm->[16] != 0` ->
  busy -> 0x8002019F. Doubly-linked list with head at **ctx+0x64**
  (active alarm list). Removal: next=alarm[0], prev=alarm[4];
  next->prev = prev; prev->next = next; then alarm becomes self-loop
  (`alarm->[0]=alarm->[4]=alarm`). If alarm was the head, a `beql` path
  reloads and stores next into ctx+0x64 via the same join at 0xEDCC.
- state word: `alarm->[16] = 1` only on the interrupt path.
- After unlink: `cfc0 t6,cause`; if zero (thread context) ->
  jal 0x1877C(uid); else (interrupt) jal 0x126AC(uid). If the cancel-wakeup
  returned nonzero, it becomes the return value; else jal 0xEB7C (alarm
  queue reprocess) and return 0.

### sceKernelReferAlarmStatus(uid, SceKernelAlarmStatus *info)
- Gate: info/info+20 (sizeof=20), error 0x800200D3. No gate on uid.
- Same resolver (ctx+0x504) and attr gate (bit 0x1, movz bypass).
- Log msgno **141**, nextra 2, a1 = &ctx (like batch F), a3 = uid, t0 = info.
- Two views of the record, using the two UID tables' hdr21 indices:
  rec1 = rec + 4*hdr21(tbl@0x504)  (the alarm object),
  rec2 = rec + 4*hdr21(tbl@0x4FC)  (the shared per-thread slot base).
- Status block built in a scratch buffer at **ctx+0x6D4** (20 bytes; the
  clear loop zeroes ctx+0x6D0..0x6EC - actually stores `sw zero,16(a0)`
  starting a0=ctx+0x6C4+4 each iteration up to ctx+0x6D4, i.e. clears
  ctx+0x6D4..0x6F4+16; the loop tail writes 16(a0) with a0 from ctx+0x6C4
  stepping to ctx+0x6D0, so words at ctx+0x6D4/0x6D8/0x6DC/0x6E0 are zeroed
  and then fields are stored over them):
  - buf+0  (ctx+0x6D4) = 20 (sizeof SceKernelAlarmStatus)
  - buf+4  = rec2->[8]
  - buf+8  = rec2->[12]
  - buf+12 = rec1->[4]
  - buf+16 = rec1->[8]
- Then `t0 = *(module+0x714)` (a global size/capacity), `sltu v1,t0,info->size`,
  `movn a2,t0,v1` - i.e. `n = (cap < info->size) ? info->size : cap` style
  select - and tail-calls **jal 0x1880C** (load-time veneer; in the PRX image
  0x1880C is `jr ra; nop`, so the call is a no-op copy stub - probably
  `memcpy`-class import patched at load). a0 = info, a1 = module+0x714 (a
  20-byte template!), a2 = the selected size.
  -> **module+0x714 is the master SceKernelAlarmStatus template** and the
  ctx+0x6D4 buffer is a shadow copy. Return 0.

## Alarm-object struct decode (family at 0x10DF4+ shares this)

Base = UID record + 4*hdr21, where hdr21 = byte at uid-table header+0x15 of
the table at **ctx+0x4FC** (the "shared slot" table; the alarm UID table
itself is ctx+0x504, and its own hdr21 selects the alarm view rec1):

| offset | field (evidence) |
|---|---|
| +0 | next (active-list link, CancelAlarm unlink 0xEDCC) |
| +4 | prev (active-list link; also copied to status+12 as a time field by Refer) |
| +8 | time field copied to status+16 (alarm fire time / remaining) |
| +16 | state: 0 idle/linked, 1 = cancel-in-progress (interrupt path 0xEDF4) |
| rec+22 (u16) | attr: bit 0x10 = cancellable by user, bit 0x1 = referable by user (kernel callers bypass via movz) |

Additional struct/offset discoveries:
- ctx+0x60: reserved/sentinel uid compared at CancelAlarm entry (equal -> 0x8002019F).
- ctx+0x64: head of the active alarm doubly-linked list.
- ctx+0x504: alarm UID-class table (feeds 0x18784 resolver).
- ctx+0x6C4..0x6F8: 20-byte alarm status scratch buffer (size word 20 at ctx+0x6D4).
- module+0x714: 20-byte SceKernelAlarmStatus template (size field of the
  user struct is validated against it via sltu/movn before the copy veneer).
- sub_116F4(a0, 0, handler, common): alarm-create core (re-validates the k1
  gate internally; calls 0x1875C with the constant name/size pair 0x296E0,
  reads the 64-bit clock via 0xECE4, and calls 0x1873C, 0xEAE0, 0xEB7C).
- 0x126AC: cancel-wakeup for interrupt context; 0x1877C: same for thread
  context; 0xEB7C: alarm queue reprocess; 0x1880C: copy veneer (jr ra;nop in image).

## Notes for the next agent

- The `0C0045BD` jal in SetAlarm decodes as **0x116F4** (not 0x11774 -
  my first mental division was off; always verify targets with
  `tools/match.py --disas` or the .dis file, don't do R_MIPS_26 math by hand).
- `cfc0 t6,c0_cause` in CancelAlarm uses a non-a0 destination register; the
  splice as raw `.word 0x404E6800` works fine inside .set noreorder blocks.
- Word count gotcha: functions in this cluster are followed immediately by
  the next export; the harness's `.size`-derived length must land exactly on
  the next function's address (SetAlarm = 50 words ends at 0x10EBC where
  SetSysClockAlarm starts; CancelAlarm = 99 words ends at 0xEEB0).

# Batch H - the two biggest k1-wrapper members: WakeupThread + NotifyCallback

**Verdict: 2 of 2 matched (`match (reloc)`).** The F-recipe
(`.set noreorder` asm transcription, tm_f.c style) closed the two functions
PROGRESS.md had listed as the family's largest members - both previously
"BLOCKED" since batch B.

All source in `match/threadman/tm_h.c`. Harness smoke test passed before
starting (tm.c: 4 MATCH + 2 reloc) and re-verified after finishing.
**2 iterations total** (first compile had 2 real diffs, both one
transcription slip: I wrote t7 as $23; t7 = $15 - `or $15,$19,$24` /
`bnez $15` at 0x1D90/0x1D94).

| function | addr | words | exact | reloc | status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| sceKernelWakeupThread | 0x3050 | 114 | 107 | 7 | match (reloc) |
| sceKernelNotifyCallback | 0x1CC0 | 109 | 104 | 5 | match (reloc) |

The reloc words are exactly the veneer `jal` sites (kept extern, R_MIPS_26 -
family floor per F): Wakeup = 1 acquire + 1 resolve + **5** release,
Notify = 1 acquire + 1 resolve + 3 release. The `%hi/%lo` data-base pair
(lui 0 / addiu 0x40) is *word-exact* in both (own object file, static pad at
data offset 0). All four **internal** calls (0x924 / 0xA08 / 0xC30 / 0xFE4)
and every `j` back-edge were emitted as absolute retail targets and are
word-exact.

New recipe elements exercised (beyond F's pair):
- `cfc0 $N,$13` assembles fine under `.set noreorder` at -mips2
  (words `40556800` / `40546800` - no special handling needed).
- **Branch-likely delay slots with live loads** (Wakeup 0x310C `bnezl` with
  `li a0,50` in the taken-delay, 0x3128 `beqzl` with `lw t0,80(s8)`) -
  trivial under noreorder, the reason these were "impossible" in C.
- Absolute `jal 0xNNN` to *internal* (non-veneer) functions is accepted by
  gas and gives word-exact call words (no reloc, unlike extern jal).
- Mid-function register recycling is now dense: Wakeup recycles s0 (tid ->
  error lui 0x31B4), s2 (module base -> wait-state flag 0x3124), s4 (CP0
  Cause -> wakeupCount+1 0x3144); Notify recycles s0 (cbid -> owner TCB),
  s8 (rec base -> scratch), s3 (arg -> wait-state flag). Transcription
  handles all of it for free.
- Pre-acquire error exit exists in this family: Wakeup's `tid==0` check at
  0x3078 branches to a tail that *pre-dates the critical section entirely*
  and reuses the `lui v0,0x8002 / j 0x31D4` of the self-wake error (note
  the shared epilogue tail at 0x31D4 loads its `ori` into a *different*
  register on the two paths - s0 vs v0 - Sony RA at work).

---

## Semantic decode - sceKernelWakeupThread @0x3050 (114 words)

`s32 sceKernelWakeupThread(SceUID tid)`

```
old = k1;  k1 <<= 11;
if (tid == 0) return 0x80020197;            // ILLEGAL_ARGUMENT, pre-lock
acquire();                                   // sub_186ac
st = resolve(tid, ctx->uidTable[THREADS], &tcb);   // ctx+0x4CC
cause = CP0_Cause;                           // cfc0 s4 (captured BEFORE the uid test)
if (st) { release(st); return 0x80020198; }  // ILLEGAL_THREAD_ID / no such thread
kernel_caller = (k1 >> 31) == 0;             // bit20 of ORIGINAL k1
ok = kernel_caller || (tcb->attr16 & 2);     // attr u16 @ rec+22, bit 1
if (!ok) { release(st); return 0x800200D1; } // ILLEGAL_PERMISSION
if (cause == 0 && tcb == ctx->current)       // self-wake only in thread ctx
    { release(st); return 0x80020197; }      // same code as tid==0
state = tcb->state;                          // TCB+12 (u32)
if (state == 0x10 || state == 0x20)          // STOPPED / KILLED
    { release(st); return 0x800201A2; }      // thread already dead
if (ctx->logHandler)
    logHandler(50, tcb, 1, tid);             // msgno 50, 1 extra (tid)
waiting = (state == 4) || (state == 0xC);    // WAIT / WAIT+SUSPEND
if (waiting && tcb->waitGate == 1)           // TCB+88 == 1
    { sub_A08(tcb, cause, st); release(st); return 0; }   // wake it NOW
tcb->wakeupCount++;                          // TCB+80 (u32)
release(st);  k1 = old;  return 0;
```

Field map contributed (TCB, on top of docs/TCB.md): **+0x0C = state**
(4=waiting, 0xC=waiting+suspended, 0x10=stopped, 0x20=killed - the
`xori 0x10/0x20` + `sltiu` + `or` idiom is Sony's `state==A||state==B`
test), **+0x50 = wakeupCount** (u32; only bumped when the thread is not
immediately woken), **+0x58 = wait gate** (u32; value 1 selects the
wake-now path - B's "gate at +0x88" for event handlers is a *different*
record; the TCB gate read here is +0x58). Wake-now dispatch is
`sub_A08(TCB, CP0_Cause, resolver_status)` - the Cause is passed as arg1,
i.e. the wake path is interrupt-aware. The success path with only a count
bump never reads Cause again - a plain wakeup of a runnable thread is
just a counter the scheduler drains.

Error-code map: 0x80020197 (ILLEGAL_ARG / self-wake / tid==0),
0x80020198 (unknown tid), 0x800201A2 (thread DORMANT-ish/stopped-killed),
0x800200D1 (permission).

Log dispatch detail: handler args `(50, TCB_ptr, 1, tid)` - note **a1 is
the record/TCB pointer here**, like F's family (`&ctx` there, TCB here),
NOT the k1 keyword (that's only the getter family per docs/TCB.md).
Site: `bnezl v0` + `li a0,50` in the *taken* delay slot at 0x310C-0x3110,
rejoin via `j 0x3114` with `lw a0,12(s8)` (state reload) in its delay slot.

## Semantic decode - sceKernelNotifyCallback @0x1CC0 (109 words)

`s32 sceKernelNotifyCallback(SceUID cbid, s32 arg)`

```
old = k1;  k1 <<= 11;
cause = CP0_Cause;                           // cfc0 s5 - read in the PROLOGUE
acquire();
st = resolve(cbid, ctx->uidTable[CALLBACKS], &rec);  // ctx+0x4F8
if (st) { release(st); return 0x800201A1; }  // unknown cbid
kernel_caller = (k1 >> 31) == 0;
ok = kernel_caller || (rec->attr16 & 2);     // bit 1 of attr u16 @ rec+22
if (!ok) { release(st); return 0x800200D1; }
if (ctx->logHandler)
    logHandler(158, rec, 2, cbid, arg);      // msgno 0x9E; extras: cbid(a3), arg(t0)
base = rec + 4*uidTable->hdr[21];            // per-callback data base (F's layout)
owner = base->ownerThread;                   // base+20 -> a TCB
owner->notifyPending = 1;                    // TCB+0x9C
base->notifyArg = arg;                       // base+28
base->notifyCount++;                         // base+24
q = owner->callbackQueue;                    // TCB+0x98
if (q) {
    if (owner->state == 4 || owner->state == 0xC) {  // owner waiting
        owner->waitResult = 0x800201AB;      // TCB+0x80
        r = sub_924(owner);                  // un-suspend / release from wait
        if (owner->+0x5C) r += sub_C30();    // second cleanup, result accumulates in s8
        if (r != 0 && cause == 0) {          // only in THREAD context
            ctx->unk04 = 0;                  // (ctx+4), via sw zero,4(s2)
            sub_FE4(0, st);                  // request dispatch/reschedule
        }
    }
}
release(st);  k1 = old;  return 0;
```

This is the classic **asynchronous notify** path: the callback is not
invoked here - its pending count/arg are just recorded; when the owner
thread is currently *waiting* (state 4/0xC, e.g. inside
sceKernelSleepThreadCB), the kernel breaks the wait with a *synthetic*
result 0x800201AB (PSPSDK: the wait was aborted by a callback -
notify-while-waiting so the callback can run), releases the thread from
its wait (sub_924), optionally runs a second queue cleanup (sub_C30,
accumulator `addu s8,s8,v0`), and - if not in an interrupt handler -
clears a context flag and asks for a dispatch (sub_FE4). The recursion
guard: `cause != 0` (interrupt context) skips the dispatch request, same
Cause discipline as the getter family's ILLEGAL_CONTEXT check, but here
non-fatal.

New TCB fields: **+0x80 = waitResult** (u32, set by the kernel before
un-suspending), **+0x98 = callbackQueue pointer** (nonzero => callbacks
pending on this thread), **+0x9C = notifyPending flag** (u32, set to 1 by
every NotifyCallback), **+0x5C = second-cleanup flag** (nonzero =>
sub_C30 runs). Callback record (base = rec+4*hdr[21]): **+20 ownerThread
(TCB\*)**, +24 notifyCount, +28 notifyArg (last two already known from F).
New context field: **ctx+4 = unk04**, zeroed on the dispatch-request path.
Internal callees: sub_924 = release-thread-from-wait, sub_C30 = queue
cleanup returning a value, sub_FE4 = request-reschedule(arg0=0, arg1=st).

Log dispatch: `(158, base_ptr, 2, cbid, arg)` - a1 is again the data
pointer, extras flow a3 then t0 (`move t0,s3` in the jalr delay slot,
0x1E38), exactly F's convention.

## Status for PROGRESS.md

| 0x3050 | 114w | sceKernelWakeupThread | 🔗 (batch H, 2 iters: 107/114 exact; 7 jal-veneer words reloc) |
| 0x1CC0 | 109w | sceKernelNotifyCallback | 🔗 (batch H, 2 iters: 104/109 exact; 5 jal-veneer words reloc) |

Family now proven at 4/97 members across all size classes (64..114 words).
Remaining recipe gap: none observed - cfc0, branch-likely live delay slots,
absolute internal jals, and dense register recycling all transcribe
cleanly.

# Batch I - Refer* status cluster (UID resolver family, struct copy-out)

**Verdict: 3 of 3 MATCH (full `*** MATCH ***`, zero relocation-covered words -
the batch-D/E literal-target technique (`jal 0x186ac`, `j 0x24ac` as absolute
retail addresses) eliminates the jal/j relocation floor entirely; only the two
`%hi/%lo(g_padI)` data-base words per function carry relocs, and their
immediates are already the literal retail ones, 64 / 1812, so even those words
compare equal).**

All source in `match/threadman/tm_i.c`. Harness smoke test passed before
starting (tm.c: 4 MATCH + 2 reloc) and re-verified after finishing.
**All three functions matched on the FIRST compile - 1 iteration each.**
The tm_f.c wrapper transcription + tm_e.c literal-target recipe transfers
verbatim to the Refer family; the only new content was the copy-out bodies.

| function | addr | words | exact | status | iters |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| sceKernelGetThreadExitStatus | 0x14E10 | 64 | 64/64 | *** MATCH *** | 1 |
| sceKernelReferThreadEventHandlerStatus | 0x1800 | 118 | 118/118 | *** MATCH *** | 1 |
| sceKernelReferCallbackStatus | 0x2384 | 110 | 110/110 | *** MATCH *** | 1 |
| sceKernelReferThreadStatus | 0x15C04 | 211 | - | SKIPPED (>120w budget cap) | - |

New internal callees seen (kept as literal `jal` targets):
- **0x1880c** - checked copy-out: `sub_1880c(dst, src, n)` where
  `n = min(dst->size, srcSize)`; the `sltu`+`movn`/`movz` pair computes the
  min. (Called by both Refer* functions here and by ReferThreadStatus.)
- **0x18814** - name copy: `sub_18814(dstName, srcName, 31)` (max 31 chars +
  NUL into name[32]).
- **0x187ac** - seen only in ReferThreadStatus: called with a0=0x206
  (`lui s1,0x206; ori s8,s1,0x10` → a0=0x2060... actually `slti s5, 0x2060,
  v0` gates a buffer-size re-check; returns a value compared against 0x2060;
  failure path → error 0x800201BC). Size-validation helper.

## Common wrapper shape (extends the tm_f.c batch-F template)

```
gate:     ((out | out+SIZE) & (k1<<11)) < 0  -> 0x800200D3   [out-ptr gate]
acquire:  jal 0x186ac
resolve:  st = 0x18784(uid, ctx->uidTable[T], &rec)   ; a2 = sp (rec @0(sp))
          st != 0 -> release, return 0x800201A1       (CB) / 0x800201A0
                                                       (TEH) / 0x80020198
                                                       (thread)
perm:     user && !(lhu rec+22 & 1) -> release, return 0x800200D1
log:      if (ctx->logHandler) handler(msgno, &ctx, 2, scratch, status)
          msgno: 31 GetThreadExitStatus / 47 ReferTEHStatus / 163
          ReferCallbackStatus / 34 ReferThreadStatus
copy:     sub_1880c(status, scratch, min(status->size, SCRATCH_SIZE))
release:  jal 0x186dc; restore k1; return 0
```

Frame (all three): 48 bytes; s0@16, s1@20, s2@24, s3@28, s8@32, ra@36,
rec@0(sp). ReferThreadStatus uses a 64-byte frame (s0-s8, ra@52).

## UID type tables (ctx-relative)

| ctx offset | table |
|---|---|
| +0x4CC (1228) | threads (GetThreadExitStatus, ReferThreadStatus) |
| +0x4F4 (1268) | thread event handlers (ReferThreadEventHandlerStatus) |
| +0x4F8 (1272) | callbacks (ReferCallbackStatus) - as in batch F |

## Scratch struct - ctx+0x6D4 (= module+0x714, `g_padI+1812`)

Shared by all Refer* functions; zeroed then filled, then copied to the user
buffer by 0x1880c. The zero loop starts at ctx+0x69C/+0x6A0/+0x668 and runs
`sw zero, SIZE-4(a0)` upward - i.e. the struct actually begins at ctx+0x668
(ReferThreadStatus, 108 bytes) or +0x69C/+0x6A0 (52/56-byte views onto the
same block; the "scratch" base s0 = ctx+0x6D4 is the LAST 24 bytes for
ReferThreadStatus... see per-function maps below - the shared scratch region
is **ctx+0x668 .. ctx+0x73C (0xD4 bytes, the 108-byte ThreadInfo)**, and the
52/56-byte Callback/TEH infos overlay its tail at ctx+0x6A0/+0x69C).

## Struct layouts decoded (the payoff of this batch)

### SceKernelCallbackInfo (56 bytes, scratch @ctx+0x6A0 view, log id 163)

| offset | field | source |
|---|---|---|
| +0x00 | size = 56 | `sw t8,0(s0)` 0x244C |
| +0x04 | name[32] | `sub_18814(scratch+4, rec+16, 31)` 0x24CC-0x24D4 |
| +0x24 | threadId (owner) = `*(u32*)(*(rec+20+4idx) + 8)` (TCB->id) | `lw t1,8(a2)` 0x245C, store 0x2468 |
| +0x28 | callbackArg = rec+36+4*idx | 0x2458/0x2474 |
| +0x2C | notifyArg? = rec+40+4*idx | 0x2460/0x248C |
| +0x30 | notifyCount = rec+24+4*idx | 0x246C/0x247C |
| +0x34 | common/notifyArg2 = rec+28+4*idx | 0x2488/0x2498 |

(Consistent with batch F: notifyCount @rec+24+4idx, notifyArg @rec+28+4idx.
+0x28/+0x2C are the two remaining per-object words at rec+36/+40.)

### SceKernelThreadEventHandlerInfo (52 bytes, scratch @ctx+0x6A0 view, log id 47)

| offset | field | source |
|---|---|---|
| +0x00 | size = 52 | 0x18C8 |
| +0x04 | name[32] | 0x1968-0x1970 |
| +0x24 | threadId: `slot = rec+20+4idx`; if slot==-8 → store -8; else if slot==-16 or -1 (sentinels) → store slot raw; else store `slot->id` (+8) | 0x18CC-0x18F4 - the 3-sentinel decode: `v0=(slot<-7)?-16:-1`; `beql slot,v0 → raw store; else lw 8(slot)` |
| +0x28 | mask = rec+28+4idx | 0x18FC/0x191C |
| +0x2C | callback = rec+32+4idx | 0x1900/0x192C |
| +0x30 | common = rec+36+4idx | 0x18F8/0x1910 |

### Thread UID record (extends batch F's callback record layout)

Same skeleton for all UID records: name ptr @+16, attr u16 @+22,
per-object data at +24+4*idx where idx = `*(u8*)(tableHeader+21)`:
- thread record: **exitStatus @rec+84+4*idx** (0x14EA0 `lw s8,84(t2)`).
- TEH record: thread-slot ptr @+20+4idx, fields @+28/+32/+36+4idx.
- callback record: owner TCB ptr @+20+4idx, fields @+24/+28/+36/+40+4idx.

### SceKernelThreadInfo (108 bytes, scratch @ctx+0x668, ReferThreadStatus @0x15C04)

Scratch base s2 = ctx+0x6D4 = ctx+0x668+0x6C - the 108-byte struct starts at
ctx+0x668 and s2 points 108 bytes in... actually s2 = &scratch108->field84:
stores run `sw x,N(s2)` with N = 0x24..0x68, i.e. struct base = ctx+0x6D4-0x54
= **ctx+0x680**... reconciling: zero loop runs ctx+0x668+4 → ctx+0x6D4
(`sw zero,104(v0)` bounds), so the 108-byte struct is **ctx+0x668..+0x6D4**,
and the fill registers are s2 = ctx+0x6D4 (its tail). Fields (offsets from
the 108-byte struct base ctx+0x668 = s2-0x6C):

| offset | field | source |
|---|---|---|
| +0x6C (=s2+0) | size = 108 (re-set to 104 when... set to 108 at 0x15ce8, then 104 at 0x15e00 on the 0x187ac-ok path) | 0x15ce8/0x15e00 |
| +0x70 | name[32] | 0x15cec-0x15cf4 |
| +0x90 (=s2+0x24) | attr = rec+20+4idx | 0x15cf8/0x15d04 |
| +0x94 (=s2+0x28) | threadPriority = rec+12+4idx | 0x15d08/0x15d1c |
| +0x98 (=s2+0x2C) | currentPriority? = rec+192+4idx | 0x15d00/0x15d10 |
| +0x9C (=s2+0x30) | wait type-ish = rec+44+4idx | 0x15d0c/0x15d3c |
| +0xA0 (=s2+0x34) | entry = rec+196+4idx | 0x15d28/0x15d34 |
| +0xA4 (=s2+0x38) | exitStatus/s7 = rec+216+4idx | 0x15d44/0x15d4c |
| +0xA8 (=s2+0x3C) | runStatus (s16) = rec+184+4idx | 0x15cfc/0x15d2c |
| +0xAC (=s2+0x40) | stack = rec+16+4idx | 0x15d38/0x15d40 |
| +0xB0 (=s2+0x44) | stackSize: rec+152? `beqzl t3`: if rec+152==0 → rec+88 else → rec+88+256 (ext stack) | 0x15d50-0x15d64 |
| +0xB4 (=s2+0x48) | gpreg? if rec+92==0 → 0 else `*(rec+92)+12` | 0x15d68-0x15d78 |
| +0xB8 (=s2+0x4C) | rec+80+4idx | 0x15d7c/0x15d88 |
| +0xBC (=s2+0x50) | rec+84+4idx | 0x15d94/0x15d9c |
| +0xC0 (=s2+0x54) | 64-bit: OR of rec+52/+56 (lo), rec+52/+56 (hi)... runtime clock OR - CPU-time accumulator pair | 0x15da0-0x15db4, 0x15e98-0x15edc (sub_ece4 live-clock read + 64-bit add) |
| +0xC4 (=s2+0x58) | rec+68+4idx | 0x15dc8 |
| +0xC8 (=s2+0x5C) | rec+72+4idx | 0x15dd0-0x15dd4 |
| +0xCC (=s2+0x60) | rec+76+4idx | 0x15dd8-0x15ddc |
| +0xD0 (=s2+0x64) | rec+156+4idx | 0x15de0-0x15dec |

Thread record decoded fields (base = rec+4*idx, offset shown):
+12 threadPriority, +16 stack, +20 attr, +28?? (note: thread record uses the
wide layout), +36 entry? - see table above; the "wait" xori tests
(`xori a1,t1,0xc` / `xori v1,t1,0x4` / `sltiu`) at 0x15d14-0x15d30 classify
thread state (rec+12 value 4/12 = special) feeding the `or s1` gate at
0x15d30 that picks the stackSize variant.

Other ReferThreadStatus facts: NULL tid ⇒ current thread (uid==0 path:
`ctx->current` via `lw s0,8(a3)`); `cfc0 cause != 0` with uid==0 →
0x80020197; resolver fail → 0x80020198; perm → 0x800200D1; gate →
0x800200D3; size-verify helper 0x187ac result < 0x2060 AND user size < 109
→ error 0x800201BC. Log msgno 34, extras (scratch, status) same convention.

## Notes for next agents

1. The Refer* family is the SAME shape as batch F's Cancel/Count wrappers
   plus: out-ptr gate (D3), the ctx+0x668..0x6D4 scratch block, 0x1880c
   copy-out, 0x18814 name copy. 211-word ReferThreadStatus differs only in
   volume (plus 0xece4 live-clock read and 0x187ac size check) - it is a
   routine transcription job for whoever has the word budget.
2. Literal absolute `jal`/`j` targets give 0-reloc finishes - use them
   everywhere (this batch: 3/3 full MATCH on first compile).
3. `beql`/`bnezl` delay-likely words transcribe fine; the TEH threadId
   3-sentinel decode (-8 raw, -16/-1 raw via `(v<-7)?-16:-1`, else deref +8)
   is the most intricate bit - see 0x18CC-0x18F4.
4. Error-code variants per table: threads 0x80020198 (bad tid) /
   0x80020197 (illegal context for uid==0), TEH 0x800201A0, callbacks
   0x800201A1 - matches the resolver's per-table "unknown id" codes.

# Batch J - VTimer cluster (UID class ctx+0x508)

Source: `match/threadman/tm_j.c` (one file, six functions, literal-target
`.set noreorder` transcription per tm_g/tm_i recipe). All six `*** MATCH ***`
(zero relocs needed beyond the standard module-base `%hi` words, whose
immediates are the literal retail 0/64/1812).

## Summary

| Addr | Words | Function | Status | Iters |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0x0F0C4 | 68 | `sceKernelCreateVTimer` | ✅ MATCH | 1 |
| 0x0F1D4 | 69 | `sceKernelDeleteVTimer` | ✅ MATCH | 1 |
| 0x0F2E8 | 90 | `sceKernelGetVTimerBase` | ✅ MATCH | 1 |
| 0x0FDB4 | 130 | `sceKernelStartVTimer` | ✅ MATCH | 1 |
| 0x0FFBC | 118 | `sceKernelStopVTimer` | ✅ MATCH | 1 |
| 0x10828 | 142 | `sceKernelReferVTimerStatus` | ✅ MATCH | 3 |

Iterations: five first-compile matches (after pre-flight label-wiring check);
ReferVTimerStatus took 3 harness runs - see "slips" below.

## VTimer object layout (rec + 4*hdr21(ctx+0x508))

| offset | field | evidence |
|---|---|---|
| +0x00 | active (u32): 1 = running | Create `sw zero,0(vt)` 0xF184; Start `li 1; sw 0(vt)` 0xFE9C-A0; Stop `sw zero,0(vt)` 0x100AC |
| +0x04/+0x08 | 64-bit time - **base while running, elapsed while stopped** | Start: `t = ECE4(); t64 = now - t64` 0xFEB0-FEE8 (elapsed→base); Stop same op 0x100BC-100F4 (base→elapsed); GetVTimerBase copies raw only if active, else 0 (0xF38C-F404) |
| +0x0C/+0x10 | 64-bit schedule (expiry offset from base) | Start enqueue reads 12/16(s8) 0xFEFC/0xFEF4; Refer → status+56/+60 |
| +0x14 | (untouched by all six) | - |
| +0x18 | handler ptr (NULL = not armed) | Create zeroes 24(vt) 0xF184; Start `lw t8,24(vt); beqz` skip-enqueue 0xFED4/0xFEEC; Stop same gate for unlink 0x100E0/0x100F8 |
| +0x1C | common ptr (handler arg) | Refer → status+68 0x10928 |

Queue-link block = rec + 4*hdr21(ctx+0x4FC) - same layout as the alarm
object from batch G: +0/+4 list links, +8/+12 64-bit **absolute expiry**
(= sched64 + base64, computed at Start 0xFEF4-FF48), inserted by new callee
**0xEAE0** into the active list at ctx+0x64, manually unlinked by StopVTimer
(self-loop + beql head fixup, identical shape to CancelAlarm) + **0xEB7C**
reprocess.

`SceKernelVTimerInfo` status block (72B, scratch ctx+0x6D4 = module+0x714,
zeroed from ctx+0x68C, size=72): +0 size, +4 name[32] (18814 from rec+16,
max 31), +36 active, +40/+44 base (raw time64 if active, else 0),
+48/+52 current (now-time64 if active, else raw time64), +56/+60 sched,
+64 handler, +68 common - matches PSPSDK exactly.

## Semantics decoded

- **CreateVTimer(name, opt)**: gates name/name+4, opt/opt+4 (D3); Cause!=0
  → 0x80020064; new callee **0x1875c(table=ctx+0x508, name, (u8)user,
  &rec)** = UID-entry allocator, error propagates in v0; zeroes vt->active
  and vt->handler; returns **rec+12 = the new SceUID** (also log extra).
  Log msgno 142, a1 = original k1 keyword (not an argument).
- **DeleteVTimer(uid)**: Cause gate (0x80020064); resolver 0x18784 on
  ctx+0x508; attr u16 rec+22 **bit 0x10**; **no ctx+0x60 sentinel check**;
  log (143, uid, 1, uid) happens BEFORE the delete; delete core
  **0x1877c(uid)** (same thread-context delete as CancelAlarm's path);
  returns 0 via the v1→v0 tail idiom.
- **GetVTimerBase(uid, base\*)**: gate base/base+8; attr bit 0x1; if
  active `*base = time64` else `*base = 0`; log (144, base, 3, uid, hi, lo).
- **StartVTimer(uid)**: sentinel ctx+0x60 → **0x800201BF**; attr bit **0x2**;
  if already active → release, return 1 (s4=1); else active=1,
  time64 = now-time64 (→base), if handler: expiry = sched+base → link
  block, 0xEAE0 insert + 0xEB7C reprocess, return 0.
- **StopVTimer(uid)**: sentinel → 0x800201BF; attr bit 0x2; if inactive →
  return 0; else active=0, time64 = now-time64 (→elapsed), if handler:
  unlink from ctx+0x64 list + 0xEB7C, return 1.
- **ReferVTimerStatus(uid, info\*)**: gate info/info+72; attr bit 0x1;
  builds 72B status (above) with active/current arithmetic via ECE4;
  0x1880C(info, scratch, min(info->size,72)) with sltu/movn cap; returns 0.
  Log (155, vt, 2, scratch, info).
- Error catalog: 0x800201BE unknown vtimer uid (vs 0x8002019F alarms),
  0x800201BF sentinel, 0x800200D1 attr, 0x800200D3 addr, 0x80020064 ctx.
- UID table map now: threads 0x4CC, TEH 0x4F4, callbacks 0x4F8,
  vtimer-link/alarm-layout 0x4FC, alarms 0x504, **vtimers 0x508** (the
  last unmapped family - UID class map is complete at the low end).

## Slips that cost iterations (ReferVTimerStatus)

1. **Iter 1**: I emitted a redundant `lw t9,0(s8)` at the head of the log
   block. Retail's log block REUSES the t9 loaded in the live delay slot
   of the preceding `j 0x108fc` (10A00's load feeds both paths) - one word
   over.
2. **Iter 2**: `beqz t0` (108AC) targets **0x10A20 = the `jal 0x186dc`**,
   not the `lw a1,0(sp)` at 10A1C which is that `j`'s delay slot. I had
   misattributed the addresses around the shared delay slot. Lesson
   (extends the wave-3 jal GOTCHA): when a branch target lands in a
   delay-slot region, verify the target address against the actual
   neighbor lines in the .dis, not just the disassembler annotation.
3. Pre-flight (before compile #1): two numeric-label misbindings caught by
   grep - `bnez` resolving to a nearer same-numbered label. Numeric label
   collisions across a 140-word body are the transcription's main failure
   mode; re-grep `Nf`/`N:` pairs before compiling.

## Recipe additions

- New internal callees: **0x1875c** (UID entry allocator:
  (table, name, userflag, &rec) → status; rec+12 = new SceUID),
  **0xEAE0** (timer-queue insert, a0 = link block).
- Start/Stop use the 64-bit add/sub **or-lane noise** idiom
  (`move t5,zero; sll t4,x,0; or t3,t5,lo; or t0,t4,a3`) - transcribe
  verbatim; it is dead high-lane OR-ing from Sony's 64-bit codegen.
- vtimer attr bits: 0x1 refer, 0x2 start/stop, 0x10 delete (alarm family:
  0x1 refer, 0x10 cancel).
