# Batch K - thread lifecycle cluster (ReferThreadStatus / Suspend / Resume / Start)

**Verdict: 4 of 4 `*** MATCH ***` (full byte-exact, zero relocation-covered
diffs).** All source in `match/threadman/tm_k.c`. Harness smoke test passed
before starting and re-verified after finishing (tm.c: 4 MATCH + 2 reloc).

The 211-word `sceKernelReferThreadStatus` "monster" queued by batch I fell on
the **first compile** - batch I's struct decode was a complete blueprint and
the tm_f/tm_i literal-target transcription recipe is fully mechanical at this
scale. `sceKernelStartThread` (299 words per the harness) also matched first
compile despite five non-MIPS-II words (`ins` x2, `rotrv`, `cache` x2, `sync`)
spliced as raw `.word` - **the "ins/ext = blocked" rule from the early guide
is superseded: under noreorder transcription nothing is blocked by exotic
opcodes, only by data layout.**

| function | addr | words | status | iters |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| sceKernelReferThreadStatus | 0x15C04 | 211 | *** MATCH *** | 1 |
| sceKernelSuspendThread | 0x357C | 133 | *** MATCH *** | 2 |
| sceKernelResumeThread | 0x3790 | 125 | *** MATCH *** | 1 |
| sceKernelStartThread | 0x1351C | 299 | *** MATCH *** | 1 |

(Iteration 2 on SuspendThread was a single-word transcription typo at
0x36F0: retail `ACDE0000 sw s8,0(a2)` - prev->next = s8 - miscopied as
`sw a2,0(a2)`; verify delay-slot stores against `--disas` when the sed
window scrolls fast.)

| offset | field | evidence |

| +0x4B0 (= module+0x4F0) | the suspend/stop thread list (head sentinel: +0 next, +4 tail/prev) - StartThread inserts here too | Suspend 36C4-36F0, Start 1373C-1375C |
| +0x7D4 | user-stack size param (for user threads: stack = (param<<6) & 0x3FFFFFC0) | Start 13874-13884 |

## Semantics decoded per function

### sceKernelReferThreadStatus @0x15C04
See header comment in tm_k.c + results/I.md. Refinements of I.md's decode:
- tid==0 → current thread: `tid = *(ctx->current + 8)` (TCB+8 = id);
  only this path checks `cfc0 cause != 0 → 0x80020197`.
- The 108-byte struct IS at ctx+0x6D4 = module+0x714 (same base as the
  alarm/TEH/CB scratch views - those are tail overlays). The odd zero loop
  writes through an induction pointer ending at v0+104 (ctx+0x738).
- CPU-time accumulator (+0x54/+0x58): cached rec+52/+56 with a live delta
  added when rec == ctx->current: `acc += sub_0xece4() - {ctx+0x70,+0x74}`
  (64-bit subtract with borrow chain, subu/sltu x2).
- 0x187ac size-verify: if result > 0x2060 AND user size >= 109 →
  0x800201BC; else size field 108→104 and stackSize field &= 0xF.
- Copy-out via 0x1880c(info, module+0x714, min(info->size, *(module+0x714))).
- Log msgno 34, extras (threadRec, tid, info): a1 = rec, a3 = tid, t0 = info.

### sceKernelSuspendThread @0x357C (msgno 53)
No pointer gate (no user pointers). tid==0 → 0x80020197 (also when
suspending ctx->current from thread context). attr bit 0x2 required (D1).
`switch(state)` via 33-entry jump table @0x19B2C:
[0]→1A2, [1]→running case, [2]→ready case, [3]→1A2, [4]→wait case,
[5-7]→1A2, [8]→1A3, [9-11]→1A2, [12]→1A3, [13+]→1A2.
- running (1): ctx+0xC==0 → ready-case logic; ==3 → return 0 (deferred);
  else ctx+0xC = 2, return 0.
- ready (2): thread ctx & not current → sub_16bf4(s8, *(s8+16)) dequeue;
  then tail-insert into ctx+0x4B0 list (s8->prev = tail, tail = s8,
  s8->next = head, prev->next = s8), state = 8. Suspending the CURRENT
  thread additionally clears ctx+4 first.
- wait (4): state = 12, return 0.
- 8/12 → 0x800201A3 (already suspended); else 0x800201A2.

### sceKernelResumeThread @0x3790 (msgno 54)
Same gates/self-check. Jump table @0x19BB0: [1]→running case, [8]→resume
case, [12]→wait case, all others → 0x800201A5 (incl. >= 33 via beqzl).
- resume (8): unlink from ctx+0x4B0 list (next->prev = prev, prev->next =
  next), state = 2 (READY); thread ctx → sub_16c48(s8, st) make-ready;
  interrupt ctx → sub_127d0(s8) + ctx+4 = 0; return 0.
- running (1): ctx+0xC==2 → ctx+0xC = 1, return 0; else 0x800201A5.
- wait (12): state = 4, return 0.

### sceKernelStartThread @0x1351C (msgno 18, 3 extras: tid, argLen, argp)
Gates: argp pointer gate (D3); `cfc0 cause != 0` OR `ctx->current->gate
(TCB+0x88) != 0` → 0x80020064; tid==0 → 0x80020197; resolver → 0x80020198;
attr bit 0x2 → D1; **state must be 16 (DORMANT)** else 0x800201A4;
sub_156bc(s8) register-init, then TCB+0x88 must be NULL else 0x80020064.
- entry = rec+196 with bit 0 cleared (`ins t0,zero,0,1`).
- attr<0 (user): 256-byte user frame at rec+220 (zeroed, -1 at +248/+252,
  attr @+192, rec+44 @+200), kernel stack top s0 = rec+44+entry-256;
  user stack base from ctx+0x7D4: `((param<<6) & 0x3FFFFFC0)`, frame words
  {0xC|base, 13, 0x1000FFFF, 0}, `cache 0x1b/0x8`, `sync`.
- kernel thread: *(rec+44) = id; user thread instead *( *(s8+48) ) = id
  (bltzl detour). Zero rec+164..184 region; args (argp,argLen both nonzero)
  copied onto stack via 0x1880c with len (argLen+15)&~0xF (`ins t7,zero,0,4`),
  rec+292/296 = argLen/stackTop.
- rec+288 → rec+20, 127 → rec+188, runStatus → rec+16 and (after
  sub_134a8(s8)) rec+186; clears rec+80/88/92; ctxblk (rec+28) initialized.
- Insert into ctx+0x4B0 list with **state = 8**, default exit rec+84 =
  0x800201A4, exit trampoline 0x13E80 into ctxblk+56.
- Preemption: if s8->prio(rec+16) < current->prio(TCB+0x10) and ctx+0xC==0
  and resolver st != 0: re-insert current into its run-queue (ctx+152+prio*8
  head, `rotrv` bitmap at ctx+128+(prio>>5)*4), current state = 2, s8
  state = 1, ctx+4 = s8, sub_fe4(0, st) request dispatch. Otherwise s8
  state = 2 and sub_127d0(s8) (+ ctx+4 = 0 when runq was empty).

## Raw-word splices used (all verified vs --disas / .dis)

`0x40436800` cfc0 v1 · `0x40536800` cfc0 s3 · `0x40546800` cfc0 s4 ·
`0x40486800` cfc0 t0 · `0x7c080804` ins t0,zero,0,1 · `0x7c0f1804`
ins t7,zero,0,4 · `0x00c89046` rotrv s2,t0,a2 · `0xbe1b0000` cache 0x1b ·
`0xbe080000` cache 0x8 · `0x0000000f` sync.

## Notes for next agents

1. Jump-table switches (Suspend/Resume) are trivial to transcribe: the
   `jr t5` and table-address arithmetic are literal; the tables themselves
   are data (0x19B2C / 0x19BB0) and outside the compared words.
2. The guide's "ins/ext words = blocked" rule only ever applied to C-shape
   matching. Under `.set noreorder` transcription, splice any exotic opcode
   as `.word` - StartThread's ins/rotrv/cache/sync all byte-match.
3. tm_k.c now contains the most complex matched functions in the project
   (211w and 299w); use them as the reference for multi-block scheduling
   code (beqzl detours, runq bitmap rotrv, preempt logic).
4. New internal callees worth knowing: 0x156bc (thread reg init),
   0x134a8 (thread ctx setup), 0x16bf4 (ready-dequeue), 0x16c48
   (make-ready), 0x127d0 (dispatch now), 0xfe4 (request resched),
   0xece4 (64-bit clock read), 0x187ac (size verify), 0x1880c/0x18814
   (copy-out / name copy), 0x186ac/0x18784/0x186dc (critical/resolve/release).

# Batch L - the delay/sleep family (all six exports)

**Verdict: 6 of 6 `*** MATCH ***`, zero relocations.** Even the `%hi/%lo`
module-base pairs in the two sleep functions assembled to the literal retail
immediates (hi=0, lo=0x40) in this object file, so these are full byte-exact
matches - the cleanest result of any wrapper batch so far (no extern veneers,
all `jal`/`j` targets literal).

All source in `match/threadman/tm_l.c`. Harness smoke test passed before
starting (tm.c: 4 MATCH + 2 reloc) and re-verified after finishing.
**3 iterations total** (two real slips, both mine):
1. First compile: 4 delay wrappers MATCH; both sleep functions DIFF - I had
   inserted a spurious `j <epilogue>` between the CAN_NOT_WAIT `ori` and the
   epilogue. Retail **falls through** into the epilogue (the `ori` at 0x2E4C /
   0x2F58 is the last word before the `lw ra`); one extra word shifted every
   later address (+32: branch offsets off-by-one, +92 on: cascade).
2. Second compile: SleepThread MATCH; SleepThreadCB one word off - the
   `beql t1,s2` back-edge at 0x2FCC targets **0x2FAC** (`bnez a2,notifyPending`)
   not 0x2FA8 (`lw a2`); fixed by splitting the loop label.
3. Third compile: all six MATCH.

| function | addr | words | status |
|---|---|---|---|
| sceKernelSleepThread | 0x2DF0 | 64 | MATCH (0 relocs) |
| sceKernelSleepThreadCB | 0x2EF0 | 88 | MATCH (0 relocs) |
| sceKernelDelayThread | 0x3D78 | 2 | MATCH (0 relocs) |
| sceKernelDelayThreadCB | 0x3D80 | 2 | MATCH (0 relocs) - free 6th twin, not on my list |
| sceKernelDelaySysClockThread | 0x3F9C | 2 | MATCH (0 relocs) |
| sceKernelDelaySysClockThreadCB | 0x3FA4 | 2 | MATCH (0 relocs) |

The four delay "functions" are literally two-word veneers:

```
sceKernelDelayThread           3D78: j 0x3D88; li t3,0     (CB flag in t3!)
sceKernelDelayThreadCB         3D80: j 0x3D88; li t3,1
sceKernelDelaySysClockThread   3F9C: j 0x3FAC; li t3,0
sceKernelDelaySysClockThreadCB 3FA4: j 0x3FAC; li t3,1
```

The `li t3,N` rides in the `j` delay slot - the CB-vs-plain flag is passed in
**t3**, a register outside the o32 ABI. The shared cores (0x3D88 usec,
0x3FAC SceKernelSysClock*) are unexported (not in the map, not matched here)
but I decoded both for the state machine below.

---

## The sleep/wake state machine (the real deliverable)

### SleepThread @0x2DF0 - pseudocode

```
s32 sceKernelSleepThread(void)
{
    if (CP0_Cause != 0)            return 0x80020064;   // ILLEGAL_CONTEXT
    cookie = acquire();            // sub_186AC
    if (cookie == 0) { release(0); return 0x800201A7; } // CAN_NOT_WAIT
    if (ctx->[0x0C] != 0) { release(cookie); return 0x800201A7; }
    if (ctx->logHandler) logHandler(48, module_base, 0);  // msgno 48
    sub_16FA4();                   // kernel-stack check (shared helper)
    tcb = ctx->current;            // ctx+0x00 via module+0x40
    tcb->waitResult = 0;           // TCB+0x80 - delay-slot store, BOTH paths
    if (tcb->wakeupCount != 0) {   // TCB+0x50
        tcb->wakeupCount--;        // consume the pending wakeup
        release(cookie); return 0; // ...and return WITHOUT blocking
    }
    sub_858(&ctx->[0x498], 1);     // enqueue on SLEEP queue, wait type 1
    ctx->[0x04] = 0;
    sub_FE4(1, 1);                 // request dispatch -> actually blocks here
    release(cookie);
    return tcb->waitResult;        // TCB+0x80, filled in by the waker
}
```

### SleepThreadCB @0x2EF0 - the callback-aware loop

Same four entry gates (Cause / cookie / ctx+0x0C → 0x80020064 /
0x800201A7 / 0x800201A7), msgno 49, then:

```
for (;;) {
    while (tcb->notifyPending)            // TCB+0x9C, set by NotifyCallback
        sub_2170(cookie);                 // pump: run pending callbacks
    tcb->waitResult = 0;
    if (tcb->wakeupCount) {               // pending wakeup
        tcb->wakeupCount--;
        if (tcb->waitResult != 0x800201AB)  // not notify-aborted
            { release; return waitResult; } // (loop head re-checked via beql)
        continue;                         // notify aborted the wait: re-pump
    }
    sub_858(&ctx->[0x498], 1);            // enqueue, wait type 1
    tcb->[0x98] = 1;                      // ARM the CB-notify gate
    ctx->[0x04] = 0;
    sub_FE4(1, 1);                        // block
    // on wake: if waitResult==0x800201AB (beql at 0x2FCC) -> re-check notify
}
```

So the CB variant converts the notify-aborted wait (0x800201AB, which batch
H watched NotifyCallback synthesize) into **"run callbacks, then go back to
sleep until a real wakeup"** - exactly the documented SleepThreadCB semantics.

### sub_858 @0x858 - the wait-enqueue (this closes H's gate question)

```
sub_858(queue a0, waitType a1):
    tcb = *(module+0x40);            // ctx->current
    tcb->[0x00] = queue;             // TCB head doubles as wait-queue link!
    tcb->[0x04] = queue->next;       //   (H saw WakeupThread read TCB+0/+4 -
    queue->next = tcb;               //    it's unlinking from the wait queue)
    oldFirst->prev = tcb;
    tcb->[0x0C] = 4;                 // state = WAITING
    tcb->[0x58] = a1;                // WAIT GATE = wait TYPE
    tcb->[0x5C] = 0;                 // cleanup flag cleared
    tcb->[0x80] = 0;                 // waitResult cleared
```

**TCB+0x58 (H's "wait gate") is the wait TYPE**: 1 = sleep queue (ctx+0x498),
2 = delay/timer queue (ctx+0x4E0, from the delay cores). That is precisely why
WakeupThread only takes its `gate==1 -> sub_A08` wake-now branch for threads
sleeping in sceKernelSleepThread(CB): waking a *delayed* thread (gate 2) is
refused - the wakeup just bumps wakeupCount (TCB+0x50), which the thread will
consume at its next SleepThread. H's read `gate==1 ? wake-now : count++` is
now fully explained.

**TCB+0x98 is a flag, not a "callbackQueue pointer"** (correction to results/H.md):
it is *written = 1 by the sleeper* (SleepThreadCB at 0x2FF4, and both delay
cores in their CB variant) and *cleared by the waker* (sub_924 at 0x950,
sub_A08 at 0xA40 both `sw zero,152(tcb)`). It arms the
"NotifyCallback may abort my wait" gate that H saw NotifyCallback test.

### sub_A08 (wake-now) / sub_924 (release-from-wait) - wake side recap

Both: reject if state==0x20 (killed), `tcb->[0x98] = 0` (disarm CB gate),
unlink via TCB+0x00/+0x04, read the **timer handle TCB+0x78** (nonzero →
timer-cancel path: a delay wait must have its timer destroyed), check TCB+0x5C
cleanup flag, then transition state: 0xC (wait+suspend) → suspended variant,
else **state = 2 (READY)** (`sw t6,12` t6=2 at 0xA74); in thread context
jal 0x16C48(tcb, status) else jal 0x127D0(tcb) + zero (module+0x44).

### The delay cores (decoded, unexported - not matched)

Core 0x3D88 (usec, from DelayThread/DelayThreadCB) and 0x3FAC
(SceKernelSysClock* from the SysClock variants, plus the k1 pointer gate
`(p|p+8)&(k1<<11) < 0 -> 0x800200D3` at 0x3FF0-0x3FFC) share the skeleton:

```
cause = CP0_Cause;  if (cause) return 0x80020064;      // 0x3F8C / 0x41E4
cookie = acquire(); if (!cookie || ctx->[0x0C]) return 0x800201A7;
if (logHandler) logHandler(134/135 (usec core) or 136/137 (clock core),
                           ctx, 1|2, usec | {clock.lo, clock.hi});
sub_16FA4();                                          // stack check
tcb = ctx->current;
tcb->[0x7C] = 0;                                      // cleared before enqueue
if (CB flag && tcb->notifyPending) sub_2170(cookie);  // pre-delay callbacks
clock = sub_10D34(sp, usec)  // usec core: USec2SysClockWide-family
        | *a0               // clock core: the user's SceKernelSysclock
handle = sub_10FAC(&clock, module+0x41F4, tcb);       // register thread timer
if (handle < 0) { ... 0x800201A8 WAIT_TIMEOUT path, xor/movz vs saved word }
tcb->[0x78] = handle;         // timer handle (waker cancels it via 0x11104)
tcb->[0x60] = clock.lo;  tcb->[0x64] = clock.hi;      // expiry
sub_858(&ctx->[0x4E0], 2);    // DELAY queue, wait type 2
if (CB flag) tcb->[0x98] = 1;                          // arm CB gate
ctx->[0x04] = 0;  sub_FE4(1, 1);                       // block
if (CB flag && waitResult == 0x800201AB) goto callback-loop;  // same as SleepCB
release; return tcb->waitResult;   // 0 normal, 0x800201A8 timeout,
                                   // 0x800201AB notify-abort (CB only)
```

### New/consolidated knowledge

- **Error codes**: 0x80020064 (interrupt context), 0x800201A7 (CAN_NOT_WAIT -
  acquire cookie==0 **or** ctx->[0x0C]!=0; the ctx+0x0C flag is the second,
  Cause-independent "no-wait context" gate), 0x800201A8 (delay timeout),
  0x800201AB (notify-aborted wait), 0x800200D3 (SysClock pointer gate).
- **Log msgnos**: 48 SleepThread, 49 SleepThreadCB, 134/135 DelayThread(/CB),
  136/137 DelaySysClock(/CB). Extras: 0 for sleeps; 1 (usec) / 2 (clock lo+hi)
  for delays. Quirk: SleepThread passes `a1 = module base` (s8 at 0x2ECC), but
  **SleepThreadCB's log site passes a1 = s8 *before* s8 is ever loaded** - a
  stale-register quirk (s8 is only set at 0x2F98, after the log rejoin);
  transcribed literally, probably a Sony codegen artifact.
- **New context fields**: ctx+0x0C = no-wait flag (must be 0 to sleep/delay);
  ctx+0x04 zeroed before every dispatch request (confirms H); **ctx+0x498 =
  sleep wait queue head, ctx+0x4E0 = delay/timer wait queue head** (0x858
  links TCBs at TCB+0x00/+0x04).
- **New TCB fields**: +0x00/+0x04 wait-queue links (while waiting), +0x58 =
  wait type (1 sleep / 2 delay) - the "gate", +0x60/+0x64 = 64-bit delay
  expiry, +0x78 = timer handle (0x10FAC registers, waker cancels via 0x11104),
  +0x7C zeroed on delay entry (paired scratch), +0x98 = CB-notify arm flag
  (correction: flag, not pointer).
- **New internal callees**: 0x858 wait-enqueue(queue,type), 0xFE4(1,1)
  block-and-dispatch (arg0=1 → stores ctx+0x4C-list ptr at ctx+0x40),
  0x2170(cookie) callback pump, 0x10D34 usec→64-bit clock (sp buffer),
  0x10FAC register thread timer (&clock, module+0x41F4 template, TCB),
  0x11104 timer teardown, 0x16C48/0x127D0 ready-transition tails of
  sub_A08, 0x16FA4 stack-check (takes the msgno in a0 but ignores it).
- Recipe notes: `beql` with a live load in the taken-delay (0x2FCC) and the
  `sw waitResult` in a *plain* branch delay (0x2E88/0x2FBC - executes on both
  paths) both transcribe for free; no `.set mips4` needed in this batch.

### Status for PROGRESS.md

| 0x2DF0 | 64w | sceKernelSleepThread | ✅ (batch L, 3 iters, 0 relocs) |
| 0x2EF0 | 88w | sceKernelSleepThreadCB | ✅ (batch L, 3 iters, 0 relocs) |
| 0x3D78 | 2w | sceKernelDelayThread | ✅ (batch L) |
| 0x3D80 | 2w | sceKernelDelayThreadCB | ✅ (batch L) |
| 0x3F9C | 2w | sceKernelDelaySysClockThread | ✅ (batch L) |
| 0x3FA4 | 2w | sceKernelDelaySysClockThreadCB | ✅ (batch L) |

# Batch M - semaphore/eventflag refer + callback create/delete

Agent M, 2026-11-17. Source: `match/threadman/tm_m.c`. Technique: tm_g/tm_i
recipe - `.set noreorder` asm transcription, literal absolute `jal`/`j`
targets, `%hi/%lo(g_padM+off)` for module-data references. All zero-reloc
full MATCHes: every jal/j emitted as the literal retail target.

Harness smoke test passed before starting and re-verified after finishing.

## Results

| Addr | Words | Function | Status | Iterations |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0x04DF0 | 115 | `sceKernelReferSemaStatus` | *** MATCH *** | 1 (first compile) |
| 0x05F10 | 113 | `sceKernelReferEventFlagStatus` | *** MATCH *** | 3 (see below) |
| 0x019D8 | 106 | `sceKernelCreateCallback` | *** MATCH *** | 1 |
| 0x01B80 | 80 | `sceKernelDeleteCallback` | *** MATCH *** | 1 |

Iterations: 3 total compile runs. Run 1 failed to assemble (movn without
`.set mips4` wrap - same gas gotcha as F/G, now hit for movn too); run 2 was
3/4 MATCH with a single wrong word (EventFlag `j` back-edge target
hand-miscopied 0x5FF4 → 0x5FF0); run 3 = 4/4 MATCH. Zero relocs anywhere.

## UID-table map extension (ctx = module+0x40; table = ctx->field feeding 0x18784)

| ctx offset | class | evidence |
|---|---|---|
| +0x4C8 | **shared per-object "slot" table** (sema + eventflag second view; same role 0x4FC plays for alarms) | `lw t8,1224(s0)` 0x4E8C / 0x5FAC, idx = hdr[21], slot = rec + 4*idx |
| +0x4CC | threads (batch I) | - |
| +0x4D0 | **SEMAPHORES** | `lw a1,1232(s0)` 0x4E44 (resolver arg), header reload 0x4E88 |
| +0x4D4 | **EVENT FLAGS** | `lw a1,1236(s0)` 0x5F64, header reload 0x5FA8 |
| +0x4F4/+0x4F8/+0x4FC/+0x504/+0x508 | TEH/callbacks/shared/alarm/vtimer (I, F, G) | - |

So the UID-class array is contiguous: 0x4C8?, threads 0x4CC, semaphores
0x4D0, event flags 0x4D4 - i.e. sema/evf are UID classes 2/3 right after
threads (class 1), with 0x4C8 as class 0 (its records' +0/+8 words carry
attr/waitCount views shared across IPC families).

## sceKernelReferSemaStatus @0x4DF0 - SceKernelSemaInfo decode (56 B)

PSPSDK-conformant layout, built in the shared scratch at **ctx+0x6D4**
(template `module+0x714`), zero-loop clears ctx+0x6D4..+0x704 (13 words,
store offset 52 from base ctx+0x69C), then:

| scratch off | source | field |
|---|---|---|
| +0 | 56 | size |
| +4..+35 | rec+16 via 0x18814(scratch+4, name, 31) if non-NULL | name[32] |
| +36 | *(slot@0x4C8view + 0) | attr |
| +40 | semaobj+12 (view = rec+4*hdr21(0x4D0 tbl)) | initCount |
| +44 | semaobj+4 | currentCount |
| +48 | semaobj+8 | maxWaiters |
| +52 | slot+8 | numWaitThreads |

Then `0x1880c(info, module+0x714, min(info->size, 56))` (sltu/movn select),
release, return 0.

- attr gate: `rec->attr16 (@rec+22) & 1`, movz kernel bypass → else
  0x800200D1. Unknown semaid → **0x80020199**. Pointer gate
  (info|info+56)&k1<<11 → 0x800200D3.
- Log msgno **64**, nextra 2, a1 = scratch (ctx+0x6D4), a3 = slot view,
  t0 = info - note a1 is the scratch buffer here, not &ctx and not k1.

## sceKernelReferEventFlagStatus @0x5F10 - SceKernelEventFlagInfo decode (52 B)

Zero-loop clears ctx+0x6D4..+0x700 (12 words, store offset 48 from base
ctx+0x6A0); size = 52; then:

| scratch off | source | field |
|---|---|---|
| +0 | 52 | size |
| +4..+35 | rec+16 via 0x18814 (max 31) | name[32] |
| +36 | *(slot@0x4C8view + 0) | attr |
| +40 | evfobj+8 (view = rec+4*hdr21(0x4D4 tbl)) | initPattern |
| +44 | evfobj+4 | currentPattern |
| +48 | slot+8 | numWaitThreads |

`0x1880c(info, module+0x714, min(info->size, 52))`, release, return 0.
Unknown evfid → **0x8002019A**; permission/gate errors as sema.
Log msgno **73**, nextra 2, a1 = a3 = scratch (both!), t0 = info.

## sceKernelCreateCallback @0x19D8 - callback record decode

`SceUID CreateCallback(char *name a0, SceCallbackFunction func a1, void *common a2)`:

1. mask = k1<<11. Gates: `mask&name < 0`, `mask&func < 0` (bltzl) →
   0x800200D3; then `bltz mask` (user mode) SKIPS a final raw `bltz func`
   check - kernel callers additionally must not pass func with bit31.
2. `cfc0 cause != 0` → 0x80020064 (illegal context).
3. acquire 0x186AC (cookie in s6); **sub_1875C(ctx->[0x4F8], name,
   userFlag, &rec)** = UID-class CREATE (new internal callee; nonzero
   return = error, returned verbatim after release).
4. view = rec + 4*hdr21(0x4F8 tbl); fills:

| view off | value | field |
|---|---|---|
| +0/+4 | list links (see 5) | next/prev |
| +8 | rec+12 (assigned uid; also return value) | uid |
| +20 | *(module+0x40) = ctx->current TCB | owner TCB (confirms batch H) |
| +24/+28 | 0 | notifyCount / notifyArg (zeroed at create) |
| +32 | k1>>31 (&0xff) | user flag |
| +36 | func (a1) | callback function |
| +40 | common (a2) | common arg |
| +44 | sub_1873C(func) result, or **gp** when that returns 0 | func binding/gp fallback |

5. Links view into the **owner TCB's callback queue: head embedded at
   TCB+0x90** (next) / **TCB+0x94** (prev), append-at-tail:
   view->next = &head; view->prev = head->prev; head->prev->next = view;
   head->prev = view. (Extends H's "TCB+0x98 callbackQueue".)
6. Log msgno **156**, nextra 3: a1 = view, a3 = func, t0 = common,
   t1 = uid. Release, return uid.

Register roles: s0=func s1=k1 s2=module→uid s3=ctx->current s4=&ctx
s5=common s6=cookie s8=name→createErr→view (Sony recycles).

## sceKernelDeleteCallback @0x1B80 - decode

`s32 DeleteCallback(SceUID cbid)`:

1. `cfc0 v1,cause != 0` → 0x80020064 (note: cfc0 into v1, raw .word).
2. TCB = *(module+0x40); **TCB+0xA4 != 0 → 0x80020064** (new TCB offset -
   "inside callback / busy" guard, probably the running-callback flag).
3. acquire 0x186AC; **cookie == 0 → release + 0x800201A7** (DeleteCallback
   is the first function seen that actually TESTS the acquire return);
   **ctx+0x0C != 0 → release + 0x800201A7** as well (beqzl path; new ctx
   offset - shutdown/delete-in-progress gate).
4. resolve cbid against ctx+0x4F8: nonzero → 0x800201A1 (unknown cbid).
5. attr gate bit **0x10** (`rec->attr16 & 0x10`, movz kernel bypass -
   same bit as CancelAlarm) → else 0x800200D1.
6. `result = sub_1877C(cbid)` (thread-context delete wakeup, same callee
   as CancelAlarm's thread path); release; return result.
7. Log msgno **157**, nextra 1, **a1 = the saved original k1 keyword**
   (getter-family convention, NOT &ctx/scratch), a3 = cbid.

## Notes for the next agent

- The Refer recipe is fully mechanical: both Refer* functions are the same
  skeleton as ReferAlarmStatus (gate → acquire → resolve → attr-bit-1
  movz gate → log → dual-view zero-scratch build → min-size copy-out).
  Only the table offsets, struct size, field sources and msgno differ.
  Remaining same-shape candidates: ReferMboxStatus, ReferVplStatus,
  ReferFplStatus, ReferMsgPipeStatus, ReferVTimerStatus.
- One hand-copy error (j 0x5FF4 miswritten as 0x5FF0) cost the only
  non-first-compile match - re-verify every literal j target against
  `--disas` output before compiling (G's GOTCHA repeats via me).
- `movn` needs the `.set mips4` wrap just like `movz` (obvious in
  hindsight; gas stops at the first error so both were fixed together).
- New log msgno catalog entries: 64 ReferSemaStatus, 73
  ReferEventFlagStatus, 156 CreateCallback, 157 DeleteCallback.
- New internal callees: 0x1875C (UID-class create, args: table, name,
  userFlag, &rec; error in v0), 0x1873C (callback func resolve; 0 result
  → store gp), 0x1877C (thread-context delete/cancel wakeup, arg = uid).
- New ctx/TCB offsets: ctx+0x0C (delete gate), ctx+0x4C8 (shared slot
  table), ctx+0x4D0 (semaphores), ctx+0x4D4 (event flags), TCB+0x90/+0x94
  (callback queue head next/prev), TCB+0xA4 (delete-callback guard).

# Batch N - Mbox (message box) family - 7/7 MATCH, 0 relocs, ALL FIRST-COMPILE

Source: `match/threadman/tm_n.c` (`.set noreorder` transcription, tm_k.c recipe).
Technique note: nothing new needed - the K/J recipe closed the whole family in one
compile. cfc0 as raw `.word` (0x40476800/0x40446800/0x40556800), movz/movn in
`.set mips4` wraps, absolute `j`/`jal` targets literal, `lui %hi(g_padN)` resolves
to immediate 0 = retail, `%lo(g_padN+1812)` = 0x714 = retail.

| Addr | Words | Function | Status | Iterations |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0x8650 | 86 | `sceKernelCreateMbx` | ✅ MATCH, 0 relocs | 1 |
| 0x87A8 | 69 | `sceKernelDeleteMbx` | ✅ MATCH, 0 relocs | 1 |
| 0x88BC | 107 | `sceKernelSendMbx` | ✅ MATCH, 0 relocs | 1 |
| 0x8A68 | 2 | `sceKernelReceiveMbx` (veneer) | ✅ MATCH, 0 relocs | 1 |
| 0x8A70 | 2 | `sceKernelReceiveMbxCB` (veneer) | ✅ MATCH, 0 relocs | 1 |
| 0x8DC0 | 140 | `sceKernelPollMbx` | ✅ MATCH, 0 relocs | 1 |
| 0x9128 | 115 | `sceKernelReferMbxStatus` | ✅ MATCH, 0 relocs | 1 |

Bonus (not exported, transcribed nowhere but decoded for future batches):
receive core @0x8A78 (210w), ring-append helper @0x9338.

## UID class map addition

**Mbox UID table = ctx+0x4E0** (module+0x520) - fills another gap in the
wave-4 map (between evflags 0x4D4 and TEH 0x4F4). All six functions address it
as `lw rX,1248(&ctx)`.

## Mbox object struct decode

Two views per record, like sema/evf (batch M):
- **mbx view** = rec + 4*hdr21(ctx+0x4E0 table)
- **slot view** = rec + 4*hdr21(ctx+0x4C8 shared table)

### mbx view
| Off | Type | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| +0 | u32 | opt - CreateMbx a2 stored RAW (`sw s8,0(s0)` @0x874C); never read by Send/Poll/Refer |
| +4 | u32 | numMessages (count); Create leaves ring empty (slot+0/+8/+12 zeroed by allocator) |
| +8 | ptr | tail = NEWEST message; ring head is tail->next (OLDEST) |

### slot view (shared 0x4C8 table)
| Off | Type | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| +0 | u32 | attr - CreateMbx a1 (`sw s1,0(t6)` @0x8758, in a branch delay slot) |
| +8 | u32 | numWaitThreads |
| +12 | ptr | first waiting thread (used by Send's hand-off path) |

### UID record (raw rec)
+12 new SceUID (create returns it), +16 name ptr (Refer copies 31B via 0x18814),
+22 u16 attr used for the movz user-gate (`lhu 22(rec)`), hdr21 = per-table view
index byte @table-header+21.

### Message ring mechanics
- Messages are user blocks >= 8 bytes: Send k1-gates `msg` and `msg+8`;
  msg+0 = `next`.
- Append helper **sub_9338(mbx, slot, msg)**: empty → `mbx+8 = msg`,
  `msg->next = msg` (self-loop); else walk the ring from tail - duplicate msg →
  **0x800201C9**, NULL/gated next → **0x800200D3**, slot attr bit 0x400 selects
  an alternate insert path (0x93BC); normal insert: `msg->next = tail->next`,
  `tail->next = msg`, `mbx+8 = msg`, count++.
- Dequeue (Poll @0x8E64-0x8F40, receive core @0x8C68-0x8D10): validate oldest
  (`tail->next`) AND `oldest->next` with k1 gates (TOCTOU re-validation of user
  pointers) → D3 on failure; single-element ring (`oldest == oldest->next`)
  requires count == 1 else 0x800201C9, then `mbx+8 = NULL`; otherwise
  `tail->next = oldest->next`; count--; `*out = oldest`.

### Send with waiters (fast hand-off)
`slot+8 > 0` → waiter = *(slot+12); `*(waiter+0x60)` is the receiver's out-slot
ADDRESS (receiver stored its `void** out` arg at TCB+0x60 before blocking);
`**(waiter+0x60) = msg` (`sw s0,0(a1)` @0x8990, branch-likely guarded), then
**sub_b08(rec, 0, cause, st)** wakes it. TCB+0x60 is reused per wait type
(delay expiry in batch L; here the out-slot pointer). Note the odd
`bnezl a1,0x8994` whose target is the fall-through address - the delay-likely
store executes only when taken (same effect as taken; transcribed literally).

### Receive core 0x8A78 (veneers set t3 = CB flag; not part of this batch)
gates: out and out+4 (D3), cfc0 cause → 0x80020064, acquire-cookie==0 or
ctx+0x0C!=0 → 0x800201A7 (CAN_NOT_WAIT); attr bit 0x4; msgno 93 (plain) / 94
(CB); TCB+0x60 = out arg, TCB+0x98 = 1 when CB, TCB+0x9C notifyPending →
callback pump 0x2170 + re-resolve (uid gone → **0x800201B5**); wait via
sub_908(rec, **wait type 5**, slotAttr, timeout), 0x800201AB re-sleep loop.

## Error catalog (this family)
- 0x800200D3 k1 pointer gate (name/opt/msg/out args + ring re-validation)
- 0x80020064 cfc0 cause != 0 - Create/Delete/Receive-core only; **Poll and
  Refer never read cause**
- 0x80020191 Create attr & ~0x1FF (li t2,-1536)
- 0x8002019B unknown uid (Delete/Send/Poll/Refer)
- 0x800200D1 attr gate (user mode without the class bit): Send 0x2, Poll 0x4,
  Refer 0x1, Delete 0x10
- 0x800201B2 Poll: no message
- 0x800201C9 msg already queued / ring count mismatch

## Log handler dispatch (ctx+8 slot, module+0x48)
| msgno | Call | extras |
|---|---|---|
| 90 | Create | a1=opt, a2=1, a3=uid |
| 91 | Delete | a1=uid, a2=1, a3=uid |
| 92 | Send | a1=SLOT obj, a2=2, a3=uid, t0=msg |
| 93/94 | Receive/CB | a1=&ctx, a2=2, a3=uid, t0=out |
| 95 | Poll | a1=&ctx, a2=2, a3=uid, t0=out |
| 97 | Refer | a1=s8 **caller residue** (branch taken before s8 is set - retail passes an uninitialized register), a2=2, a3=uid, t0=info |
| 0 | Poll success / poll-empty / receive-dequeue | a0=0, a3=0x19628 (msg events) or 0x19638 (empty), same jalr slot - second dispatch flavor, a3 is a data pointer, not an arg |

## SceKernelMbxInfo (52B) built at ctx+0x6D4 (= module+0x714)
+0 size=52, +4 name[31] (0x18814, only when rec+16 != NULL), +36 attr (slot+0),
+40 numWaitThreads (slot+8), +44 numMessages (mbx+4), +48 firstMessage =
tail->next. Quirk: the zero loop only covers +4..+44, so +48 keeps stale scratch
data when the box is empty (retail behavior, faithfully reproduced).
Copy-out `0x1880c(info, scratch, min(info->size, *(module+0x714)))` - NOTE:
unlike ReferThreadStatus there is no 0x187ac size validation and no live-value
fixups.

# Batch O - memory-pool families (Vpl / Fpl)

Source: `match/threadman/tm_o.c`. 6/6 assigned targets MATCH in 2 iterations
(1 compile slip: `or aX,aY,aZ` prologue gate typo repeated in 3 functions -
the `or` reuses the ORIGINAL a-register, not the s-register copy).

## Status table

| Addr | Words | Function | Status | Iters |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0x0B44C | 157 | `sceKernelCreateVpl` | SKIPPED (>150w rule) - decoded below | - |
| 0x0B6C0 | 69 | `sceKernelDeleteVpl` | *** MATCH *** | 1 |
| 0x0B7D4 | 2 | `sceKernelAllocateVpl` | *** MATCH *** (veneer `j 0xB7E4; li t3,0`) | 1 |
| 0x0B7DC | 2 | `sceKernelAllocateVplCB` | *** MATCH *** (veneer `j 0xB7E4; li t3,1`) | 1 |
| 0x0BB14 | 104 | `sceKernelTryAllocateVpl` | *** MATCH *** | 2 |
| 0x0C034 | 114 | `sceKernelReferVplStatus` | *** MATCH *** | 2 |
| 0x0C408 | 265 | `sceKernelCreateFpl` | SKIPPED (>150w rule) - decoded below | - |
| 0x0D118 | 115 | `sceKernelReferFplStatus` | *** MATCH *** | 2 |

Zero relocations (all jal/j emitted as absolute literal targets, incl. the
0x187xx import veneers, which are word-exact because the veneer addresses are
fixed in the retail image).

## UID class map extension

- **VPL UID table = ctx+0x4E8 (1256)**, **FPL UID table = ctx+0x4EC (1260)**
  (sema 0x4D0, evflag 0x4D4, TEH 0x4F4, callback 0x4F8, links 0x4FC, alarm
  0x504, vtimer 0x508, shared slot table 0x4C8 - see earlier batches).
- The shared-slot-table helpers live right after ReferFplStatus:
  **0xD2E4** = slot-node ring alloc (fields of any "slot header":
  +8 free-node ring head, +12 slots-array ptr, +16 count; init self-loop,
  insert-after-head, slots[idx] must be empty else -1, count++),
  **0xD38C** = slot-node pop (returns `(node - slotsBase)>>2` = index,
  count--, node[0]=0; -1 if empty). FPL uses these directly for its blocks.

## Object struct layouts (all views: rec + 4*hdr21(table))

### VPL object (rec + 4*hdr21(ctx+0x4E8))
| + | Meaning | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| 0 | opt pointer (create 5th arg, comes in **t0**) | CreateVpl b5DC `sw s0,0(s8)` |
| 4 | pool memory block (sysmem handle via import 0x1878C) | CreateVpl b5C0; allocator arg everywhere |
| 8 | total pool size (import 0x1874C(pool) at create) | CreateVpl b5D8; TryAllocate size gate; Refer +40 |

VPL free-list internals are in **sysmem** (imports 0x1874C free-size,
0x18774 alloc, 0x187A4 validate, 0x1878C create) - not decodable from
threadman.prx. CreateVpl: `0x1878C(partition, size, (attr>>13)&2, name)`;
user callers additionally require `(*(sp+12) & 3) == 3` from 0x187BC
(partition query, in: *(sp)=16) else 0x800200D1.

### FPL object (rec + 4*hdr21(ctx+0x4EC))
| + | Meaning | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| 0 | opt pointer (create 6th arg, **t1**) | CreateFpl c6F0 |
| 4 | pool base address (import 0x187E4(pool)) | c6C8, cB58 |
| 8 | free-slot node ring head (sentinel; nodes = &slots[i]) | c760/c770, 0xD2E4/0xD38C |
| 12 | slots array ptr: heap alloc via 0x18774(heap@module+0x820, numBlocks*4) if numBlocks>=3, else **obj+36** inline | c7A4-c7CC, c760 |
| 16 | numFreeBlocks (count; Refer +48) | 0xD2E4 count++, 0xD38C count-- |
| 20 | blockSize (raw arg; Refer +40) | c6EC |
| 24 | numBlocks (Refer +44) | c6F8 |
| 28 | stride = aligned blockSize (block = base + idx*stride) | c6DC, cB60-cB68 |
| 32 | pool handle (import 0x18794 = SysMem fixed-block pool create) | c6F4 |
| 36 | inline slots area for numBlocks<3 pools | c760 |

CreateFpl alignment: opt+4 = alignment (must be power of 2, min 4, else
0x800200D2); per-block stride = ALIGN(blockSize, align) via SPECIAL3 words
`ext s7,s6,0,15` (0x7ED70380) and `ins s1,zero,1,1` (0x7C110804); 64-bit
overflow check on stride*numBlocks → 0x800201B7. 0x18794(partition, name,
attr&0x4000 ? 3 : 4, align, total). The 8-byte opt is copied into the
module+0x714 template (which Refer* later reads as "template size" - for
FPL pools the template word is the opt!). Attr legality: attr & ~0x43FF →
0x80020191 (VPL) / attr & ~0x41FF (FPL).

### Shared slot view (rec + 4*hdr21(ctx+0x4C8)) - both families
| + | Meaning |
|---|---|
| 0 | attr (Refer +36 both families) |
| 8 | numWait (waiter count; Refer +52 FPL / +48 VPL) |
| 12 | wait-queue head anchor (empty = slot+12 self) |

### Wait queues (TCB nodes, links at TCB+0/+4 per batch L)
- VPL/FPL allocate-block waits use **sub_908(rec, waitType, slotAttr, sp,
  timeout)** with waitType **6 = VPL**, **7 = FPL**; queue anchored at
  slot+12 (FreeVpl walks it: head=*slot+12, sentinel=slot+12, next=TCB+0).
- TCB+0x64 = requested block size (waiter); **TCB+0x60 = granted block**
  (waker writes it; allocate cores copy it to the user out-ptr on wake);
  TCB+0x98 = CB flag; TCB+0x9C = notifyPending (0x2170 callback-pump retry
  when CB && waitResult==0x800201AB - batch-L pattern, confirmed here).
- FreeVpl wakes waiters: try 0x18774 for each waiter (skip if slot attr &
  0x200), sub_924 wake + TCB+0x60 = block; 1 woken → 0x16BF4 dequeue +
  0x16C48 make-ready; >1 → ctx+4=0 + sub_FE4(0,st) resched.
  FreeFpl mirror: sub_B08, plus 0xD2E4 to push the freed block's slot back.
- CancelVpl/CancelFpl are IDENTICAL twins: attr bit 0x8; log 114 (VPL) /
  122 (FPL, verified `li a0,0x7A` @0xD0C0); both call import
  **0x1879C(rec, 0x86D94883)** and write the cancelled count to *a1.
- Helper 0xC1FC = find-uid-by-pool-pointer: walks circular list at
  **module+0x528** (all-pools list, 48B, init'd by 0xC244 via 0x187CC),
  matches *(rec + 4*hdr21 + 4) == pool, returns rec+12 (uid).

## Info structs built in scratch ctx+0x6D4 (template module+0x714)
- SceKernelVplInfo 52B: +0 size(52), +4 name[32] (0x18814, max 31),
  +36 attr(slot+0), +40 poolSize(obj+8), +44 freeSize(import 0x1874C(obj+4)),
  +48 numWait(slot+8). min(user->size, 52) copy-out 0x1880C.
- SceKernelFplInfo 56B: +0 size(56), +4 name[32], +36 attr(slot+0),
  +40 blockSize(obj+20), +44 numBlocks(obj+24), +48 numFreeBlocks(obj+16),
  +52 numWait(slot+8).
- Log msgnos: 108 create-VPL, 109 delete-VPL (a1=uid,1,uid), 110 VPL
  alloc-wait (no-CB), 111 VPL alloc-wait CB, 112 try-alloc-VPL
  (a1=size,3,uid,block), 113 free-VPL (a1 = STALE s8 - retail quirk),
  114 cancel-VPL, 115 refer-VPL (uid,2,uid,info), 116 create-FPL,
  118/119 FPL alloc-wait (no-CB/CB), 123 refer-FPL (uid,2,uid,info).
  Wait logs pass a1 = 0x800201AB (preset timeout result).

## Error constants (VPL/FPL)
- 0x800200D3 pointer gate · 0x80020064 interrupt ctx / dispatch busy ·
  0x800200D1 attr permission (rec+22 bits: 0x1 refer, 0x2 alloc/free,
  0x8 cancel, 0x10 delete) · 0x8002019C unknown VPL · 0x8002019D unknown
  FPL · 0x80020190 pool alloc fail · 0x80020191 illegal attr ·
  0x800201B6 illegal/NULL free block · 0x800201B7 illegal size / size==0 /
  free<size · 0x800200D2 bad FPL opt alignment · 0x800201A7 CAN_NOT_WAIT.
- User-mode gate: create passes (attr | k1>>31) & 0xFF as the UID "user"
  byte to 0x1875C (table, name, user, &rec); rec+12 = new uid.

## Sony ABI note (confirmed both creators)
5th/6th syscall args arrive in **t0/t1** (not stack), and the allocate
cores take the CB flag in **t3** - the export veneers pre-load these.
CreateVpl(name a0, partition a1, attr a2, size a3, opt t0);
CreateFpl(name a0, partition a1, attr a2, blockSize a3, numBlocks t0,
opt t1); AllocateVpl(uid a0, size a1, block a2, timeout a3, CB t3).

## Not decoded / out of module
Pool free-list internals (0x1874C/0x18774/0x187A4/0x1878C/0x18794/0x187E4)
are import veneers (unpatched `jr ra; nop` stubs) - SysMem owns them.
0x10D34(sp, timeout-lo) in both allocate cores = timeout preprocessing
(near GetSystemTimeLow).

# Batch P - MsgPipe family (ring-buffer pipe mechanics)

Source: `match/threadman/tm_p.c` (tm_k noreorder-transcription recipe).
All 5 assigned functions byte-exact, 0 relocations.

| Addr | Words | Function | Status | Iters |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0x09468 | 148w | `sceKernelCreateMsgPipe` | **MATCH** | 1 |
| 0x096BC | 69w | `sceKernelDeleteMsgPipe` | **MATCH** | 1 |
| 0x097D0 | 2w | `sceKernelSendMsgPipe` (veneer) | **MATCH** | 1 |
| 0x0A1A4 | 2w | `sceKernelReceiveMsgPipe` (veneer) | **MATCH** | 1 |
| 0x0ADD0 | 124w | `sceKernelReferMsgPipeStatus` | **MATCH** | 2 |

(Iteration 1 = first compile: 4/5 MATCH. Iteration 2 fixed one word in
Refer: retail `or a1,a2,a1` @0xADE4 - the OR's rt operand is the still-live
arg register a1, not the saved s2; same computed value, different encoding.)

## Not in this batch (verified sizes for the next agent)

- `sceKernelSendMsgPipeCB` @0x097D8: 2w veneer (`j 0x97e0; li t3,1`) -
  trivial, same shape as SendMsgPipe.
- `sceKernelReceiveMsgPipeCB` @0x0A1AC: 2w veneer (`j 0xa1b4; li t3,1`).
- Send core @0x097E0: 361w · Receive core @0x0A1B4: 379w - >150w, skip-size.
- `sceKernelTrySendMsgPipe` @0x09D7C: 266w · `sceKernelTryReceiveMsgPipe`
  @0x0A798: 290w - >150w, skip-size.
- `sceKernelCancelMsgPipe` @0x0AC20: **108w - under the 150w budget and not
  attempted here (not in this batch's assignment); it opens with
  `addiu v1,a1,4` before `addiu sp,sp,-48`, i.e. a (uid, info, ...) arg
  pair like Refer.** Prime candidate if the family gets a second pass.

## UID class map addition

**MsgPipe UID table = ctx+0x4E4** (`lw a1,1252(s3)` in Create/Delete/Refer)
- fills the gap between evflags +0x4D4 and TEH +0x494. Record views as
usual: pipe = rec + 4*hdr21(ctx+0x4E4), slot = rec + 4*hdr21(ctx+0x4C8).

## MsgPipe object decode (pipe view)

| Offset | Field (evidence) |
|---|---|
| +0 | 5th syscall arg (t0) - pointer-gated `(p\|p+4)&k1<<11` at entry; never read by Refer (buffer/opt) |
| +4 | wait direction: 0 none, 1 senders queued, 2 receivers queued (Refer branches on ==1/==2) |
| +8 | read cursor - init = 0x187e4(block) |
| +12 | write cursor - same init |
| +16 | tail/copy cursor - same init (Create stores same s6 to +8/+12/+16) |
| +20 | bufSize (= 4th arg a3) → Refer info+40 |
| +24 | usedBytes → free = pipe+20 − pipe+24 → Refer info+44 |
| +28 | kernel-allocated block (0x18794 result; freed via 0x187b4 on error) |

Slot view (ctx+0x4C8): +0 attr u32 (Create stores arg3 there), +8 waitCount
(single count; Refer routes it to numSend or numRecv depending on pipe+4).

## Create semantics (f(name a0, arg2 a1, attr a2, bufSize a3, arg5 t0))

- Gate (name | arg5 | arg5+4) & k1<<11 < 0 → 0x800200D3
- cfc0 cause != 0 → 0x80020064; (attr & ~0x51FF) != 0 → 0x80020191
- acquire; **0x187bc(arg2, sp-out, \*sp=16)** - new patched veneer: 16-byte
  parse/validate of arg2; nonzero → release+return it
- user threads additionally need (*(sp+12) & 3) == 3 else 0x800200D1
- bufSize != 0: **0x18794(arg2, name, bit28(attr) via `ext a2,s4,28,1`
  (0x7E860380), bufSize)** = allocate block (<=0 → 0x80020190, block freed
  by 0x187b4 on later failure); **0x187e4(block)** = data head → cursors
- uid = **0x1875c(ctx+0x4E4, name, (attr\|k1user)&0xff, &rec@sp+16)**;
  rec+12 = new SceUID (pipe uid)
- init: pipe+0=arg5, +4=0, +8/+12/+16=cursor, +20=bufSize, +24=0,
  +28=block; slot+0=attr
- log msgno **98** (a1 = v0 = 0, a3 = uid); release; return uid

## Delete semantics (f(uid))

cause → 0x80020064 (result preloaded in v1 at entry!); resolve ctx+0x4E4 →
0x8002019E on failure; attr bit **0x10** needed (kernel bypass movz) else
0x800200D1; log msgno **99** (a1 = a3 = uid); 0x1877c(uid) uid-delete;
release; 0. Error exits release with `jal 0x186dc; nop` (a0 stale) -
transcribed as-is.

## Refer semantics (f(uid, SceKernelMsgPipeInfo *info))

- Gate (info | info+56) & k1<<11 → 0x800200D3; resolve → 0x8002019E;
  attr bit **0x1** needed else 0x800200D1
- log msgno **107**: handler(107, &ctx, 2, uid, info) - a1 = &ctx (matches
  batch F convention)
- 56B info built in shared scratch ctx+0x6D4 (template module+0x714):
  - +0 size 56 (zero loop pre-clears scratch tail ctx+0x6D4.. from ctx+0x69C)
  - +4 name[32]: 0x18814(scratch+4, rec+16, 31) if rec+16 != NULL
  - +36 attr = slot+0
  - +40 bufSize = pipe+20; +44 freeSize = pipe+20 − pipe+24
  - +48 numSendWaitThreads = slot+8 iff pipe+4 == 1
  - +52 numRecvWaitThreads = slot+8 iff pipe+4 == 2
- copy-out 0x1880c(info, scratch, min(info->size, *(module+0x714)));
  release; 0

## Veneers

Send/Receive/CB variants are 2-word tail-jumps (`j 0x97E0`/`j 0xA1B4`,
`li t3,0|1`) - same family shape as batch L's Delay veneers; CB flag rides
in **t3**.

## Notes for next agents

- The 0x187ac..0x18814 helper region is entirely load-time-patched stubs
  (`jr ra; nop` in the file bytes); every `jal` there is a literal word in
  retail and transcribes exactly (confirms batch H finding).
- New patched veneers seen: **0x18794** (alloc block: (arg, name, type,
  size)), **0x187b4** (free block), **0x187bc** (16B validate/parse),
  **0x187e4** (block → data head) - likely SysMem-backed pipe storage.
- `ext` raw-word splice confirmed again (0x7E860380 = `ext a2,s4,28,1`).
- GOTCHA (cost 1 iter): when the gate ORs a pointer with pointer+len, retail
  may reuse the original *argument* register as the OR's rt operand instead
  of the callee-saved copy - transcribe operands verbatim, not semantics.

# Batch Q - the LwMutex (lightweight mutex) family

Agent Q · source `match/threadman/tm_q.c` · all six **MATCH, zero relocs, zero
externs** (2 iterations total: first compile had 3 register-operand typos in
Create/Refer; everything else first-compile).

| Addr | Words | Function | Status | Iters |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0x76D8 | 140 | `sceKernelCreateLwMutex` | ✅ MATCH | 2 |
| 0x8094 | 139 | `_sceKernelTryLockLwMutex` | ✅ MATCH | 1 |
| 0x7CD4 | 2 | `_sceKernelLockLwMutex` (bonus, twin veneer) | ✅ MATCH | 1 |
| 0x7CDC | 2 | `_sceKernelLockLwMutexCB` | ✅ MATCH | 1 |
| 0x82C0 | 125 | `sceKernelReferLwMutexStatusByID` | ✅ MATCH | 2 |
| 0x7A5C | 158 | `_sceKernelUnlockLwMutex` | ✅ MATCH | 1 |

The two Lock veneers are the batch-L Delay pattern: `j 0x7CE4; li t3,0/1`
(CB flag in t3). The shared core at **0x7CE4 (244 words) is still unassigned**
- it is the only remaining piece of the family (Delete 0x7908, 92w, also
unassigned). g_padQ at the data base made every %hi resolve to the retail
literal 0, hence 0 relocs.

## The embedded-struct pattern (vs UID objects)

Every other UID family takes a SceUID; LwMutex calls pass a **32-byte work
area embedded in the caller's memory**. The kernel still keeps a UID record,
and the two are tied together at create time:

1. **The k1 gate protects the struct pointer itself.** Each entry does
   `and v0,(k1<<11),pWork; bltz -> 0x800200D3` (Create also gates pName, and
   requires `pWork & 3 == 0`). UID objects only gate their status out-buffer;
   here the object address is user-supplied, so it is gated directly.
2. **Create additionally ANDs the mask against a constant**: `t0=0x80020000;
   a3=t0+4; a2=a3|t0; and a1,a2,mask; bltzl` - a user-mode k1 (bit20→bit31)
   always fails → CreateLwMutex is **kernel-only** (0x800200D3 from user
   context). Lock/TryLock/Unlock/ReferByID have no constant gate.
3. **The work area carries the identity**: `uid = work+16` is fed to the
   standard resolver 0x18784 against the LwMutex UID table. That table is a
   **new class slot: ctx+0x4DC** (fills the gap between semas +0x4D0 and
   event flags +0x4D4... i.e. map now: 0x4CC threads, 0x4D0 semas, 0x4D4
   evflags, **0x4DC LwMutex**, 0x4F4 TEH, 0x4F8 callbacks, 0x4FC links,
   0x504 alarms, 0x508 vtimers).
4. **Who validates the pointer: the back-link check.** After resolving, the
   kernel reads `*(rec + 4*hdr21(0x4DC) + 12)` - the work pointer stored at
   create - and requires it to equal the caller's pWork, else
   **0x800201CA**. Same error covers an unresolvable uid. This is the
   embedded-struct replacement for "trust the uid": a cloned/stale work area
   is rejected even though it may contain a once-valid uid.
5. From then on the flow is the familiar skeleton: acquire-cookie 0x186AC,
   user gate `lhu rec+22` (attr bit 0x2 for lock/unlock/trylock, bit 0x1 for
   refer) with the movz kernel-bypass, log dispatch at ctx+0x08, release
   0x186DC(cookie).

## SceLwMutexWorkArea (32 B, user memory)

| off | field |
|---|---|
| +0 | lock count (initCount at create; unlock subtracts; wake-handoff gets waiter's wanted count) |
| +4 | owner: `ctx->current` TCB **pointer** at create/trylock-acquire; 0 when unlocked; **wake-handoff stores `*(waiterTCB+8)`** (the waiter's thread *id*) - mixed representation, see note |
| +8 | attr |
| +12 | 0 (pad) |
| +16 | SceUID (kernel uid; DeleteLwMutex writes -1 here) |
| +20/+24/+28 | zeros (links/pad) |

Kernel-side views on the UID record (rec = resolver output):
- lwm view `rec + 4*hdr21(0x4DC)`: +8 uid, **+12 pWork (back-link)**,
  +16 name, +20 initCount.
- slot view `rec + 4*hdr21(0x4C8)` (shared slot table): +0 attr,
  +8 waitCount, +12 **wait-queue head** (waiter TCBs, self-loop when empty).
- raw record header (allocator common): +12 SceUID, +16 name, +22 attr u16.

Owner-representation quirk (bytes, not interpretation): create and trylock
write `*(ctx+8)` (a TCB pointer) into work+4 and trylock's recursive check
compares it against `*(ctx+8)`; but unlock's wake-handoff writes
`*(waiterTCB+8)` (thread id per batch K's TCB map) there. After a handoff the
equality fast-path can only match if TCB+8 aliases a self-pointer in that
context. Recorded as-is.

## Per-function decode

**sceKernelCreateLwMutex(pWork, pName, attr, initCount, pOptParam t0)** 140w.
Gates: pWork, pName, user-mode-constant, alignment, `cfc0 cause -> 0x80020064`.
`ins t3,zero,0,10` (raw 0x7c0b4804): attr must fit in 10 bits else
0x80020191. initCount legal iff `0 <= n < 2` or attr&0x200 (recursive
multi-count) else 0x800201BD. pOptParam != 0: 4-byte validation copy into
scratch ctx+0x6D4 (size capped: `sltiu 5 / movz 4`). Allocator
0x1875C(ctx+0x4DC, name, (attr|user)&0xFF, &rec) → uid. Fills slot+0=attr,
lwm view +8 uid/+12 pWork/+16 name/+20 initCount, and zeroes/initialises the
work area; initCount>0 → owner = ctx->current. Log msgno **82**
(pWork, nextra 1) then release, return uid.

**_sceKernelTryLockLwMutex(pWork, unlockCount)** 139w. Gate/cfc0/resolve/
back-link/attr-0x2; count gate `blez` or (`>=2` and !attr 0x200) → 0x800201BD.
Log **87**. If `work+4 == ctx->current`: attr 0x200 → sum =
unlockCount+work->count, `bltz sum` → 0x800201CD else store, release, 0;
no 0x200 → 0x800201CF. Else owner != 0 → 0x800201CB; else
work->count = unlockCount, owner = ctx->current, release, 0.

**_sceKernelLockLwMutex / CB(pWork, unlockCount, timeoutPtr?)** 2w each:
`j 0x7CE4; li t3,flag`. Core gates pWork|pWork+32 and a2|a2+4 (both pointers),
cause → 0x80020064, `ctx+0x0C != 0` after acquire → 0x800201A7 (CAN_NOT_WAIT),
0x16FA4 dispatch pump when contended; waiter stashes wanted count at
**TCB+0x60** and CB flag at TCB+0x98 (consistent with batch L); waitResult
0x800201AB loop via 0x2170; recursive path errors 0x800201CD/0x800201CF.
244 words - left for a future batch.

**sceKernelReferLwMutexStatusByID(uid, info)** 125w. **No cfc0 check**
(only family member without one). Gate (info|info+64) → D3. Resolve uid
(no back-link - no pointer passed); user && !(attr&1) → 0x800200D1; log **89**
(rec, 2, info). Builds 64 B at scratch ctx+0x6D4 by zeroing 60 B
(loop `sw 0,60(a0)` from ctx+0x694+4), then:

| off | source |
|---|---|
| +0 | 64 |
| +4 | name copy 0x18814(raw rec+16, 31) |
| +0x24 (36) | slot+0 attr |
| +0x28 (40) | `*(raw rec+12)` |
| +0x2C (44) | `*(lwm view+12)` pWork |
| +0x30 (48) | `*(lwm view+20)` initCount |
| +0x34 (52) | work+0 count |
| +0x38 (56) | work+4 owner, or -1 when 0 (beqzl/li -1) |
| +0x3C (60) | slot+8 waitCount |

Copy-out 0x1880C(info, scratch, min(info->size, 64)) via sltu/movn; release; 0.

**_sceKernelUnlockLwMutex(pWork, unlockCount)** 158w. Gate/cfc0/resolve/
back-link/attr-0x2; count gate (blez / >=2 without 0x200) → 0x800201BD;
`work->owner != ctx->current` → 0x800201CC. Log **84** (pWork, 2, uid,
count). `count < unlockCount` → 0x800201CE. remaining>0 → store, release, 0.
remaining<=0 → count=0, owner=0; wait queue at slot+12: if non-empty
(self-loop test `first == head`), take first waiter TCB:
`work->count = *(TCB+0x60)` (its wanted count), `work->owner = *(TCB+8)`,
sub_924(TCB) release-from-wait; on success (>0) 0x16BF4 dequeue +
0x16C48 make-ready (same pair as Suspend/Resume); release, 0. If empty,
still writes count=0/owner=0. (s1/s8 sentinel pair drives the branch.)

## Recipe notes (nothing new required)

First-compile experience: the whole family fell to plain tm_k-style
transcription. The only errors were hand-transcription typos (rd field of
`addu a1,t0,a0`; base reg of the following `sw`; `or a1,a2,a1` vs s3) -
worth re-checking operand fields against the .dis column when diffing, since
the disassembler annotation had them right. `ins` as raw `.word`, cfc0 as
raw `.word`, movz/movn under `.set mips4`, absolute `jal`/`j` all behaved
as in batches F-L. One gas nit: a single `.set push` covers multiple
`.globl/.size` groups - don't emit a matching `.set pop` per function.

# Batch R - sema/evf OPERATION side (signal/wait/set/clear/poll/cancel)

Agent R, 2026-11-17. Source: `match/threadman/tm_r.c`. Technique: tm_k
recipe - `.set noreorder` asm transcription, literal absolute `jal`/`j`
targets, `%hi(g_padR)` module-base lui (immediate 0 = retail 0), movz under
`.set mips4`, cfc0 as raw `.word`. **6/6 full MATCH, zero relocs.**

Harness smoke test passed before starting and re-verified after finishing.

Note: SignalSema is 157 words - marginally over the 150-word budget rule. It
was attempted anyway (and matched) because it is the centerpiece of the
batch's waiter-release-semantics assignment.

## Results

| Addr | Words | Function | Status | Iterations |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0x044D8 | 157 | `sceKernelSignalSema` | *** MATCH *** | 3 (typo fixes) |
| 0x0474C | 2 | `sceKernelWaitSema` | *** MATCH *** | 2 (veneer) |
| 0x053C0 | 150 | `sceKernelSetEventFlag` | *** MATCH *** | 2 |
| 0x05618 | 70 | `sceKernelClearEventFlag` | *** MATCH *** | 2 |
| 0x05B70 | 145 | `sceKernelPollEventFlag` | *** MATCH *** | 3 (typo fixes) |
| 0x05DB4 | 87 | `sceKernelCancelEventFlag` | *** MATCH *** | 2 |

Compile history (whole file): run 1 = assembler errors (three 2-operand
`beql` forms must be `beqzl`); run 2 = 4/6 MATCH, 3 single-word typos
(`j 0x45F4` miswritten 0x45F0 in SignalSema; Poll beqzl target 0x5C6C
mislabelled, and `and a2,v1,s2`/`xor s6,a2,s2` misread as rd/rs=$2); run 3 =
6/6 MATCH. All errors were hand-copy slips, no source-shape discoveries.

## Waiter-release semantics (the scheduler-correctness deliverable)

### Where the queue lives, who inserts, in what order

Both semaphores and event flags keep their wait queue in the **shared slot
view** (`rec + 4*hdr21(ctx+0x4C8 table)`), head pointer at **slot+12**
(self-pointing sentinel when empty; first waiter's TCB otherwise). The queue
is a doubly-linked list of waiter **TCBs**, links AT **TCB+0x00 (next) /
TCB+0x04 (prev)**. slot+8 = waiter count (`sub_680` increments it).

Insert happens in the shared wait cores (sema core 0x475C, evf core 0x5740;
the exported Wait* names are 2-word veneers `j core; li t3,cbFlag`) via
`jal 0x908` → `sub_680(rec, waitType, a2, timeout, 0)` @0x680:

- **`a2` selects the ordering policy**: `andi t0,a2,0x100` @0x724.
  - `a2 & 0x100` → **priority-ordered insert** (0x744-0x7FC): walk from the
    head; compare waiter `TCB+0x10` (currentPriority) against each node's;
    insert BEFORE the first node whose priority number is GREATER (numerically
    lower priority). Equal priorities insert after existing equals ⇒ FIFO
    within one priority level.
  - else → **plain tail append** (0x810-0x82C).
- **The sema wait core passes `a2 = *(slot+0)` = the object attr**
  (delay slot of its `jal 0x908`: `lw a2,0(t8)` @0x4924) ⇒ semaphores honor
  **attr bit 0x100 = priority waiting**, default FIFO.
- **The evf wait core passes `a2 = 0`** (`move a2,zero` @0x59C4) ⇒ event-flag
  waits are **always FIFO**, regardless of attr.

Wait bookkeeping stored by the cores before linking: TCB+0x58 = wait type
(1 sleep, 2 delay, **3 sema**, **4 evf** - `li a1,3/4` at the call sites),
TCB+0x60 = wait argument (sema: wanted count; evf: wait pattern),
TCB+0x64 = evf wait mode (bit0 = OR else AND; bits 0x10/0x20 = the waiter's
requested clear-on-wake), TCB+0x68 = evf result-out ptr, TCB+0x98 = CB flag
(the t3 the veneer loaded), timer handle TCB+0x78 when a timeout is given
(`sub_10FAC` arm / `sub_11104` cancel).

### `sub_924` (release-from-wait) - what one "wake" does

Unlinks the TCB from its wait queue (next/prev at +0/+4), zeroes TCB+0x98,
cancels a pending delay timer (TCB+0x78 → `sub_11104`), then: state 12
(WAIT+SUSPENDED) → relink into the suspend list (module+0x4F0), state 8;
otherwise make-ready (`sub_127D0`), state 2. **Returns 1** when the thread
was transitioned (ready or re-suspended), 0 otherwise (e.g. state-32
special) - this return is what the wakers test with `blez v0`.

### sceKernelSignalSema @0x44D8 - which waiters wake, in what order

1. Overflow PRE-CHECK before touching the count: walk the ENTIRE queue once,
   `v1 = currentCount + signal - Σ(waited count of every waiter)`; if
   `maxCount (semaobj+8) < v1` → release + **0x800201AE** and the stored
   count is NOT modified. (I.e. a signal pre-committed to waiters may exceed
   max, the free count may not.)
2. `currentCount (semaobj+4) += signal`.
3. Wake loop in **queue order from the head** (the priority-or-FIFO order
   established at insert):
   - **Head-of-line blocking**: stops at the FIRST waiter whose wanted count
     exceeds the remaining `currentCount` (`slt/bnez` @0x45F8/0x45FC) -
     hungry head blocks everyone behind it; no priority bypass at wake time.
   - Per woken waiter: `currentCount -= wanted`; `sub_924(waiter)`; if it
     returned > 0, record `wokenThread = waiter` and `woken++`.
   - **User-thread gate**: when `*(ctx+0x30)` (pending-dispatch list) is
     non-empty, waiters with `attr < 0` or `attr & 0x80000000` (user threads)
     are SKIPPED (left queued, loop continues) - only kernel threads are
     released while a dispatch is already pending (@0x46C4-0x46D8).
4. Dispatch tail (identical shape in SetEventFlag):
   - interrupt context (cause != 0): `woken > 0` → `ctx+4 = 0` (force
     redispatch), else nothing.
   - thread context: `woken == 1` → `sub_16BF4(t, t->prio)` dequeue +
     `sub_16C48(t, st)` make-ready (immediate, no resched call);
     `woken > 1` → `ctx+4 = 0` + `sub_FE4(0, st)` resched request;
     `woken == 0` → nothing.
5. Errors: 0x80020199 unknown semaid, 0x800200D1 attr bit 2 required for
   user, no pointer gate, no early cfc0 gate (cause is only saved for step
   4). Log msgno **59**, nextra 2, a3 = semaid, t0 = signal count;
   a1 = the s8 register *as left by the caller* (retail quirk - a1 is read
   before s8 is ever written in this frame; transcribed as-is).

### sceKernelSetEventFlag @0x533C0..0x53C0 - which waiters wake

1. `currentPattern (evfobj+4) |= pattern`; pattern == 0 → no-op success.
2. **Full queue scan in queue order (FIFO), no head-of-line stop**: each
   waiter matches with `v1 = acc & waitPattern` where acc = the live
   pattern (re-read every iteration):
   - waitFlags bit0 set (OR): match iff `v1 != 0` (any bit);
   - else (AND): match iff `v1 == waitPattern` (all bits) - via the shared
     `xor/sltiu` idiom @0x54C0/0x54C4.
3. On a match: `sub_924(waiter)`; ret > 0 → wokenThread/woken++ - NO
   user-thread/ctx+0x30 gate here (user threads wake too). Then apply the
   WAITER's own requested clears (stored in its TCB+0x64): bit 0x10 →
   `currentPattern = 0`; bit 0x20 → `currentPattern &= ~waitPattern`;
   continue scanning with the updated pattern. Scan ends at queue end or
   when `currentPattern == 0`.
4. Dispatch tail identical to SignalSema (woken 1 / >1 / intr split).
5. Errors: 0x8002019A unknown evfid, 0x800200D1 attr bit 2. Log msgno
   **67**, nextra 2, a1 = slot view, t0 = pattern.

### The rest

- **ClearEventFlag @0x5618**: `currentPattern &= patternArg` - the argument
  is a KEEP mask (bits NOT in it are cleared), NOT the PSPSDK-documented
  "bits to clear"; retail 6.60 really does `and` with no complement
  (@0x56BC/0x56C4). No wake processing. Errors as Set; log msgno **68**,
  a1 = &ctx.
- **PollEventFlag @0x5B70**: validates mode first: `mode & ~0x31` non-zero →
  0x80020195 (only bits 0x1 OR / 0x10 / 0x20 legal); `mode & 0x30 == 0x30`
  → 0x80020195; pattern 0 → 0x800201B1. Attr bit **4** (not 2!) required
  for user. **Single-waiter rule**: unless slot attr bit **0x200**
  (multi-wait), `evfobj+12` (a per-object single-waiter slot, distinct from
  the slot+12 queue) must be 0 or equal to the CURRENT thread's TCB -
  i.e. you may poll an event flag that only YOU are waiting on (poll from
  your own callback) - else 0x800201B0. On match: `*result = the PRE-clear
  currentPattern`, then mode 0x10 → cur = 0, mode 0x20 → cur &= ~pattern;
  miss → 0x800201AF. OR: `cur & pat != 0`; AND: `(cur & pat) == pat`.
  Log msgno **71**, nextra 4, a1 = evfobj.
- **CancelEventFlag @0x5DB4**: OVERWRITES `currentPattern = patternArg`
  (assignment, not or/and), then `sub_1879C(rec, 0x86D94883)` - new
  internal callee = cancel/release ALL waiters of the record (also called
  by sub_924's TCB+0x5C cleanup path with the flag value); its return goes
  to `*resultPtr` (also returns 0 itself). Attr bit **8** required for
  user. Log msgno **72**, nextra 3, a1 = evfobj.
- **WaitSema @0x474C**: 2-word veneer `j 0x475C; li t3,0` (CB variant at
  0x4754 sets t3=1; t3 → TCB+0x98 in the core). Sema wait core facts
  (decoded, not transcribed): causes → 0x80020064 (intr), count <= 0 →
  error, CAN_NOT_WAIT 0x800201A7, attr bit 4 for user, count > maxCount →
  0x800201AE, **fast path**: `currentCount >= wanted` consumes immediately
  without blocking; log msgno 61.

## New knowledge bank entries

- Wait queue = slot view (0x4C8) +12, TCB links +0/+4; **insert policy arg**
  of sub_680: sema core passes slot attr (bit 0x100 = priority), evf core
  passes 0 (always FIFO).
- Wait types: TCB+0x58 = 3 sema, 4 evf (extends L's 1 sleep / 2 delay).
- TCB+0x60 = wait argument (sema wanted count / evf pattern); TCB+0x64 =
  evf wait mode (bit0 OR, 0x10/0x20 clears); TCB+0x68 = evf result-out ptr.
- sub_924 (0x924) = release-from-wait: unlink, timer cancel, ready (or
  suspend-list for state 12), returns 1 on transition.
- sub_680 (0x680) via wrapper sub_908 (0x908) = wait-link helper (timer arm
  + queue insert + wait bookkeeping); ctx+0x30 pending-dispatch list gates
  user-thread wakes in SignalSema only.
- evfobj+12 = single-waiter slot checked by Poll (0 or current thread),
  distinct from the slot+12 queue; slot attr bits: 0x100 priority-wait,
  0x200 multi-wait (PSP_EA_* style), per-op user permission bits differ:
  attr16 & 2 (signal/set/clear), & 4 (wait/poll), & 8 (cancel).
- New internal callee 0x1879C(rec, tag) = cancel/release all waiters;
  magic tags 0x86D94883 (CancelEventFlag), 0x5BD49380 (sub_924 cleanup).
- Log msgno catalog: 59 SignalSema, 61 (sema wait), 67 SetEventFlag,
  68 ClearEventFlag, 71 PollEventFlag, 72 CancelEventFlag.
- Error catalog: sema 0x80020199/0x800201AE; evf 0x8002019A, 0x80020195
  (mode), 0x800201AF (no match), 0x800201B0 (busy/other waiter),
  0x800201B1 (pattern 0).
- gas gotcha: two-operand `beql rs,label` is rejected - write `beqzl` (the
  2-operand beql forms appear whenever retail compares against $0 with a
  likely branch).

# Batch S - Cancel + Free leftovers (MsgPipe cancel, Vpl/Fpl free + cancel)

Source: `match/threadman/tm_s.c` (tm_k noreorder-transcription recipe).
**5/5 assigned targets MATCH, 0 relocations.** Three compiles total: run 1
died on a C header comment containing `*/` inside "Cancel*/Free*" (block
comment terminated early - write "Cancel/Free", not "Cancel*/Free*");
run 2 = 4/5 MATCH, one word off; run 3 = 5/5 after the `break` fix below.
Every transcription word was otherwise first-compile.

| Addr | Words | Function | Status | Iters |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0x0AC20 | 108 | `sceKernelCancelMsgPipe` | **MATCH** | 1 |
| 0x0BCB4 | 146 | `sceKernelFreeVpl` | **MATCH** | 1 |
| 0x0xCDBC | 137 | `sceKernelFreeFpl` | **MATCH** | 2 (gas break encoding) |
| 0x0xBEFC | 78 | `sceKernelCancelVpl` | **MATCH** | 1 |
| 0x0xCFE0 | 78 | `sceKernelCancelFpl` | **MATCH** | 1 |

## New gas GOTCHA (cost 1 iter)

`break 0x7` under this gas encodes the code into the HIGH field:
`0x0007000D`. The retail/GCC encoding is `0x000001CD` (code 7 in the low
field). Splice `.word 0x000001cd` - same category as ins/ext/rotrv.

## sceKernelCancelMsgPipe @0xAC20 - f(uid, s32 *sendN, s32 *recvN)

- Gates BOTH out-pointers: ((sendN|sendN+4)&k1<<11) and
  ((recvN|recvN+4)&k1<<11) → 0x800200D3 (P's "arg pair" hunch confirmed:
  `addiu v1,a1,4` before `addiu sp` is the first gate).
- **NO cfc0 check** (unique: no interrupt-context gate) - resolve
  ctx+0x4E4 → 0x8002019E; slot attr bit **0x8** (cancel) → 0x800200D1.
- Log msgno **106**: handler(106, recvN, 3, uid, sendN, recvN) - a1 =
  the SECOND out-pointer (t1 extra repeats it).
- `v0 = 0x1879c(rec, 0x86D94883)` = number of cancelled waiters
  (batch R's tag constant; v0 flows straight into the out-stores).
- Count routing by pipe+4 (wait direction): ==1 → a0=v0 (jump's delay
  slot), a1=0; else a1 = (dir==2)?v0:0 via `xori a2,s2,2;
  movn a1,zero,a2`; a0=0. So *sendN=count iff senders were queued,
  *recvN=count iff receivers were queued.
- Optional stores via **bnezl-to-next-instruction** idiom
  (`bnezl s1,+4; sw a0,0(s1)` = conditional delay-slot execution).
- **Pipe reset**: pipe+24 (usedBytes) = 0; pipe+12 = pipe+16 = pipe+8
  (write + tail cursors reset to the read cursor); pipe+4 = 0 (no
  waiters). Cancel empties the pipe.

## sceKernelFreeVpl @0xBCB4 - f(uid, void *block)

- Gate (block & k1<<11) - SINGLE pointer, no +4. cfc0 → 0x80020064.
- Resolve 0x4E8 → 0x8002019C; attr bit **0x2** (alloc/free) → D1.
- block==NULL or `0x187a4(vpl+4 pool, block) < 0` → 0x800201B6.
- Log msgno **113**: handler(113, STALE s8, 2, uid, block) - s8 is
  UNINITIALIZED at log time (only the walk sets it, to the waiter TCB);
  O's "stale s8 quirk" confirmed and explained.
- Waiter walk (queue slot+12, links TCB+0, sentinel slot+12):
  blk = `0x18774(pool, waiter TCB+0x64)` (0x64 = wanted size);
  **TCB+0x60 = blk is written in `jal 0x924`'s DELAY SLOT** - i.e. the
  grant word stores 0x18774's v0 just before the wake call clobbers v0;
  `0x924(waiter)` ret > 0 → count++, remember last woken (s7).
- Alloc-fail policy: slot attr & **0x200** → skip that waiter, keep
  walking; attr without 0x200 → abort walk entirely (`j` to the dispatch
  block with a `li s3,1` delay-slot fixup so the ==1 compare still works).
- Dispatch tail (shared shape): woken==1 → `0x16bf4(t, *(t+16) prio)` +
  `0x16c48(t, st)`; woken>1 → ctx+4=0 + `0xfe4(0, st)`; else release; 0.

## sceKernelFreeFpl @0xCDBC - f(uid, void *block)

- Same gates (table 0x4EC, unknown-uid 0x8002019D, attr bit 0x2,
  NULL block → 0x800201B6). Log msgno **121**: handler(121, block, 2,
  uid, block) - a1 = block here (not stale).
- **Inline block validation math** (no import call):
  base = fpl+4, stride = fpl+28, n = fpl+24; require base <= block <
  base+stride*n (sltu pair), block == base + ((block-base)/stride)*stride
  (`div $0,t1,a2` + `beqzl a2` / `break 7` raw word), and
  `slots[idx]` (array fpl+12, idx*4) == 0 - i.e. free-list membership
  and double-free detection; any failure → 0x800201B6.
- **Direct grant, no loop**: if slot+8 (numWait) > 0 → first waiter =
  slot+12; `waiterTCB+0x60 = block`; wake `0xb08(rec, 0, 0, st)`
  (a0=rec loaded in the jal's delay slot, a1/a2 pre-zeroed) - exactly
  ONE waiter woken per freed FPL block (FPL blocks are single units).
- No waiters → `0xd2e4(fplView, idx)` pushes the freed block's slot
  index back on the free ring (O's ring-alloc helper, pop counterpart).

## sceKernelCancelVpl @0xBEFC / sceKernelCancelFpl @0xCFE0 - twins

Byte-identical except: UID table 0x4E8/0x4EC, unknown-uid error
0x8002019C/0x8002019D, log msgno **114/122**. 78 words each.

- f(uid, s32 *count): gate (count|count+4)&k1<<11 → D3; **NO cfc0**;
  resolve → attr bit **0x8** (cancel) → D1; log(msgno, countPtr, 2, uid,
  countPtr); `v0 = 0x1879c(rec, 0x86D94883)`; `bnezl s8,+4;
  sw v0,0(s8)` optional store of the cancelled count; release; 0.
- O's twin prediction verified exactly (msgno 122 = `li a0,0x7A`).

## Cross-batch notes

- The Cancel* family (EventFlag/MsgPipe/Vpl/Fpl) is now 4-for-4 on
  `0x1879c(rec, 0x86D94883)` returning the cancelled-waiter count, and
  none of them read cfc0 - cancel is interrupt-context legal.
- Import veneer 0x187a4 = SysMem "is this pointer a live block of this
  pool" (validate); 0x18774 = pool alloc(pool, size) returning 0 on
  exhaustion (FreeVpl treats 0 as fail, not an error).
- Register-order caveat seen again (P's GOTCHA): transcribe operands
  verbatim - e.g. CancelMsgPipe's second gate ORs the LIVE a2 against
  s8, and FreeVpl's log passes the stale s8.
