# Batch T - the thread wait/wake-adjacent family

**Verdict: 5 of 5 `*** MATCH ***` on the FIRST compile, zero relocations.**
All source in `match/threadman/tm_t.c`. Harness smoke test passed before
starting (tm.c: 4 MATCH + 2 reloc) and re-verified after finishing.

| function | addr | words | status | iters |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| sceKernelWaitThreadEnd | 0x3AC0 | 2 | *** MATCH *** (veneer `j 0x3AD0; li t3,0`) | 1 |
| sceKernelWaitThreadEndCB | 0x3AC8 | 2 | *** MATCH *** (veneer `j 0x3AD0; li t3,1`) | 1 |
| sceKernelCancelWakeupThread | 0x3218 | 73 | *** MATCH *** | 1 |
| sceKernelDonateWakeupThread | 0x333C | 144 | *** MATCH *** | 1 |
| sceKernelReleaseWaitThread | 0x14C20 | 124 | *** MATCH *** | 1 |

The shared WaitThreadEnd core @0x3AD0 is **170 words** (0x3AD0-0x3D78) - over
the 150w budget, so it is DECODE-ONLY below (not transcribed/matched).
Note on sizes: DonateWakeup is 144w (0x357C-0x333C), not the ~80w a first
glance suggests - measure against the next export (SuspendThread @0x357C)
before budgeting.

---

## 1. CancelWakeupThread @0x3218 (73w) - msgno 51

```
s32 sceKernelCancelWakeupThread(SceUID tid)
{
    if (tid == 0) {                       // tid==0 = CURRENT thread here
        if (cfc0_cause) return 0x80020197;
        tid = ctx->current->[8];          // TCB+8 = id
    }
    acquire; resolve(tid, threadsTable, &rec);    // != 0 -> 0x80020198
    if (user && !(lhu rec+22 & 0x8)) -> 0x800200D1;   // attr bit 0x8
    if (logHandler) log(51, threadRec, 1, tid);
    old = target->[0x50];                 // TCB+0x50 wakeupCount
    target->[0x50] = 0;                   // the cancel
    release; return old;                  // RETURNS the cancelled count
}
```

- The log block's `j 0x32B4` back-edge reuses the store at 0x32B4; the load
  of the old count rides in the `j` delay slot (0x32FC) instead of the
  pre-log load - transcribed literally.
- Unlike Suspend/Resume/Wakeup (tid==0 -> 0x80020197), CancelWakeup treats
  tid==0 as "current thread", gated only by Cause (0x80020197 in interrupt
  context). CancelWakeup on self from a thread is legal.
- Return value = the previous pending wakeup count (PSPSDK: number of
  cancelled wakeups), 0 if none were pending.

## 2. DonateWakeupThread @0x333C (144w) - msgno 52

```
s32 sceKernelDonateWakeupThread(SceUID tid)
{
    if (tid == 0) return 0x80020197;              // (unlike CancelWakeup!)
    if (cfc0_cause) return 0x80020064;
    cookie = acquire();
    if (!cookie || ctx->[0x0C] != 0) { release; return 0x800201A7; } // CAN_NOT_WAIT
    sub_16FA4();                                  // kernel-stack check
    resolve -> 0x80020198; attr bit 0x2 -> D1;
    target = rec + 4*hdr21;  target == ctx->current -> 0x80020197;
    if (target state == 0x10 || 0x20) return 0x800201A2;   // dead
    if (logHandler) log(52, target, 2, tid, myCount);       // extras: tid, count
    s6 = current->[0x50];  current->[0x50] = 0;    // take MY pending wakeups
    sub_858(&ctx->[0x498], 1);                     // donor sleeps: SLEEP queue,
                                                   //   wait type 1, links TCB+0/+4
    current->[0x80] = 0;                           // waitResult (delay slot)
    target->[0x50] += s6;                          // DONATE (delay slot of the
                                                   //   state test: ALWAYS executes)
    if (target state == 4 || 12)                   // WAITING / WAIT+SUSP
        if (target->[0x58] == 1)                   // wait TYPE == sleep
            sub_924(target);                       // release target NOW
    ctx->[4] = 0; sub_FE4(1, 1);                   // block the donor
    release; return current->[0x80];               // donor's waitResult
}
```

- **Donate = wakeup transfer + self-sleep.** The count store lands in the
  delay slot of the state branch, so the donation happens for ANY live
  target state; the sub_924 wake-now only fires when the target is actually
  blocked in a sleep wait (state 4/12 AND waitType 1 - same discriminator
  WakeupThread uses via sub_A08). A target that is READY/RUNNING just
  accumulates the donated count for its next SleepThread.
- The woken sleeping target does NOT consume the donated count -
  SleepThread's blocked return path returns waitResult without re-reading
  TCB+0x50, so the donated count stays pending on the target.
- s8 is reloaded `s8 = &ctx` inside the `j` delay slot at 0x34E4 - the
  wake path lands mid-block at 0x34B4 (`ctx+4 = 0`) with s8 rebound.

## 3. ReleaseWaitThread @0x14C20 (124w) - msgno 28

```
s32 sceKernelReleaseWaitThread(SceUID tid)
{
    if (tid == 0) return 0x80020197;
    acquire; resolve -> 0x80020198;
    if (user && !(lhu rec+22 & 0x2)) -> 0x800200D1;      // attr bit 0x2
    if (thread_ctx && target == ctx->current) -> 0x80020197;  // skipped in intr ctx
    if (target state != 4 && != 12) -> 0x800201A6;       // NOT_WAITING
    wb = target->[0x5C];                                  // object wait-block ptr
    if (wb && user && !(lhu wb+22 & 0x8)) -> 0x800200D1;  // object attr bit 0x8 (cancel)
    if (logHandler) log(28, tid, 1, tid);                 // a1 = tid (not rec!)
    r = sub_924(target);                                  // release-from-wait
    target->[0x80] = 0x800201AA;                          // WAIT_RELEASED (delay slot)
    if (wb) r += sub_C30();                               // cancel object side
    if (r && thread_ctx) { ctx->[4] = 0; sub_FE4(0, st); } // dispatch request
    release; return 0;
}
```

- **Works on ANY wait type** - the gate is the STATE (4/12), never TCB+0x58
  (see "interaction with L's wait-type machine" below).
- sub_924 = the generic release-from-wait (decoded in L/batch R): rejects
  state 0x20, disarms TCB+0x98, unlinks the wait queue via TCB+0/+4, and if
  the timer handle TCB+0x78 is nonzero runs the delay-timer teardown -
  that is what "releases" a DELAYED thread cleanly (no dangling alarm).
- sub_C30 @0xC30 = `jal 0x1879C(rec, 0x85FE2B4C)` - the shared
  cancel-all-waiters primitive, third tag now catalogued (0x86D94883
  CancelEventFlag, 0x5BD49380 sub_924 self-cleanup, **0x85FE2B4C
  ReleaseWaitThread**). Called only when the target was waiting on a UID
  OBJECT (wait-block TCB+0x5C nonzero) - it purges the object's waiter
  list so the object doesn't keep a stale waiter.
- The released thread resumes with waitResult 0x800201AA
  (SCE_KERNEL_ERROR_WAIT_RELEASE), which its wait core (Sleep/delay/sema/
  evf/mbx core) returns to the application.
- Error-path quirk: the D1 block reuses s0 as scratch for the error code
  (`lui s0,0x8002` in the release-jal delay slot).

## 4. WaitThreadEnd veneers + the 170w core @0x3AD0 (decoded, NOT matched)

```
sceKernelWaitThreadEnd   3AC0: j 0x3AD0; li t3,0
sceKernelWaitThreadEndCB 3AC8: j 0x3AD0; li t3,1     // CB flag in t3 again

core(SceUID tid, SceUInt32 *timeout):                 // t3 = CB flag
    gate ((timeout | timeout+4) & k1<<11) < 0 -> 0x800200D3
    tid == 0 -> 0x80020197;  cause != 0 -> 0x80020064
    cookie = acquire(); !cookie || ctx->[0x0C] -> 0x800201A7 (CAN_NOT_WAIT)
    sub_16FA4(); resolve -> 0x80020198; attr bit 0x4 -> D1     [bit 4 = wait perm]
    target = rec+4*hdr21; target == ctx->current -> 0x80020197
    if (CB) {
        if (logHandler) log(56, &ctx, 1, tid);                 // msgno 56 (CB)
        for (;;) {                                             // callback pump
            while (current->[0x9C] /*notifyPending*/) sub_2170(cookie);
            re-resolve(tid) != 0 -> 0x800201B5   // target deleted under us
            ... state check / wait ...
        }
    } else if (logHandler) log(55, &ctx, 1, tid);              // msgno 55 (plain)
    for (;;) {
        if (timeout) clock64 = sub_10D34(sp, *timeout);        // usec -> ticks
        if (target state == 0x10 || 0x20) { result = target rec+84; break; }
            // dead/finished: return its EXIT STATUS (rec+84, seeded
            // 0x800201A4 by StartThread, overwritten by ExitThread)
        current->[0x98] = CB flag;                             // arm CB gate
        result = sub_908(target, 9, 0, sp, timeout);
            // wait TYPE 9 = thread-end; waiters queue ON THE TARGET's
            // slot-view waiter list (same +12 TCB-list field UID objects
            // use), timeout clock on stack, raw timeout ptr as 5th arg (t0)
        if (!(CB && result == 0x800201AB)) break;              // 1AB = notify abort
        // else: loop -> re-pump callbacks and re-wait
    }
    release; return result;
```

- **New wait type: 9 = thread-end** (TCB+0x58 family: 1 sleep, 2 delay,
  3 sema, 4 evflag, 5 mbx-receive, 9 thread-end). The waited-on "object"
  is the target thread itself - ExitThread presumably walks the target's
  waiter list and completes each waiter with the exit status.
- WaitThreadEnd returns the target's EXIT STATUS once the target is dead
  (states 0x10 DORMANT-after-exit / 0x20 killed), without blocking.
- CB variant = the standard SleepThreadCB loop shape (sub_2170 pump +
  0x800201AB notify-abort retry), plus a RE-RESOLVE after every callback
  batch (0x800201B5 if the tid died while callbacks ran) - stricter than
  SleepThreadCB, which never re-resolves.
- msgnos: 55 (plain) / 56 (CB); log a1 = &ctx.

---

## Interaction with L's wait-type machine (TCB+0x58)

L established TCB+0x58 = wait TYPE (1 sleep, 2 delay) and that
WakeupThread's `gate==1` check only breaks SLEEP waits. This batch maps
the full discriminator behavior of the wake family:

| waker | gate on TCB+0x58? | delayed thread (type 2) | sleeping thread (type 1) | object waiter (types 3/4/5/9) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| WakeupThread (H) | YES - type 1 only | refused: wakeupCount++ (TCB+0x50), timer keeps running | sub_A08 wake-now | refused: count++ |
| CancelWakeupThread | NO - only touches TCB+0x50 | zeroes pending count, returns old | ditto (if count was pending) | ditto |
| DonateWakeupThread | YES for the wake-now (type 1 only) | donation accumulates in TCB+0x50; timer keeps running | donation AND sub_924 wake-now | donation accumulates only |
| ReleaseWaitThread | NO - gates on STATE 4/12 instead | RELEASED: sub_924 unlinks + cancels the delay timer (TCB+0x78), waitResult 0x800201AA, -> READY | RELEASED: unlinked from sleep queue, waitResult 0x800201AA, -> READY | RELEASED if object attr bit 8; sub_C30 (0x1879C tag 0x85FE2B4C) purges the object waiter list; waitResult 0x800201AA |
| thread exit | (completes type-9 waiters) | - | - | WaitThreadEnd core returns rec+84 exit status |

So: **ReleaseWaitThread is wait-type-blind** - it releases a delayed
thread exactly as well as a sleeping one; the ONLY extra requirement for
object waits is the TCB+0x5C wait-block pointer being present, its slot
attr bit 0x8 (cancel permission), and the sub_C30 object-side purge.
Wakeup/Donate remain sleep-only for their immediate-wake effect, because
breaking a delay wait early would need the timer teardown sub_924 does.

## New/consolidated facts

- **msgnos**: 28 ReleaseWaitThread (a1 = tid - unusual, most pass the
  record), 51 CancelWakeupThread, 52 DonateWakeup (2 extras: tid, count),
  55 WaitThreadEnd / 56 WaitThreadEndCB (a1 = &ctx).
- **attr permission bits on the thread record (u16 rec+22)**: 0x1 refer,
  0x2 suspend/resume/start/release-wait/donate, 0x4 wait-thread-end,
  0x8 cancel-wakeup (+ the object-side 0x8 cancel bit seen by R/N).
- **CancelWakeupThread is the only tid==0-means-current member of the
  wake family** (Suspend/Resume/Start/Wakeup/Donate/ReleaseWait/WaitEnd
  all reject tid==0 with 0x80020197).
- **sub_C30 = 0x1879C(rec, 0x85FE2B4C)** - third cancel-all-waiters tag.
- **0x800201A6** = SCE_KERNEL_ERROR_NOT_WAITING_HANDLER? (state not 4/12
  in ReleaseWaitThread); **0x800201AA** = WAIT_RELEASED waitResult;
  **0x800201B5** = thread deleted while waiting (CB re-resolve failure).
- Wait types now: 1 sleep, 2 delay, 3 sema, 4 evflag, 5 mbx, 9 thread-end.
- Recipe: nothing new needed - the tm_k literal-target transcription
  recipe closed all 345 words first-compile. One thing worth repeating:
  verify function SIZE against the next export address before estimating
  effort (Donate looked ~80w in the middle of the listing, is 144w).

| 0x3AC0 | 2w | sceKernelWaitThreadEnd | ✅ (batch T, 1 iter, 0 relocs) |
| 0x3AC8 | 2w | sceKernelWaitThreadEndCB | ✅ (batch T, 1 iter, 0 relocs) |
| 0x3218 | 73w | sceKernelCancelWakeupThread | ✅ (batch T, 1 iter, 0 relocs) |

| 0x14C20 | 124w | sceKernelReleaseWaitThread | ✅ (batch T, 1 iter, 0 relocs) |

# Batch U - LwMutex internals: the shared lock core + Delete

Agent U · source `match/threadman/tm_u.c` · both **MATCH, zero relocs** (2
compiles total: run 1 = Delete MATCH first-compile + core 5 diffs, all one
class of hand-transcription typo; run 2 = both MATCH).

| Addr | Words | Function | Status | Iters |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0x7CE4 | **236** | `sub_07CE4` - shared Lock core (INTERNAL) | ✅ MATCH | 2 |
| 0x7908 | **85** | `sceKernelDeleteLwMutex` | ✅ MATCH | 1 |

Size correction: Q.md estimated the core at 244w and Delete at 92w. Actual
extents: core 0x7CE4..0x8093 (236w, TryLock starts 0x8094), Delete
0x7908..0x7A5B (85w, Unlock starts 0x7A5C).

## How an INTERNAL function was compared (new method, documented)

`tools/match.py` only auto-compares names present in `orig/threadman-map.json`
(export map) - `sub_07CE4` is not an export and would never be compared.
Neither results/K.md nor results/G.md documents an internal-function trick
(their batches were all exports), so the method used here:

1. Name the transcription `sub_07CE4` in tm_u.c with `.globl`/`.type`/`.size`
   so it lands in the .o's `.symtab` with a size (the harness's parse_obj
   picks up exactly that shape).
2. Compile with the normal harness (`python3 tools/match.py
   match/threadman/tm_u.c`) - this simultaneously auto-compared the export
   `sceKernelDeleteLwMutex` living in the same .o and left `build/tm_u.o`
   behind.
3. Diff the symbol's words against retail at 0x7CE4 with a 40-line helper
   (`/tmp/cmp_internal.py`) that **imports** `parse_obj` / `retail_words` /
   `mask_for` from `tools/match.py` - so the ok / reloc / DIFF categories are
   byte-for-byte the same rules the harness uses. No tools/ modification.

Usage: `python3 /tmp/cmp_internal.py build/tm_u.o sub_07CE4 0x7CE4 236`.
This recipe is reusable for any internal function (patch the symbol name,
address and word count).

## Pointer-notation refinement (byte-backed, used below)

Two distinct things the earlier notes called "ctx":

- **S** = the global control struct at module+0x40 (`addiu r,gpad,64` gives
  &S): S+0 = pointer **P** to the current thread's TCB, S+8 = user log
  handler, S+0x0C = dispatch-state gate (CAN_NOT_WAIT check), S+0x4C8 slot
  table, S+0x4DC LwMutex table.
- **P** = `*(S+0)` = current thread's TCB. In this core: P+8 = owner
  identity, P+0x10 = current priority, P+0x60 = wanted count, P+0x98 = CB
  flag, P+0x9C = notifyPending - i.e. the established TCB offsets.

**Q.md's owner-representation quirk is RESOLVED**: create/trylock fast-acquire
store `*(P+8)` into work+4 - P is the current TCB, so that is its **thread
ID** (TCB+8), the exact same field Unlock's wake-handoff writes from the
waiter (`*(waiterTCB+8)`). The owner field holds a thread ID uniformly; it
was never a TCB pointer. (TryLock's `lw t3,8(s1)` also reads P+8 - its s1 was
reloaded to P two instructions earlier.)

## sub_07CE4 - the shared Lock core (decode)

Entry: (a0 = pWork, a1 = unlockCount → s4, a2 = timeout ptr → s2,
**t3 = CB flag** ← the veneers' `li t3,0/1`; s7 = t3).

1. **Gates**: `(pWork | pWork+32) & k1<<11` → D3; `(timeout | timeout+4) &
   mask` → D3 (the 4-byte object gated at both ends, like the refer out-buffer).
2. `cfc0 cause` → 0x80020064. Acquire 0x186AC (cookie → s6, delay slot of the
   following beqz). **cookie == 0 OR *(S+0x0C) != 0 → 0x800201A7**
   (CAN_NOT_WAIT - released with the cookie first).
3. `jal 0x16FA4` - dispatch pump (as Q.md guessed).
4. Resolve `work->uid` via 0x18784(S+0x4DC tbl, &rec): st → 0x800201CA;
   **back-link** `recView4DC+12 != pWork` → 0x800201CA. uid stashed at sp+20
   (delay slot of the resolver jal - stores PRE-call v0, i.e. the uid; this
   is also the log a3 and the re-resolve a0 later).
5. User gate `lhu rec+22 & 2` (movz kernel bypass) → 0x800200D1.
6. **Count gate** on unlockCount (s4): `blez` or (`>=2` and !(slot+0 attr &
   0x200)) → 0x800201BD - note the 0x200 test reads **slot+0**, like
   TryLock/Unlock, not rec+22.
7. **Log**: CB variant msgno **86**, non-CB **85** - handler(85|86, pWork,
   a2=2, a3=uid, t0=unlockCount). (Fills the msgnos 82/84/85/86/87/89 family
   catalog: 85 = Lock, 86 = LockCB.)
8. **Recursive path** (`work->owner == *(P+8)`, beql, delay loads slot attr):
   attr 0x200 → `count += unlockCount`, `bltz` → 0x800201CD else store,
   release, 0. No 0x200 → 0x800201CF. Identical shape to TryLock's.
9. **Timeout arm**: timeout ptr != 0 → `a1 = *timeout; jal 0x10D34(sp,
   *timeout)` - helper (verified: prologue at 0x10D34, calls clock reader
   0xECD4) that fills the 8-byte wait-timer block at sp+0/sp+4. Then
   `sltu a3,zero,s7` (CB flag) and `0x800201AB` are parked at sp+24/sp+28.
10. **Wait loop** (label 0x7ED0): CB → check `*(P+0x9C)` notifyPending; if
    pending → `jal 0x2170` (callback pump) then **re-resolve** the uid
    (0x18784; failure → **0x800201B5** = mutex deleted while waiting) and
    rejoin the loop head; !CB → straight in.
11. **Enqueue decision** (waitCount = slot+8):
    - `waitCount <= 0` → owner check.
    - slot attr & 0x100 (priority-ordered): inline pre-check `curPrio(P+0x10)
      < headWaiterPrio(slot+12 → TCB+0x10)` (slt; strictly less) - if the
      caller would outrank the head, owner check first; else enqueue.
    - FIFO (no 0x100): enqueue directly.
    - **Owner check** (0x7F24): `work->owner == 0` → **fast acquire**:
      count = unlockCount, owner = *(P+8), release, return 0 (no queueing).
    - **Enqueue**: TCB+0x60 = wanted count (unlockCount), TCB+0x98 = CB flag,
      `jal 0x680(rec, 13, slot->attr, sp, timeoutPtr)` - **wait type 13 =
      LwMutex**; the ordered insert happens inside 0x680 keyed on the attr
      passed in a2 - the SAME mechanism/callee the sema wait core uses
      (batch R), so **queue order = priority (attr 0x100) or FIFO**, decided
      per-object by the slot attr.
    - After wake: `CB && result == 0x800201AB` → retry the wait loop
      (callback abort); else release, return the wait result (0 = woken by
      Unlock's handoff - the unlocker already wrote count/owner).
12. Dead code note: `addiu t1,s8,12` (work+12) computed in the `blez` delay
    slot at 0x7EF8 but t1 is reloaded at 0x7F90 before any use - a leftover,
    transcribed as-is.

### Answers to the batch questions

- **Priority inheritance? NO.** Nothing in the core (or in Unlock, per Q)
  ever raises the holder's priority - no write to any TCB+0x10 except reads.
  The only priority logic is *queue order* (attr 0x100). A LwMutex is the
  "lightweight" primitive precisely because it skips inheritance.
- **Recursive counting? YES, gated**: owner == current requires attr 0x200
  (else 0x800201CF); then count += unlockCount with signed-overflow →
  0x800201CD. The count gate also allows unlockCount >= 2 only with 0x200.
- **Wake-handoff order** (with Unlock from batch Q): Unlock hands the lock to
  the FIRST waiter in the slot+12 queue (count = waiter's TCB+0x60 wanted,
  owner = waiter's TCB+8 id, sub_924 + 0x16BF4/0x16C48 wake pair). That
  queue's order is fixed at insert time by this core via 0x680: attr 0x100 →
  priority order, else FIFO. So: **FIFO handoff by default, priority-ordered
  when attr 0x100, no inheritance, no bypass** (the inline slt pre-check only
  short-circuits into the owner fast-path when the mutex is momentarily
  unlocked and the caller outranks the head waiter).

## sceKernelDeleteLwMutex (decode)

Textbook family skeleton, 85 words, first-compile MATCH:

- gate pWork → D3; cfc0 cause → 0x80020064; acquire (cookie → s3, captured in
  the resolver's jal delay slot as usual); resolve work->uid; st → 0x800201CA;
  back-link → 0x800201CA; user gate **attr bit 0x10** (delete) → 0x800200D1.
- **Log msgno 83**: handler(83, pWork, a2=**1**, a3=uid) - completes the
  family catalog (82 create / 83 delete / 84 unlock / 85 lock / 86 lockCB /
  87 trylock / 89 refer).
- `jal 0x1877C(uid)` frees the UID record, then **work+16 (uid) = -1** and -
  in the release jal's delay slot - **work+4 (owner) = -1** (not 0!), return 0.
- **No waiter check**: Delete never looks at slot+8/waitCount - deleting a
  contended LwMutex strands waiters (they will hit the CB/re-resolve path's
  0x800201B5, or the 0x800201CA back-link on their next entry).

## Recipe notes

- The transcription recipe from batches F-L needed nothing new: numeric
  registers, movz under `.set mips4`, cfc0 as raw `.word`, absolute `jal`/`j`
  targets (all verified against `--disas`, including the new callee 0x10D34).
- Typo class worth flagging (the only 5 diffs, all in run 1): I wrote t6/t7
  as **$22/$23** (those are s6/s7) - t6/t7 are **$14/$15**. Register-number
  slips for the t4-t7 range are easy because the disassembler's symbolic
  names mask the numbers; double-check t4-t7 operand numbers specifically.
- Smoke test passed before starting; `tm.c`, `tm_q.c` and `tm_u.c` all MATCH
  together in one harness run (full family now: 9/9 exports + internal core).

<!-- ==== V.md ==== -->

# Batch V - MsgPipe SEND CORE (internal, 0x97E0)

Source: `match/threadman/tm_v.c` (tm_k/tm_p noreorder-transcription recipe).
Compared with `build/vdiff.py` (new internal-function word diff harness,
validated 4/4 against batch P's known matches incl. the 0x97D0 veneer).

| Addr | Words | Function | Status | Iters |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0x097E0 | 359w | `sub_097E0` (send core behind SendMsgPipe veneers) | **MATCH** | 2 |

**359/359 words exact, 0 relocation-dependent diffs.** (3 compiles total:
run 1 = 342/359 - 15 `j label` words emitted R_MIPS_26 relocs, fixed by
using absolute `j 0xNNNN` targets, + 2 branch words off by one, see GOTCHA
below; run 2 = 344 due to a comment-mangling bug in my own sed script; run
3 = clean.) The 6 module-base `lui rX,%hi(g_padV)` words assemble to imm
0x0000, byte-exact in the .o like all previous batches.

**Size correction**: the send core is 359 words (0x97E0-0x9D78), not the
361w recorded in results/P.md/PROGRESS.md. TrySendMsgPipe starts at 0x9D7C
(`addiu sp,sp,-80` prologue) and spans 266w to 0xA1A0, immediately before
the ReceiveMsgPipe veneer @0xA1A4 - boundaries all cross-check.

## Kernel-view signature

```
s32 sub_097E0(SceUID uid a0, void *sendbuf a1, u32 size a2,
              u32 waitMode a3, u32 *pSentSize t0, u32 *pTimeoutUsec t1,
              int cbFlag t3)
```
Args 5/6 ride in t0/t1 (this module's >4-arg convention); the CB flag rides
in t3 set by the 2w veneers (`j 0x97e0; li t3,0|1`).

## Entry gates (in order)

1. k1 pointer gates: `((sendbuf|sendbuf+size|size) & k1<<11) < 0`,
   `((pSentSize|pSentSize+4) & mask) < 0`, `((pTimeout|pTimeout+4) & mask)
   < 0` → 0x800200D3
2. `ins` clears waitMode bit 0; `(waitMode & ~1) != 0` → **0x80020195**
   (only waitMode 0/1 legal)
3. `cfc0 cause` != 0 → 0x80020064 (cannot block in interrupt)
4. 0x186ac cookie == 0 OR ctx+0x0C != 0 → 0x800201A7 (CAN_NOT_WAIT,
   dispatch pending) - same gate pair as batch L's sleep family
5. `0x16fa4()` - NEW callee: kernel-stack snapshot/guard (reads
   `*(module+0x40)` TCB, +0x14 attr, +0x2C kernel-stack base, subtracts
   sp); **result ignored** - called for side effect before blocking
6. resolve uid via 0x18784(ctx+0x4E4) → 0x8002019E
7. user threads need `(lhu rec+22 & 2)` else 0x800200D1 (send = attr bit
   0x2, same bit plan as batch N's Mbx Send)
8. pipe+20 (bufSize) != 0 && bufSize < size → **0x800201BC**
9. log: handler(**100** non-CB / **101** CB, a1 = live caller-s8 residue,
   4 extras: uid, sendbuf, size) - a1 is genuinely an uninitialized-s8
   read in retail; transcribed verbatim
10. pTimeoutUsec != 0 → `0x10d34(sp, *pTimeoutUsec)`: NEW callee = 64-bit
    **absolute deadline builder** (`now` from 0xECE4 + usec, stored as 8
    bytes at sp). a2/a3 are stale garbage at this call - ignored by callee.

## The send loop

Views: pipe = rec+4*hdr21(ctx+0x4E4), slot = rec+4*hdr21(ctx+0x4C8),
TCB = `*(module+0x40)` (**ctx+0x00 = current TCB** - refines batch C's
"ctx+0x08" note; 0x16FA4 reads it the same way).

Loop head (reached from the top and from the CB retry):
- CB flag && TCB+0x9C (notifyPending) != 0 → `0x2170(cookie)` callback
  pump, re-resolve uid (fail → **0x800201B5**, pipe deleted while pumping),
  retry. This is the CB loop: a blocked CB sender with an abort bounces
  through the pump and re-runs the whole decision with fresh state.
- pipe+4 selects the data path:

### pipe+4 == 2 (RECEIVERS queued): direct handoff
If slot+8 (waiter count) > 0: walk the receiver TCB list (links at TCB+0):
- n = min(my remaining, receiver's want [TCB+0x64])
- `0x1880c(dst = recv TCB+0x60, src = my cursor, n)` - bounded copy-out
  straight into the waiting receiver's buffer (no ring-buffer transit)
- recv.dst += n, recv.want -= n; if recv.pSent (TCB+0x6C):
  `*pRecv = recv.size - recv.want`
- wake via `0x924(recvTCB)` iff recv.waitMode == 1 (wait-any) or want == 0;
  slot+4 = 1 while the head receiver stays partially served, = 0 on wake;
  sub_924 results accumulate in 40(sp)
- repeat while my rem > 0 && pipe+4 still == 2 && slot+8 > 0, else fall
  through to the buffer path / finish

### pipe+4 == 1 (SENDERS queued): FIFO discipline
Block behind them - EXCEPT the priority bypass: slot attr & 0x100 AND
slot+4 == 0 (no partial handoff in progress) AND my TCB+0x10 prio <
head sender's prio → jump straight to the ring-buffer path (9A14→9A1C).
Otherwise set the 48(sp) "senders queued" flag and go to finish → block.

### buffer path (pipe+4 == 0 or bypass/fall-through)
free = pipe+20 − pipe+24; n = min(free, rem). Write via `0xafc0(pipe,
src, n)` - NEW callee, ring-buffer write (updates cursors/used; probably
also wakes receivers internally) - only when:
- waitMode == 1 (wait-any) && n > 0, OR
- waitMode == 0 (wait-all) && n == rem (whole message fits)

Then src += n, rem -= n.

### finish / partial-send handling (the deliverable)
- if pSentSize: `*pSentSize = size - rem` (sent so far - a partial send
  returns success 0 with the count)
- "partial" = (size != rem); return-now condition:
  `(waitMode != 1) || partial` AND `(waitMode != 0) || (rem == 0)`
  → i.e. waitMode==1 returns after ANY progress; waitMode==0 returns only
  when rem == 0; a size==0 call returns immediately
- otherwise BLOCK: pipe+4 = 1 (claim senders-queued direction), TCB wait
  fields: +0x60 src cursor, +0x64 rem, +0x68 size, +0x6C pSentSize,
  +0x70 waitMode, +0x74 = 0, +0x98 CB flag; if the 48(sp) senders-queued
  flag is set, slot+4 = partial flag (=0 on this path); then
  `0x908(rec, waitType 8, attr & 0x100, deadline@sp, pTimeout)`
  - **wait type 8 = msgpipe send**; priority-ordered insert iff attr
  bit 0x100 (same selector plan as batch R's sema waits)
- enqueue result == 0x800201AB && CB → reload src/rem from TCB and retry
  the loop (the CB loop above); non-CB AB returns 0x800201AB
- epilogue: woken count (40(sp)) != 0 → ctx+4 = 0 + `0xfe4(0, cookie)`
  dispatch request (shared batch R tail); release 0x186dc; return
  s0 = 0 / 0x800201AB

## Confirmation of P.md decode

pipe+4 = **1 senders queued / 2 receivers queued** confirmed from the
write side (blocking sender stores 1; the handoff runs on ==2). Ring
cursors never touched here except through 0xafc0. slot+4 = partial-
handoff/priority-lock flag (read by the bypass gate, set while a queued
receiver stays partially served, cleared on wake).

## GOTCHAs (2 iterations' worth)

- **`.dis` annotation glitches**: `beqz s1 @0x9A1C` (1220005A) really
  targets 0x9B88 (the `j`), not the annotated 0x9B84 (the `lw t5` reload
  - its delay slot already loaded t5); `beqzl v0 @0x9D10` (5040FF26)
  really targets 0x99AC (the `beqz`), not 0x99A8 (the `lw v0` - the
  delay-likely already loaded v0). The OTHER branch to the same region
  (`beqz s1 @0x9C2C`, 1220FFD5) does target 0x9B84 because t5 is stale
  after the handoff's jals. Raw words are authoritative - recompute every
  branch target from the word, never trust the annotation when a delay
  slot makes the "obvious" target's first instruction redundant.
- `j` to a local asm label emits R_MIPS_26 on the label - use absolute
  `j 0xNNNN` (retail target) for every intra-function jump, as tm_p did.

## Harness note

`build/vdiff.py [obj] [addr] [symbol]` - word diff for internal functions
(match.py only compares mapped exports). Validated against tm_p.o's four
matches first. Reusable for the remaining cores: Receive core @0xA1B4
(379w per P.md - recheck), TrySend @0x9D7C (266w), TryReceive @0xA798
(290w), CancelMsgPipe @0xAC20 (108w).

# Batch W - MsgPipe RECEIVE CORE (internal, 0xA1B4) + TrySendMsgPipe (0x9D7C)

Source: `match/threadman/tm_w.c` (tm_v noreorder-transcription recipe). Compared
with `build/vdiff.py`, first re-validated against tm_v.o's known 359/359 match.

| Addr | Words | Function | Status | Iters |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0x0A1B4 | 377w | `sub_0A1B4` (receive core behind ReceiveMsgPipe/CB veneers 0xA1A4/0xA1AC) | **MATCH** | 1 (364/377 -> clean) |
| 0x09D7C | 266w | `sub_09D7C` = `sceKernelTrySendMsgPipe` | **MATCH** | 2 (262/266 -> clean) |

**643/643 words exact across both, 0 relocation-dependent diffs** (4 compiles
total; the first rejected on a gas error, see GOTCHAs). TrySend is a mapped
export but transcribed as an internal (`sub_09D7C`) so vdiff covers both
uniformly - match.py prints "(no mapped export functions found)" for this file,
expected.

**Size correction**: the receive core is **377w** (0xA1B4-0xA794), not the 379w
carried in results/P.md - exactly the +2 skew P had for the send core (V found
359 not 361). TryReceive @0xA798 follows immediately; boundaries cross-check.

## Kernel-view signatures

```
s32 sub_0A1B4(SceUID uid a0, void *recvbuf a1, u32 size a2,
              u32 waitMode a3, u32 *pRecvSize t0, u32 *pTimeoutUsec t1,
              int cbFlag t3)                     /* frame 128 */
s32 sub_09D7C(SceUID uid a0, void *sendbuf a1, u32 size a2,
              u32 waitMode a3, u32 *pSentSize t0) /* frame 80, no timeout */
```

## Receive core 0xA1B4 - semantics

### Entry gates (in order)
1. k1 pointer gates recvbuf/size, pRecvSize, pTimeoutUsec → 0x800200D3
   (the pTimeout gate has no dedicated fail branch - it falls into gate1's lui)
2. `(waitMode & ~1) != 0` → 0x80020195 ; `cfc0 cause` != 0 → 0x80020064
3. cookie == 0 OR ctx+0x0C != 0 → 0x800201A7 (CAN_NOT_WAIT), then
   `0x16fa4()` stack snapshot (result ignored), resolve ctx+0x4E4 →
   0x8002019E
4. user threads need `(lhu rec+22 & 4)` else 0x800200D1 - **receive = attr
   bit 0x4** (send was bit 0x2)
5. pipe bufSize != 0 && bufSize < size → 0x800201BC
6. log: handler(**103** plain / **104** CB, a1 = s8 residue - same stale-
   register quirk as send's 100/101; extras a3=uid, t0=recvbuf, t1=size,
   t2=waitMode)
7. pTimeoutUsec != 0 → `0x10d34(sp, *usec)` absolute deadline

### The receive loop
Views: pipe = rec+4*hdr21(ctx+0x4E4), slot = rec+4*hdr21(ctx+0x4C8),
TCB = `*(module+0x40)`. CB retry: TCB+0x9C notifyPending → 0x2170 pump +
re-resolve (fail → 0x800201B5) - identical shape to send.

- **pipe+4 == 2 (SENDERS queued)** - priority bypass gate first:
  slot attr **0x1000** && slot+4 == 0 && my TCB+0x10 prio < head sender's
  → jump straight to the ring-read path; else set the 52(sp)
  senders-queued flag and go finish (block behind them). NOTE the bit is
  **0x1000 here, not 0x100** - see block enqueue below for the pairing.
- **ring read**: used = pipe+24; if used != 0: n = min(used, want), NEW
  callee `0xb094(pipe, dst, n)` - the ring-buffer READ counterpart of
  0xafc0 - then want -= n, dst += n, re-read pipe+4 (0xb094 can wake
  producers / flip direction).
- **pipe+4 == 1 after that (senders queued)** - direct handoff FROM each
  blocked sender (walk the waiter TCB list, links at TCB+0):
  - direct copy: n = min(sender's rem [TCB+0x64], my want) via
    `0x1880c(my dst, sender's src cursor [TCB+0x60], n)`; sender src += n,
    rem -= n; if sender pSent [TCB+0x6C]: `*pSent = sender size - rem`
  - **ring-drain** (the new part): free = bufSize - used; n2 =
    min(free, senderRem); the receiver PUSHES the sender's remaining bytes
    into the ring via `0xafc0(pipe, senderSrc, n2)` iff
    **(senderMode == 1 && n2 > 0) OR (senderMode == 0 && free >= sRem)** -
    the exact mirror of the send-side ring-write discipline (wait-any moves
    any progress; wait-all only moves whole messages)
  - **sender wake conditions**: `0x924(senderTCB)` iff
    **(senderMode == 1 && the receiver copied ≥1 byte directly) OR
    sender's rem == 0**; slot+4 = 0 in the delay slot on wake
  - **partial sender** (wait-all, rem > 0, not drainable): slot+4 = 1 and
    the walk STOPS (jumps to finish) - the sender stays queued
    partially-served, exactly like send's treatment of receivers
  - loop to next sender only while pipe+4 still == 1 AND slot+8 > 0;
    0x924 results accumulate in 44(sp)

### finish / partial-receive (the deliverable)
- if pRecvSize: `*pRecvSize = size - rem` - **a partial receive returns
  SUCCESS (0) with the count of bytes actually received**; "partial" =
  (size != rem)
- return-now condition mirrors send: `(waitMode != 1) || partial` AND
  `(waitMode != 0) || (rem == 0)` → **waitMode 1 (wait-any) returns after
  ANY progress; waitMode 0 (wait-all) only when the buffer is drained to
  rem == 0**; size == 0 returns immediately
- otherwise BLOCK: pipe+4 = **2 (claim receivers-queued direction)**, TCB
  wait fields: +0x60 dst cursor, +0x64 want, +0x68 size, +0x6C pRecvSize,
  +0x70 waitMode, **+0x74 = 1** (send stores 0 - a direction tag on the
  blocked waiter), +0x98 CB flag; if the senders-queued flag was set,
  slot+4 = partial flag; then `0x908(rec, waitType 8, (slotattr>>4)&0x100,
  deadline@sp, pTimeoutUsec)` - **wait type 8 is shared by send AND
  receive**; the receiver's priority-ordered insert selector is
  `(slotattr >> 4) & 0x100` = slot attr bit 0x1000, which is why the
  bypass gate reads 0x1000 while send's is 0x100
- enqueue result 0x800201AB && CB → reload dst/want from TCB+0x60/+0x64
  and retry the loop; non-CB AB returns 0x800201AB
- epilogue: woken count 44(sp) != 0 → ctx+4 = 0 + `0xfe4(0, cookie)`
  dispatch request; release 0x186dc; return 0 / 0x800201AB

## TrySendMsgPipe 0x9D7C - semantics

- gates: sendbuf/pSentSize k1 gates → 0x800200D3 (no timeout argument at
  all); `(waitMode & ~1) != 0` → 0x80020195 checked BEFORE enter-critical
  (error path restores k1 but never releases); then 0x186ac cookie,
  resolve → 0x8002019E, attr bit 0x2 → 0x800200D1, bufSize < size →
  0x800201BC; log msgno **102** (a1 = s8 residue, t2 = waitMode)
- **cfc0 cause is read AFTER resolve and never errors** - TrySend cannot
  block so interrupt context is legal; the cause is only used at the end:
  woken != 0 && cause == 0 → ctx+4 = 0 + `0xfe4(0, cookie)` before the
  plain release+return-0
- pipe+4 == 1 (senders queued): same priority bypass as the send core
  (attr 0x100 && slot+4 == 0 && prio win) else immediate **0x800201B3**
- pipe+4 == 2 && waitMode == 0: walks the ENTIRE receiver queue first and
  adds every waiter's want [TCB+0x64] to free - a whole-message fit via
  direct handoff counts even with a full ring; still short → 0x800201B3
- buffer path: ring write `0xafc0(pipe, src, min(free,rem))` iff
  (mode1 && n > 0) or (mode0 && n == rem); receivers handoff identical to
  the send core's (copy into TCB+0x60, wake 0x924 iff rMode == 1 or
  want == 0, slot+4 = 1 while partially served, woken accumulates at
  12(sp))
- **returns 0x800201B3 iff it would block**: (mode0 && rem > 0) or
  (mode1 && nothing sent - `size ^ rem == 0`); else 0

## .dis annotation glitches found in 0x9D7C-0xA798 (raw words authoritative)

1. `0xA04C 01104821` annotated **"addiu t1,t0,s0"** - the word is opcode
   SPECIAL/funct 0x21 = **addu**; gas rejects 3-register addiu ("register
   value used as expression"), which is what exposed it.
2. `0xA14C 080027B5` annotated **"j 0x9ed0"** - raw word targets
   **0x9ED4** (the `li s2,1`); the `lw a3,4(s5)` at 0xA150 is its DELAY
   SLOT, so the reload is not skipped, only the redundant `li`.
3. `0xA698 8E2E0010` annotated **"lw s8,16(s1)"** - rt field is 14, i.e.
   **lw t6,16(s1)**; proven by the following `slt t4,t5,t6`.
Plus two branch targets where the .dis was right but the naive label
placement is wrong (same class as V's): beqzl @0x9F4C targets 0x9F6C
(its delay slot already loaded s3) and beqzl @0xA72C targets 0xA37C
(delay slot already loaded v0).

## GOTCHAs (4 compiles' worth)

- The gas rejection (addiu/addu above) was the only toolchain issue; all
  other first-run diffs were transcription typos (dest register $2 vs $6
  for `a2` in three places - writing the C-level arg name as a register
  number) and one inverted jal/delay-slot pair at 0xA6E8 (retail puts
  `jal 0x186dc` first, `lw a0` in the delay slot).
- Frame maps: receive core 128B (rec@16, uid@20, mode@24, pRecv@28,
  pTmo@32, cookie@36, head@40, woken@44, size@48, sqflag@52, CB@56,
  mode!=1@60, mode!=0@64, CBnz@68, deadline@sp+0..7); TrySend 80B
  (rec@0, pSent@4, cookie@8, woken@12, size@16, cause@20).

## Remaining MsgPipe internals (unassigned)

TryReceive @0xA798 (290w per P.md - expect ~288 by the +2 pattern) and
CancelMsgPipe @0xAC20 (108w). TryReceive should be this receive core
minus blocking (compare TrySend's deltas against the send core: no
timeout arg gates, no 16fa4/908/2170, cause deferred to the dispatch
gate, B3 on would-block).

<!-- ==== X.md ==== -->

# Batch X - IPC create/delete: sema, event flag + the 157-word VPL creator

Source: `match/threadman/tm_x.c`. 5/5 assigned targets MATCH, ALL
FIRST-COMPILE, zero relocations (module-base `lui %hi(g_padX)` words resolve
to imm 0, same as retail `lui rX,0`).

NOTE: batch X is a relaunch of a dead agent whose reported matches
(CreateSema/DeleteSema/CreateEventFlag/DeleteEventFlag) were LOST with its
workspace - no tm_x.c/results/X.md existed in the canonical tree. All five
functions below were (re)done from scratch here; iteration count 1 each.

## Results

| Addr | Words | Function | Status | Iters |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0x04248 | 95 | `sceKernelCreateSema` | *** MATCH *** | 1 |
| 0x043C4 | 69 | `sceKernelDeleteSema` | *** MATCH *** | 1 |
| 0x05138 | 93 | `sceKernelCreateEventFlag` | *** MATCH *** | 1 |
| 0x052AC | 69 | `sceKernelDeleteEventFlag` | *** MATCH *** | 1 |
| 0x0B44C | 157 | `sceKernelCreateVpl` | *** MATCH *** | 1 (O.md decode closed) |

## The Create* skeleton (now 4 families confirmed: sema/evf/mbx/vpl)

All four creators are the SAME shape, differing only in table offset, attr
mask width/idiom, object-view store list and msgno:

1. `k1<<11` mask in v1; save args to s-regs; **name gate** `mask&name < 0`
   -> 0x800200D3 (bltz).
2. **opt gate** `(opt | opt+4) & mask < 0` -> 0x800200D3 (bgez skip). opt is
   the LAST declared argument: 5th arg in **t0** (sema: `a2=t0+4; a1=a2|t0`),
   4th arg in **a3** (evf: `a2=a3+4; a1=a2|a3`).
3. `cfc0 cause != 0` -> 0x80020064.
4. attr legality check (3 idioms seen):
   - sema `ins t1,zero,0,9` + bnez -> 0x80020191 (mask 0x1FF)
   - evf `li t2,-768; and t1,attr,t2` + bnez -> 0x80020191 (mask 0x2FF)
   - vpl `li t2,-17408; and t1,attr,t2` + bnez -> 0x80020191 (mask 0x43FF)
   - mbx `li t10,-1536; and` (0x1FF, batch N)
5. `jal 0x186AC` cookie; ctx = module+0x40 via `lui+addiu 64`.
6. **`jal 0x1875C(table, name, (attr | k1>>31) & 0xFF, &rec)`** - the UID
   "user" byte = (attr | usermode) truncated to 8 bits, NOT just the user
   flag as earlier batches implied (matches O.md's CreateVpl note; now
   confirmed as the family-wide rule for sema/evf/vpl).
7. On success: reload BOTH table headers, idx bytes at hdr+21, obj view =
   rec+4*idx(own table), slot view = rec+4*idx(0x4C8); store fields;
   `uid = rec+12`.
8. Optional log dispatch: handler at **ctx+8** (`lw tX,8(ctx)`) -
   `jalr tX` with args (msgno, a1, nextra=1, uid). **a1 varies by family**:
   sema/evf/mbx pass **opt**; CreateVpl passes the **object view pointer**
   (s8 recycled). Delete* pass **uid**.
9. `jal 0x186DC(cookie)` release; return uid (in v0 via delay slot).

## The Delete* skeleton (sema/evf identical twins of N's DeleteMbx)

`DeleteSema(uid)` = DeleteMbx with table ctx+0x4D0, msgno 58, unknown-uid
**0x80020199**; `DeleteEventFlag` = ctx+0x4D4, msgno 66, **0x8002019A**.
Both: cfc0 -> 0x80020064, acquire, resolve 0x18784, attr16 rec+22 bit 0x10
(movz kernel bypass) -> 0x800200D1, log (msgno, uid, 1, uid), `0x1877C(uid)`,
release, return. Register/branch structure is word-identical between the two
(only the 3 immediates + addresses change) - as with CancelVpl/CancelFpl in
batch S, twin functions are transcribe-once-copy-thrice.

**Delete* return value quirk**: the success path hardcodes `move v1,zero`
after release - `sceKernelDeleteSema`/`DeleteEventFlag` return 0 and
DISCARD the 0x1877C result (the Delete* family routes every exit through
v1; error paths `ori v1,...`). Same shape observed in N's DeleteMbx.

## Object layouts created

- **Sema obj view** (rec+4*hdr21(ctx+0x4D0)): +0 opt, +4 currentCount(=init),
  +8 maxWaiters(=max), +12 initCount snapshot (=init, stored AGAIN);
  slot view +0 attr. (Matches ReferSemaStatus field order, batch M.)
- **Evf obj view** (ctx+0x4D4): +0 opt, +4 currentPattern(=init), +8
  initPattern(=init), +12 zero, +16 zero; slot +0 attr.
- **Vpl obj view** (ctx+0x4E8): +0 opt, +4 sysmem pool handle (0x1878C
  result), +8 pool free size (0x1874C(pool) queried at create - refines
  O.md's "+8 total pool size": it stores the FREE size result); slot +0 attr.

## CreateVpl @0x0B44C - O.md's skipped 157-worder, now closed

`SceUID CreateVpl(name a0, partition a1, attr a2, size a3, opt t0)`:

1. name/opt k1 gates -> 0x800200D3; cause -> 0x80020064;
   `attr & ~0x43FF` -> 0x80020191; `size == 0` -> **0x800201B7**
   (beqz BEFORE acquire - no release needed, k1 restore only).
2. acquire; **partition query `0x187BC(partition, sp)`** with `*(sp)=16`
   preset (store in the jal delay slot); nonzero -> release, return verbatim.
3. User-mode gate `bgez k1` (mask sign) skips for kernel: user callers need
   `(*(sp+12) & 3) == 3` else release + **0x800200D1** (load of sp+12 sits
   in the bgez delay slot - executed on both paths, harmless for kernel).
4. UID create 0x1875C(ctx+0x4E8 tbl, name, (attr|user)&0xff, sp+16).
5. `jal 0x1878C(partition, size, (attr>>13)&2, name)` - the pool create;
   arg setup interleaves `srl a1,attr,13; andi a2,a1,2` with view math;
   `uid = rec+12` rides the jal delay slot.
6. `blez pool` -> **0x1877C(uid) rollback + release + 0x80020190**.
7. `0x1874C(pool)` -> vpl+8; vpl+0 = opt; slot+0 = attr (delay slot of the
   user-pool branch).
8. **attr bit 0 = "user pool"**: `0x187C4(pool, sp+20)` returns the sysmem
   pool record; when it returns NULL the record is read to sp+20, and its
   attr16 (rec+22) gets bit **0x1** OR'd in (`sh`). Nonzero return skips the
   tagging (bnez back-edge lands on the `addiu t5,t6,64` ctx line - the
   `lui t6` re-load rides the bnez delay slot; target intentionally skips
   the lui).
9. log dispatch (msgno **108**, a1 = vpl obj view, 1, uid); release; uid.

New internal callee: **0x187C4(pool, &outRec)** - pool-handle -> sysmem
record resolver (NULL = record stored to outRec; semantics inferred from
usage, SysMem owns it).

## Register-role notes (transcription aids)

- Create*: k1 save in s3 (sema/evf) / s2 (vpl); cookie in s6/s5/s7; Sony
  recycles s8 heavily (opt -> slot idx -> obj view in CreateVpl; uid in
  Deletes) and s0 (st -> uid).
- Delete*: return value lives in **v1** the whole function, `move v0,v1`
  only in the epilogue - every error tail does `ori v1,...` in the j delay.
- CreateVpl epilogue restores s0-s8+ra from a 10-slot frame (80 bytes);
  CreateSema 64 bytes; the four smaller functions 48.

## Iteration log

1 compile run, 0 fixes needed. Pre-compile checklist caught the only
potential error before assembling: the `bnez v0` back-edge at 0xB634
targets the `addiu` at 0xB5EC (label must skip the `lui`, which executes
in the branch's own delay slot) - worth remembering when a branch target
sits mid-`lui+addiu` pair.

# Batch Y - thread termination & deletion (the exit path family)

**Verdict: 5 of 5 `*** MATCH ***` (full byte-exact, zero relocation-covered
diffs - even the module-base `lui` words assemble to the literal 0).** All
source in `match/threadman/tm_y.c`. Harness smoke test passed before starting
and re-verified after finishing (tm.c: 4 MATCH + 2 reloc).

| function | addr | words | status | iters |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| sceKernelDeleteThread | 0x132B4 | 125 | *** MATCH *** | 3 |
| sceKernelExitThread | 0x139C8 | 63 | *** MATCH *** | 3 |
| sceKernelExitDeleteThread | 0x13AC4 | 63 | *** MATCH *** | 3 |
| sceKernelTerminateThread | 0x13BC0 | 94 | *** MATCH *** | 3 |
| sceKernelTerminateDeleteThread | 0x13D38 | 82 | *** MATCH *** | 3 |

Iteration log (4 compiles total): run 1 = assembler abort (branch to a label
I renamed mid-decode - retail sends BOTH the cause error at 0x132DC and the
gate error at 0x132F0 to 0x1349C); run 2 = "no mapped export functions" (my
scripted `.size` insertions nested into the first function's tail - .size is
REQUIRED, one per function, or parse_obj sees no symbol); run 3 = DeleteThread
DIFF: I had dropped the `ori v0,v0,0x64` delay slot of `j 0x133FC` @0x13444
and initially misread which of the 0x1345C/0x13460 pair was the `j` vs its
delay `ori v0,v0,0x1a4`; run 4 = 5/5 MATCH. **GOTCHA worth repeating: when an
error tail chains `lui / j epi / ori`, the ori is the j's DELAY SLOT - verify
against raw neighbour lines, and jump targets like `j 0x1345C` land on the
j, not the ori.**

## Sizing note

DeleteThread is 125 words (0x132B4-0x134A8); a separate 28-word internal leaf
starts at 0x134A8 (writes 0xdeadbeef/0x7f800001 patterns - thread-template
init, next to StartThread). TerminateDelete ends exactly at the 0x13E80 exit
trampoline.

---

## 1. The exit pair - exports are thin; the core never returns

### sceKernelExitThread @0x139C8 - msgno 19
```
s32 sceKernelExitThread(s32 exitStatus)          // s8 = a0
{
    if (cfc0_cause) return 0x80020064;           // 13AF4 -> out (returns a1)
    cur = ctx->current;
    if (cur->gate /* TCB+0x88 */ != 0) return 0x80020064;
    .word 0x70050024;                            // SPECIAL2 $0,a1->$0 filler
    if (0 /* a1==0x80020064 const; beqz DEAD */) ;
    if (ctx->[0x0C] /* dispatch state */ != 0)
        if (sub_187ac() > 0x307FFFFF) return 0x800201A7;   // a1 set, -> out
    if (exitStatus < 0) exitStatus = 0x800200D2;           // slti+movn
    cookie = acquire();                           // 13A58
    cur->[84] = exitStatus;                       // 13A74 DELAY SLOT of the
                                                  // log branch - BEFORE log
                                                  // and BEFORE the core
    if (logHandler) logHandler(19, exitStatus, 1, exitStatus);
    sub_178C0(0, cookie, exitStatus);             // NEVER RETURNS
}
```
- The only reachable `jr ra` is the error gate (returns a1 = 0x80020064 or
  0x800201A7). The fall-through after `jal 0x178C0` is the log block - i.e.
  the core is `noreturn` in layout, exactly like the 0x13E80 trampoline.
- `0x70050024` = opcode 0x1C (SPECIAL2), rs=$0, rt=a1, rd=$0, funct 0x24 -
  an Allegrex/Sony word writing its result to $0. Since rd=$0 it cannot
  change a1, so the following `beqz a1` is provably dead. Spliced raw.

### sceKernelExitDeleteThread @0x13AC4 - msgno 20
Byte-twin with three deltas: `beqz` instead of `beqzl` for the dispatch-state
check; after the (optional) log it RELOADS `a2 = *(current+84)` and calls
`sub_178C0(1, cookie, status)` with the DELETE flag = 1; log msgno 20.

## 2. The terminate pair - veneers over sub_17A14

```
s32 sceKernelTerminateThread(SceUID tid)          // msgno 21
s32 sceKernelTerminateDeleteThread(SceUID tid)    // msgno 22 - identical shape
{
    if (tid == 0) return 0x80020197;
    if (cfc0_cause) {                             // interrupt context: VALIDATE,
        if (sub_187ac() > 0x307FFFFF) return 0x80020064;   // then CONTINUE
    } else if (ctx->[0x0C] != 0) {                // dispatch busy:
        if (sub_187ac() > 0x307FFFFF) return 0x800201A7;   // CAN_NOT_WAIT
    }
    cookie = acquire();
    if (resolve(tid, threadsTable, &rec)) { release; return 0x80020198; }
    if (user && !(lhu rec+22 & 0x2)) { release; return 0x800200D1; }
    if (logHandler) logHandler(21|22, cookie, 1, rec->[12]/*state*/);
    r = sub_17A14(rec, 0|1 /*delete*/, cookie);
    release; return r;
}
```
- TerminateThread is legal from an INTERRUPT handler (entry only validates
  0x187ac and proceeds); TerminateDeleteThread is not - sub_17A14 itself
  rejects `deleteFlag && cause!=0` with 0x80020064.
- Log a1 = the acquire COOKIE here (unusual; most families pass rec/&ctx),
  extra a3 = target state.
- sub_17A14(rec, deleteFlag, cookie) decode (partial, decode-only):
  - target==current in thread ctx -> 0x80020197; state==0 -> 0x80020198;
  - target already dead (0x10/0x20): plain Terminate -> 0x800201A2;
    TerminateDelete -> full DeleteThread-style cleanup: TEH class 8
    (sub_1580(8, rec, cookie, tid)), unlink, sub_155B8(rec, cookie),
    uid free 0x1877C(rec+8), return 0;
  - live target -> kill path at 0x17B68 (not decoded; over budget).

## 3. sceKernelDeleteThread @0x132B4 - msgno 17

```
s32 sceKernelDeleteThread(SceUID tid)
{
    if (cfc0_cause) return 0x80020064;
    if (ctx->current->gate != 0) return 0x80020064;
    if (tid == 0) return 0x000001A4;   // RETAIL QUIRK (see below)
    cookie = acquire();
    if (resolve(tid, threadsTable, &rec)) { release; return 0x80020198; }
    if (user && !(lhu rec+22 & 0x10)) { release; return 0x800200D1; }
    if (recView == *(ctx+0x50C) || recView == *(ctx+0x510))   // sentinel uids
        { release; return 0x80020198; }
    st = recView->[12];
    if (st != 0x10 && st != 0x20) { release; return 0x800201A4; } // must be dead
    if (recView->[136] /* gate */ != 0) { release; return 0x80020064; }
    if (logHandler) logHandler(17, rec, 1, tid);
    sub_1580(8, rec, cookie, tid);       // TEH dispatch, class 8 = delete
    sub_60D4(rec); sub_66F0(rec);        // teardown A/B (also in 178C0 chain)
    unlink rec from its list (rec+0/+4); recView->[12] = 0;   // state = 0
    sub_155B8(rec, cookie);              // final cleanup
    sub_1877C(recView->[8]);             // free the SceUID
    release; return 0;
}
```
- **RETAIL QUIRK (transcribed literally):** `beqz tid` @0x132F8 jumps to
  `j 0x1345C` @0x13494, which lands on the shared `j 0x133FC` @0x1345C whose
  DELAY slot is `ori v0,v0,0x1a4` @0x13460. On this path v0 = 0 (the gate
  load from 0x132EC, fall-through of `bnez`), so the function returns
  **0x000001A4**, not 0x800201A4. (The 0x800201A4 block's own `lui` at
  0x13458 is skipped.)
- New ctx fields: **+0x50C and +0x510 hold two sentinel thread-rec pointers**
  (the uid-table's idel/ sentinel entries) that Delete refuses.
- NO waiter handling here: a deletable thread is already dead, so its
  type-9 waiters were completed when it exited (see below).

## 4. Exit-path semantics (the deliverable)

### Who runs the TCB teardown
The Exit* exports only gate + store + log; the never-return core
**sub_178C0(deleteFlag, cookie, exitStatus)** (0x178C0, ~60w visible) does:

1. re-acquire, recompute the rec view, assert `ctx+0x0C == 0` (else an
   0x18724 panic string loop - "assertion failed" style);
2. if deleteFlag: `current->gate (TCB+0x88) |= 8`;
3. **sub_1580(4, current, cookie)** - thread-event-handler dispatch,
   class 4 = thread-exit (classes now known: 2 trampoline/start-side @13E80,
   4 exit, 8 delete);
4. teardown chain: **0x11E3C(current)**, **0xB48(rec, exitStatus)**
   (completes type-9 waiters - below), **0x60D4**, **0x66F0**, **0x26CC**,
   **0xE0D4** (all take the TCB; 0x60D4/0x66F0 also appear in DeleteThread,
   so they are state-independent teardown);
5. if (deleteFlag || gate&8): sub_1580(8, current, cookie) (delete event),
   state = 0, **0x126AC(current->id)** (uid free, same import CancelAlarm's
   interrupt path uses), ctx+4 = 0, FE4(1,1) - switch away;
   else: insert current into the **dead-thread list at ctx+0x4A8**
   (links at TCB+0/+4), **state = 16 DORMANT**, ctx+4 = 0, FE4(1,1);
6. then 0x18724(...) + `break` - the export's caller NEVER resumes. (This is
   why WaitThreadEnd can return rec+84 for state 0x10 threads later.)

The **0x13E80 trampoline** that StartThread installs at ctxblk+56 is the
entry-side twin: when a thread's entry function RETURNS, control lands there;
it runs sub_1580(2, current, cookie) and sub_134A8(ctxblk) and REBUILDS the
thread's context block (rec+292/296, rec+216 exit status -> ctxblk+176,
fresh exit-trampoline/ctxblk links, 0x7f800001 NaN-fill of the FPU save area,
`ins` @0x13F30 raw word 0x7C037384 merging attr bits into rec+20) - the
thread is recycled onto the dormant list rather than freed.

### When the exit status lands at rec+84
In BOTH Exit* exports, `sw s8,84(current)` (TCB+0x54) executes in the delay
slot of the log-dispatch branch - i.e. BEFORE the log handler runs and before
sub_178C0 is called. ExitDelete even reloads its third argument from
`*(current+84)` after the log call. rec+84 is the single source consulted by
WaitThreadEnd's dead-target fast path (batch T) and ReferThreadStatus
(exitStatus field, batches I/K).

### How the waiters of wait-type 9 (thread-end) are completed
**sub_B48(threadRec, exitStatus)** @0x0B48:
```
slotView = rec + 4*hdr21(*(module+0x508 /* = ctx+0x4C8 slot table */));
head = slotView->[12];                          // waiter TCB list (TCB+0/+4)
for (w = head; w != &slotView->[12]; w = w->[0]) {
    w->[0x80] = exitStatus;                     // TCB+0x80 = waitResult
    sub_924(w);                                 // release-from-wait (L/R/T)
}
return count;
```
So the completion primitive for thread-end waiters is a plain per-waiter
`waitResult store + sub_924` walk over the target's own waiter list - NOT
the 0x1879C cancel-all tags (those are for UID objects being deleted).
Consistency check vs batch T: WaitThreadEnd queues on exactly that list via
sub_908(target, 9, ...) and its CB re-resolve fails with 0x800201B5 if the
tid dies (state 0x20 / uid freed) while callbacks ran - which is what
DeleteThread/TerminateDelete produce.

Interaction matrix with deletion:
- **ExitThread**: waiters completed with the user status; TCB kept, state 16,
  on the ctx+0x4A8 dead list (deletable later by DeleteThread).
- **ExitDeleteThread**: waiters still completed first (same chain), then the
  delete leg frees the uid (0x126AC) and state=0 - waiting threads racing
  with deletion get either the status (completed) or 0x800201B5 (CB path).
- **TerminateThread**: sub_17A14's live-kill path (0x17B68, undecoded) -
  dead target returns 0x800201A2 without touching waiters.
- **TerminateDeleteThread / DeleteThread on a dead thread**: NO waiter walk
  (already empty - completed at exit time); TEH class 8, teardown, unlink,
  sub_155B8, uid free.

## New facts banked

- **msgnos**: 17 DeleteThread (a1 = rec), 19 ExitThread (a1 = status),
  20 ExitDeleteThread (a1 = status), 21 TerminateThread (a1 = **cookie**),
  22 TerminateDeleteThread (a1 = cookie); a3 extra = tid/state/status.
- **0x187AC import = runtime validation**, result must be <= 0x307FFFFF
  (user-space top). Called when ctx+0x0C != 0 (dispatch pending) in all four
  Exit/Terminate twins, and additionally at entry when cfc0 Cause != 0 in
  Terminate* (where PASSING lets the call proceed from interrupt context).
- **New internals**: 0x178C0 exit core (noreturn), 0x17A14 terminate core,
  0x1580 TEH dispatcher (classes 2/4/8), 0xB48 type-9 waiter completion,
  0x11E3C / 0x60D4 / 0x66F0 / 0x26CC / 0xE0D4 teardown chain, 0x155B8 final
  cleanup, 0x126AC uid free (id arg), 0x18724 panic veneer, 0x134A8 context
  rebuild, 0x13E80 exit trampoline.
- **New ctx fields**: +0x4A8 dead-thread list head (links at TCB+0/+4),
  +0x50C / +0x510 sentinel thread-rec pointers (Delete rejects).
- **TCB**: +0x54 exit status (= rec+84), +0x88 gate (bit 8 set on
  exit-delete), +0x80 waitResult receives the exit status for type-9
  waiters.
- **SPECIAL2 filler word 0x70050024** (rd=$0 - result discarded) appears only
  in the Exit twins; spliced raw. Functionally a nop.
- Thread attr permission bits (u16 rec+22) now: 0x1 refer, 0x2 suspend/
  resume/start/release/donate/TERMINATE, 0x4 wait-end, 0x8 cancel-wakeup,
  0x10 delete.

## Status for PROGRESS.md

| 0x132B4 | 125w | sceKernelDeleteThread | ✅ (batch Y, 3 iters, 0 relocs; tid==0 retail quirk returns 0x1A4) |
| 0x139C8 | 63w | sceKernelExitThread | ✅ (batch Y, 3 iters, 0 relocs; noreturn into 0x178C0 core) |
| 0x13AC4 | 63w | sceKernelExitDeleteThread | ✅ (batch Y, 3 iters, 0 relocs) |
| 0x13BC0 | 94w | sceKernelTerminateThread | ✅ (batch Y, 3 iters, 0 relocs; interrupt-context allowed) |
| 0x13D38 | 82w | sceKernelTerminateDeleteThread | ✅ (batch Y, 3 iters, 0 relocs) |

# Batch Z - thread priority/control miscellany

**Verdict: 5 of 5 `*** MATCH ***` (full byte-exact, zero relocation-covered
diffs).** All source in `match/threadman/tm_z.c`. Smoke test re-verified after
finishing (tm.c: 4 MATCH + 2 reloc). 2 compile iterations total (iteration 1
had 5 matches-worth of the classic t5/t6/t7 = $13/$14/$15 register-name slips
in freshly-hand-transcribed blocks + one j-target typo 0x159c0→0x159c4).

| function | addr | words | status | iters |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| sceKernelChangeThreadPriority | 0x14854 | 122 | *** MATCH *** | 2 |
| sceKernelRotateThreadReadyQueue | 0x14A3C | 121 | *** MATCH *** | 2 |
| sceKernelReleaseThreadEventHandler | 0x0143C | 81 | *** MATCH *** | 1 |
| sceKernelGetThreadStackFreeSize | 0x15928 | 89 | *** MATCH *** | 2 |
| sceKernelGetThreadmanIdType | 0x16784 | 122 | *** MATCH *** | 1 |

Decode-only (over the 200w budget): **sceKernelGetThreadmanIdList @0x163E0
(233w)** - decode at the bottom.

## THE DELIVERABLE - run-queue bitmap + heads mechanics

Decoded from 0x16bf4 (dequeue), 0x16c48 (make-ready), 0x14598 (change-priority
core) - all verified against `--disas`/`.dis` raw words.

### Layout (refines results/K.md)

- **heads/anchors**: `slot(p) = ctx + 152 + 8*p` is an 8-byte RING ANCHOR
  `{+0 next/first, +4 prev/last}` that is itself a member of a circular
  doubly-linked list of thread TCBs (links at TCB+0/+4). Empty ⟺
  `slot->next == slot` (self-loop) - this is the `beq v0,a2` empty check in
  RotateThreadReadyQueue. Batch K's "runq heads ctx+152+prio*8" confirmed;
  the anchor is *in* the ring, not just a head pointer.
- **bitmap**: word `ctx + 136 + 4*(p>>5)`, bit `rotrv(0x80000000, p)` =
  bit `31-(p&31)` set ⟺ queue p non-empty. K.md's "ctx+128+(prio>>5)*4" is
  the *computed pointer base*; every actual access is `+8` from it
  (`lw/sw X,8(t0)` in 0x16bf4/0x16c48/StartThread) = **ctx+136+4k** -
  confirmed independently by the clz-scan reading +136/+140/+144/+148.
  4 words cover priorities 0..127; sits just below the heads array, no
  overlap.

### The Allegrex clz instruction (new, explains 8 raw words module-wide)

The undecodable `0x...16` words (disassembler prints raw hex, e.g.
`01c06816` @0x14A94/0x147D4) are **SPECIAL funct 0x16 = `clz rd,rs`** - a
non-standard Allegrex encoding (MIPS32r2 clz is funct 0x20). Both clz cascades
(`RotateThreadReadyQueue` @0x14A88-0x14ABC, and inside 0x14598
@0x147C8-0x14800) compute **the priority of the most-urgent non-empty run
queue**:

```
r = clz(bm0)                       # bm0 = ctx+136 (prio 0-31)
if bm0 == 0: r = clz(bm1) + 32     # +140
if bm1 == 0 too: r = clz(bm2) + 64 # +144
if bm2 == 0 too: r = clz(bm3) + 96 # +148
all zero -> r = clz(0)+96 = 128    # clz(0)=32; cascade adds exactly 32 per
                                   # skipped zero word (addiu after each movz)
```

(movz picks the lower word's clz when non-zero; the `+32` chain exploits
clz(0)=32.)

### Insert (make-ready / preempt-requeue) - 0x16c48 and 0x14598 @0x146E4

Insert new thread N at the FRONT of slot(p)'s ring:
```
first    = slot(p)->next            # == heads[p], a TCB
N->prev  = first->prev              # == old tail (or slot when empty)
N->next  = first
first->prev = N
N->prev->next = N                   # slot->next = N  => N becomes heads[p]
bitmap[ctx+136+4*(p>>5)] |= rotrv(0x80000000, p)   # set bit
```
In 0x16c48 (make-ready of T when T->prio < current->prio, ctx+0xC==0,
resolver st != 0) the thread inserted at current's priority is the **CURRENT**
thread (preempt): current state=2, current->next=first etc., bitmap bit for
current->prio set, target state=1, `ctx+4 = target`, `fe4(0, st)`.
0x14598 @0x146E4-0x14740 does the identical dance (rotrv 0x00d99846,
`or s2,t8,s3`, `sw s2,8(t0)`) when a READY thread's new priority beats
current: current is re-queued at current->prio, target state=1, ctx+4 =
target, `fe4(0, st)`.

### Dequeue - 0x16bf4(rec, prio) and 0x14598 @0x1469C

```
next = rec->next; prev = rec->prev
next->prev = prev
prev->next = next
if (next == prev && next != rec)   # sole thread in an anchor ring:
    bitmap[ctx+136+4*(prio>>5)] &= ~rotrv(0x80000000, prio)  # clear bit
```
(the `next == rec` self-loop case is a detached thread - unlink is a no-op and
the bit stays). Bitmap bit is ONLY cleared when the ring becomes empty;
RotateThreadReadyQueue never touches the bitmap at all.

### How the two exports drive it

- **sceKernelChangeThreadPriority(tid, prio)** never touches bitmap/heads
  itself: it always stores the s16 at **rec+186** (delay slot of the ceiling
  branch), then only if `newprio <= lw rec+188` (the priority ceiling - 127
  set by StartThread) tail-calls **0x14598(rec, prio, st)** which does the
  deque/requeue dance above; if `newprio > rec+188` it releases and returns 0
  with NO queue move (the new priority takes effect on next natural enqueue).
  prio==0 means "current thread's prio" (TCB+0x10). Range gates: user
  (attr<0) 8..119, kernel 1..126, else 0x80020193; state 0x10/0x20 →
  0x800201A2; attr bit 0x2 → D1; msgno 26 (a1=prio, a3=tid, t0=prio).
  Extra 0x14598 decode: current-thread path re-runs the clz-scan and
  preempts only if `newprio >= scan`; waiting targets (state 4/12) whose wait
  slot attr has bit 0x100 (priority-ordered wait queue) get unlinked from the
  wait queue (0x890) + 0xC30 cleanup + dispatch request.
- **sceKernelRotateThreadReadyQueue(prio)**: (interrupt && prio==0) → prio =
  clz-scan result (most-urgent non-empty queue); range gate user <120 /
  kernel <127 → 0x80020193 (prio 0 exempt); (thread && prio==0) → prio =
  current->prio. If `prio == current->prio` there is NO list rotation -
  it yields: ctx+0xC!=0 → return 0; interrupt → state=2 + 0x127d0 +
  ctx+4=0; thread → (st!=0: state=2 + 0x127d0 + ctx+4=0) then `fe4(0,st)`.
  Otherwise the pure ring rotation (bitmap untouched - queue stays
  non-empty):
  ```
  h = slot->next                     # head thread
  h->next->prev = h->prev            # 2nd's prev = tail-ish
  oldtail = slot->prev
  h->prev = oldtail; slot->prev = h  # h becomes tail
  h->prev_old->next = h->next_old    # ring closes over h at the front
  h->next = slot; oldtail->next = h  # h appended behind anchor
  ```
  i.e. classic round-robin: head thread moves to the back; heads[p] becomes
  the old second thread. msgno 27 (a1=a3=prio).

## Per-function decode notes

### sceKernelReleaseThreadEventHandler @0x143C (81w, msgno 46)
`f(uid)`: cfc0 cause!=0 OR ctx->current->gate (TCB+0x88)!=0 → 0x80020064;
acquire cookie==0 OR ctx+0xC!=0 → 0x800201A7; resolve against **TEH table
ctx+0x4F4** → fail 0x800201A0 (note: A0, not 198); user requires attr
(lhu rec+22) bit **0x10** else D1; log(46, **original k1 keyword**, 1, uid);
return `0x1877c(uid)` (uid free) - no list unlink visible here.

### sceKernelGetThreadStackFreeSize @0x15928 (89w, msgno 33)
`f(tid)`: cause!=0 → 0x80020064; tid==0 → current thread's id; attr bit 0x1
→ D1; after release: `mark = *(rec+196)` (entry field), `base =
*(rec+44) + 16` (kernel-stack anchor +16), `n = (mark>>2) - 4`; scans u32
words upward while `== 0xFFFFFFFF`; returns `count*4` (bytes of untouched
stack fill). The loop bound `(rec+196 >> 2)-4` treats the entry-point value
as a word count - dimensionally odd but it is only a cap; the 0xFF fill ends
the scan first.

### sceKernelGetThreadmanIdType @0x16784 (122w, msgno 166)
`f(uid)`: acquire; **new callee 0x187c4(uid, &rec)** = table-free uid
resolve (fail → 0x800200D2); user requires `lbu rec+22 != 0` (attr LOW BYTE,
not bit-masked!) else D2; log(166, uid, 1, uid); then scans 14 tables via
**new callee 0x187fc(rec, table)** (nonzero ⟺ rec belongs to table):
| table ctx+ | type | | table ctx+ | type |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0x4CC threads | 1 | | 0x4F8 callbacks | 8 |
| 0x4D0 semas | 2 | | 0x4F4 TEH | 9 |
| 0x4D4 evflags | 3 | | 0x504 alarms | 10 |
| 0x4E0 mboxes | 4 | | 0x508 vtimers | 11 |
| 0x4E8 vpls | 5 | | **0x4D8 mutexes** | 12 |
| 0x4EC fpls | 6 | | **0x4DC lwmutexes** | 13 |
| 0x4E4 msgpipes | 7 | | **0x4F0 queue-links** | 14 |
else → 0x800200D2. **UID class map additions: 0x4D8 = kernel Mutexes,
0x4F0 = a 15th table** (the ctx+0x4F0 list - same offset batch K called the
suspend/stop list - is a UID table too).

### sceKernelGetThreadmanIdList @0x163E0 (233w - DECODE ONLY)
`f(type, u32 *idBuf, u32 bufSizeWords?, u32 *outCount)`: k1 pointer-gates
both idBuf (+4*arg2) and outCount (+4) → D3; acquire; log(165, 66, ...);
type gate: `(type-1) < 63` via jump table @0x19E34 - 15 entries mirroring
the IdType enumeration (1..14, each case = `a1 = ctx+table; j 0x164e0`
loop), plus special types **64/65** → thread-wait queues (a3 = s5+1176 =
ctx+0x498 sleep-queue and ctx+0x4A0); type 0 / >65 → **0x800201BB**. The
shared loop walks the table's record ring (`first = *(table)`, self-loop
end, advance `rec = rec->next`): user gate `lbu rec+22 != 0` (movz 255 for
kernel, same as IdType); while `count < size`: `idBuf[count] = *(rec+12)`
(rec+12 = the SceUID); count++. Release; if outCount: `*outCount = count`;
return count.

## New callees catalog (verified via --disas)

- **0x14598** change-priority core (122w+, internal): deque/requeue dance +
  clz-scan + preempt; used by ChangeThreadPriority.
- **0x187c4(uid, &rec)** table-free uid resolver (also used by IdList).
- **0x187fc(rec, table)** uid-class membership test (nonzero = found).
- 0x890 (wait-queue unlink in 0x14598's priority-ordered reposition),
  0xc30 cleanup - consistent with wave-3/6 catalog.
- Allegrex `clz` = SPECIAL funct 0x16 - raw `.word` splice; there are
  8 such words module-wide (2 cascades × 4).

## GOTCHAs for next agents

1. **t5/t6/t7 = $13/$14/$15** - the recurring transcription slip strikes
   again (cost iteration 1 on three functions). When the .dis annotation
   says tN, immediately map t0-t7 → $8-$15 before writing anything.
2. The .dis annotation of `25ab0020` (`addiu t3,t5,32`) is correct - decode
   rs/rt from the hex, don't "fix" it to match your expectations; the clz
   words' rd is t5 not t3 (0x01c06816 = clz t5,t6).
3. Log rejoin `j` targets: the delay slot of the j often re-loads the value
   the fall-through path loads one word earlier - check whether the j target
   is the load or the instruction AFTER it (StackFreeSize rejoin is
   `j 0x159c4` with `lw s0,196(s8)` in the delay slot, while fall-through
   executes that same lw at 0x159c0).

| function | addr | words | status | iters |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| sceKernelCreateThread | 0x12CF8 | 367 | *** MATCH *** (raw 367/367) | 1 (first compile) |
| sceKernelExtendThreadStack | 0x1512C | 101 | independently matched here, but batch DD (tm_dd.c, concurrent) owns it - duplicate removed from tm_aa.c; decode below retained | 1 |

Verified with an unmasked word diff (parse_obj + retail_words, no mask_for).
CreateThread reloc words: 10, all R_MIPS_HI16 on `lui %hi(g_padAA)`
module-base words, imm 0 = retail 0. No exotic-opcode problems: 5 INS
words spliced raw.

Raw-word splices used (all verified vs `--disas`):
`0x40436800` cfc0 v1 · `0x7c12fec4` ins s2,zero,27,32 (= s2 &= 0x07FFFFFF)
· `0x7c12bdc4` ins v0,zero,27,24 (dead) · `0x7c02d804` ins v0,zero,8,28
(= v0 &= 0xFF) · `0x7c153804` ins a1,zero,0,8 (= a1 &= ~0xFF) ·
`0x7c113804` ins s1,zero,0,8 (= s1 &= ~0xFF, round-256).

**INS field decode confirmed empirically** (rd = size-1, sa = pos):
matches K's `ins t0,zero,0,1` = 0x7c080804 and `ins t7,zero,0,4`
(16-align), and 0x7c113804 here which rounds (size+255) down to 256.

## sceKernelCreateThread @0x12CF8 - the TCB constructor map

Signature (args 5/6 arrive in t0/t1, kernel convention):
`s32 f(char *name, SceKernelThreadEntry entry, int initPriority, int
stackSize, u32 attr, SceKernelThreadOptParam *option)`

### Gates (in order)
1. `(name|entry|option|option+8) & (k1<<11) < 0` → 0x800200D3
   (three separate bltz tests; note option pair is `or(option+8, option)`).
2. `cfc0 cause != 0` OR `*(module+0x40) current->gate (TCB+0x88) != 0`
   → 0x80020064.
3. `attr & 0x070F9F00` (lui 0x70f | ori 0x9f00) → 0x80020191.
4. USER-MODE CALLER only (k1 mask < 0): attr &= 0x07FFFFFF; then if
   `attr & 0x00800000` clear → `attr |= (current->attr & 0xFF)` (inherit
   current thread's low attr byte), else `attr |= 0x80000000` (force user
   bit). User threads: prio range 8..119 (kernel 1..126) and then
   `attr |= 0xFF` unconditionally on the in-range path.
5. `entry & 3` → 0x80020192; `stackSize < 512` → 0x80020194.

### Thread-record fields initialized (record = rec + 4*hdr[21], threads
table ctx+0x4CC) - extends I.md/K.md; authoritative constructor map:

| offset | value written | source line |
|---|---|---|
| +0 / +4 | ctx+0x4A8 list links (next = head sentinel, prev = old tail) | 0x13048-0x1306C |
| +12 | 16 (DORMANT state) | 0x13064 |
| +16 | initPriority | 0x12EB8 |
| +20 | attr (sanitized) | 0x12EC0 |
| +44 | kernel stack: 0x187E4(block) data head | 0x12FD0 |
| +48 | user-stack anchor block data head (2048B, kernel partition 1) | 0x131A0 |
| +60 | 100B VFPU-ish context: 0xBC400000 at +0, 1 at +4/+8, rest zeroed | 0x131B0-0x131E0 |
| +84 | 0x800201A2 (default exit status) | 0x13060 |
| +184/+186 | initPriority (s16 x2) | 0x12ECC/0x12ED0 |
| +188 | 127 (prio ceiling - confirms K.md) | 0x12EB4 |
| +192 | entry (the REAL entry slot - K.md's "entry = rec+196" label is wrong, see below) | 0x12EC8 |
| +196 | stackSize+255 (rounded size; StartThread computes stackTop = rec+44 + (rec+196 & ~1)) | 0x12FBC |
| +204 | 2048 (user anchor size) | 0x13170 |
| +216 | 0x1873C(entry) result, or gp if NULL result | 0x12EE0/0x12EE8 |
| +288 | attr copy (StartThread moves it to +20) | 0x12EC4 |
| +300 | option pointer | 0x12EBC |
| +304 | kernel-stack block handle | 0x12FAC |
| +308 | user-anchor block data head | 0x1317C |
| +312 | partition id (mpid) | 0x12FB4 |
| +316 | partition id (forced 1 for user attr) | 0x12FB8 |

K.md CORRECTION: StartThread's "entry = rec+196 with bit 0 cleared" is
actually the STACK SIZE field (stackTop = dataHead(rec+44) + (size & ~1));
the entry pointer lives at rec+192. (ReferThreadStatus reads info->entry
from rec+196 per K's listing - recheck that decode; either the info layout
differs or the ReferThreadStatus label needs revisiting.)

### Allocation sequence
- Critical section; option copied to module+0x72C scratch via 0x1880c with
  len = option->size < 9 ? size : 8 (i.e. min(size,8)); scratch+4 = mpid.
- `0x1875C(threadsTbl, name, (attr|usermode)&0xFF, &rec)` UID create;
  rec+12 = new SceUID (t6, saved at 8(sp)); all failures below free it
  via 0x1877C and return 0x80020190 (alloc) / the callee's own error.
- Kernel attr (attr >= 0) requires kernel entry: `entry >= 0` (user-range
  address) → free uid + 0x800200D3 (path lands `j 0x12d80` ON the ori).
- Partition: option->mpid if nonzero else (attr < 0 ? 2 : 1);
  `0x187BC(part, info@module+0x714 {size=16})` query → kernel partition
  needs `(info+12)&3 == 0`, user partition needs `== 3`, else free uid +
  0x800200D1.
- Kernel stack block: `0x18794(partition, name@module+0x734, ((attr>>22)^1)
  &1, stackSize+255)` → rec+304; data head 0x187E4 → rec+44.
- User attr extra: 2048B block in partition 1 (0x18794(1, module+0x734,
  1, 2048)) → rec+308, size → rec+204; data head → rec+48.
- If ctx+0x7E4 ∈ {1,3}: `0x18774(ctx+0x7E0, 100)` → rec+60; zero loop
  over 96 bytes then +0=0xBC400000 (VFPU MMIO), +4=+8=1.
- Thread context block: `0x187EC(ctx+0x7E0, 320, desc@module+0x724
  {+0=8, +4=64})` → rec+28 (the ctx sub-block StartThread uses); attr
  bit 0x4000 (VFPU) → second `0x187EC(..., 576, ...)` → rec+32.
- Insert into the ctx+0x4A8 list (Y.md's dead-thread list - dormant
  threads park there; StartThread later moves them to ctx+0x4B0).
- Release; if `attr & 0x100000`: stack handoff `0x18764(rec+44|-48, -1,
  rec+196|2048)` before the TEH path.
- Re-acquire + TEH dispatch `0x1580(rec, class 1, cookie, uid)` + release;
  log msgno **16** `(16, modulebase_in_s8, 2, entry)` when handler set.
  Returns the uid (8(sp)).

Errors: D3 gates, 64 context, 191 attr mask, 192 entry align, 193 prio,
194 stack size, 190 alloc fail, D1 partition-attr mismatch, plus raw
errors propagated from 0x1875C / 0x187BC (returned via s8).

### New callees (not previously in the guide)
- **0x1881c** - name-copy variant: `(dst, 31, typeId, src)` where typeId
  = 0x197C0 (create), 0x197CC (user-anchor), 0x197D8 (extend) - the same
  family as jump-table ids 0x19B2C/0x19BB0.
- **0x187ec** - context-block allocator: `(pool=ctx+0x7E0, size, desc)`
  with desc = module+0x724 {+0=8, +4=blocksize}: 320 → thread ctx block
  (rec+28), 576 → VFPU ctx (rec+32).
- **0x18764** - stack handoff/protect `(rec+44|rec+48, -1, size)`.
- **0x187b4** - free block (known from P, confirmed here).
- **0x17eb4** - stack-switch runner (see below).

### ctx/module fields added
- module+0x72C (ctx+0x6EC): 8B SceKernelThreadOptParam scratch {size, mpid}.
- module+0x724 (ctx+0x6E4): 0x187EC descriptor {+0=8, +4=64}.
- ctx+0x7E0: pool id used by 0x187EC/0x18774; ctx+0x7E4: VFPU-mode
  selector (1 or 3 arms the 100B context alloc).
- ctx+0x4A8: dormant-thread list at CREATE time (not just dead threads).

## sceKernelExtendThreadStack @0x1512C

`s32 f(SceSize size, SceKernelThreadEntry func, void *arg)`:
- `s1 = (size+255) & ~0xFF` via raw INS; `size < 512` → 0x80020194.
- s8 = ctx->current (raw rec pointer). Enter critical; the COOKIE is
  captured in 0x1881c's delay slot (`move s0,v0` - E.md lazy pattern).
- `0x1881c(ctx+0x6D4 scratch, 31, 0x197D8, *(current-4*hdr21 + 16))`
  - note the SUBU record view (current − 4*idx).
- `0x18794(current->rec+312 partition, scratch, ((attr>>22)^1)&1, size)`
  → new stack block; fail → 0x80020190; release(cookie).
- `0x187E4(block)` → head; **new callee 0x17eb4(block, head, size, func,
  arg)** = the stack-switch runner (runs func on the extended stack);
  arg5 passed in t0.
- Fail → `0x187b4(block)` free + return the runner's error.
- Success → log msgno **41** `(41, block, 3, size, func, arg)` and
  return **0xDEADBEEF** (lui/ori dead/beef into a3 → v0) - Sony marker.
