# Batch BB - FULL Mutex family (+ PollSema / CancelSema)

Agent BB, 2026-11-17. Source: `match/threadman/tm_bb.c`. Technique: tm_r
recipe - `.set noreorder` asm transcription, literal absolute `jal`/`j`
targets, `%hi(g_padBB)` module-base lui, movz under `.set mips4`, cfc0 and
`ins` as raw `.word`. **7/7 full MATCH, zero relocs, two compile runs**
(run 1: 5/7 + 3 hand-copy slips; run 2: 7/7).

Harness smoke test passed before starting and re-verified after finishing.

## Results

| Addr | Words | Function | Status | Iterations |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0x04A34 | 102 | `sceKernelPollSema` | *** MATCH *** | 2 (j-target + none) |
| 0x04BCC | 103 | `sceKernelCancelSema` | *** MATCH *** | 2 (t7 = $15, twice) |
| 0x06284 | 155 | `sceKernelCreateMutex` | *** MATCH *** | 1 |
| 0x064F0 | 69 | `sceKernelDeleteMutex` | *** MATCH *** | 1 |
| 0x06A6C | 2 | `sceKernelLockMutex` | *** MATCH *** | 1 |
| 0x06A74 | 2 | `sceKernelLockMutexCB` | *** MATCH *** | 1 |
| 0x0705C | 127 | `sceKernelCancelMutex` | *** MATCH *** | 1 |

Compile slips (both run 1): (a) CancelSema `sll t7,t8,2`/`addu s8,a2,t7`
hand-copied with t7 = $23 instead of **$15** (the guide's t5/t6/t7 =
$13/$14/$15 gotcha, third recurrence); (b) PollSema log-rejoin `j` written
0x4AF0 - retail word 0x080012BB really targets **0x4AEC**, the delay slot
carrying `move a3,s5` (batch-J shared-delay-slot class: raw word beats the
obvious label).

## THE DELIVERABLE: does the full mutex do priority inheritance?

**No. The full mutex implements PRIORITY CEILING (attr 0x400), never
priority inheritance.** The holder's priority is raised only to the mutex's
static ceiling at ACQUIRE time and re-evaluated at RELEASE time; no code
path anywhere in the family raises a holder to a WAITER's priority. The
LwMutex's "queue order only" finding (Q/U) therefore extends to the full
mutex, with ceiling as the only boost mechanism - and the full mutex is the
only kernel object found so far that manipulates a thread's effective
priority at all.

### The mechanism, instruction by instruction

**TCB fields (extends batch Z's priority model):**
- TCB+0x10 - run-queue priority (as before).
- **TCB+0xBA (186) - BASE priority (s16)** (Z's rec+186).
- **TCB+0xBC (188) - EFFECTIVE priority ceiling** imposed by currently-held
  mutexes; 127 = unconstrained. This is the "ceiling rec+188" Z saw in
  ChangeThreadPriority's `newprio <= ceiling` gate.
- **TCB+0xA8 (168) - head of the owned-mutex list**, a doubly-linked ring
  whose nodes are the mutexobj views themselves (links mutexobj+0/+4,
  sentinel = TCB+0xA8).

**`sub_6604(rec, TCB, count)` - acquire bookkeeping** (6 call sites:
CreateMutex-created-locked, LockMutex-core fast acquire, TryLock acquire,
UnlockMutex waiter handoff, CancelMutex re-acquire, +1 at 0x12E0C):
mutexobj+20 = count, mutexobj+24 = owner TCB; links the mutexobj into the
owner's TCB+0xA8 list - attr 0x400 mutexes are inserted **sorted by
ascending ceiling** (mutexobj+28; walk `slt myCeiling,nodeCeiling`, insert
before first larger), all others tail-appended. Then, for 0x400 mutexes
only: `t7 = ceiling < TCB+188 (effective)`:
- taken → `TCB+188 = ceiling`; if `ceiling < TCB+0x10` (runq prio) →
  `jal 0x14598(TCB, ceiling, 0)` - the ChangeThreadPriority core requeues
  the thread at the boosted priority;
- not taken → no boost.
Without 0x400 the function does the plain list append and nothing else.

**`sub_7480(rec)` - relinquish** (2 call sites: UnlockMutex full release,
CancelMutex):
mutexobj+20 = 0, owner = 0 (only when the old owner was a valid kernel
pointer - checked via `(t0>0) && ((~t0)>0)`); unlinks from the owner's
owned-list; then re-evaluates: if the list is now EMPTY → `TCB+188 = 127`,
runq priority = base (TCB+0xBA) via 0x14598(TCB, base, 0). If mutexes
remain, it inspects the FIRST remaining entry (the most-urgent ceiling,
thanks to the sorted insert): attr 0x400 → `TCB+188 = its ceiling`, requeue
at `min(ceiling, base)`; no 0x400 remaining → effective = 127, requeue at
base. This is textbook Immediate Priority Ceiling, restored on release.

**`sub_66F0(TCB)` - mass release at thread exit/delete/terminate** (4 call
sites: 0x133B8 in DeleteThread, 0x175B0/0x17958/0x17DA0 in the exit/
terminate cores): walks the dead thread's owned-mutex list. Per mutex:
attr **0x800** → just unlink and set owner = **-1** (mutex stays locked
forever, waiters stranded - 0x800 is a "persistent" flavor); otherwise
count = 0, owner = 0 and `jal 0xC30` purges the wait queue (cancelled
count accumulated in s4). Afterwards, if the thread was boosted
(`TCB+0x10 != TCB+0xBA`): effective = 127, runq = base, and if anything
was released → ctx+4 = 0 + `jal 0xFE4(0,0)` resched.

**Where PI would live and does not:** the shared lock core 0x6A7C (246w,
decoded, unassigned) enqueues via `jal 0x908(rec, 12, slotattr, sp, sp)` →
`sub_680` - the GENERIC wait-link helper (timer arm, waitCount++, FIFO-or-
priority insert by `selector & 0x100`, TCB+0x58 = wait type **12**, FE4).
sub_680 never reads the owning object's holder and touches no TCB but the
waiter's. Blocking on a held mutex therefore does not boost anyone; the
only priority manipulation in the whole family is the static ceiling at
acquire/release. (Attr bits: 0x100 priority-ordered queue - same selector
mechanism as semaphores; 0x200 recursive; 0x400 priority ceiling; 0x800
persistent-across-exit; legal mask 12 bits via `ins t1,zero,0,12` =
0x80020191.)

**Lock core 0x6A7C fairness detail** (the closest thing to a PI-like
interaction - but it only orders the queue): when the queue is non-empty,
the core compares MY priority against the first eligible waiter (user
waiters skipped only while ctx+0x30 pending-dispatch is busy): FIFO mutex,
or head waiter at-least-as-urgent → straight to enqueue; only when I beat
the head AND `mutexobj+24 == 0` does it fast-acquire via 0x6604 (queue
jump under priority ordering). UnlockMutex hands off to the first waiter
through 0x6604, so the ceiling boost is applied to the NEW owner on
handoff; the releasing owner's boost is restored first (0x7480 runs before
the handoff walk).

### Mutex object layout (mutexobj = rec + 4*hdr21(ctx+0x4D8), type 12)

| off | field |
|---|---|
| +0/+4 | owned-list links (next/prev into TCB+0xA8 ring) |
| +8 | SceUID |
| +12 | pOptParam (create arg) |
| +16 | initCount |
| +20 | current lock count |
| +24 | owner TCB pointer (0 unlocked, -1 after exit-release of a 0x800 mutex) |
| +28 | **ceiling priority** (127 default; from ctx+0x6D8 word if non-zero else current TCB+0x10) |

Create-with-attr-0x400 validates the ceiling range: kernel 1..126, user
8..119 (0x80020193), and if initCount > 0 (created locked) additionally
requires the creator's base priority (TCB+0xBA) >= ceiling. Note the
ceiling default reads **ctx+0x6D8** - the shared status-scratch +4 - a
strange reuse (normally 0 → falls back to current priority); recorded
as-is.

## Per-function decode

**sceKernelCreateMutex(pName, attr, initCount, pOpt)** 155w. Gates: pName
and (pOpt|pOpt+8) k1 gates → D3 (opt window is +8 here, sema used +4);
cfc0 → 0x80020064; attr 12-bit mask (`ins` raw 0x7C095804) → 0x80020191;
user + attr 0x400 → 0x80020191 (kernel-only ceiling); initCount legal iff
0 <= n < 2 or attr 0x200 (recursive) else 0x800201BD. Template copy:
zeroes module+0x714/+718, then `0x1880C(template, pOpt, min(size,8))`
(size >= 9 clamped to 8 via movz). Ceiling block (attr 0x400) as above →
0x80020193 on out-of-range or locked-with-lower-base. UID create
`0x1875C(ctx+0x4D8, name, (attr|user)&0xFF, &rec)`; fills slot+0 = attr,
mutexobj +8 uid/+12 pOpt/+16 initCount/+20 count=initCount (delay slot -
always stored)/+24 owner 0/+28 ceiling; initCount > 0 → `jal
0x6604(rec, currentTCB, initCount)` (creator becomes owner, ceiling
boost). Log msgno **74** (a1 = a3 = uid, nextra 1). Errors also
0x80020064. Returns uid.

**sceKernelDeleteMutex(muid)** 69w - the Delete twin (same shape as
DeleteSema/DeleteEventFlag). cfc0 → 0x80020064; resolve vs ctx+0x4D8 →
**0x800201C3** (mutex unknown-uid); attr bit 0x10 → D1; log msgno **75**
(a1 = a3 = muid, nextra 1); `0x1877C` frees the UID; return 0 via the
v1 route. **NO waiter walk and NO owner check** - deleting a locked mutex
leaves the owner's TCB+0xA8 owned-list pointing into freed memory and
strands waiters (harsher than LwMutex delete, which only strands
waiters). Interrupt-legal shape: returns the preloaded v1.

**sceKernelLockMutex / LockMutexCB(muid, count, timeout)** 2w each:
`j 0x6A7C; li t3,0/1` (CB flag in t3). Shared core 0x6A7C (246w, DECODED
ONLY - over the 200w budget, unassigned): gates (timeout|timeout+4) → D3,
cfc0 → 0x80020064, cookie == 0 or ctx+0x0C != 0 → 0x800201A7
CAN_NOT_WAIT, `0x16FA4` stack snapshot, resolve → 0x800201C3, attr bit 2
→ D1, count validity as create; **owner == current** → recursive path
(attr 0x200: count += lockCount, overflow 0x800201C6; else 0x800201C8);
CB arm: TCB+0x9C notifyPending → `0x2170` pump + re-resolve (fail →
0x800201B5); timeout non-NULL → `0x10D34(sp, *timeout)` deadline; fast
acquire / block decision as described above; block = TCB+0x60 = count,
TCB+0x98 = CB flag, `0x908(rec, 12, slotattr, sp, sp)` wait type **12**,
result 0x800201AB loops only for CB; log msgnos **77** (Lock) / **78**
(LockCB), a1 = mutexobj, a2 = 2, a3 = muid, t0 = count.

**sceKernelCancelMutex(muid, newCount, pWaiters)** 127w. **Interrupt-legal
iff newCount == 0** (cause != 0 && newCount != 0 → 0x80020064 - unique
gate shape in the family, cf. TerminateThread). pWaiters gate (p|p+4) →
D3; resolve → 0x800201C3; attr bit 8 → D1; newCount < 0 → use
mutexobj+16 (initCount); count validity as create (0x800201BD); log
msgno **80** (a1 = t0 = newCount, a2 = 3, a3 = muid, t1 = pWaiters);
then in order: `jal 0x7480(rec)` - full relinquish INCLUDING the owner's
ceiling restore; `jal 0x1879C(rec, 0x86D94883)` - cancel ALL waiters;
if newCount != 0 → `jal 0x6604(rec, currentTCB, newCount)` - re-acquire
for the caller with a fresh ceiling boost; `*pWaiters` = cancelled count;
return 0. So CancelMutex = "kick everyone, hand the mutex to ME with
newCount" (or destroy the lock state when newCount == 0).

**sceKernelPollSema(semaid, signal)** 102w - **no cfc0 gate** (like
ReferLwMutexStatus). signal <= 0 → 0x800201BD (before acquire); resolve →
0x80020199; attr bit 4 → D1; signal > maxCount (semaobj+8) → 0x800201BD;
log msgno **62** (a1 = semaobj, nextra 2, a3 = semaid, t0 = signal);
**fairness gate**: `jal 0x4D68(slotView)` - new internal helper that
returns 1 iff a queued waiter should be served first: FIFO queue
non-empty → 1; priority queue (slot attr 0x100): 1 iff head waiter's
TCB+0x10 <= my priority (user waiters skipped while ctx+0x30 busy) -
non-zero → **0x800201AD**; currentCount < signal → 0x800201AD (same
error, would-block); else semaobj+4 -= signal, return 0.

**sceKernelCancelSema(semaid, count, pCount)** 103w - **no cfc0 gate**
(interrupt-legal). pCount gate (p|p+4) → D3; resolve → 0x80020199; attr
bit 8 → D1; count > maxCount → 0x800201BD; log msgno **63** (a1 =
semaobj, nextra 3, a3 = semaid, t0 = count, t1 = pCount); `jal
0x1879C(rec, 0x86D94883)` cancels all waiters (same tag as
CancelEventFlag); **currentCount = count, or = semaobj+12 (the create
initial value) when count < 0** (reset-to-init sentinel); `*pCount` =
number of cancelled waiters; return 0.

## New knowledge bank entries

- **Priority ceiling is the ONLY priority-manipulation mechanism in
  threadman's sync objects**: TCB+0xBA base / TCB+0xBC effective-ceiling /
  TCB+0xA8 owned-mutex ring; helpers 0x6604 (acquire+boost, sorted insert),
  0x7480 (relinquish+restore), 0x66F0 (exit mass-release; attr 0x800 mutexes
  become permanently locked, owner = -1). No inheritance anywhere: sub_680
  (wait enqueue, types 1/2/3/4/5/8/9/12/13) never touches a holder.
- Wait type **12 = full mutex** (extends the catalog: 1 sleep, 2 delay, 3
  sema, 4 evf, 5 mbx, 8 msgpipe, 9 thread-end, 13 LwMutex).
- Mutex UID class ctx+0x4D8 (type 12) confirmed in code; mutex errors:
  0x800201C3 unknown, 0x800201C4 try-lock-would-block, 0x800201C5
  not-owner, 0x800201C6 recursive overflow, 0x800201C7 count underflow,
  0x800201C8 not-recursive, 0x800201BD count, 0x80020193 ceiling; sema
  errors: 0x800201AD poll-failed/would-block.
- Log msgno catalog: 62 PollSema, 63 CancelSema, 74 CreateMutex, 75
  DeleteMutex, 77 LockMutex, 78 LockMutexCB, 79 TryLockMutex, 80
  CancelMutex, 76 UnlockMutex.
- Internal callees verified by --disas: 0x4D68 (poll fairness check),
  0x6604, 0x7480, 0x66F0 (4 exit-path call sites), 0x14598.
- Unassigned mutex-family remainder: lock core 0x6A7C (246w - decode in
  this file), UnlockMutex 0x67F4 (158w, decoded), TryLockMutex 0x6E54
  (~130w, decoded), ReferMutexStatus 0x7258 (decoded only by absence).
- GOTCHA recurrences: t7 = $15 (3rd time); log-rejoin `j` into a delay
  slot (0x4B70 → 0x4AEC, raw word over intent).

# Batch CC results - Tlspl family + KTLS family (both TLS systems)

Source: `match/threadman/tm_cc.c` (only file written; `.set noreorder`
transcription per tm_c.c/tm_m.c recipe; absolute `jal`/`j` targets, raw
`.word` for the two `ext` sites, `movz` under `.set mips4`). Smoke test
passed before starting and re-verified after (tm.c: 4 MATCH + 2 reloc,
unchanged). **6/6 MATCH, 0 relocations** - every word including all
`jal`/`j` targets byte-compared by the harness.

| # | Function | Retail | Words | Status | Iters |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `sceKernelAllocateKTLS` | 0x11A24 | 78 | **MATCH** | 2 (one j-target swap) |
| 2 | `sceKernelFreeKTLS` | 0x11B5C | 67 | **MATCH** | 1 |
| 3 | `sceKernelGetThreadKTLS` | 0x11C68 | 116 | **MATCH** | 1 |
| 4 | `sceKernelDeleteTlspl` | 0x0DA10 | 128 | **MATCH** | 3 |
| 5 | `_sceKernelAllocateTlspl` | 0x0DC10 | 174 | **MATCH** | 4 |
| 6 | `sceKernelFreeTlspl` | 0x0DEC8 | 131 | **MATCH** | 2 |
| - | `sceKernelCreateTlspl` | 0x0D574 | 295 | **DECODE ONLY** (over 200w rule) | 0 compiles |

Iteration notes: all diffs were transcription slips, zero structural
surprises: (a) 4 forward/backward label typos (`Xf` vs `Xb`) - the family
floor; (b) one duplicated numeric-label number (two different targets,
one label) - assembler caught it; (c) one `j` landing on the `ori`
error-build at 0xDC80 instead of the epilogue at 0xDC84 (retail quirk:
the k1-gate tail `j 0xDC80` targets the ORI, re-running
`ori v0,0x800200D3` after its own `lui` in the delay slot); (d) one
j-target swap in AllocateKTLS (success goes to the epilogue 0x11ABC,
the log path loops back to the release at 0x11B20).

---

## The two TLS systems side by side

### User TLS (Tlspl) - pools the KERNEL allocates up front

`sceKernelCreateTlspl(name, partition, attr, size, count, opt)`
(295w, decoded only):

1. k1 pointer gates on `name` (4B) and `opt` (8B - the struct is two
   words: {align@+0, size@+4}) → 0x800200D3.
2. `cfc0` Cause → 0x80020064; attr mask `~0xBED0` → 0x80020191;
   `size < 1 || count < 1` → 0x800201B7.
3. Scans the 16-word **Tlspl uid mirror table @module+0x6D4** for a
   zero slot (idx 0..15); full → 0x800201D1. (So only 16 Tls pools may
   exist at once - the same "16" that sizes KTLS descriptors.)
4. opt: NULL → align 0; else copies min(8, opt->size... actually
   `(optsize<9?8:optsize)` bytes of opt into the 8-byte template at
   module+0x714 (0x1880C), align = template word +4. Alignment must be
   a power of two (else 0x800200D2), minimum 4; `blockSize =
   alignUp(size, align)` (raw `ins` @0xD6CC clears bits 2-3 for the
   default align-4).
5. `count*blockSize` computed as a 64-bit product with full overflow
   check (three mult/multu adds) → 0x800201B7 on overflow.
6. Partition check `0x187BC(partition, sp)` - user callers (k1 sign)
   need `*(sp+12) & 3 == 3` (user-accessible partition) else
   0x800200D1. `count >= 3` → the free-index table is allocated from
   the threadman sysmem pool `*(module+0x820)` (0x18774 + 0x18764
   memset), else it is the two inline words at pool+36/+40.
7. **`0x18794(partition, name, 3|4, totalSize, align)` allocates ONE
   SysMem block for the whole pool** (blez fail → 0x80020190 after
   table rollback); `0x187E4(block)` → dataHead. THE POOL OWNS THIS
   MEMORY - blocks are never individually freed, only recycled.
8. `0x1875C(tlsplTbl=*(module+0x530), name, (attr|user)<<0? (u8), &rec)`
   - the class-14 UID allocator; `rec+12` = new uid. **INS @0xD880
   patches bits 3-6 of the uid with the mirror-slot index**, then the
   patched uid is stored to `rec+12` and to `mirror[idx]`. This is why
   every Tlspl op does `ext uid,3,4` → `(uid>>3)&0xF` = slot idx.
9. Record init (pool view = rec+4*hdr21(*(module+0x530)), slot view =
   rec+4*hdr21(*(module+0x508))): pool {+0 opt, +4 dataHead, +8 0,
   +12 freeIndexTable, +20 size(arg3), +24 count, +28 blockSize,
   +32 sysmemBlock, +36/+40 inline-table area}; slot {+0 attr,
   +8 waiterCount}; `for i<count: 0xD338(pool, i)` builds the free ring
   (+16 = freeCount = count after the loop).
10. Log msgno **124** (a1 = idx, a2 = 1, a3 = patched uid, t0 =
    *(base+0x48)); mirror[idx] = patched uid (in the bnez delay slot -
    stored on both log and no-log paths); returns the patched uid.

`_sceKernelAllocateTlspl(uid, outBlock, timeout)` (0xDC10, matched):
k1 gates on out + timeout (D3); intr → 64; **module+0x4C busy gate**
(cookie test → 0x800201A7); `ext uid,3,4` mirror check + uid resolve
(0x800201D0); attr bit 0x2 with kernel k1-sign bypass (movz) →
0x800200D1; per-thread table `*(P+0xDC)` + 0x40 + 4*idx: **existing
block is returned directly through `*outBlock` (k1-gated, D3), no
re-allocation, return 0**. New: waiterCount (slot+8) == 0 → the CURRENT
thread enqueues itself: `TCB+0x98 = 0`, **wait type 14** via
`0x908(rec, 14, slot->attr, deadline@sp, timeoutPtr)` with the timeout
deadline built by `0x10D34(sp, *timeout)`; the grant lands in
`TCB+0x60` and is stored to the per-thread slot + `*outBlock` +
memset. If waiters already exist, `0xD3DC(pool)` pops a free index and
the block is computed as `pool+4(dataHead) + blockSize * idx`. Logs
msgno **126** (a1 = timeout, a2 = 1, a3 = uid). 0x16FA4 kstack snapshot
(result ignored) after the mirror check.

`sceKernelFreeTlspl(uid)` (0xDEC8, matched): same mirror/resolve/attr
gates; block = per-thread `*(P+0xDC)+0x40+4*idx` (k1-gated);
`0xE424(pool, block)` validates + returns the index (fail → propagate
error). **Waiters present (slot+8 > 0): the block is handed DIRECTLY
to the head waiter** - `waiterTCB+0x60 = block`, then
`0xB08(rec, 0, 0, cookie)` immediate wake (no memset, no ring push);
no waiters: memset + `0xD338(pool, idx)` push-back. Logs msgno **129**
(a1 = block, a2 = 2, a3 = uid, t0 = block). Errors 64/1D0/D1/D3.

`sceKernelDeleteTlspl(uid)` (0xDA10, matched): mirror + resolve +
attr bit 0x10 (kernel bypass) gates; pool+16 freeCount vs pool+24
count: `== count` → delete; `== count-1` → ONE outstanding block (must
be the CURRENT thread's - `0xE424(pool, block)` reclaims it: clear the
current thread's slot, memset, fall into the delete path); anything
else → 0x800201D2 (busy). Delete: `mirror[idx] = 0`, `0x1877C(uid)`
frees the UID, log msgno **125** (a1 = &P, a2 = 1, a3 = uid),
hardcoded return 0. Errors 64/1D0/D1/D2.

**Thread-exit walker 0xE0D4 (internal, decoded while transcribing
FreeTlspl's neighborhood)**: for a dying thread record a0: table =
`*(a0+0xDC)`, for each of the 16 slots: block = `table+0x40+4*i`;
if block non-zero and the slot's mirror uid matches (`(uid^i)` check
via `xor/sltiu` + `sltu` `zero<block`), the pool is resolved
(`0x18784(mirror[i], *(module+0x530))`), `0xE424(pool, block)`,
waiter → `0xB08(rec, 0, 0, cookie)` direct re-grant, else memset +
`0xD338(pool, idx)`. I.e. dying threads return their Tlspl blocks and
sleeping allocators are served immediately.

### Kernel KTLS - slots lazily materialized per thread

`sceKernelAllocateKTLS(size, func, arg)` (0x11A24, matched): finds the
first free **global descriptor** (module+0x554, 24B each, ids 0..15);
all 16 taken → 0x800201C1; intr → 0x80020064. Fills desc {+0 inUse=1,
+4 0 (refcount), +8 size, +12 func, +16 arg, +20 = `0x1873C(func)` or
`gp`}; logs msgno **167** (a1 = desc, a2 = 4, a3 = size, t0 = func,
t1 = arg); returns the id (in t2 through the epilogue).

`sceKernelFreeKTLS(id)` (0x11B5C, matched): id >= 16 or desc+0
(unUse) == 0 → 0x800201C0; desc+4 (refcount) != 0 → 0x800201C2;
else desc+0 = 0, return 0. Logs msgno **168** (a1 = a3 = id, a2 = 1).
NOTE: FreeKTLS frees the DESCRIPTOR only - per-thread values live
until their thread dies (or are manually reclaimed).

`sceKernelGetThreadKTLS(id, tid, flag)` (0x11C68, matched): id 0..15
else NULL; intr → NULL; tid==NULL → base = `*(module+0x40)` (the
current thread's record - see below), else tid resolved through the
threads UID table (`*(module+0x50C)`, 0x18784) and base =
`rec + 4*hdr21`. **Value = `*(base+0xE0+4*id)`**; desc->inUse == 0 →
NULL. Value already set → returned (peek; flag ignored on this path
except as log arg). Value NULL and flag != 0 → **lazy allocation**:
`0x18774(pool=*(module+0x820), size=desc+8)` block, memset via
`0x18804(block, 0, size)`, `desc+4 (refcount)++` (reloaded via
`lw s1,4(s2)` + addiu - a *ref* per referencing thread), store to the
slot. Logs msgno **170** (a1 = value-or-block, a2 = 4, a3 = id, t0 =
tid-or-*(P+8), t1 = flag). NULL on alloc failure. GetKTLS@0x11F48
(batch A) is its `id,0,1` veneer.

**KTLS thread-exit walker 0x11E3C (internal, decoded - sits between
GetThreadKTLS and GetKTLS)**: for a dying thread record (arg a0):
scans ids 15..0 via the SAME two structures (`record+0xE0+4*i` values,
module+0x554+24*i descriptors, i counted DOWN with `s4=15` counter);
for each set value: **if the dying record == `*(module+0x40)`** the
destructor runs INLINE (`gp` swapped: s0 = gp; gp = desc+20; call
`desc+12 (dtor)(value, desc+16 arg)`; gp restored) else `s8 = 1`
(deferred - the caller of the walker handles it); then
`0x187A4(pool, value)` validates/frees the sysmem block, `*(valueSlot)
= 0`, `desc+4 (refcount) -= 1`. This walker is the KTLS refcount
decrement site - `desc+4` counts THREADS currently holding a value,
FreeKTLS refuses to free a descriptor while any thread still does.

---

## The two systems, contrasted (the deliverable)

**Who owns the memory**
- Tlspl: ONE SysMem block (`0x18794`, partition + 3|4 select, size =
  count*blockSize) allocated at CREATE time, owned by the pool object
  (pool+32 = handle). Blocks are recycled, never freed per-thread;
  the block is freed only when the pool is deleted (`0x1877C` frees
  the uid; the SysMem block goes with it - FreeTlspl's waiter-handoff
  shows no `0x187B4` per block).
- KTLS: memory is allocated BY threadman, lazily, PER THREAD, from
  its own sysmem pool `*(module+0x820)` (0x18774), on the FIRST
  GetThreadKTLS(id, ., flag!=0) by that thread; zeroed with 0x18804;
  reclaimed at thread exit (walker validates via 0x187A4 and frees).

**Per-thread association mechanics**
- KTLS: the value POINTER is stored INLINE in the thread's control
  record - `record+0xE0+4*id`, 16 words, fixed offset, no per-thread
  table indirection. Current thread = `*(module+0x40)` directly;
  arbitrary tid → UID resolve (threads table) then
  `rec+4*hdr21(threads)` view. So KTLS ids are GLOBAL slots (16
  descriptors, AllocateKTLS assigns) but VALUES are per-thread.
- Tlspl: association goes through ONE extra indirection - the thread
  record carries a POINTER at +0xDC to a per-thread block table, and
  the block pointer lives at `table+0x40+4*idx` where **idx =
  (uid>>3)&0xF was embedded into the SceUID at create time (INS
  0xD880)** and cross-checked against the 16-entry uid mirror table
  @module+0x6D4 on every operation (a cheap "does this uid really own
  slot N" test that avoids resolving the UID on the hot path).

**Sharing model**: Tlspl is a WAITING allocator (wait type **14**,
0x908, priority-ordered iff slot attr 0x100 - the sema/LwMutex rule;
waiters get blocks handed to them via the standard TCB+0x60 grant +
0xB08/0x924 wake machinery at FreeTlspl/thread-exit). KTLS never
blocks: GetThreadKTLS allocates or returns NULL.

**Structural finds (new this batch)**
- P = `*(module+0x40)` IS A THREAD RECORD (the current thread's):
  the KTLS exit walker compares its a0 (a dying thread record) against
  `*(module+0x40)` for the inline-dtor path, and GetThreadKTLS's
  current-thread path indexes P+0xE0 with the SAME layout the walker
  uses for arbitrary thread records. This supersedes the round-1
  "context struct" reading: the fields at P+0x48 (log handler),
  P+0x50.. are per-thread fields of the current thread, not a global
  ctx (log handler at +0x48 of a THREAD record - a per-thread log
  hook, explaining handler(29,...) reads "from the current thread").
  (P+0x08 is loaded as a log arg here; batch C called TCB+0x08 = id -
  consistent, this would be the current thread's id. P+0x00/+0x04 =
  wait-queue links per H/W vs V's "ctx+0 current TCB" - unresolved,
  outside this batch.)
- New ctx/module offsets: `module+0x4C` = dispatch-busy gate (0x1A7),
  `module+0x530` = Tlspl class-14 UID table ptr (batch Z's "+0x4F0
  type-14" is the same table, base+0x40-relative), `module+0x6D4` =
  16-word Tlspl uid mirror (new data region - also the status-scratch
  base, so the scratch and mirror overlap by design: scratch is
  transient, mirror persistent), `module+0x714` = 8B opt template,
  `module+0x820` = threadman sysmem pool handle (KTLS blocks + Tlspl
  index tables), thread record `+0xDC` = per-thread Tlspl block-table
  pointer, `+0xE0` = 16-word inline KTLS value array.
- New internal callees: `0xE424(pool, block)` → block index/validate;
  `0xD338(pool, idx)` free-ring push/init; `0xD3DC(pool)` free-ring
  pop; `0x18804(block, 0, size)` zero-init; `0x1873C(func)` dtor
  validate (returns replacement or gp); exit walkers `0x11E3C` (KTLS)
  and `0xE0D4` (Tlspl); `0x187A4(pool, ptr)` block validate/free.
- SceUID layout trick: **Tlspl uids carry their mirror-slot index in
  bits 3-6** (`ext a0,s0,3,4` / `ins s6,a0,3,4` pairs) - the uid IS
  the (index-embedded) handle.
- Wait type 14 = Tlspl (fills the class-14 correspondence); log msgnos
  124/125/126/129 (Tlspl), 167/168/170 (KTLS); errors 0x800201D0/D1/D2
  (Tlspl unknown/full/busy), 0x800201C0/C1/C2 (KTLS id/free-slot/busy),
  0x800201B7 (size/count/overflow), 0x80020190 (alloc fail).
- Recipe confirmation: `beqzl/beqz` non-likely delay slots always
  execute - retail places "both paths" side effects (mirror store at
  0xD8F4, refcount reload at 0x11DB8) in them; and the tail-gate
  `j 0xDC80` landing on the ORI of the error-build (not the epilogue)
  is a real retail quirk worth remembering.

3 compiles total: run 1 = 4/6 (two transcription slips), run 2 = 6/6,
run 3 = 9/9 (all three vtimer getters first-compile).

| function | addr | words | status | iters |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| _sceKernelReturnFromChangeStack | 0x14F10 | 71 | *** MATCH *** | 1 |
| sceKernelExtendThreadStack | 0x1512C | 101 | *** MATCH *** | 1 |
| sceKernelExtendKernelStack | 0x152C0 | 190 | *** MATCH *** | 2 |
| sceKernelCheckThreadStack | 0x157C8 | 88 | *** MATCH *** | 1 |
| sceKernelCheckCallback | 0x2078 | 62 | *** MATCH *** | 1 |
| sceKernelChangeCurrentThreadAttr | 0x14308 | 164 | *** MATCH *** | 2 |
| sceKernelGetVTimerBaseWide (bonus) | 0xF450 | 75 | *** MATCH *** | 1 |
| sceKernelGetVTimerTime (bonus) | 0xF57C | 104 | *** MATCH *** | 1 |
| sceKernelGetVTimerTimeWide (bonus) | 0xF71C | 86 | *** MATCH *** | 1 |

Iteration-1 slips (both classic transcription-typo class):
1. ExtendKernelStack @0x154A8: retail `0220D821 move k1,s1` - the k1
   restore at the free-block epilogue reads the SAVED k1 out of **s1**
   (set by `move s1,s2` @0x15458), not s2. Miscopied as `move k1,s2`.
2. ChangeCurrentThreadAttr @0x1447C: the FPU-context fill loop has its
   countdown decrement **as the first word of the loop body**
   (`2463FFFF addiu v1,v1,-1` at the loop head, then `sw`, then
   `bgez v1,head`), 127..0 = 128 iterations. Dropping it shifted every
   later branch offset by -1 (visible as N vs N-1 offset pairs).

Sizing note: the 0x152C0-0x157C8 gap is 322w but the EXPORT is only 190w
(ends 0x155B4); 0x155B8 (64w, thread-exit cleanup, called by DeleteThread)
and 0x156BC (68w) are separate INTERNAL helpers - both decode-only here.

## Raw-word splices used (all verified vs --disas)

`0x0003FFCD` break 0x3,0x3ff · `0x7C113804` ins s3,zero,0,8 (dead sched) ·
`0x7C163804` ins s6,zero,0,8 (dead sched) · `0x7C067384`/`0x7C077384`/
`0x7C197384`/`0x7C11F784` ins ..,zero,14,15 · `0x70130026`/`0x70130024`/
`0x70000026` SPECIAL2 stack-switch barriers · movz wrapped in
`.set push/.set mips4/.set pop` (F recipe, 3 sites in vtimer getters).

## The stack-extension mechanics (the deliverable)

### Where the old stack is parked - sceKernelExtendKernelStack @0x152C0

Kernel-only export (`bltzl k1` user -> 0x800200D1; func pointer must have
bit31 -> 0x800200D3; size >= 512 else 0x80020194). args (size, func, arg):

1. **Name + allocate**: `0x1881C(scratch=ctx+0x6D4, 31, str, rec+16)`
   builds a block name (string constants differ: 0x197C8 user variant /
   0x197D4 kernel variant - adjacent strings); `0x18794(TCB+0x13C partition,
   name, 1 /*kernel*/, size+255, 0)` -> uid, fail -> 0x80020190.
   `0x187E4(uid)` -> data pointer; the **thread id (TCB+0x08) is stored at
   data[0]** (the anchor-word convention from StartThread).
2. **Park**: inside a critical section (cookie from 0x186AC), a 16-byte
   frame is built ON THE OLD STACK at sp+0..12:
   `frame[0] = TCB+0xD0` (previous park head - a linked list!),
   `frame[4/8/12] = old {TCB+0x2C data, TCB+0x130 uid, TCB+0xC4 size}`
   (kernel thread) or old `{TCB+0x30, TCB+0x134, TCB+0xCC}` (user thread,
   bltz attr split @0x153EC/0x15514), then `TCB+0xD0 = sp` pushes the frame
   as the new park-list head. New values stored into the TCB slots.
3. **Switch**: `sp = data + (size+255)` after saving old sp in s0, framed
   by SPECIAL2 words 0x70130026/0x70130024/0x70000026 (also `srl k1,k1,0x10`
   between the call and restore - cache-barrier idiom, spliced raw);
   `jalr func(arg)`; sp restored from s0.
4. **Unpark**: reload frame from TCB+0xD0, restore the three TCB fields,
   `TCB+0xD0 = frame[0]` (pop), release critical section (0x186DC),
   free the block `0x187B4(uid)`, return func's result. attr bit 0x20 adds
   a `0x18764(data, 0, size)` call on BOTH ends (pre-switch @0x15560 with
   a1=-1 when attr&0x10==0, post-switch @0x154E4 with a1=0) - 0x18764 is a
   cache-maintenance range op, not a free (ReturnFromChangeStack uses it
   with a0=TCB+0x2C, a2=TCB+0xC4 as a range).

So the OLD stack is never "moved": the extension is re-entrant bookkeeping
parked on the old stack itself, threaded through TCB+0xD0.

### sceKernelExtendThreadStack @0x1512C - the thin sibling

Same gates minus kernel-only (user-legal: partition slot is TCB+0x138 and
the "user" alloc byte is `(attr>>22)^1`). It does NOT switch stacks itself:
everything after alloc is delegated to **0x17EB4(uid, data, size+255, func,
arg)** which runs func on the extended stack and returns its result; on
negative result the block is freed (0x187B4) and the error returned.
Log msgno 41 (uid, 3 extras: size, func, arg). **Retail quirk: on success
the function returns 0xdeadbeef** - `lui/ori` deadbeef sequence at
0x15294-0x152A4 (the func result in s0 is discarded).

### _sceKernelReturnFromChangeStack @0x14F10 - how return restores

Pops the SINGLE frame at **TCB+0xD4 (212)** - a different anchor from the
D0 park list (0x17EB4 must set it for thread-based extensions; the
D0-list walker at 0x156BC handles both on exit):
- Gates: CP0 Cause != 0 or TCB+0xD4 == 0 -> `break 0x3,0x3ff` (panic loop,
  raw word 0x0003FFCD).
- Enter critical; restore TCB stack fields from the frame:
  `{TCB+0x2C <- frame[4], TCB+0x130 <- frame[8], TCB+0xC4 <- frame[12],
  TCB+0xD4 <- frame[0]}` and copy `{frame[0x10] -> [TCB+0x24]+4,
  frame[0x18] -> [TCB+0x24]+8, frame[0x14] -> [TCB+0x24]+0xC}` into the
  exit-info block (TCB+0x24 - same block whose +0x18 the exit path reads).
- Release (the last TCB store sits in the jal's delay slot);
  attr&0x20 -> `0x18764(TCB+0x2C, 0, TCB+0xC4)` cache range op;
  parked block (TCB+0x130) freed via 0x187B4; **returns [TCB+0x24]+0x18**.

### sceKernelCheckThreadStack @0x157C8 (msgno 32) vs CheckThreadKernelStack

Same 64-byte-threshold overflow reporter tail (0x186AC / 0x186E4(cur+0x1C) /
`0x177C4(cur, attr>>31, [cur+0x1C]+0xB4)` / FE4(1,1)) but with THREE delta
paths: kernel current (sp - TCB+0x2C, 0 if slot empty), user current
(sp - TCB+0x30), and **user-thread caller: delta = [TCB+0x24]+8 - TCB+0x2C**
(checks the user thread's parked kernel-stack anchor, reported via
0x177C4(TCB, 0, anchor)). Always returns the delta. cfc0-first like the
Kernel variant; a1 for the log call is the raw data base.

## What CheckCallback's pump executes

### sceKernelCheckCallback @0x2078 (62w, msgno 162)

Gates: Cause==0 and TCB+0xA4 (dispatch guard, also DeleteCallback's) == 0
else 0x80020064; critical cookie else 0x800201A7; ctx+0x0C dispatch state
== 0 else 0x800201A7. If TCB+0x9C notifyPending: `jal 0x2170` (the pump,
INTERNAL 132w @0x2170-0x2380, not matched - decode below) with a0 = the
critical cookie; return value = **(pump result != 0)** i.e. TRUE iff at
least one callback executed; else return 0. Log(162, k1, 1, result).

### The pump sub_2170 (decoded, not transcribed)

Walks the CURRENT thread's callback queue (anchor TCB+0x90, s5 = TCB+0x90;
empty when first == anchor; clears notifyPending in the branch delay):
per node s8 (object view = node, record = s8 - 4*hdr21(0x4F8 table)):
- skip if notifyCount (obj+24 view = record slot) == 0 or attr (obj+16) != 0;
- consume one notification: notifyCount/notifyArg (obj+24/+28) zeroed,
  **TCB+0xA0 callback-nesting++ and TCB+0xA4 = record** (dispatch guard),
  optional log(7, obj, 5, uid, common, notifyArg) then
  **`jal 0x2944(arg=obj+32, func=obj+36, count, notifyArg, common=obj+40,
  0, gp=obj+44)`** - the invoke thunk that switches gp and calls the USER
  callback function;
- after invoke: guard restored, notifyCount-- and nesting--;
  result != 0 && attr==0 -> **auto-delete via 0x1877C(obj+8)** (PSPSDK
  "return nonzero to delete"); attr!=0 && notifyCount==0 -> unlink from
  queue + self-loop + `0xB48(rec, 0)` waiter completion, deleted-count += 1;
- loop until back at the anchor. Epilogue: if ctx+0x0C != 0 ->
  `0x14124(1, 0)`; if any callbacks were DELETED (sp+4 counter) ->
  ctx+4 = 0 + FE4(0) (dispatch request - 0xB48 may have readied waiters).
  Returns the number of callbacks EXECUTED.

### sceKernelChangeCurrentThreadAttr @0x14308 (164w, msgno 25)

Both args `ins ..,zero,14,15` (bits 14..28 must be clear after mask ->
legal bits 0..13 and 29..31 only, else 0x80020191); Cause / TCB+0x88 gate
-> 0x80020064. attr = (attr & ~arg0) | arg1. **attr bit 0x4000 = FPU
enable**: when newly set, lazily `0x187EC(&ctx+0x7E0, 576, sp{8,64})`
allocates a 576-byte FPU context (fail -> attr reverted via ins, release,
0x80020190); initializes 128 registers to 0x7F800001 (countdown fill
loop) and a control block at +512..572 (0xE4, 63, 0, 63, 0,
0x3F800001/2/4/8, 1.0f x3); sets the bit in [TCB+0x1C]+52 and
[TCB+0x24]+0, ORs it into CP0 Status (bit 14), then `0x12C04(TCB)`.
When newly clear: mirrors cleared, Status &= ~0x4000. Returns 0.

## VTimer getter bonus (msgnos 145/151/152 added to J's catalog)

All three resolve via 0x18784 on the ctx+0x508 table, gate on attr
(record+22) bit 1 (refer; movz kernel bypass), obj view = rec +
4*hdr21. Errors: 0x800201BE unknown uid, 0x800200D1 attr, 0x800200D3
pointer gate (GetVTimerTime: TWO out slots a1 / a1+8, mask (p|p+8) &
k1<<11 - note the shifted mask STAYS in k1 for that function).
- **GetVTimerBaseWide**: returns stored obj+4/+8 (the BASE while
  running); a STOPPED timer (obj+0 == 0) skips the load and returns
  {0,0} - retail quirk (field holds elapsed then, per J.md).
- **GetVTimerTime / TimeWide**: stopped -> stored obj+4/+8 (elapsed);
  running -> `0xECE4() - {obj+4,+8}` 64-bit with the borrow-chain
  (sltu/subu/subu) idiom. Time writes the two out slots and returns 0;
  TimeWide returns v0/v1 (-1/-1 on error).

## Notes for next agents

1. The SPECIAL2 barriers 0x7013xxxx/0x7000xxxx splice as raw .word with
   no side effects on surrounding label math - the stack-switch core of
   ExtendKernelStack is the reference for any sp-modifying transcription.
2. `move k1,s1` vs `s2` at 0x154A8 - when a k1-save lives in a register
   that gets REUSED (s1 = `move s1,s2` @0x15458), check WHICH saved copy
   each restore reads; two different saves of the same k1 exist in one
   function here.
3. Countdown loops may put the decrement at the loop HEAD (before the
   store) - a dropped word there shifts every later branch by 1; the
   N vs N-1 offset-pair signature identifies it instantly.
4. Unmatched neighbors decoded: 0x155B8 (64w exit-cleanup, walks
   TCB+0xD0/0xD4 park lists freeing parked stacks 0x18764/0x187B4),
   0x156BC (68w, pops TCB+0xD0 frames), 0x2170 pump (132w, above),
   0x2944 invoke thunk, 0x12C04 FPU-status apply, 0x187EC FPU-context
   allocator. The 0x18764(ptr, mode, size) cache-range op now has 3
   call-site shapes banked.

# Batch EE - Mutex family COMPLETION (UnlockMutex + lock core + TryLock + Refer)

Agent EE, 2026-11-18. Source: `match/threadman/tm_ee.c`. Technique: tm_bb
recipe - `.set noreorder` asm transcription, literal absolute `jal`/`j`
targets, `%hi(g_padEE)` module-base lui, movz/movn under `.set mips4`, cfc0
as raw `.word`. **4/4 full MATCH, zero relocs** (3 exports via tools/match.py,
the 246w internal core via build/vdiff.py - validated on tm_v.o first).

Harness smoke test passed before starting (tm.c: 4 MATCH + 2 reloc) and
re-verified after finishing, together with tm_bb.c (7/7 still MATCH).

## Results

| Addr | Words | Function | Status | Iterations |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0x67F4 | 158 | `sceKernelUnlockMutex` | *** MATCH *** | 3 (see below) |
| 0x6A7C | 246 | `sub_06A7C` - shared lock core (INTERNAL, behind BB's 2w veneers) | *** MATCH *** (vdiff) | 1 |
| 0x6E54 | 130 | `sceKernelTryLockMutex` | *** MATCH *** | 1 |
| 0x7258 | 121 | `sceKernelReferMutexStatus` | *** MATCH *** | 1 |

Iteration note: UnlockMutex run 1 had 4 wrong words, all mine (one hand-copy
slip `lw s0,0(s1)` for 12(s1); one label one-word early; then two
branch-target arithmetic slips of the *new* class below). The core, TryLock
and Refer were first-compile.

## NEW GOTCHA class: branch-likely targets that land on ANOTHER branch's delay slot

UnlockMutex's waiter walk @0x6960-0x69D4 has THREE interacting likely
branches; computing their targets by eye cost 2 iterations:

- `beqzl a2 @0x69C4` (offset FFE9 = −23) targets **0x696C = the `lw a2,96(s0)`
  of the serve block** - NOT the block head 0x6968. Reason: the beqzl's own
  delay slot `lw a0,0(sp)` @0x69C8 already supplies a0 on the taken path, so
  the target SKIPS the identical `lw a0,0(sp)` @0x6968 (branch-likely delay
  slot = taken-path side effect, then lands past the redundant copy).
- `bnel s0,v1 @0x69D0` (offset FFE3 = −29) targets **0x6960 = the `bnezl`
  itself**; its delay slot `lw s8,48(a0)` @0x69D4 is the loop's reload, so
  the loop head really is the gate, not the reload.
- Arithmetic: target = branch_addr + 4 + (s16)offset*4 - do it with the hex
  in hand, never by eyeballing the .dis annotation (the annotations here were
  right; my first two mental offsets were each off by one instruction).

## Handoff & ceiling mechanics BB could not see (core deliverable)

**sceKernelUnlockMutex(muid, unlockCount)** - BB knew it calls 0x7480 then
0x6604; the exact protocol, now byte-pinned:

1. Gates: cfc0 cause → 0x80020064; resolve vs ctx+0x4D8 → 0x800201C3;
   attr bit 2 (kernel bypass via movz) → D1; count validity identical to
   create/lock (0 <= n < 2 or attr 0x200) → 0x800201BD.
2. **owner != current TCB → 0x800201C5 (NOT_OWNER)** - checked BEFORE the
   count underflow test.
3. Log msgno **76** (a1 = mutexobj, a2 = 2, a3 = muid, t0 = unlockCount).
4. currentCount < unlockCount → **0x800201C7** (count underflow - BB listed
   the error but not its trigger; it is a strict `<`, equal-to-zero is legal).
5. newCount = current − unlockCount:
   - **newCount > 0 → partial unlock: just store it and return 0. NO waiter
     walk** (a recursive mutex keeps its owner and all waiters).
   - newCount <= 0 → `jal 0x7480(rec)` FULL relinquish (count=0, owner=0,
     unlink from TCB+0xA8 ring, ceiling re-evaluated/restored on the
     RELEASED owner), then the waiter handoff walk.
6. **Handoff walk**: exactly **ONE** waiter is served per Unlock call. Walk
   the slot-view waiter list (slot+12, links at waiter TCB+0):
   - while ctx+0x30 (pending-dispatch) is busy, USER waiters (TCB+0x14 attr
     bit 31) are skipped; the first KERNEL waiter is chosen;
   - chosen waiter: `0x6604(rec, waiterTCB, *(waiterTCB+0x60))` - the
     acquire+boost helper with the waiter's WANTED count as the new count,
     **applying the priority-ceiling boost to the NEW owner at handoff**
     (confirms BB's inference: 0x7480 restores the releaser first, then
     0x6604 boosts the receiver) - then `0x924(waiterTCB)` wake.
   - 0x924 result > 0 (wait→ready transition) → dispatch tail
     `0x16bf4(woken, woken->prio) + 0x16c48(woken, cookie)`; result <= 0 →
     waiter stays (suspended) - unlock still returns 0.
   - Waiters behind the first served one are left queued (they now block on
     the new owner).

**Lock core sub_06A7C (246w, shared by LockMutex/LockMutexCB via t3)** -
BB's decode confirmed and refined:

- Timeout pointer gate (t|t+4) → D3; cfc0 → 64; **cookie==0 OR ctx+0x0C!=0
  → 0x800201A7** (CAN_NOT_WAIT, checked in that order); `0x16FA4` stack
  snapshot; resolve → C3; attr bit 2 → D1; count validity → BD.
- **The block/fast-acquire decision** (0x6C44-0x6CAC), exactly:
  - waitCount (slot+8) <= 0 → owner check only;
  - queue non-empty: if ctx+0x30 busy → scan for the first KERNEL waiter
    (user waiters invisible to the fast path - they can be queue-jumped);
    else the HEAD waiter is the candidate;
  - **block (a2=1) if: candidate exists AND (slot attr 0x100 == 0 [FIFO]
    OR my TCB+0x10 prio >= candidate TCB+0x10)** - i.e. queue-jump needs a
    PRIORITY-ordered mutex AND strictly more urgent me;
  - queue pass (a2=0) → fast acquire **iff mutexobj+24 owner == 0**, else
    block. So an unowned-but-waited FIFO mutex still blocks the caller
    (fairness: waiters first) - sharper than BB's wording: the owner==0
    fast path is only reachable after the queue objection test passes.
- Block: TCB+0x60 = count, TCB+0x98 = CB flag, `0x908(rec, 12, slot->attr,
  sp /*deadline*/, timeout)` - wait type 12, **selector = raw slot attr**
  (so 0x100 chooses priority-ordered insert inside 0x680, matching sema).
- Deadline: timeout non-NULL → `0x10D34(sp, *timeout)`; the retry flag
  `sltu t7,0,s6` (= CB armed) is computed in the branch delay slot and
  RE-computed after the jal - stored at sp+28.
- CB machinery: notifyPending (TCB+0x9C) → `0x2170(cookie)` pump +
  **re-resolve** (deleted-while-pumping → 0x800201B5); after 0x908 returns,
  `result == 0x800201AB && retry-flag` → loop back to the CB-arm (0x6C2C);
  plain Lock never loops.
- Log: msgno **77** (plain) / **78** (CB), a1 = mutexobj, a2 = 2, a3 = muid,
  t0 = count - logged BEFORE the block decision (once per call, not per
  retry - the retry loop rejoins at 0x6C2C, past the log).
- Recursive owner==current path: attr 0x200 → count += current (overflow →
  0x800201C6), store, return 0; else 0x800201C8. Note `move a0,s3` sits in
  the overflow branch's parent delay slot serving both paths (both-paths
  side-effect class, CC's 0xD8F8 recurrence).

**sceKernelTryLockMutex(muid, count)** 130w: identical gate ladder (cause,
C3, attr bit 2, count validity), then owner dispatch:
- owner == current → recursive path exactly as the core's (0x1C6/0x1C8);
- owner == 0 → `0x6604(rec, current, count)` acquire **with NO queue/
  fairness check at all** (TryLock ignores queued waiters - unlike the
  blocking core's queue-objection test, and unlike PollSema's 0x4D68
  fairness gate; the full mutex family's Try is the most permissive),
  release, return 0;
- owner != 0 → **0x800201C4** SCE_KERNEL_ERROR_WAIT_TIMEOUT-status-style
  would-block (BB catalogued C4; trigger confirmed: ANY other owner).
- Log msgno **79** (a1 = count!, a2 = 2, a3 = muid, t0 = count) - the ONLY
  mutex logger whose a1 is the count, not the object.

**sceKernelReferMutexStatus(muid, info)** 121w: (info|info+56) k1 gate → D3;
resolve → C3; attr bit 1 (kernel bypass) → D1; **no cfc0 check at all**
(interrupt-legal, like PollSema/CancelSema and ReferLwMutexStatus). Builds a
56B SceKernelMutexInfo in the shared scratch ctx+0x6D4 (= module+0x714):

| off | field |
|---|---|
| +0x00 | size = 56 (ALWAYS stored via bnez delay slot) |
| +0x04 | name[32] - copied via `0x18814(scratch+4, *(rec+16), 31)` iff the UID record's name ptr is non-NULL, else left zero |
| +0x24 | attr (slot view +0) |
| +0x28 | initCount (mutexobj+16) |
| +0x2C | currentCount (mutexobj+20) |
| +0x30 | ownerId: owner TCB+8 tid; **owner == 0 → −1; owner == −1 (0x800 mutex stranded by exit) → −2** |
| +0x34 | waitCount (slot view +8) |

Copy-out `0x1880C(info, module+0x714, min(info->size, 56))` - the min is
`sltu/movn` against the size word just stored in the template. The zero-fill
loop is a single `bne` whose DELAY SLOT does the store (a0 from ctx+0x69C up
to scratch - 14 iterations, delay slot covers the final word). Log msgno
**81** (a1 = scratch, a2 = 2, a3 = muid, t0 = info).

**The ownerId == −2 quirk** is the refer-side signature of BB's 0x800
exit-release: sub_66F0 leaves owner = −1 on "persistent" mutexes, and Refer
maps that to −2 (SCE_KERNEL_MUTEX_NOT_OWNER_HOLDER? undocumented) - a
distinguishable "locked forever by a dead thread" state, different from the
plain unlocked −1.

## Mutex family status: COMPLETE 12/12

Create/ Delete/ Lock/ LockCB/ CancelMutex/ UnlockMutex/ TryLockMutex/
ReferMutexStatus exports + the 2w veneers' shared core sub_06A7C +
internals 0x6604/0x7480/0x66F0 (decoded by BB) - every function in the
0x6284-0x7438 range is now either matched or decode-complete. Only
0x6604/0x7480/0x66F0 bodies remain untranscribed (unassigned).

## New knowledge bank entries

- **One-waiter handoff per Unlock**: 0x6604(rec, waiterTCB, waiter->wanted)
  then 0x924; ceiling boost transfers to the new owner; remaining waiters
  re-block on the new owner. Partial unlock (count still > 0) serves NOBODY.
- **Fast-acquire gate in the core**: block iff candidate waiter exists AND
  (FIFO OR not strictly more urgent); else owner==0 → acquire. ctx+0x30
  busy hides USER waiters from the gate (they can be jumped).
- **TryLockMutex has NO fairness gate** (contrast PollSema 0x4D68).
- Underflow 0x800201C7 fires only when currentCount < unlockCount (strict).
- ReferMutexStatus ownerId: 0 → −1, −1 → −2 (dead-0x800-owner sentinel).
- Log msgno catalog additions confirmed: 76 UnlockMutex (a1 = mutexobj),
  79 TryLockMutex (**a1 = count**), 81 ReferMutexStatus (a1 = scratch).
- GOTCHA recurrence: branch-likely target arithmetic (see new class above);
  both-paths delay-slot `move a0,s3` (CC class, in the core's recursive
  path and TryLock's).

# Batch FF - the last big creators + TryReceiveMsgPipe

Source: `match/threadman/tm_ff.c` (.set noreorder transcription, tm_cc/tm_x
recipe; jal/j absolute retail targets; cfc0/ins/ext raw `.word`; movz/movn
under `.set push/.set mips4`). Smoke test verified before and after.

**3/3 MATCH, 0 relocations** - 2 compiles (first rejected: movz/movn need
the mips4 wrap; second = 9 transcription slips, third clean). 850 words
total, all byte-compared by the harness.

| # | Function | Retail | Words | Status | Iters |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `sceKernelCreateFpl` | 0x0C408 | 265 | **MATCH** | 1 fix pass |
| 2 | `sceKernelCreateTlspl` | 0x0D574 | 295 | **MATCH** | 1 fix pass |
| 3 | `sceKernelTryReceiveMsgPipe` | 0x0A798 | 290 | **MATCH** | 1 fix pass |

Iteration detail: single fix pass across all three (9 word diffs):
- CreateFpl: 2 diffs - `addiu a3,t0,-1`/`and s1,a3,a1` written with t3
  ($11) instead of a3 ($7) (the dis annotator names confused with my s7).
- CreateTlspl: 5 diffs - `move a0,v0`, `or t7,v0,t8`, `andi a2,t7,0xff`
  written into $23; inline-table path `addiu t6,s1,36`/`sw t6,40(sp)`
  written into $22 (t6 = $14, my s6 mis-map).
- TryReceive: 2 diffs - both LABEL-TARGET off-by-one (the CC "family floor"
  gotcha): `blez a1` @0xAA04 targets **0xA964** (the beqz, i.e. finish
  entry AFTER the pRecv reload), not 0xA960; `beql s3,a0` @0xAB50 targets
  **0xA91C** (the bnez) while its delay slot runs the sltu - placing the
  label on the sltu made the offset one word too far.

---

## 1. sceKernelCreateFpl @0x0C408 (265w) - closes the FPL family

`sceKernelCreateFpl(name a0, partition a1, attr a2, blockSize a3,
numBlocks t0, opt t1)` - 5th/6th args in t0/t1 per the Sony ABI note.

1. k1 gates: name (4B) + opt (8B: opt..opt+8) → 0x800200D3.
2. cfc0 → 0x80020064; attr & ~0x41FF → 0x80020191; blockSize<1 or
   numBlocks<1 → 0x800201B7.
3. Cookie; template module+0x714 zeroed (2 words); opt copy min 8B
   (0x1880C); **template+4 = align** (this is the "template size" word
   ReferFplStatus later reads).
4. Stride: default align-4 path via raw `ins s1,zero,0,1` on blockSize+3;
   opt align must be power-of-2 (else 0x800200D2) and >= 4 (smaller
   promoted to 4); stride = alignUp(blockSize, align). **`ext s7,s6,14,1`
   (attr bit 14) is computed but its result register is only ever read by
   nothing on the main path - dead except as live-register padding.**
5. 64-bit stride*numBlocks overflow (mult/multu/mult triple) → 0x800201B7.
6. Partition query 0x187BC (user needs *(sp+12)&3==3 else 0x800200D1);
   numBlocks>=3 → index table allocated from threadman pool
   `*(module+0x820)` (0x18774 + 0x18764 memset(count*4)), else inline at
   obj+36/+40.
7. **0x18794(partition, name, attr&0x4000 ? 4 : 3, total, align)** - one
   SysMem block; fail → table rollback + 0x80020190. obj+4 = 0x187E4
   dataHead.
8. 0x1875C(fplTbl ctx+0x4EC, name, (attr|user)&0xff, &rec) - returns 0 on
   success (error → free table+block, release, return st); uid = rec+12.
   **dataHead rides to 36(sp) in the jal delay slot (pre-call v0).**
9. Object init (obj = rec+4*hdr21(0x4EC), slot = hdr21(0x4C8)):
   slot+0 = attr; obj {+0 opt, +4 dataHead, +8 0, +12 indexTable,
   +16 built by ring, +20 blockSize, +24 numBlocks, +28 stride, +32 block};
   `for i<count: 0xD2E4(pool, i)` builds the free ring (obj+16 = freeCount).
10. Log msgno **116** (a1 = obj view, a2 = 1, a3 = uid); release; return uid.

## 2. sceKernelCreateTlspl @0x0D574 (295w) - CC's decode confirmed 1:1

`sceKernelCreateTlspl(name a0, partition a1, attr a2, size a3, count t0,
opt t1)`.

Identical skeleton to CreateFpl (same gates, same attr mask ~0x41FF, same
opt/align/stride machinery with raw ins/ext words, same 64-bit overflow
check) plus the Tlspl specials decoded by CC, now confirmed at byte level:

- **Mirror scan happens BEFORE the opt/stride block**: 16-word table at
  module+0x6D4 (= template-0x40), first zero slot wins (bnezl loop); all 16
  taken → 0x800201D1.
- Pool UID table = `*(module+0x530)` (= ctx+0x4F0, class 14); the uid
  returned is rec+12 **patched by raw `ins s6,a0,3,4`** (bits 3-6 = mirror
  idx) and stored BACK to rec+12, then to mirror[idx] at `sw s6,0x694(s2)`
  in the **bnez delay slot of the log-handler test** - stored on both the
  log and no-log paths (CC's "both-paths side effect in a non-likely delay
  slot" gotcha, byte-confirmed).
- Log msgno **124** (a1 = mirror idx, a2 = 1, a3 = patched uid).
- Free ring via **0xD338** (not FPL's 0xD2E4): pool {+0 opt, +4 dataHead,
  +8 freeCount, +12 indexTable, +20 size, +24 count, +28 stride, +32 block,
  +36/+40 inline table}.
- New quirk vs FPL: on uid-alloc failure the sysmem block is freed with
  `move a0,s7` (block kept live in a saved reg) instead of a stack reload,
  and the 64-bit check inserts `move v0,zero` mid-sequence (live-reg
  difference only, no semantic delta).

## 3. sceKernelTryReceiveMsgPipe @0x0A798 (290w) - receive core minus block

`s32 TryReceive(uid a0, buf a1, size a2, waitMode a3, pRecvSize t0)`.
Frame 80: waitMode@4(sp), pRecv@8, cookie@12, cause@24, size@20, woken@16.
W's prediction was right in structure, wrong in two details:

- **Would-block error is 0x800201B4 (SCE_KERNEL_ERROR_MPF_EMPTY), not B3** -
  TrySend's B3 is MPF_FULL. The task brief's "B3" was wrong; PSPSDK
  confirms B4 = msgpipe empty.
- Size is **290w, not 288** - W's "+2 skew" guess did not materialize
  (P's skew came from veneer entry counting, not the cores).

Semantics (all mirrors of W's receive core):
- Gates: buf k1-gate, pRecv k1-gate → D3; `(waitMode & ~1) != 0` via raw
  `ins t2,zero,0,1` → 0x80020195 (checked BEFORE enter-critical); resolve
  ctx+0x4E4 → 0x8002019E; attr bit 0x4 (kernel bypass) → D1;
  bufSize != 0 && bufSize < size → 0x800201BC. **Cookie is never spilled:
  it is captured at 12(sp) in the 0x18784 jal delay slot (pre-call v0)** -
  the same shared-delay-slot idiom as the creators' dataHead.
- cfc0 read AFTER resolve, never errors - only gates the final dispatch
  (woken != 0 && cause == 0 → ctx+4 = 0 + 0xFE4(0, cookie)), exactly like
  TrySend.
- dir==2 (receivers queued): priority bypass = slot attr 0x1000 &&
  slot+4==0 && my TCB+0x10 prio < head receiver's, else B4.
- Ring read 0xB094(pipe, dst, min(used, size)); dir==1 (senders queued):
  direct handoff from each blocked sender (0x1880C copy, sender
  rem/src/cursor bookkeeping at TCB+0x64/+0x60, *pSent at TCB+0x6C =
  size(TCB+0x68) - rem), ring-drain 0xAFC0(pipe, senderSrc, min(free,rem))
  iff (sMode==1 && n2>0) || (sMode==0 && free>=sRem), wake 0x924 iff
  (sMode==1 && copied) || rem==0, slot+4 = 1 on partial service - the full
  receive-core discipline.
- mode0 pre-scan (dir==1): adds ALL queued senders' rem to available bytes
  before the fits test (mirror of TrySend's receiver pre-scan).
- Return: B4 iff (mode1 && nothing received) || (mode0 && rem>0); else
  `*pRecvSize = size - rem` (partial receive returns 0) and 0. Log msgno
  **105** (a1 = s8 stale residue, t0 = buf, t1 = size, t2 not passed -
  4-extras flavor).

## New reusable facts

- **INS as AND-mask**: `ins rt,zero,0,1` (0x7C0a0004-class words) = clear
  bit 0 = `rt &= ~1`; TryReceive's waitMode check uses it - decode INS
  with rs=zero as a bit-clear, not an insert.
- **INS rd-field is MSB not size-1**: `0x7C9630C4` = `ins s6,a0,3,4`
  (field bits 3..6); decode msb=rd, lsb=sa, size=msb-lsb+1.
- Delay-slot value capture across a call is the module-wide idiom for
  "save the pre-call v0" (cookie @A874, dataHead @C658) - never assume a
  sw in a jal delay slot stores the callee result.
- The mid-sequence `j` targets (j 0xC5C4 landing on a `j` whose delay is
  the error `ori`, v0 lui preloaded in the gate branch's delay) recur in
  BOTH creators - it is the standard create-family error-tail shape, not a
  one-off quirk.
- All five SPECIAL3 words (4 ins + 3 ext across the two creators + 1 ins in
  TryReceive) spliced as raw `.word`; `movz`/`movn` sites (8 across the
  three) under `.set push/.set mips4` - no new gas behaviors.

## Family status after this batch

- FPL family COMPLETE (Create now matched; Delete/Allocate/Free/TryAlloc/
  Refer/Cancel were M/O/S).
- Tlspl family COMPLETE (Create closes CC's decode-only member).
- MsgPipe: TryReceive closes the last big core; only CancelMsgPipe @0xAC20
  (108w) remains unassigned/untouched in the family.

# Batch GG - vtimer setters + SetSysClockAlarm twin

Source: `match/threadman/tm_gg.c` (one file, six functions, literal-target
`.set noreorder` transcription per tm_j recipe). All six `*** MATCH ***`
(zero relocs - every `j`/`jal` emitted as an absolute retail target).

## Summary

| Addr | Words | Function | Status | Iters |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0x0F874 | 182 | `sceKernelSetVTimerTime` | ✅ MATCH | 2 |
| 0x0FB4C | 154 | `sceKernelSetVTimerTimeWide` | ✅ MATCH | 2 |
| 0x10194 | 169 | `sceKernelSetVTimerHandler` | ✅ MATCH | 2 |
| 0x10438 | 160 | `sceKernelSetVTimerHandlerWide` | ✅ MATCH | 2 |
| 0x106B8 | 92 | `sceKernelCancelVTimerHandler` | ✅ MATCH | 2 |
| 0x10EBC | 60 | `sceKernelSetSysClockAlarm` | ✅ MATCH | 3 |

All first-compile-able in one pass; iter 1 had 7+7+3+2+0+1 word diffs
(register slips + label direction + symbolic `j` relocs), closed in two
more compiles.

## THE ANSWER: Set-on-active-timer re-arms the queue - YES

All three mutating setters re-arm a RUNNING timer's queue entry:

- **SetVTimerTime[Wide] active path**: reads now64 twice via 0xECE4;
  old face = now − vt->base(+4/+8); stores NEW base = now − newTime
  (so base + schedule = now ⇒ the new schedule elapses from *now*);
  if handler(+0x18) != NULL: manually UNLINKS the link block
  (rec+4*hdr21(ctx+0x4FC)) from the active list at ctx+0x64 (with
  beql head fixup when link == head), recomputes
  link->expiry(+8/+12) = sched(+0xC/+0x10) + NEW base, self-loops the
  link, `jal 0xEAE0` re-INSERT (ordered), `jal 0xEB7C` reprocess.
  The OLD face value is written back through the in/out clock pointer
  (or v0/v1 for Wide). Inactive path: plain swap of the face at +4/+8,
  no queue touch.
- **SetVTimerHandler[Wide]**: if active && old handler: unlink old entry
  (no EB7C on the unlink); installs handler(+0x18), sched(+0xC/+0x10
  from clock arg), common(+0x1C), user flag(+0x14), marker(+0x20);
  then if active && new handler: expiry = sched + base → 0xEAE0
  insert; EB7C only if still active. So swapping the handler on a
  running timer re-arms it with the new schedule immediately.
- **CancelVTimerHandler**: if active && handler: unlink + 0xEB7C
  (reprocess - an earlier pending entry may now be due); handler = 0.
  No EAE0 (pure disarm).

## Semantics decoded

- **SetVTimerTime(uid, SceKernelSysClock *time)**: cfc0 Cause gate
  (0x80020064); k1<<11 gate on time/time+8 (D3); acquire 0x186ac /
  release 0x186dc (cookie in s7); resolver 0x18784(ctx+0x508, uid, &rec)
  → 0x800201BE; attr u16 rec+22 bit 0x2 with movz kernel bypass →
  0x800200D1; log (146, vt, 3, uid, time.hi, time.lo). NO sentinel
  check (unlike Start/Stop).
- **SetVTimerTimeWide(uid, lo, hi)**: same machine; NO address gate
  (value args); Wide error paths return **0xFFFFFFFFFFFFFFFF** (li
  v0/v1 −1, not error codes!); attr fail ALSO returns −1 (after
  release); success returns old face in v0/v1; log (153, vt, 2, vt,
  lo); cookie parked at 4(sp), not a callee-saved reg.
- **SetVTimerHandler(uid, clock*, handler, common)**: sentinel ctx+0x60
  FIRST → 0x800201BF; gates: clock/clock+8 (D3); **user threads may
  only set user-space handlers, kernel threads only kseg handlers**
  (mask&handler sign test; kernel: bgezl handler → D3); resolve → 1BE;
  attr bit 0x2 → D1; log (149, vt, 5, vt, clock.hi, clock.lo, handler,
  common). Marker: `jal 0x1873c(handler)` result → vt+0x20, gp if 0.
- **SetVTimerHandlerWide(uid, lo, hi, handler t0, common t1)**: 64-bit
  3rd arg arrives in (a2 lo, a3 hi), 4th/5th in **t0/t1**; sentinel →
  1BF; only the handler is pointer-gated (D3, same user/kernel-space
  rule); log (154, vt, 4, vt, lo, handler, common). Marker vt+0x20 =
  **gp directly - NO 0x1873c call** ( Wide handlers are kernel-side).
- **CancelVTimerHandler(uid)**: sentinel → 1BF; resolve → 1BE; attr
  0x2 → D1; log (150, vt, 1, vt); active&&handler → unlink + 0xEB7C;
  vt+0x18 = 0. Schedule/time/common fields left intact.
- **SetSysClockAlarm(clock*, handler, common)**: NO UID table, NO
  acquire/release, NO Cause check - a thin wrapper:
  handler==NULL → 0x800200D3; clock/clock+8 gate (D3); user/kernel
  handler-space gate (D3); `jal 0x116F4(clock.lo, clock.hi, handler,
  common)` = the alarm core (SetAlarm passes (usec, 0, ...) - same
  callee, confirming G's decode); log (139, handler, 5, clock.hi,
  clock.lo, handler, common, uid); returns the new alarm SceUID.
  60 words only. Note the log-handler load here is module+0x48 via a
  bare lui (per the STRUCTURAL CORRECTION: per-thread record slot).

## Recipe notes (extends the guide)

- **`j <asm-local-label>` emits R_MIPS_26** (wave-6 note confirmed the
  hard way): a wrong-target symbolic `j` still shows as "match (reloc)"
  because mask_for only compares the opcode - CancelVTimerHandler
  false-passed with 5 reloc words on iter 1. Always literal `j 0xADDR`.
- Numeric labels LEAK ACROSS `__asm__` blocks (they land in one .s):
  a `Nb` with no preceding N in the same function silently binds to a
  PREVIOUS function's N label (manifests as negative branch offsets).
  Cost 1 iteration here; re-grep `Nb` uses against label positions.
- t9 is **$25**, t1 is $9 - hand-transcribing the or/sltu/subu noise
  lanes, the disassembler's register names must be mapped through the
  numeric ABI, not decimal-stripped. Slips clustered exactly there.
- Wide twins: 64-bit 3rd syscall arg = (a2,a3), 4th/5th args in t0/t1;
  Wide error returns are −1 (v0/v1), not SCE error codes.

## VTimer object map (extends J)

| offset | field | new evidence |
|---|---|---|
| +0x14 | u32 user flag (k1>>31 of the setter) | SetVTimerHandler stores t2=srl k1,0x1f here (0x102D8/0x10570) |
| +0x20 | handler marker: sub_1873c(handler) result, or gp; Wide stores gp directly | 0x102DC-0x102F0 / 0x10574-0x10580 |

+4/+8 semantics refined: it is the timer's FACE - schedule-relative
base while running (expiry = base + sched), elapsed face while stopped;
SetVTimerTime reads/writes it through and returns the previous reading.

# Batch HH - wait-family stragglers (evf/sema wait veneers + dispatch suspension + exit/TEH)

Agent HH, 2026-11-18. Source: `match/threadman/tm_hh.c`. Technique: tm_r
recipe - `.set noreorder` asm transcription, literal absolute `jal`/`j`
targets, `%hi(g_padHH)` module-base lui (assembles to literal 0), movz under
`.set mips4`, `cfc0`/`ins` as raw `.word`. **8/8 full MATCH, zero relocs.**

Harness smoke test passed before starting. Two compile iterations total:
run 1 = one assembler error (a forward `beqzl` targeted a label I had placed
on the *following* instruction - target was mid-sequence, fixed by adding the
label on the bnel itself) plus 5/8 MATCH; run 2 = 8/8 MATCH. Only real slip:
three `bnez v1` hand-copied as `bnez v0` (the cfc0 result lives in v1, not
v0, in this family - the cause register is v1 throughout).

## Results

| Addr | Words | Function | Status | Iterations |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0x05730 | 2 | `sceKernelWaitEventFlag` | *** MATCH *** | 1 (veneer) |
| 0x05738 | 2 | `sceKernelWaitEventFlagCB` | *** MATCH *** | 1 (veneer) |
| 0x04754 | 2 | `sceKernelWaitSemaCB` | *** MATCH *** | 1 (veneer) |
| 0x14034 | 60 | `sceKernelSuspendDispatchThread` | *** MATCH *** | 2 |
| 0x14240 | 50 | `sceKernelResumeDispatchThread` | *** MATCH *** | 2 |
| 0x0394 | 79 | `sceKernelSuspendAllUserThreads` | *** MATCH *** | 2 |
| 0x16D34 | 30 | `_sceKernelExitThread` | *** MATCH *** | 1 |
| 0x01140 | 191 | `sceKernelRegisterThreadEventHandler` | *** MATCH *** | 1 |

(0x0394 row: address is 0x03984, 79 words ending at 0x3ABC inclusive.)

## The veneers (R's prediction confirmed)

```
sceKernelWaitEventFlag    5730: j 0x5740; li t3,0
sceKernelWaitEventFlagCB  5738: j 0x5740; li t3,1   (shared evf wait core)
sceKernelWaitSemaCB       4754: j 0x475C; li t3,1   (R's WaitSema twin)
```

Same 2-word shape as every other wait family: CB flag rides in **t3** (the
register outside the o32 ABI) through the `j` delay slot. The evf wait core
0x5740 is the one R decoded (always-FIFO insert, selector 0).

## Dispatch suspension - how ctx+0x0C really works (the deliverable)

### sceKernelSuspendDispatchThread @0x14034 (60w)

```
cause = cfc0;                if (cause) return 0x80020064;
cookie = acquire(0x186AC);   if (!cookie) { release(0); return 0x80020066; }
if (ctx->logHandler) logHandler(23, &ctx, 0);          // msgno 23
old = ctx->[0x0C];                                     // dispatch state
if (old == 0) { ctx->[0x0C] = 1; ret = 1; } else ret = 0;
P = *(module+0x40);                                    // current TCB
if (P->attr < 0)                                       // USER thread
    *(P+0xDC)+0xC4 = 1;                                // per-thread mirror!
release(cookie); return ret;                           // 1 = we suspended
```

Key findings:
- **Returns 1 if THIS call performed the suspension, 0 if already suspended**
  (SceKernelDispatchThreadState save/restore semantics - the return value is
  the previous state for ResumeDispatchThread(1) to feed back).
- **The famous "StartThread pair wording"**: it does NOT return the old
  state; it returns whether it *transitioned*. (No StartThread-style
  wording in the disasm - plain `move v0,s3` with s3 = 0/1.)
- **User threads ALSO set a per-thread mirror**: when
  `P->attr & 0x80000000` (bit 31 = user), it stores 1 at
  `*(P+0xDC)+0xC4` - the same 220(v1)/196 mirror site sub_14124 uses
  (`lw a2,220(a3); sw zero,196(a2)` / `li a3,1; sw a3,196(a2)`).
  So suspension is tracked BOTH globally (ctx+0x0C) and per-user-thread
  (a mirror in the thread's Tlspl block-pointer block at P+0xDC, offset
  0xC4 - same anchor CC identified as the per-thread Tlspl pointer).

### sceKernelResumeDispatchThread @0x14240 (50w)

```
state = a0 (argument!);      if (cause) return 0x80020064;
cookie = acquire;            if (!cookie) { release(0); return 0x80020066; }
if (ctx->logHandler) logHandler(24, cookie, 1, state);  // msgno 24
result = sub_14124(state, 0, cookie);                   // the real resume
release(cookie); return result;
```

- Takes the SAVED STATE as its argument (a0) - matches SuspendDispatchThread's
  return value being fed back in.
- The work is delegated to **sub_14124(state, 0, cookie)** - decoded:

```
sub_14124(state a0, a1, cookie a2):
    P = *(module+0x40);  S = module+0x40 block;
    if (state == 0)                       // "suspend" call:
        if (S->[0x0C] == 0) { S->[0x0C] = 1; /* transition */ }
        // falls through with a1 = P->attr
        if (user) *(P+0xDC)+0xC4 = 1;  else nothing; return 0;
    else                                   // "resume" call:
        old = S->[0x0C];  S->[0x0C] = 0;
        if (P->attr < 0) *(P+0xDC)+0xC4 = 0;  // clear user mirror
        if (old == 2)  { insert P into suspend list (module+0x4B0,
                         links at P+0/+4, state 8), S+4 = 0, FE4(1, 0) }
        // old 0 or 1: nothing; old 3 + a2==0: terminate path via
        // sub_17A14(P - 4*hdr21(threads), 0, ...) - the current thread
        // was suspended WITH termination pending; a2!=0 variant at
        // 0x14234 jumps to the never-return core 0x178C0(0, a1, 0x1AC)!
```

- **ctx+0x0C states (K's 0/1/2/3 decode confirmed and refined)**:
  - 0 = running normally,
  - 1 = dispatch suspended (set by SuspendDispatchThread),
  - 2 = suspended AND the current thread parked on the suspend list
    (ResumeDispatchThread(2) re-inserts it and requests dispatch),
  - 3 = suspended with thread termination pending (resume routes into
    the exit/terminate core).
- **The interaction**: ctx+0x0C is the *scheduler lock*, but L's batch
  showed every wait entry gates on it (CAN_NOT_WAIT 0x800201A7 if != 0) -
  that's the "cannot sleep while dispatch is suspended" rule, and it's why
  SuspendDispatchThread must run in thread context with the cookie held.
  The per-thread +0xC4 mirror (only for user threads) is what
  NotifyCallback/CheckCallback consult per-thread - consistent with CC's
  module+0x4C dispatch-busy gate (0x800201A7) reading the global side.

### sceKernelSuspendAllUserThreads @0x3984 (79w)

```
cause gate -> 0x80020064;  acquire -> 0x80020066 on fail;
if (logHandler) - NO log site in this one;
walk the THREAD UID table ring (ctx+0x4CC, first record = *table):
    for each record view (+4*hdr21(threads)):
        if (attr >= 0 && !(attr & 0x80000000)) continue;   // kernel: skip
        state = view->[0x0C];
        if (state >= 33) continue;                          // dead: skip
        switch (jump table @0x19C34, 33 entries):           // state cases
            runnable (1/2): 16BF4(view, prio) dequeue, insert into
                            suspend list module+0x4F0 (links at
                            view+0/+4), state = 8;
            waiting (4/12): state = 12;                      // add SUSPEND bit
            others:        no change (already suspended/dormant)
ctx->[0x30] = 1;                        // the pending-dispatch flag R saw!
release; return 0;
```

- **This function is the writer of ctx+0x30 = 1** - the very flag that
  makes SignalSema skip user-thread waiters (R's decode). SuspendAllUser
  Threads marks "dispatch pending" so the next Signal/SetEventFlag leaves
  user threads queued.
- Uses the **same 33-entry state jump table @0x19C34** family K found at
  0x19B2C/0x19BB0 (third table of the family) - only two cases wired:
  runnable→suspend-list (state 8) and waiting→12 (WAIT+SUSP).
- No cookie==0 CAN_NOT_WAIT variant here - plain 0x80020066.

## _sceKernelExitThread @0x16D34 (30w)

```
P = *(module+0x40);  inner = P+0x24;
status = inner->[0x18];                  // saved v0 of the thread
result = sceKernelExitThread(status);    // jal 0x139C8 (Y's matched fn) - DOES return!
k1 <<= 11 (after saving);
sub_1752C(P - 4*hdr21(threads));         // jal 0x1752C - post-exit teardown
return result;
```

- Calls the **exported** sceKernelExitThread (0x139C8) as a normal function
  and KEEPS its return value - unlike ExitThread itself which never returns,
  here the 0x178C0 core must have a path back (thread ctx, so the teardown
  parks this as a "dying" record instead of the never-return).
- **sub_1752C(rec)** (decoded, 0x1752C-0x177AC+): the shared post-exit
  teardown - if ctx+0x0C != 0 → 0x177AC deferred variant; else unlinks the
  record's wait links (+0/+4), runs the waiter-completion chain
  **sub_B48(rec, 0x800201AC)**, sub_2570, sub_27B4, KTLS walker 0x11E3C,
  0x60D4, mutex mass-release 0x66F0, 0x26CC, Tlspl walker 0xE0D4 - the
  same teardown set Y saw in the 0x178C0 core, factored out as a callee.
- **Argument quirk**: passes `P - 4*hdr21` (record-pointer *minus* the view
  offset) - i.e. the raw record base, not the view; sub_1752C re-adds the
  idx internally (`s0 = s1 + 4*hdr21(threads)`).

## sceKernelRegisterThreadEventHandler @0x1140 (191w)

```
k1 gates on name (a0) and handler (a3) pointers -> 0x800200D3;
    user thread also requires handler < 0x80000000 (kernel addr OK);
    if P+0x88 gate != 0 -> 0x80020064;  cfc0 cause -> 0x80020064;
mask (a2): ins t1,zero,0,4 (raw 0x7C091804) - only bits 0-3 legal;
    mask & 0xF != 0 -> 0x80020196;
acquire; cookie==0 -> 0x80020066 (via e64 build with a0=0 release);
special tids: -1, -8, -16 (xor/sltiu idiom):
    kernel: fall to create with s5 = tid (no class check);
    user:   must equal CURRENT tid (else 0x80020198);
tid == 0: mask must be exactly 4 (0x80020191 otherwise),
    target becomes the CURRENT thread (P+8);
resolve tid via 0x18784 vs threads table (fail 0x80020198);
user needs rec attr bit 2 (0x800200D1);  user threads must match the
    CURRENT thread's attr>>28 class nibble (xor 0x80000000 + srl 28);
create: 0x1875C(TEH table ctx+0x4F4, name, userByte, &rec);
    fail -> release + return its error;
fill TEH view (rec + 4*hdr21(TEH)):
    +0x14 target TCB view, +0x18 user flag, +8 uid (rec+12),
    +0x1C mask, +0x20 handler, +0x24 common, +0x28 gp (0x1873C(handler)
    result, else raw gp register);
link view into the GLOBAL TEH ring anchor ctx+0x4C0 (append at tail);
log(45, view, 5, tid, mask, handler, common, uid);   // msgno 45, 5 extras
release; return uid;
```

- **TEH records are stored as VIEWS** (rec + 4*hdr21(0x4F4)) - the TEH UID
  class uses the same hdr21 dual-view scheme as semas/evfs; the anchor
  ring at **ctx+0x4C0** is a new global (Z's error catalog knew 0x1A0
  "no TEH"; this is where they live).
- The **special tids**: -1 = any thread of matching class, -8/-16 = the
  two SceThreadEventTarget-ish sentinels (kernel callers only, or user
  if equal to self). This is the retail encoding of PSPSDK's
  `SCE_TEH_TARGET_ALL`-style registration.
- **tid==0 + mask==4** is the "current thread, all events" form.
- msgno **45** with 5 extras: (tid, mask, handler, common, uid) - a1 is the
  TEH view pointer.

## New knowledge bank entries

- **Dispatch-state field**: ctx+0x0C (0=run, 1=susp, 2=susp+parked,
  3=susp+terminate-pending); user threads keep a mirror at
  *(P+0xDC)+0xC4. SuspendDispatchThread returns 1 iff it transitioned
  0→1. ResumeDispatchThread(state) delegates to sub_14124; state==2
  re-parks the current thread (suspend list module+0x4B0, FE4(1,0)).
- **ctx+0x30 writer found**: SuspendAllUserThreads sets it to 1 (R only
  saw it read).
- **sub_14124** = resume-core(state, a1, cookie); **sub_1752C** =
  post-exit teardown(rec-base) - B48 with status 0x800201AC, then the
  8-callee teardown chain; **0x177AC** = its ctx+0x0C!=0 deferred
  variant.
- **_sceKernelExitThread** = the user-visible ExitThread entry that
  RETURNS (status from [P+0x24]+0x18, teardown after, returns
  ExitThread's value) - veneer around Y's matched 0x139C8.
- New error: 0x80020066 (release(0) flavor of CAN_NOT_WAIT - the
  cookie==0 variant that K/L batches saw as 0x800201A7 when ctx+0x0C
  was the blocker; here cookie==0 alone yields 66 not A7).
- TEH ring anchor ctx+0x4C0; TEH view layout +8 uid, +0x14 target,
  +0x18 user, +0x1C mask, +0x20 handler, +0x24 common, +0x28 gp;
  msgno 45; special tids -1/-8/-16; tid 0 requires mask==4.
- msgnos: 23 SuspendDispatch, 24 ResumeDispatch, 45 RegisterTEH.
- State jump table family: third table @0x19C34 (after K's 0x19B2C/
  0x19BB0), wired for runnable→8 and waiting→12 only.
- gas gotcha reconfirmed: forward `beqzl` to a label that must land ON
  the next branch (mid-sequence targets are legal and sometimes the
  *following* instruction is the real target - add the label there,
  don't assume it goes on a block head).

# Batch II - refer/misc sweep (17 functions, ALL MATCH, zero relocs)

**Verdict: 17 of 17 `*** MATCH ***`, zero relocation-covered words.** Source in
`match/threadman/tm_ii.c`. Smoke test passed before starting (tm.c: 4 MATCH +
2 reloc) and re-verified after finishing. 16 of 17 matched on the FIRST
compile; ReferTlsplStatus needed 1 fix (a hand-transcription register typo in
a %lo override: lw rt is $9/t1, not $25/t9).

Production note: the file was generated by a decoder script (/tmp/gen_ii.py,
kept outside the repo) that decodes each retail word independently and emits
the noreorder asm - branches/jumps computed from RAW offset fields, never the
.dis annotations (per the wave-3 GOTCHA). Only the module-data `%hi/%lo`
sites were hand-listed (24 of them). This eliminates the hand-transcription
typo class almost entirely (1 typo in 1406 words vs. the 4-6 of batches
K/R/U).

| # | function | addr | words | iters | status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | sceKernelReferThreadProfiler | 0x17230 | 19 | 1 | MATCH |
| 2 | sceKernelReferGlobalProfiler | 0x1727C | 11 | 1 | MATCH |
| 3 | sceKernelSendMsgPipeCB | 0x097D8 | 2 | 1 | MATCH |
| 4 | sceKernelReceiveMsgPipeCB | 0x0A1AC | 2 | 1 | MATCH |
| 5 | sceKernelAllocateFpl | 0x0C940 | 2 | 1 | MATCH |

(Size note: map-gap sizing said ReferTlsplStatus ≈670w because the next
