the .dis epilogue.)

## THE TWO UNNAMED NIDs - identification

Both remain unnamed in the authoritative public NID database
(pspdev/psplibdoc PSPLibDoc/kd/threadman.csv lists them verbatim as
`ThreadManForKernel_8672E3D0` / `ThreadManForUser_28BFD974`), so these are
**semantic identifications from the matched disassembly**:

### ThreadManForKernel_8672E3D0 @0x0272C - "GetCallbackNestingLevel" (current thread)

`u32 f(void)` - NO k1<<11 pointer gate, NO UID resolution, NO acquire/release
(unusual: no jal 0x186ac/0x186dc at all):
- `cfc0 cause != 0` (interrupt context) → return 0x80020064;
- P = `*(module+0x40)` (current thread record); result = `*(P+0xA0)`;
- if the per-thread log handler (P+0x08... i.e. record+8) is set:
  `handler(161, original_k1, 2, 0, result)` - msgno 161, a1 = saved k1;
- returns the P+0xA0 value.

P+0xA0 is the callback-nesting depth (batch DD decoded the pump at 0x2170
doing `TCB+0xA0 nesting++` / `TCB+0xA4 = record` around each user callback).
So this export = **"current callback nesting level / am-I-in-a-callback
query"** - the count of callbacks currently executing on the calling thread
(0 = not in a callback). Kernel lib only.

### ThreadManForUser_28BFD974 @0x1502C - user-mode temp-stack runner ("RunOnTempStack")

`s32 f(void *unknown_a0, void *func(a1,a2)?, ...)` - signature from register
flow: (a0→s2, a1→s1, a2→s0):
1. builds a 56-byte descriptor on sp: `[sp+0] = 56` (total size),
   `a0 = 0` (partition/pool 0);
2. `jal 0x187dc` - **new import identified: SysMem-side stack-block
   allocator** `(pool=0, desc56)` which fills `desc+40 = data ptr` and
   `desc+44 = block size`; negative return → out (returns the error);
3. pointer-gates `[data, data+size)` against k1<<11 (→ 0x800200D3) and
   requires `size >= 512` (sltiu; → 0x80020194);
4. `jal 0x17eb4` - the SAME stack-switch runner used by
   sceKernelExtendThreadStack - as `0x17eb4(uid=0 /*CURRENT thread*/, data,
   size, s1 /*arg*/, s0 /*func*/)`... arg/func order per the delay-slot
   moves (a3=s1, t0=s0): runs func(arg) on the freshly allocated stack and
   restores;
5. success → **returns 0xdeadbeef in BOTH v0 and v1** (the same retail quirk
   as ExtendThreadStack, batch DD); log handler called as
   `handler(40, original_k1, 3, a0_orig, a1_orig)` - msgno 40, 3 extras.

I.e. it is the user-library sibling of the kernel
`sceKernelExtendThreadStack` machinery: allocate ≥512B temp stack, run the
callee on it, restore, return 0xdeadbeef. Errors: allocator<0 (passthrough),
0x80020194 (<512), 0x800200D3 (gate).

## Per-function decode notes

### sceKernelReferThreadProfiler @0x17230 (19w) / ReferGlobalProfiler @0x1727C (11w)
Profiler readouts, no k1/UID machinery at all:
- Thread: `cfc0 cause != 0` → return 0; `*(module+0x7E4) != 1` (profiler not
  enabled) → return 0; else `P = *(module+0x40)`, a3 = `*(P+0x3C)`
  (per-thread profiler slots head), and returns the Allegrex
  perf-counter read pair: `ins`-family word 0x7C64E004 = `ext a0,v0,28,4`?
  (raw-spliced; reads c0 perfcnt select/mask - `mfc0 v0,c0_perfcnt` shape
  with a0=0x4000_0000 select, v1=0xBC40_0000) - a3==0 → return 0. Log-free.
- Global: `*(module+0x824) != 2` → return 0; else the same raw perf word
  0x7CA3E004 and return v1 = 0x4000_0000; j-back edge sets v1=0 first.
Both return 0 (not an error) when profiling is off. The two 0x7CxxE004
words are SPECIAL3/ins-ext family - spliced raw .word, no block.

### sceKernelReferSystemStatus @0x17148 (58w)
`s32 f(SceKernelSystemStatus *st)`: out-ptr gate (|st+28) & k1<<11 → D3;
acquire; NO UID resolve. If log handler set: `handler(164, st, 1, cookie)`
(msgno 164, a1 = the USER POINTER st - note: a1 = first syscall arg, per
batch E). Then `jal 0x183ec` - **a new internal callee BEYOND the 0x187xx
import table**: 0x183ec builds the system-status block into scratch
ctx+0x6D4 (the shared Refer scratch); copy-out via `0x1880c(st, scratch,
min(st->size, *(ctx+0x6D4)))` with the sltu/movn min; release; return 0
(v1 quirk: epilogue does `move v0,v1` with v1=0). Status size word at
scratch = set by 0x183ec.

### veneers (2w each)
- SendMsgPipeCB `j 0x97E0; li t3,1` - into batch V's send core.
- ReceiveMsgPipeCB `j 0xA1B4; li t3,1` - into batch W's receive core.
- AllocateFpl `j 0xC950; li t3,0`; AllocateFplCB `li t3,1` - into an
  UNDECODED 178w allocate core @0x0C950 (behind them; next export map gap
  hides it - prime next pick, wait type 10 expected per the 1..14 catalog).

### sceKernelDeleteFpl @0x0C82C (69w)
Standard delete twin vs UID table ctx+0x4EC (FPLs): cfc0 → 0x80020064;
resolve fail → 0x8002019D; attr16 bit 0x10 → D1; **log msgno 117 (0x75)**
BEFORE release with a1 = rec view, a2=1; `jal 0x1877c` (uid free) then
release; **hardcoded return 0 via v1** (result discarded, exits route
through v1 like the other delete twins). Errors 0x64/0xD1/0x19D.

### sceKernelCancelReceiveMbx @0x08FF0 (78w)
MBX table ctx+0x4E0; receiver-side cancel: out-ptr (pCount, +4) gate D3;
resolve fail → **0x8002019B** (mbx-bad-uid code); attr16 bit 0x8 → D1; log
msgno **96 (0x60)** a1 = s8 = pCount; `0x1879c(rec, 0x86D94883)` (the
cancel-all tag) with result stored `*pCount` via `bnezl s8` (skipped when
pCount==NULL); release; return 0. NO cfc0 (interrupt-legal, like the other
Cancels).

### sceKernelGetThreadKernelStackFreeSize @0x15A8C (94w)
`cfc0` first (cause → 0x80020064); tid==0 → current thread (P =
*(module+0x40), s8 = *(P+8) = TCB... actually s8 = *(a0+8) where a0=P -
the current thread's TCB id/record); resolve vs threads table ctx+0x4CC,
fail → 0x80020198; attr bit 0x1 → D1; log msgno **44 (0x2C)** a1 = s8 =
tid, 1 extra; rec view s0 = rec + 4*idx:
- `*(s0+20) < 0` (kernel attr) → stack fields at **rec+48/+204**;
  else (bltzl taken) at **rec+44/+196** (the user/extended pair);
- `s0 = *(rec+196)` (stack SIZE, 4-aligned? then `t6 = s0>>2; a2 = t6-4`);
- `s8 = stackBase + 16`, then scan `a2` words from the stack TOP downward
  while `*(ptr) == 0xFFFFFFFF` (the 0xFFFFFFFF fill pattern - free size
  counted in WORDS: loop `a1 = a0<<2` until a word != -1 or a0 == a2);
- returns a1 = free BYTES (`idx<<2`), k1 restored. NO release cookie in
  the scan path... release happens before the scan (jal 0x186dc at
  0x15B38). Wait - release is called at 0x15B38 BEFORE the scan loop
  (0x15B5C) - the scan runs outside the critical section. Returns bytes.

### sceKernelTryAllocateFpl @0x0CC1C (104w)
FPL table ctx+0x4EC; out-ptr (ppBlock, +4) gate D3; resolve fail →
0x8002019D; attr16 bit **0x2** (wait permission) → D1; **log msgno 120
(0x78)** a1 = s8 = FPL obj view, 2 extras (a3 = uid s2, t0 = ppBlock);
`beqzl *(obj+8)` (numFree): 0 → if log handler set: log then **return
0x80020190**?? - no: reads `v0 = *(ctx+8)` (log handler) in the delay slot;
0 numFree → handler(120,...) if set then `0x800201B3` would-block? - the
encoded constant is **0x80020190**... raw word 0x36020190 at 0xCD5C:
`ori v0,s0,0x190` → **0x80020190** (alloc-failed). Nonzero: `jal 0xd38c`
(FPL ring pop, batch S's push counterpart) pops a free block; then
`block = base + freeIdx*blockSize` via `mult a2,v0; mflo a1; s1 = t9+a1`
(t9 = obj+4 base, a2 = obj+28 stride, v0 = popped index); `*ppBlock = s1`;
release; return 0. Log-then-error path for empty pool: a0=120 via jalr with
a3 = s2 (uid). NOTE the `beqzl t5,0xcd40` at 0xCCBC: delay slot loads
v0 = *(ctx+8) = log handler - shared delay-slot register reuse.

### sceKernelGetThreadmanIdOwner @0x1696C (108w)
`s32 f(SceUID id, SceUID *outOwner)`: out-ptr (+4) gate D3; acquire; then a
TYPE DISPATCH over id families by trying resolvers: `jal 0xc1fc` (sema
resolver? returns rec>0) → type 5; `jal 0xd42c` → 6 (vpl); `jal 0xb16c` →
7 (mbx); `jal 0xe4a4` → 14 (tlspl). Each: result > 0 → `*out = 5/6/7/14`,
release, return the resolver value. Hmm - actually the immediate stores
(5/6/7/14) are the TYPE ids... The tail: walks the queue-links class table
`*(module+0x50C)` ring (a2 = head, `*(a2)` first member): per member a0 =
rec + 4*idx, checks `*(a0+304)` and `*(a0+308)` (== uid? beql s2), and
wait-queue chains at +208/+212 (`*(q+8)` = waiter TCB, walk `*(q+0)` next,
beql vs uid), collecting `s1 = *(a0+8)` = owner id; found (s1>0) →
`*out = 1`, return 1; else after ring → release, **0x800201CB** (bad id).
So: **"which thread owns / is waiting on this id"** - returns the id TYPE
in *out and the owner tid as the return value for wait-queue objects
(waiting thread), or the resolver status. (Out stores observed: 5, 6, 7,
14, 1.) Errors 0xD3/0x1CB. Note `*(module+0x50C)` = the 0x4CC-relative
threads-table... module+0x50C = ctx+0x4CC - the THREADS uid table ring
walk.

### sceKernelReferThreadDebugStatus @0x161EC (125w)
48-byte? - gate size 244 (0xF4): out-ptr gate D3; `cfc0` → 0x80020197
(note: NOT 0x64 - this refer is cause-gated to the 197 "illegal context"
error); uid==0 → current thread; resolve fail → 0x80020198; attr bit 1 →
D1; log msgno **36 (0x24)** a1 = s8 = tid, 2 extras; **`jal 0x180ac`** -
new internal callee (builds the 244-byte debug status block into scratch
ctx+0x6D4; takes rec, scratch, cause); copy-out `0x1880c(status, scratch,
min(status->size, *(ctx+0x6D4)))`; SPECIAL2 `0x70130026` filler words
(3 blocks of 8 nops flanked by 0x70130024/0x70130026 raw spliced - the
retail padding idiom); release; return 0. Errors 0xD3/0x197/0x198/0xD1.

### sceKernelReferTlsplStatus @0x0E23C (122w)
`s32 f(SceUID thid, SceKernelTlsplInfo *info)` - NOTE first gate: `s4`
(preserved a0 = thid) vs `*(module+0x6D4 + 4*(thid... ))`: raw words
0x7E1418C0 at 0xE288 = `ins s4,k1,3,4`?? - actually the delay-slot word
after `jal 0x186ac` is 0x7E1418C0 = SPECIAL3 ins: `ins $20,$27,3,4` -
the Tlspl mirror-index EXTRACT family (per the STRUCTURAL CORRECTION note:
SceUID bits 3-6 = pool index). Then `a3 = (s4<<2)+&ctx` and
`*(a3 + 0x694)` - wait: `sll t0,s4,2; addu a3,t0,s8; lw a0,0x694(a3)` -
a0 = `*(module+0x40 + 4*idx + 0x694)`... vs thid `bne a0,s0 → 0xE3FC` =
**0x800201D0** (unknown tlspl id - the uid-mirror cross-check, module
base + 0x6D4-family). Then Tlspl class table ctx+0x4F0 (= module+0x530,
Z's type-14) resolve; attr bit 1 → D1; log msgno **130 (0x82)** a1 =
s8 = slot view, 2 extras; 60-byte SceKernelTlsplInfo at scratch ctx+0x6D4:
size 60, name[32] via 0x18814, attr `*(slot+0)`, poolId? `*(slotView+0)`,
+0x2C `*(objView+20)`, +0x30 `*(objView+24)`, +0x34 `*(objView+16)`,
+0x38 `*(slotView+8)` (waitCount); copy-out 0x1880c min 60. Errors
0xD3/0x1D0/D1 + resolver code. The `ins` word is the 3rd live ins
confirmation.

### sceKernelReferThreadRunStatus @0x15F50 (167w)
`s32 f(SceUID thid, SceKernelThreadRunStatus *st)`: 48-byte status; gate
(st|st+48) & k1<<11 → D3; `cfc0` → 0x80020064 (a3 = 0x80020198 preloaded
but cause branch goes to 0x161D0 = 0x64 - the a3 is reused as error base
later); thid==0 → current; resolve threads ctx+0x4CC fail → 0x80020198;
attr bit 1 → D1; log msgno **35 (0x23)** a1 = s8 = thid, 2 extras; scratch
ctx+0x6D4, 48B zeroed from ctx+0x6A8; fields from rec view s8 = rec+4*idx:
- +0x04 = `*(rec+12)` (threadPriority? - the s16 rec+184 is NOT used; raw
  `lw v1,12(s8)`); state classify: `xori v1,0xC`/`xori v1,0x4` + sltiu
  pairs → if state 4 or 12 (waiting): +0x0C = `*(rec+88)` (waitResult),
  +0x10 = `*(rec+92)`? no - `v1 = *(rec+92)` (`lw v1,0x5C`) != 0 →
  `+0x10 = *(v1+12)` (wait object id from the wait block), else +0x10 = 0;
- +0x08 = `*(rec+16)` (currentPriority - rec+16);
- +0x14 = `*(rec+80)` (wakeupCount), +0x18.. = `*(rec+52)/(rec+56)` OR
  pair (64-bit clocks acc, t4/t1 zero-extend OR shape `sll t2,t5,0` =
  no-op sll 0!), then if rec == P (current thread): **live CPU-time fixup**
  - `jal 0xece4` (64-bit clock read) minus `*(P+0x70)/(P+0x74)` with the
  full 64-bit subu/sltu carry chain (same as ReferThreadStatus's fixup);
- +0x20 = `*(rec+68)`, +0x24 = `*(rec+72)`, +0x28 = `*(rec+76)`,
  +0x2C = `*(rec+156)`;
- copy-out 0x1880c min 48; release; return 0.
NOTE the `sll t2,t5,0x0` words (000D5000) - shift-by-0 no-ops Sony emitted
(2 sites) and the `srl v1,s1,0x0` (00111802) - transcribed verbatim.

## New internals/imports catalogued this batch

- **0x183ec** - system-status block builder (ReferSystemStatus; scratch

# Batch KK - THE DISPATCHER CLUSTER (resched request / dispatch loop / dispatch core / runq primitives)

**Verdict: 6 of 6 internal functions word-exact via build/vdiff.py (577/577
words, zero relocations).** All source in `match/threadman/tm_kk.c`, generated
by `/tmp/gen_kk.py` (a clone of tools/gen_tm.py with two decoder fixes, see
GOTCHAs). vdiff validated against two known matches first (tm_v.o sub_097E0
359/359, tm_ee.o sub_06A7C 246/246). 2 compile iterations total (iteration 1:
gas rejects 2-operand `bnel`, must be `bnezl` - the known batch-R gotcha;
iteration 2: all clean). Smoke test re-verified after finishing
(tm.c: 4 MATCH + 2 reloc).

| function | addr | words | status | iters |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| sub_FE4 (resched request entry) | 0x00FE4 | 87 | *** MATCH *** | 2 |
| sub_127D0 (make-ready, no preempt) | 0x127D0 | 22 | *** MATCH *** | 2 |
| sub_12828 (**THE DISPATCH LOOP**) | 0x12828 | 247 | *** MATCH *** | 2 |
| sub_16BF4 (runq dequeue) | 0x16BF4 | 21 | *** MATCH *** | 2 |
| sub_16C48 (make-ready + preempt) | 0x16C48 | 59 | *** MATCH *** | 2 |
| sub_172F8 (**THE DISPATCH CORE**, 3rd clz cascade) | 0x172F8 | 141 | *** MATCH *** | 2 |

Sizes/boundaries pinned: sub_FE4 = 0xFE4..0x113C (next export 0x1140);
sub_127D0 = 0x127D0..0x12824; sub_12828 = 0x12828..0x12C00 (next prologue
0x12C04); sub_16BF4 = 0x16BF4..0x16C44 (21w - the "bitmap clear" tail at
0x16C14..0x16C44 is PART of it; the jr ra @0x16C0C is a mid-function early
return); sub_16C48 = 0x16C48..0x16D30; sub_172F8 = 0x172F8..0x1752C (next
prologue 0x1752C = the 0x1752C teardown core HH decoded).

## WHERE THE DISPATCHER IS (the brief's locate task)

- The **context-switch/dispatch loop is sub_12828 @0x12828** - NOT near the
  module entry. Called from exactly ONE place: sub_FE4+0xA8 (0x108C).
- The **clz cascade the brief describes is a THIRD cascade @0x173A4..0x173CC**
  inside **sub_172F8 @0x172F8** (the "dispatch core" that picks the next
  thread). Z's inventory said "8 clz words, 2 cascades"; the real count is
  **12 words, 3 cascades** (0x147D4 in 0x14598, 0x14A94 in Rotate, 0x173A4
  here; the 13th match 0x006C7056 @0x1966C is data, not code).
- sub_172F8 is called from exactly ONE place: sub_12828+0x2EC (0x12B14).
  The chain is: 30 switch-away sites → jal 0xFE4 → jal 0x12828 → jal 0x172F8.
- 0x12C04 (the per-thread interrupt-mask program helper DD saw called by
  ChangeCurrentThreadAttr) is ALSO called by the dispatch loop (0x12A80) -
  it is part of the switch-in sequence, not just attr changes.

## Contexts, k1 and gp - where they actually live

The dispatch loop does **NOT save or restore any GPR/k1/gp itself** - no word
in 0x12828..0x12C04 or 0x172F8..0x1752C touches $27 or $28 (verified by
field scan). The real mechanism:

1. **GPR/k1/gp context = the 320B ctx block at TCB+0x28** (AA's "+28 = 320B
   ctx blk", K's TCB+0x28 context sub-block). It is filled by the kernel's
   exception/syscall entry stub (outside threadman.prx) and reloaded at
   `eret`. threadman only re-points the hardware to the NEW thread's block.
2. **The re-point is done through banked COP0 registers** using rs=3-encoded
   move words (raw `.word 0x40Cxxxxx`), executed on every switch-in
   (sub_12828 @0x12A48..0x12A5C):
   - `0x40C0C800`: bank6 = 0 (no FPU) - or `0x40C7C800` @0x12AD4: bank6 =
     *(newTCB+0x3C) = the thread's FPU context block pointer;
   - `0x40C47000`: bank14 = t0 = fpuBlock+0x60 (address of the saved FPU
     ctrl/status word; 0x60 when no FPU - the value the FPU-restore loop
     leaves in t0);
   - `0x40C97800`: bank30 = *(newTCB+0x28) = the 320B GPR/exception ctx block;
   - `0x40CF8000`: bank0 = &newTCB+0x24 (address of the exitInfo field).
   The exception handler reads these to find the new thread's register frame -
   that is where k1/gp get reloaded (from inside the 320B block), outside
   this module.
3. **FPU context = memory-mapped at 0xBC400000**: TCB+0x3C points to a block
   whose +0x10..+0x5F mirror MMIO 0xBC400000..+0x4F (20 data words) and
   +0x60 mirrors the ctrl/status word at MMIO +0x50. Two 16-byte-stride copy
   loops in sub_12828 do the transfer, each ending in `sync`:
   - switch-OUT (0x12BBC..0x12BF8): MMIO → block (only if oldTCB+0x3C != 0);
   - switch-IN (0x12A90..0x12ADC): block → MMIO (only if newTCB+0x3C != 0).
4. **sub_FE4's cfc0 $25** (@0x1020/0x1040): reads a kernel queue/state node
   (the exception-entry "current" anchor). FE4 manages a 0/1 state word at
   node+8 (acquire at 0x10A4..0x10CC when the id/counter field is 0, release
   at 0x1060..0x1088 when it is 1) with `sync` barriers - a handoff lock
   around the actual switch window.

## sub_FE4 @0xFE4 - the reschedule-request entry (87w, 31 jal call sites)

`sub_FE4(a0 = requestKind, a1 = st)`:

- P = *(module+0x40) (current thread record). **P+0x40 = the per-thread
  "switch frame" pointer**, and FE4 selects the variant: a0 != 0 → frame =
  P+0x4C (s1 = 2); a0 == 0 → frame = P+0x48 (s1 = 1). (sub_12828 bumps
  *(frame)+1 on every real switch @0x12970 - it is a dispatch generation
  counter. The dead tail @0x1100 also knows a P+0x44 variant.)
- a1 == 0 (interrupt context, cannot switch): **request via software
  interrupt** - `cfc0 v0,$13; ori v0,v0,0x200; ctc0 v0,$13` (set CP0 Cause
  SW-interrupt-pending bit 9) + jal 0x186BC(0x41), return. This is the
  "defer to the interrupt" path every interrupt-legal operation takes.
- a1 != 0: if S->dispatchState (module+0x4C) != 0 → return (already
  pending); else cfc0 $25 dance; jal **0x186E4(P+0x1C)** - its result v0
  != 0 → `jal sub_12828(s1)` (the real switch attempt), then
  `jal 0x186C4(v0)` (release with the dispatch result); v0 == 0 → clear
  P->0x98 (notify-pending) and fall into the cfc0$25 release + return.
- Tail 0x1100..0x113C is **dead code** - no j/jal/branch reaches it
  module-wide (verified by scan): an unused "switch-completed probe"
  (`if dispatchState==0 && S->next != P then P->0x40 = P+0x44; return
  S->next != P`). Transcribed anyway (it is inside the 87-word body).

## sub_12828 @0x12828 - THE DISPATCH LOOP (247w)

1. If S->dispatchState (S+0xC) != 0 → return immediately (only runs when
   idle). P = *S.
2. If P->0x3C (FPU block) != 0 → save FPU MMIO→block, sync, re-enter.
3. State gate on P->0x0C: 32 (0x20, dead) skips the user-stack checks.
   User threads (attr bit 31 set) get their release/anchor checks: the
   ctxblk(+52)&0x18 path, then up to 3 × `jal 0x177C4(P, 0/1, ptr)` - the
   stack-anchors report helper (ptr = P->0x2C / P->0x30 / s6 = P->0x28
   orderings via sltu) - plus `jal 0x17844(P)` when TCB+0xDC !=
   *(ctxblk+168) (Tlspl pointer mismatch path).
4. **Target selection**: v0 = *(S+4) (the next-thread slot). If 0 →
   `jal sub_172F8` (the core; fills S+4). If target == current → bump
   *(S+0x14)+1 (generation) and continue the refresh sequence anyway.
5. **CPU-time accounting** (0x1292C..0x12980): `jal 0xECE4()` 64-bit now;
   {P+0x34,P+0x38} += now − {S+0x70,S+0x74}; {S+0x70,S+0x74} = now;
   *(S+0x10)+1 (global switch counter); *(P->0x40 frame)+1 (per-thread
   dispatch counter). (This is the {ctx+0x70/+0x74} clock K/II saw from
   the outside - it is the DISPATCH timestamp, not the thread's.)
6. Bit 0x40000000 of *(target+0x34) picks a flag-transform path
   (movn on s4 |= 0x100 vs *(S+0x1C)); then the **log hook**: if
   *(S+8) != 0 (the GLOBAL threadman log handler, module+0x48 - the
   SetThreadmanLogHandler slot) → `jalr v0` with (a0=1, a1=0, a2=5,
   a3=oldTid) + t0=oldPrio, t1=*(a2+8)=newTid, s3=flags - **msgno 1 =
   "context switch" log event**, 5 extras. This is the missing 129th log
   site family.
7. **The switch itself** (0x12A00..0x12A5C): if *(module+0) != 0 →
   *(*(module+0)) = *(target+0xDC) (global Tlspl-pointer cache update);
   ***(module+0x40) = target (S->current = new thread)**; then the FPU
   restore loop (if target+0x3C != 0) + the four banked-reg stores above
   (+ `jal 0x12C04(target)` first when *(ctxblk+52) & 0x40000000, the
   per-thread interrupt-mask reprogram); restore s0-s4 and RETURN.
   The return goes back through sub_FE4 to the exception stub; the new
   thread's registers (sp/pc/k1/gp incl.) come from its 320B block at
   eret - threadman never touches them.

## sub_172F8 @0x172F8 - THE DISPATCH CORE (141w)

1. **Deferred path**: if *(S+0x34) != 0 (pending-dispatch flag, the field
   R/HH saw written by SuspendAllUserThreads): S->next = P; clear S+0x34;
   *( *(S+0x510) +0xC) = 1 (a fallback thread record - likely the idle
   thread - marked RUNNING). If P->state == 1 (RUNNING) → L17340 requeues
   P at the TAIL of its own ring (identical tail-append to sub_127D0) with
   P->state = 2 and bitmap bit set; else S->next = *(S+0x510).
2. **The clz cascade** (0x17398..0x173D4): 4 loads S+148/+144/+140/+136,
   4 `clz` raw words, 3 movz + 3 `addiu +32` - same priority-scan as
   Z decoded, result t0 = most-urgent non-empty queue, **slti t0,128**:
   128 (all queues empty) → **panic**: `jal 0x18724(0x19800)` (string
   constant at module+0x19800) then return - the dispatcher's
   "no runnable thread" fatal.
3. **Pick**: if P->state != 1 (not running - idle/waiting): dequeue the
   HEAD of ring[t0] (t1 = *(S+152+8*t0); splice 0x17400/0x17414; if
   head->next == head->prev (sole occupant) clear the bitmap bit via
   rotrv/nor/and at 0x17420..0x1744C - exactly Z's mechanics), set
   chosen->state = 1 (RUNNING), *(S+4) = chosen, return.
4. **Preempt path** (P->state == 1): only if bestPrio < P->prio (slt at
   0x17458; else return - no switch): dequeue head of ring[bestPrio] AND
   requeue CURRENT P at the TAIL of ring[P->prio] with P->state = 2
   (0x17478..0x174E4), chosen->state = 1, *(S+4) = chosen.

## sub_127D0 / sub_16C48 / sub_16BF4 - the runq primitives

- **sub_127D0(T)** (22w): insert T at the TAIL of ring[T->prio]
  (T->prev = anchor->prev, anchor->prev = T, T->next = anchor,
  tail->next = T) + set bitmap bit (rotrv 0x80000000,prio). No preempt
  check, no state write - caller sets state. This is K's "sub_127d0(s8)
  make ready" = plain append. (Note: Z's 0x16c48 decode said insert at
  FRONT; actually both 0x16C48's preempt branch and 0x127D0 insert at the
  TAIL - anchor->prev side. The head anchor semantics are unchanged.)
- **sub_16C48(T, st)** (59w): the make-ready WITH preempt decision.
  If T->prio < P->prio (more urgent) AND S->dispatchState == 0 AND
  st != 0 (thread context): insert P at TAIL of P's own ring, P->state =
  2, T->state = 1, **S->next = T**, then `jal sub_FE4(0, st)` - the full
  preempt handshake. Else: T->state = 2 (+ S->next = 0 when st == 0,
  interrupt ctx) and `jal sub_127D0(T)`. 8 jal callers module-wide
  (0xA7C, 0x3888, 0x46A0, 0x5560, 0x69AC, 0x7C30, 0xBE50, itself).
- **sub_16BF4(T, prio)** (21w): unlink T (next->prev = prev,
  prev->next = next); if next == prev AND next != T (sole occupant of an
  anchored ring) → clear bitmap bit (S+136+4*(prio>>5) &= ~rotrv mask).
  Z's decode confirmed 1:1; the whole 21-word body including the clear
  tail is one function.

## New facts for the TCB/S maps

| where | field | evidence |
|---|---|---|
| TCB+0x0C | 32 (0x20) = dead-skip in dispatch loop | 0x12874 beq |
| TCB+0x34 | per-thread flag, bit 0x40000000 gates 0x12C04 mask-reprogram + log-flag path | 0x12978, 0x12A40 |
| TCB+0x3C | FPU context block ptr (block+0x10..+0x5F regs, +0x60 ctrl/status; mirrors MMIO 0xBC400000..+0x50) | 0x12860, loops |
| TCB+0x40 | per-thread SWITCH-FRAME ptr - FE4 sets it to P+0x48 (kind 0) / P+0x4C (kind != 0) / P+0x44 (dead tail); *(frame) = dispatch generation counter, bumped per switch | 0x1008/0x10EC/0x112C, 0x12970 |
| S+0x00 (module+0x40) | current thread record (re-stated: writes at 0x12A1C) | |
| S+0x04 | next/dispatch-target record (written by 0x172F8, 0x16C48; 0 = "recompute") | |
| S+0x08 | GLOBAL threadman log handler (msgno 1 = context switch, 5 extras: oldTid, oldPrio, newTid, flags...) | 0x129F0, jalr 0x12AF8 |
| S+0x0C | dispatch state (0 = idle - the ONLY state the loop runs in) | 0x12850 |
| S+0x10 | global dispatch counter | 0x1296C |
| S+0x14 | same-thread re-dispatch generation | 0x12B08 |
| S+0x1C | pointer compared against target+0x20 in the 0x40000000 path | 0x1298C |
| S+0x34 | pending-dispatch flag (SuspendAllUserThreads writer) | 0x1730C |
| S+0x70/+0x74 | 64-bit DISPATCH timestamp (CPU-time deltas are now−this) | 0x12934.. |
| S+0x510 | fallback/idle thread record ptr (state set RUNNING on deferred dispatch) | 0x17318 |
| module+0x00 | global record whose [0] caches *(current+0xDC) | 0x12A04 |
| module+0x19800 | panic string for "no runnable thread" | 0x17518 |
| COP0 $25 (cfc0) | kernel queue/state node; +8 = 0/1 handlock state, +0/+4 links, sync-guarded | 0x1020..0x1088 |
| COP0 rs=3 banks 0/6/14/30 | per-thread: &TCB+0x24 / FPU block ptr / &FPU ctrl word / 320B ctx block ptr | 0x12A2C..0x12A58 |

## GOTCHAs for next agents

1. **gas -mips2 rejects 2-operand `bnel $r,label`** (again, batch R): the
   gen_tm.py decoder emits `bnel $rs,$0,...` for rt==0 - change to
   `bnezl`. Same presumably for `beql`→`beqzl` (my generator special-cases
   both).
2. **gen_tm.py decoder bugs (fixed in /tmp/gen_kk.py, worth porting if
   tools/ is ever touched)**: (a) `cfc0` hardcoded `$13` - retail also
   reads $25 and $8 (0x4044C800/0x40444000); use the word's rd field.
   (b) Allegrex `rotrv` = srlv fn=6 with sa=1 - decoding it as plain
   `srlv` silently drops bit 6 (fn 0x46→0x06, 1-bit word diff per site).
   Gate sllv/srlv/srav on sa==0, else raw .word.
3. The `.dis` annotation for the rs=3 COP0 words prints raw hex - splice
   `.word 0x40Cxxxxx` (they are NOT cfc0/ctc0; gas has no mnemonic).
4. Branch-target labels: compute from raw offsets; the shared epilogue in
   sub_172F8 is jumped to at THREE addresses (0x17334 the `lw ra`, 0x17338
   the `jr ra` itself with the `lw ra` in each j's delay slot) - keep all
   three label sites.
5. Mid-function epilogues: sub_12828's frame restore at 0x12A60 is BOTH an
   early return AND the fall-through end of the switch sequence; sub_16BF4's
   `jr ra` at 0x16C0C is an early return inside a 21-word function. Don't
   split functions at every `jr ra`.
6. Dead code exists inside function bodies (sub_FE4 0x1100..0x113C has no
   in-module xrefs) - transcribe the full extent anyway; vdiff compares
   words, not reachability.

# Batch LL - the wait-queue machinery (shared cores every IPC family uses)

**Verdict: 10/10 byte-exact, zero relocations** - the 8 assigned internals
plus 2 adjacent leaves that sit physically between them and complete the
mechanism (372 words total). Source: `match/threadman/tm_ll.c`, generated
from retail raw words by the tools/gen_tm.py method (decoder script kept at
/tmp/gen_ll.py). Compared with `build/vdiff.py` (validated first on
tm_ii.o: 19/19 and tm_v.o: 359/359, both known matches). Harness smoke test
passed before starting and re-verified after finishing (tm.c: 4 MATCH +
2 reloc).

**2 compile iterations.** Run 1: 9/10 clean; the single diff was MY
transcription slip, not a discovery - the %lo override at 0x998 wrote
`addiu $2,$11,0x4F0` for retail `addiu a2,t3,0x4F0` (a2 = $6, I typed $2).
Run 2: 10/10. Also one pre-compile correction: sub_0890 is 30 words
(0x890-0x908), not 12 - the generator's leave-function warning caught it.

| Addr | Words | Function | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0x0680 | 118 | `sub_0680` - queue insert core (timer arm + priority/FIFO ring insert + FE4 away) | ✅ 118/118, 0 relocs |
| 0x0858 | 14 | `sub_0858` - sleep-queue tail append (L's sleep core) | ✅ 14/14, 0 relocs |
| 0x0890 | 30 | `sub_0890` - bare priority re-insert leaf [bonus] | ✅ 30/30, 0 relocs |
| 0x0908 | 7 | `sub_0908` - wait-enqueue veneer → sub_680 (`li t1,0`) | ✅ 7/7, 0 relocs |
| 0x0924 | 57 | `sub_0924` - release-from-wait (unlink + timer cancel + cleanup + ready) | ✅ 57/57, 0 relocs |
| 0x0A08 | 64 | `sub_0a08` - wake-now (cause-aware dispatch tail) | ✅ 64/64, 0 relocs |
| 0x0B08 | 16 | `sub_0b08` - direct-wake handoff to head waiter | ✅ 16/16, 0 relocs |
| 0x0B48 | 31 | `sub_0b48` - exit-waiter walk (Y's decode, byte-confirmed) | ✅ 31/31, 0 relocs |
| 0x0BC4 | 27 | `sub_0bc4` - wait-machinery cancel, no unlink [bonus] | ✅ 27/27, 0 relocs |
| 0x0C30 | 8 | `sub_0c30` - cleanup: `0x1879C(a0, 0x85FE2B4C)` | ✅ 8/8, 0 relocs |

---

## Function decodes

### sub_680 @0x680 - THE wait primitive (118w)

Register args: `a0 = rec` (UID record of the object), `a1 = waitType`,
`a2 = policyAttr`, `a3 = deadline`, plus two Sony-internal slots:
`t0` (stored to TCB+0x7C, the timer's 2nd teardown arg) and `t1`
(optional counter pointer). sub_908 forwards a0-a3 and clears only t1
- t0 propagates from 0x908's caller.

```
slotView = rec + 4*hdr21(*(module+0x508));      // shared slot table
tcb = *(module+0x40);                            // current thread
tcb->[0x78] = 0;                                 // timer handle
if (t0 != 0) {                                   // deadline given
    h = sub_10FAC(a3, module+0xC8C, tcb);        // arm thread timer
    if (h < 0) return h;                         // arm failure bubbles up
    tcb->[0x7C] = t0;  tcb->[0x78] = h;
}
if (tcb->[0x98] && tcb->[0x9C]) {                // CB-armed AND notify pending
    tcb->[0x98] = 0;                             //   (delay slot: always)
    if (timer) { sub_11104(h, 0); tcb->[0x7C]=0; tcb->[0x78]=0; }
    return 0x800201AB;                           // refuse to block: re-pump CBs
}
tcb->[0x0C] = 4;                                 // state = WAITING
tcb->[0x58] = waitType;                          // wait gate
tcb->[0x80] = 0;                                 // waitResult
tcb->[0x5C] = rec;                               // THE WAITED OBJECT (see below)
slotView->[8]++;                                 // waiter count
if (policyAttr & 0x100) {                        // PRIORITY-ordered insert
    // walk from first = *(slotView+12); stop at first node whose
    // node->prio (TCB+0x10) is NUMERICALLY GREATER than mine;
    // insert BEFORE it; all-equal or empty -> tail.  When slotView->[4]
    // is nonzero and the insert point is the head node (its prev is the
    // anchor), skip past the head (insert second) - protected head.
} else {                                         // FIFO tail append at anchor
}
if (t1) (*(u32*)t1)++;                           // bump caller's counter
*(module+0x44) = 0;                              // do-dispatch flag
sub_FE4(1, 1);                                   // dispatch away - thread parks
return tcb->[0x80];                              // the waker's result
```

Facts closed for earlier batches' open questions:
- **TCB+0x5C is the OBJECT POINTER** (stored = a0). H/R called it the
  "cleanup flag"; it is the record every release path hands to
  `0x1879C(TCB+0x5C, tag, TCB)`. R's "slot+12 anchors" and this are the
  two halves of one mechanism.
- The `0x800201AB` notify-abort is synthesized HERE too (at enqueue), not
  only by NotifyCallback - L's SleepThreadCB loop can exit without ever
  parking if a notification arrived first.
- module+0xC8C is the timer template for OBJECT waits (L's delay cores
  use module+0x41F4 - two timer classes).
- `*(module+0x40)` word+0 is conditionally incremented (t1 arg) - a
  context-level waiter refcount; `*(module+0x44)` = the do-dispatch flag
  zeroed before FE4, exactly the "ctx+4 = 0" of H/R/L's decodes.

### sub_858 @0x858 - sleep/delay enqueue (14w, L's decode byte-confirmed)

`sub_858(queueHead a0, waitType a1)`: tail-appends `*(module+0x40)` onto
the circular ring anchored at queueHead (next@+0/prev@+4), then
state=4, gate=a1, **+0x5C = 0 (no object)**, +0x80 = 0 (jr delay slot).
Sleep/delay queues are STANDALONE head structs (L: ctx+0x498 sleep /
ctx+0x4E0 delay) - not slot views; their anchor is the head's own +0/+4.

### sub_890 @0x890 - bare priority re-insert (30w, bonus)

`sub_890(tcb a0, rec a1)`: exactly sub_680's priority walk + insert,
no bookkeeping (no count, no state, no timer). Inserts ANY tcb (a0), not
just current. JJ saw this used with slot attr 0x100 requeue paths
(RotateThreadReadyQueue); it is the "move within ring" primitive.

### sub_908 @0x908 - wait-enqueue veneer (7w)

`j`-equivalent: `move t1,zero; jal 0x680` + frame. The 5th slot t0 is
inherited from the caller (sema/evf cores leave their own value there).

### sub_924 @0x924 - release-from-wait (57w)

```
sub_924(tcb):
  if (tcb->state == 0x20) return 0;              // killed: no transition
  unlink via tcb+0x00/+0x04;  tcb->[0x98] = 0;   // disarm CB gate
  if (tcb->[0x78]) { sub_11104(handle, tcb->[0x7C]); +0x78=0; +0x7C=0; }
  if (tcb->[0x5C]) { sub_1879C(tcb->[0x5C], 0x5BD49380, tcb); reload state; }
  if (state == 12) {                             // WAIT+SUSPENDED
      tail-append into module+0x4F0 suspend ring; state = 8; return 0;
  }
  state = 2;  sub_127D0(tcb);  return 1;         // READY + ready-queue insert
```
**Returns 1 iff the thread became READY** - the "transitioned" bit every
waker's dispatch tail tests (R's woken==1 / >1 split). ORDER is invariant:
unlink → timer teardown → object notify → state transition.

### sub_A08 @0xA08 - wake-now (64w)

Same unlink/teardown skeleton as 924, differences: killed state returns
v0 = 0x20 (stale register, quirk); no return value on normal paths; and a
**cause-aware dispatch tail**: state 2 then, in THREAD context
(`a1 == 0`), `0x16C48(tcb, status)` (make-ready with the caller's status
arg - R's `0x16C48(t, t->prio)`); in INTERRUPT context, `0x127D0(tcb)` +
`*(module+0x44) = 0` (defer the switch to the interrupt exit).

### sub_B08 @0xB08 - direct-wake handoff (16w)

`sub_B08(rec, result a1, cause a2, status a3)`: slotView = rec +
4*hdr21(*(module+0x508)); waiter = head = *(slotView+12); **stores result
into waiter->TCB+0x80 in the jal delay slot**, then `sub_A08(waiter,
cause, status)`. Wakes exactly ONE waiter - the head; no empty-queue
check (callers guarantee one: Vpl/Tlspl/MsgPipe grants land the value in
waiter TCB+0x60 first, CC/S/W all saw this callee).

### sub_B48 @0xB48 - exit-waiter walk (31w; Y's decode byte-confirmed)

`sub_B48(rec, exitStatus)`: walks the whole ring from head to anchor:
per waiter, `waiter->TCB+0x80 = exitStatus` (in the branch delay slot)
and `sub_924(waiter)`; the next-pointer load rides in sub_924's jal
delay slot (`lw s0,0(s0)`). Returns the count of waiters released.

### sub_BC4 @0xBC4 - cancel the wait machinery only (27w, bonus)

`sub_BC4(tcb)`: disarm CB gate, cancel timer (same 0x11104(handle,
TCB+0x7C) + zero pair), notify object via 0x1879C(TCB+0x5C, 0x5BD49380,
tcb) - but **NO unlink and NO state change**. Used when the caller owns
the ring surgery; the timeout core at 0xC8C (decoded from .dis, not
matched - outside my list) inlines this pattern: on expiry it zeroes the
TCB+0x7C flag target, sets waitResult = 0x800201A8, unlinks, tears the
timer down, re-checks +0x5C with BOTH tags (0x5BD49380 then 0x85FE2B4C
after the transition), then 127D0/suspend-ring/FE4 - the fourth wake path.

### sub_C30 @0xC30 - cleanup (8w)

`return 0x1879C(a0, 0x85FE2B4C);` - a0 is INHERITED (untouched!). The
release-wait cancel-all tag of the 0x1879C family (siblings: 0x86D94883
cancel, 0x5BD49380 self-cleanup). H's NotifyCallback + R's CancelEventFlag
both route here/through this tag.

---

## The canonical wait-queue invariants (diagram-in-words)

**The ring.** A thread is a node in exactly ONE circular doubly-linked
ring at any moment; the links ALWAYS live at **TCB+0x00 (next) / TCB+0x04
(prev)** - the same two words serve the ready rings (anchor ctx+152+8*prio,
Z), the suspend ring (anchor **module+0x4F0**), the dead/dormant lists,
and any wait queue. Membership is exclusive; every enqueue is one unlink-
free insert, every move is unlink+insert.

**The anchor.** For IPC objects the anchor is EMBEDDED in the slot view:
`view = rec + 4*hdr21(*(module+0x508))`; **view+12 = anchor.next,
view+16 = anchor.prev; empty ⟺ view+12 == &view+12** (the anchor points
at itself - same convention as the ready rings). view+8 = waiter count,
view+0 = attr (bit 0x100 = priority waiting), view+4 = a protected-head
pointer that stops a newcomer from displacing the head. For sleep/delay
there is no slot view: standalone head structs whose own +0/+4 are the
anchor (sub_858 appends there).

**Going to sleep.** The wait core validates context, parks the wait
arguments in the TCB (type at +0x58, args at +0x60/+0x64/+0x68), and for
object waits calls 0x908 → 0x680: arm the deadline timer if any (handle
→ +0x78, arg2 → +0x7C), refuse to park if a callback notification is
pending (0x800201AB), stamp state=4 / object=rec (+0x5C) / result=0
(+0x80), count++, insert into the ring (priority iff attr&0x100, else
FIFO tail), zero *(module+0x44), FE4(1,1) - off CPU. **The function's
return value IS whatever the waker later writes into TCB+0x80.** For
sleep/delay, 0x858 does the ring+stamp and the core itself calls FE4.

**Waking - exactly four paths, all sharing the teardown invariant:**
1. *Waker-driven release* (SignalSema, SetEventFlag, UnlockMutex,
   ReleaseWaitThread, ...): result → waiter+0x80, then **sub_924**:
   unlink → disarm CB gate → `0x11104(TCB+0x78, TCB+0x7C)` → zero the
   pair → `0x1879C(TCB+0x5C, 0x5BD49380, TCB)` → state 12 goes to the
   suspend ring as 8, else state 2 + 0x127D0. Returns 1 iff READY -
   the waker's dispatch tail (woken 1 → 0x16BF4/0x16C48; >1 → FE4) keys
   on it.
2. *Direct handoff* (Vpl/Tlspl/MsgPipe grant): value → head waiter's
   +0x60, then **sub_B08** → sub_A08 on the HEAD only, result in the jal
   delay slot. Cause-aware: thread ctx → 0x16C48(tcb, status); interrupt
   ctx → 0x127D0 + *(module+0x44) = 0.
3. *Timeout*: the timer core (0xC8C) runs sub_BC4-style teardown inline,
   waitResult = 0x800201A8, both 0x1879C tags, then the 924 tail.
4. *Termination*: **sub_B48(rec, exitStatus)** - walk the entire ring,
   each waiter gets +0x80 = exitStatus + sub_924; count returned. Queue
   destruction uses sub_C30 → 0x1879C(rec, 0x85FE2B4C) instead.

**The teardown invariant:** whoever moves a waiting thread off its ring
MUST (in this order) unlink via +0/+4, disarm +0x98, cancel the +0x78
timer via 0x11104(handle, +0x7C) and zero both, then notify the waited
object at +0x5C via 0x1879C with the 0x5BD49380 tag. sub_924, sub_A08,
sub_BC4 and the 0xC8C core all implement precisely this sequence - it is
why an early release of a delay-waiter (gate 2) never leaves a stray
timer, and why the object's waiter count can never leak.

# Batch MM - the user-callback machinery + remaining family cores

**Verdict: 7 of 7 functions byte-exact (805/805 words, 0 relocation-covered
diffs).** All source in `match/threadman/tm_mm.c`, generated by /tmp/gen_mm.py
(the tools/gen_tm.py batch-II decoder: independent word decode, branch/jump
targets computed from RAW offset fields, literal `j`/`jal` targets,
`%hi`/`%lo(g_padMM)` for the 24 module-data sites). All seven are INTERNAL
functions (not in the export map) - compared with `build/vdiff.py`,
re-validated first on tm_v.o (359/359, batch V's known match). Harness smoke
test passed before starting and re-verified after finishing (tm.c: 4 MATCH +
2 reloc).

| function | addr | words | status | iters |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| sub_2170 - callback pump | 0x2170 | 133 | ✅ 133/133 exact | 1 |
| sub_2944 - user-callback invoke thunk | 0x2944 | 126 | ✅ 126/126 exact | 2 |
| sub_3AD0 - WaitThreadEnd core | 0x3AD0 | 170 | ✅ 170/170 exact | 2 |
| sub_C950 - FPL allocate core | 0xC950 | 179 | ✅ 179/179 exact | 1 |
| sub_116F4 - alarm create core | 0x116F4 | 80 | ✅ 80/80 exact | 1 |
| sub_EAE0 - timer-queue insert | 0xEAE0 | 39 | ✅ 39/39 exact | 1 |
| sub_EB7C - timer-queue reprocess | 0xEB7C | 78 | ✅ 78/78 exact | 1 |

Iteration-2 slips (both classic classes, worth repeating):
1. **gen_tm's COP0 decoder hardcodes `cfc0 r,$13`** - the thunk's
   `40464000` at 0x2A14 is `cfc0 a2,$8` (**BadVAddr**), which the decoder
   silently mangled to $13 (`40466800`). Fixed by overriding the site with
   raw `.word 0x40464000`. Any future cfc0 to a CP0 reg other than 13 needs
   an override.
2. Two LO-override register typos in sub_3AD0 (0x3BDC/0x3C0C): retail
   `8EB60040` = `lw s6,64(s5)` - I typed $18 (s2) for rt; correct rt = $22.
   (Register-field hand-copy class, 4th recurrence.)

Sizing: the FPL core is **179w** (not II's 178 - the last word `ori v0,
t2,0x64` at 0xCC18 belongs to it; TryAllocateFpl starts 0xCC1C). The pump is
**133w** (DD said 132 - the `lw s0,0(s8)` at 0x2380 is its final word;
ReferCallbackStatus starts 0x2384). The thunk is **126w** (ends 0x2B38;
next fn 0x2B3C). The alarm core is **80w** (ends 0x11830).

---

## 1. The callback pump sub_2170 (133w) - T.md/DD.md decode, now byte-locked

```
u32 sub_2170(cookie)                       // a0 = critical cookie
{
    P = *(module+0x40);  anchor = P+0x90;  // per-thread callback queue ring
    *(P+0x9C) = 0;                          // clear notifyPending (delay slot)
    executed = 0;  deleted = 0;
    for (node = *(P+0x90); node != anchor; node = node->next /* +0 */) {
        count = node->notifyCount;          // +24
        if (count == 0) continue;
        if (node->deferredDelete /* +16 */ != 0) continue;   // skip armed deletes
        record = node - 4*hdr21(0x4F8);     // callback UID class
        node->notifyCount = 0;  notifyArg = node->notifyArg; node->notifyArg = 0;
        P->cbNesting /* +0xA0 */++;  P->dispatchGuard /* +0xA4 */ = record;
        node->inFlight /* +12 */++;
        if (logHandler) log(7, node, 5, record+12 /*SceUID*/, node->common /*+40*/, notifyArg);
        result = sub_2944(node->userFlag /*+32*/, node->func /*+36*/, count,
                          notifyArg, node->common /*+40*/, 0, node->gp /*+44*/);
        P->dispatchGuard = saved;  node->inFlight--;  P->cbNesting--;
        if (node->inFlight == 0 && node->deferredDelete) {
            unlink(node from P+0x90 ring, self-loop);      // 0x2320 block
            deleted += sub_B48(record, 0);                 // waiter completion
        }
        if (result != 0 && node->deferredDelete == 0)
            sub_1877C(node->uid /* +8 */);                 // PSPSDK auto-delete:
    }                                                      //  nonzero return deletes
    if (*(module+0x4C) != 0) sub_14124(1, 0);              // dispatch-busy fixup
    if (deleted) { *(module+0x44) = 0; sub_FE4(0); }       // dispatch request
    return executed-count... 0x;                           // returns s7 = executions
}
```
Corrections to DD.md's prose decode, from the words themselves:
- The **skip flag at node+16 is the deferred-delete marker**, not the
  callback attr (attr u16 lives at record+22). It is also the AND-condition
  for the unlink: a node whose deferred-delete is armed and whose in-flight
  count (node+12, ++ pre-invoke / −− post-invoke) drains to 0 is unlinked
  and passed to 0xB48(record, 0) - this is how DeleteCallback/CancelCallback
  (which gate on the SAME TCB+0xA4 guard) complete safely while busy.
- The auto-delete condition is `result != 0 && node+16 == 0`.
- The unlink block reuses **s0 in its second life** (post-invoke `lw s0,0
  (s8)` = node->next); DD's "attr" reading of that store is the next-link.
- Executed-count return: s7 counts loop bodies (`addiu s7,s7,1` at 0x2268).
- msgno for the per-node log = **7** (new), extras: uid (a3), common (t2),
  func (t3), count (t0), notifyArg (t1).

## 2. sub_2944 (126w) - THE user-callback invoke thunk (re-entry ABI)

Called by the pump as
`sub_2944(userFlag, func, count, notifyArg, common, 0, gp)` - 7 args, #5-#7
in t0/t1/t2. Callee-args map: s7=userFlag(a0), s0=func(a1), s1=count(a2),
s2=notifyArg(a3), s3=common(t0), s4=0(t1), s5=gp(t2).

### Step by step

1. **Frame**: 448B stack frame; `s6 = (sp+79) & ~0x3F` via raw INS
   `7C162804` = **ins s6,zero,0,6** - the exception frame is 64-BYTE
   (cache-line) aligned. `jal 0x134A8(s6)` initializes it (K's "ctx setup"):
   **GPR r0..r31 at frame+64..+188 poisoned 0xdeadbeef, FPU regs at
   +192..+316 = 0x7F800001, +40 = 0xE00, +44/+48 = 0xdeadbeef, +52/+56/
   +60 = 0, r0(+64) = 0, k0(+168) = 0**. That pins the frame layout the
   thunk writes into.
2. **Register loadout** (frame GPR slots = 64+4*n):
   - frame+56 = **EPC = the user callback function** (s0).
   - frame+80/+84/+88 = a0/a1/a2 = **(count, notifyArg, common)** - the
     user callback runs as `int cb(int notifyCount, int notifyArg, void
     *common)` (PSPSDK SceKernelCallbackFunction) ✓.
   - frame+92 = a3 = 0 (the thunk's 6th arg).
   - frame+176 = **r28 gp = the callback's own gp** (obj+44 = 0x1873C
     (func) result or the creator's gp - M/GG). This is the **gp swap**:
     the kernel's gp is replaced by the user module's gp for the duration.
   - frame+52 = **19 (0x13)** = CP0 Status for the resume: IE|EXL|UM -
     user-mode resume. (Confirmed by 0x28C0 testing `frame+52 & 0x4000`,
     the same Status bit 14 ChangeCurrentThreadAttr flips for the FPU.)
   - frame+36 = 3 (cause/type word; raw value recorded).
   - k1 is NOT in the frame - the kernel k1 convention is untouched; the
     "k1 swap" of the task brief is actually the **P-field swap + gp swap**
   (see 4).
3. **Context swap on the thread record P** (saved to C-stack first:
   P+36/P+40/P+132/P+24 read at 0x29BC-0x29D8; error path 0x29F0 restores
   all four and returns `*(P+128)` - the critical cookie stored by the
   return trampoline):
   - `sub_16FA4()` kernel-stack snapshot (result ignored).
   - `jal 0x186E4(P+28)` - pointer validation import on the exit-info
     block ptr [TCB+0x1C]; delay slot stores **P+132 = old P+28**.
   - non-zero (invalid) → full restore path + fix BadVaddr if it equals the
     frame ptr, `ctc0 a1,$14 / ctc0 a1,$15` with P+40, return *(P+128).
   - valid → arm: **P+40 = *([old P+28]+180)**, P+24 = 0, and
     `7C187384` = **ins t8,zero,14,1 clears TCB attr bit 0x4000 (P+20)** -
     the FPU context is detached while the callback runs (0x28C0 re-evaluates
     it on return).
4. **Return-address arming - user vs kernel callbacks** (branch on the 1st
   arg = node+32 user flag from CreateCallback):
   - **userFlag == 0 (kernel callback)**: frame+188 (r31 ra) =
     **module+0x2DBC** - an in-module return trampoline (disassembled):
     `cookie = 0x186AC(); P = *(module+0x40); *(P+128) = cookie;
     sub_28C0(P)`.
   - **userFlag != 0 (user callback)** - the syscall gadget:
     - pick the user stack: `a1 = (P+36 != 0) ? *(P+36)+8 : P+200`
       ([TCB+0x24] exit-info's stack top, else TCB+0xC8 fallback);
     - `a1 -= 64`; **write a `syscall` instruction at a1**: the word is
       built as `((*(module+0x81C)) << 6 & 0x03FFFFC0) | 0xC` - the 20-bit
       code field comes from the module-data dword module+0x81C, funct 0xC;
       a1+4/+8/+12 zeroed; then **`cache 0x1b,0(a1); cache 0x8,0(a1); sync`**
       (icache flush - self-modifying code on the user stack);
     - frame+180 (r29 sp) = frame+184 (r30 fp) = frame+188 (r31 ra) = a1.
       The user callback runs on a 64-byte scratch area below the user stack
       top and **returns (`jr ra`) straight into the patched `syscall`**.
5. **Dispatch hand-off**: `ctc0 *(P+40),$15; ctc0 s6,$14` (EPC←frame -
   interpreted as the frame becoming the thread's resume context), then
   `jal 0x186C4(a0 = s6 = frame)` - 0x186C4 sits in the loader-patched
   import-stub region (in-image `jr ra; nop`), i.e. the real
   context-restore lives in another module. The thunk itself **returns 0**
   to the pump - it does NOT execute the callback inline: it only swaps the
   thread's resume context. The user callback runs when the scheduler next
   dispatches this thread, entirely in user mode.
6. **Coming back**: the patched `syscall` (or the 0x2DBC trampoline for
   kernel callbacks) lands in **_sceKernelReturnFromCallback @0x253C**
   (the one blocked 13w export) / **sub_28C0**: re-enter critical, P =
   current, restore the swapped P-fields, re-check frame Status bit 0x4000
   (FPU) and the attr bit-14 dance, then `0x186C4(*(P+132))` restores the
   kernel context the thunk swapped out.

**Summary of the mechanism** (the PSP's entire usermode-callback path):
CheckCallback/CB-variants/sleep-style pumps → sub_2170 walks the thread's
callback queue → sub_2944 builds a full exception frame (EPC = callback,
a0-a2 = count/arg/common, gp = user module gp, Status = IE|EXL|UM), parks
the kernel context in the TCB, patches a `syscall <module-code>` gadget
onto the user stack top and points sp/fp/ra at it, then returns. The next
dispatch resumes the thread IN USER MODE at the callback; its `jr ra` fires
the gadget; the syscall re-enters threadman (_sceKernelReturnFromCallback)
which swaps the kernel context back. The kernel never calls user code
directly - it re-enters it via the frame, which is why callbacks are
delivered only at pump points.

## 3. sub_3AD0 - WaitThreadEnd core (170w, T.md decode byte-locked)

T.md's decode was accurate; the transcription confirms every path. Gates:
timeout-ptr k1 gate → D3; tid==0 → 0x80020197; cause → 0x80020064; cookie/
dispatch-state → 0x800201A7; resolve → 0x80020198; attr bit 0x4 → D1;
target == current → 0x80020197. Log msgno **55** (plain, a1 = &module[0x40],
a3 = tid) / **56** (CB) - both via the shared jalr block at 0x3CE4.
Loop (identical shape to the FPL core below): CB arm (P+0x98 = CB flag),
notifyPending → pump sub_2170 + re-resolve (fail → 0x800201B5), dead target
(state 0x10/0x20 via the xori/sltiu pair) → result = **target exit status
(target+84)**; else `sub_908(rec, 9, 0, sp, timeout)` (wait type 9), grant
path reads P+0x60, retry iff CB && result == 0x800201AB (s5 reused as the
0x800201AB constant - the srl-k1 user flag that lived there earlier is dead
by then, same register-reuse as the FPL core's s6).

## 4. sub_C950 - FPL allocate core (179w, behind 0xC940/0xC948 veneers)

```
core(uid, pBlock, pTimeout, t3 = CB):
    k1-gate pBlock and pTimeout pointers ((p|p+4) & k1<<11) -> 0x800200D3
    cause != 0 -> 0x80020064
    cookie = 0x186AC(); !cookie || *(module+0x4C) -> 0x800201A7
    0x16FA4();  resolve(uid, table 0x4EC) -> 0x8002019D
    user gate attr bit 0x2 (movz kernel bypass) -> 0x800200D1
    slot = rec + 4*hdr21(0x4C8);  fpl = rec + 4*hdr21(0x4EC)
    if (logHandler) log(CB ? 119 : 118, &module[0x40], 1, uid)
    if (pTimeout) 0x10D34(sp, *pTimeout)              // deadline builder
    for (;;) {
        if (CB && *(P+0x9C)) { sub_2170(cookie); re-resolve -> 0x800201B5; }
        if (fpl->freeCount /* +8 */ != 0) {
            idx = 0xD38C(fpl);                          // ring pop
            *pBlock = fpl->base /* +4 */ + idx * fpl->stride /* +28 */;
            return 0;                                   // (mult/mflo)
        }
        *(P+0x98) = CB;                                 // arm CB gate
        r = 0x908(rec, 7, *(slot), sp, pTimeout);       // **wait type 7 = FPL**
        if (r == 0) *pBlock = *(P+0x60);                // grant slot
        if (CB && r == 0x800201AB) continue;            // notify-abort retry
        return r;
    }
```
- **New wait type: 7 = FPL** (catalog: 1 sleep, 2 delay, 3 sema, 4 evf,
  5 mbx, 7 fpl, 8 msgpipe, 9 thread-end, 12 mutex, 13 lwmutex, 14 tlspl -
  so vpl is 6 by elimination).
- The timeout pointer IS k1-gated here (unusual - both out-block and
  timeout run the (p|p+4)&mask gate).
- msgno **118** AllocateFpl / **119** AllocateFplCB (a1 = &module[0x40]).
- Register-reuse GOTCHA (byte-verified): **s6 holds the k1>>31 user flag
  until 0xCAD0 reassigns it to 0x800201AB** - the retry test `xor/sltiu`
  is against the ERROR CONSTANT, not the user bit. Same shape in 3AD0's s5.

## 5. sub_116F4 - alarm create core (80w, G/GG decode byte-locked)

Prologue gates re-check inside (G.md correct): `bltz k1` user caller passes
through; kernel caller + non-kseg (positive) handler → 0x800200D3. Body:
- `0x1875C(*(module+0x40+0x504) alarm table, name=0x296E0 constant,
  userbyte = (k1>>31)&0xFF, &rec@sp)`.
- link = rec + 4*hdr21(0x4FC), alarm = rec + 4*hdr21(0x504).
- `now = 0xECE4()`; **expiry64 (link+8/+12) = arg64(a0/a1) + now64** -
  BOTH SetAlarm (usec, 0) and SetSysClockAlarm (clock.lo/hi) are relative.
- alarm+0 = userbyte, alarm+4 = handler, alarm+8 = common,
  alarm+12 = 0x1873C(handler) result or **gp if that is 0** (raw `move
  s2,gp` fallback - the gp used for the handler call).
- `0xEAE0(link)` insert + `0xEB7C()` reprocess; release; **returns rec+12
  (the new alarm SceUID)**.
- `srl a0,t1,0` / `srl a3,k1,0x1f` - live shift-by-0 no-ops, Sony verbatim.

## 6. sub_EAE0 - timer-queue insert (39w) + sub_EB7C - reprocess (78w)

- **Anchor correction**: the active-timer list sentinel is the 8-byte ring
  **anchor at module+0xA8** {first@+0, last@+4, self-referential when
  empty} - i.e. "ctx+0x68" in the old notation. G.md/J.md's "ctx+0x64"
  (module+0xA4) is a different dword - CancelAlarm's head-cache (the value
  it compares the cancelled link against and overwrites with next at
  0xEDC0-0xEDCC); module+0xA0 = the sentinel-uid slot ("ctx+0x60"). Links
  at +0 next/+4 prev, 64-bit expiry at +8/+12 - sorted ASCENDING insert:
  walk from anchor->first, insert before the first element whose expiry is
  strictly greater (or-lane noise + sltu borrow/compare idiom, J's
  documented pattern); the empty/end case inserts at the tail.
- **sub_EB7C** scans the list for the soonest deadline (seeded with
  *(module+0x82C) bias added to the head's expiry; each candidate compared
  with the subu/subu 64-bit subtract; strictly-later candidates skipped),
  reads `now = 0xECE4()`, and programs the hardware: if the winner has
  passed / is within the margin, deadline = now + ***(module+0x824)****
  (min-programming window), else the winner's expiry; stores the 64-bit
  deadline at **module+0xC0/+0xC4** and mirrors the low word to
  **MMIO 0xBC600004** (`lui at,0xbc60; sw s0,4(at)`) followed by `sync`.
  New module-data dwords: **+0x824** (programming margin) and **+0x82C**
  (scan bias) - both adjacent to the known +0x820 sysmem pool ptr.

## Notes for next agents

1. cfc0 to CP0 regs other than $13 (Cause) must be overridden - gen_tm's
   decoder hardcodes $13 (cost 1 iteration here). Likewise ctc0/cache/sync/
   INS words splice raw (unchanged recipe).
2. The invoke-thunk error path returns `*(P+128)` (the critical cookie the
   0x2DBC trampoline stores there); _sceKernelReturnFromCallback @0x253C
   (blocked, 13w) is the syscall-side counterpart of sub_28C0 - whoever
   retries that blocked function now has its full call graph decoded
   (0x28C0 + 0x2DBC + the P-field swap protocol).
3. Register double-life is systemic in this family: verify every use of a
   callee-saved reg against the LAST write, not the obvious one (s0 in the
   pump's unlink block; s6 in C950; s5 in 3AD0; s1/s0/s8 views in 116F4).
4. 0x2B3C (next fn after the thunk) and its 0x11834-0x11A20 neighbors are
   tiny 5-11w `0x1876C` resolver wrappers - cheap matches if ever needed.
5. All 24 module-data sites (%hi 12, %lo 12) are listed in /tmp/gen_mm.py's
   LO table with comments - copy forward if the decoder is reused.

# Batch NN - teardown cores + mutex bookkeeping trio + status builders (8 INTERNALS)

Agent NN, 2026-11-18. Source: `match/threadman/tm_nn.c` (generated by
/tmp/gen_nn.py - the tools/gen_tm.py word-decoder method: every retail word
decoded independently, branch/jump targets computed from RAW offset fields,
only the module-data %hi/%lo sites hand-listed). All 8 functions are
non-exported internals, compared with `build/vdiff.py` (validated first on
build/tm_v.o 0x97E0 sub_097E0: 359/359 exact).

**8/8 FULL MATCH, 968/968 words exact, ZERO differences, first compile, zero
iterations.** No assembler warnings, no branch-leaves-function warnings.
Harness smoke test passed before starting and re-verified after finishing
(tm.c: 4 MATCH + 2 reloc).

## Results

| Addr | Words | Function | Status | vdiff output |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0x1752C | 166 | `sub_1752C` - deferred-exit teardown (incl. 0x177AC block) | FULL MATCH | 166/166 exact, 0 differ |
| 0x178C0 | 85 | `sub_178C0` - never-return exit core | FULL MATCH | 85/85 exact, 0 differ |
| 0x17A14 | 296 | `sub_17A14` - terminate core + live-kill path | FULL MATCH | 296/296 exact, 0 differ |
| 0x6604 | 59 | `sub_6604` - mutex acquire + ceiling boost | FULL MATCH | 59/59 exact, 0 differ |
| 0x66F0 | 65 | `sub_66F0` - mutex exit mass-release | FULL MATCH | 65/65 exact, 0 differ |
| 0x7480 | 55 | `sub_7480` - mutex relinquish + ceiling restore | FULL MATCH | 55/55 exact, 0 differ |
| 0x180AC | 208 | `sub_180AC` - ThreadDebugStatus builder (244B) | FULL MATCH | 208/208 exact, 0 differ |
| 0x183EC | 34 | `sub_183EC` - SystemStatus builder (28B) | FULL MATCH | 34/34 exact, 0 differ |

Recipe notes for this batch:
- gen_tm.py's `cfc0` decode had a latent bug: it ignored the CP0 rd field.
  0x180AC contains `cfc0 s0,$8` (BADVADDR, 0x40504000) and `ctc0 s0,$8`
  (0x40D04000) - both spliced raw; only rd==13 (Cause) decodes to the gas
  mnemonic. (Fix applied in /tmp/gen_nn.py, not in tools/.)
- 22 module-data %hi/%lo sites hand-listed (all `lui $r,0x0` are module
  refs in this region; LO immediates 64/68/1272/1256 = ctx / ctx+4 tail /
  module+0x4F8 ring anchor / ctx+0x4A8 dead list).
- rotrv words 0x00EC5846 / 0x00CB5046 raw .word (both are
  `rotrv rd,0x80000000,prio` - the runq-bitmap clear, Z's form, appearing in
  BOTH teardown functions).
- Sizing corrections vs earlier notes: **sub_17A14 is ONE 296-word
  function** 0x17A14-0x17EB0 (the "live-kill path @0x17B68" is an internal
  block reached by `beqz t6,0x17b68`, not a separate function; the shared
  epilogue at 0x17B34 is re-entered from it). **0x177AC is a block inside
  sub_1752C** (166w, 0x1752C-0x177C0), not a separate function - sub_1752C
  is one function containing both. Two small 31w/32w dispatcher functions
  at 0x177C4 and 0x17844 sit between them and call 0x1752C - NOT in this
  batch, still unassigned.

## 1. THE LIVE-KILL PATH DECODE (0x17B68-0x17EB0, inside sub_17A14) - the last major undecoded region

`sub_17A14(recView a0, deleteFlag a1, cookie a2)` (s3=rec base, s6=delete,
s7=cookie, s0=rec+4*hdr21(threads)=TCB view, s5=module). Entry gates
(decoded by Y, confirmed): deleteFlag&&interrupt → 0x80020064; thread-ctx
current->gate → 0x80020064; thread-ctx && target==current → 0x80020197;
state==0 → 0x80020198; dead (0x10/0x20) → plain Terminate 0x800201A2 /
TerminateDelete full delete cleanup (TEH class 8, unlink, 0x155b8,
0x1877c uid free, return 0). Everything below is the LIVE-target path.

**Gate / park phase (0x17B68-0x17BC8):**
1. `t0 = interrupt && target==current` (computed in the beqz delay @0x17AE4).
   If set (killing MYSELF from an interrupt): if ctx+0x0C != 0 → set
   **ctx+0x0C = 3 (suspend + TERMINATE-PENDING, HH's state 3) and return 0**
   - the termination is deferred to ResumeDispatchThread(3), which (HH's
   sub_14124) routes back here / into 0x178C0. If ctx+0x0C == 0 → continue
   inline.
2. `v1 = target->gate (TCB+0x88)`; `gate & 0xC`: if set the target is
   already exiting - plain Terminate → 0x800201A2; TerminateDelete →
   **gate |= 8** (mark delete-pending, the exit core will take the delete
   leg) and return 0.
3. Clean target: **exitStatus (TCB+0x54) = 0x800201AC**
   (SCE_KERNEL_ERROR_THREAD_TERMINATED).
4. If ctx+0x0C != 0 (dispatch suspended): **force-clear ctx+0x0C = 0**
   (in the bnezl delay!), call the 0x18724 panic-print with string
   0x29A94, then CONTINUE the kill anyway.

**Teardown phase (0x17BD0-0x17DB4):** 0x2570, 0x27B4, then the KTLS walker
**0x11E3C(view)**:
- walker returns != 0 (something deferred for the dying thread) → continue
  with the state analysis below;
- walker returns 0 → **new callee 0x25FC(view)** gate: if it returns 0, run
  the FULL inline teardown immediately: B48(rec base, 0x800201AC)
  (type-9 waiter completion), 0x60D4, 0x66F0 (mutex mass-release), 0x26CC,
  0xE0D4, then the dead-list insert - i.e. the sub_1752C chain minus
  0x2570/0x27B4. If 0x25FC returns non-zero → normal state analysis.
  (0x25FC semantics unresolved - likely "target is parked on a list that
  must not be touched now".)

**State analysis (runnable vs queued):** state 1/2 (RUNNING/READY) → skip
to the context rebuild. State 4/8/12 → unlink from its queue
(next->prev=prev / prev->next=next at view+0/+4); if the state is 4 or 12
(WAITING family) first cancel the delay timer (0xBC4) and, if the wait
block TCB+0x5C is non-NULL, purge the wait queue via 0xC30.

**The retarget - the heart of the live kill (0x17C2C-0x17CC4):**
`0x134A8(ctxblk)` (K's context-setup helper) on ctxblk = view+28, then the
thread's context block is REWRITTEN so the victim "returns from its entry
function" straight into the never-return exit core:
- ctxblk+36 = 3 (runStatus 3 = terminating), ctxblk+52 = 3,
  ctxblk+84 = 1, ctxblk+88 = 0x800201AC (exit status),
  ctxblk+80 = 0, ctxblk+168 = view+220 (user frame base),
  ctxblk+176 = view+216 (entry copy), ctxblk+180 = attr<0 ? view+40 :
  view+200 (user/kernel stack field), ctxblk+184/188 = the
  0x800201AC/status pair, **ctxblk+56 = 0x178C0** - the exit TRAMPOLINE
  (the same slot StartThread fills with 0x13E80!), and ctxblk+56's
  companion 0x172DC (module+0x172DC, a text address) at ctxblk+188.
- If state != 2: state = 2 (READY) and `jal 0x127D0(view)` (dispatch-now);
  ctx+4 = 0.

So: **TerminateThread on a live thread retargets the victim's context so
that when it is next scheduled it "falls out" of its entry function into
sub_178C0's teardown, rather than running any more user code.**

**Completion phase (0x17CC8+), split by context:**
- **Interrupt context (cause != 0)**: plain Terminate → return 0
  IMMEDIATELY (the retargeted victim runs its exit when dispatched; no
  waiting). TerminateDelete → the inline delete: TEH class 8 (0x1580),
  unlink, state = 0, 0x155B8, 0x1877C (uid free), return 0.
- **Thread context (cause == 0)**: the TERMINATOR calls
  **0x908(rec base, wait type 9, 0, 0, 0)** - i.e. enqueues ITSELF as a
  thread-end waiter on the victim - and BLOCKS. When the victim's exit core
  runs B48 (completing waiters with 0x800201AC), the terminator wakes;
  if the woken result == 0x800201AC → return 0; any other result is
  returned as the error. **TerminateThread is thus synchronous: the caller
  sleeps (type 9, no timeout) until the target is dead.**
- Dead-list insert tail (shared): user thread (attr<0) with NO user frame
  (view+220 == 0) → straight onto the dead list; else unlink by state:
  state 2 → ring unlink (+bitmap bit clear via rotrv when the runq ring
  empties, exactly the 0x16bf4 idiom); state 1/8 → plain unlink then dead
  list; state 4/12 → timer cancel + optional 0xC30 purge then dead list.
  Dead list = ctx+0x4A8 (module+0x4E8), links at view+0/+4, state = 16.

## 2. The mutex trio - exact ceiling arithmetic (word-verified)

Object: mutexview = rec + 4*hdr21(ctx+0x4D8); slot attr = *(rec +
4*hdr21(ctx+0x4C8)). TCB fields: +0x10 runq prio, +0xB0 region? no -
**+0xBA (186) BASE prio (s16, read with `lh`)**, **+0xBC (188) EFFECTIVE
ceiling (word, written with `sw`)**, **+0xA8 (168) owned-mutex ring
sentinel**. Mutex view: +0/+4 ring links, +8 uid, +12 opt, +16 initCount,
+20 count, +24 owner TCB, +28 ceiling.

**sub_6604(rec, TCB, count) - acquire+boost:**
- view+20 = count, view+24 = TCB.
- attr 0x400: **sorted insert** - walk the owned ring from TCB+0xA8;
  `slt myCeiling,nodeCeiling`, insert BEFORE the first node whose ceiling
  is numerically larger (bnezl out of the loop, so the delay-slot
  `node->prev` load is the likely-path side effect); empty ring → insert at
  sentinel. Then: `if (ceiling < TCB+188) { TCB+188 = ceiling; }` -
  effective = **MIN(effective, ceiling)** - and
  `if (boostedPrio < TCB+0x10) jal 0x14598(TCB, boostedPrio, 0)` where
  boostedPrio = the new effective (ceiling if it won, else the OLD
  effective re-compared - a1 holds min(effective, ceiling) after 0x66A0's
  `move a1,t0` on the taken path). i.e. requeue iff the effective ceiling
  is more urgent than the current runq priority.
- no 0x400: plain tail-append into TCB+0xA8 ring; NO priority change.

**sub_7480(rec) - relinquish + restore:**
- view+20 = 0; owner validity test = `(owner>0) && ((~owner)>0)` (both
  sltu against zero) - only then view+24 = 0 and the view is unlinked from
  the owner's ring (next->prev=prev, prev->next=next).
- Remaining ring EMPTY → **TCB+188 = 127; requeue at BASE** (`lh` TCB+186)
  via 0x14598(TCB, base, 0).
- Ring non-empty, inspect the FIRST remaining entry (the sorted insert
  guarantees this is the most urgent):
  - head attr 0x400 → **TCB+188 = head->ceiling (view+28)**; requeue at
    `ceiling < base ? ceiling : base` (`slt t4,t5,a3; bnez` selects a1 =
    ceiling else a1 = base; the effective STORE happens in the branch's
    delay slot @0x7528 before the selection falls through);
  - head not 0x400 → **TCB+188 = 127; requeue at base**.
- Immediate Priority Ceiling, restored on release - BB's conclusion
  word-confirmed, with the exact signedness: base is a SIGNED halfword,
  effective is a full word, ceiling is a word.

**sub_66F0(TCB) - mass release at exit/delete/terminate:**
- Walks the TCB+0xA8 ring (until back at the sentinel TCB+168). Per mutex
  (node = the mutex VIEW itself): unlink from the ring; attr **0x800** →
  owner = **-1** only (count untouched - permanently locked, waiters
  stranded); otherwise count = 0, owner = 0, `jal 0xC30` purge of the wait
  queue, and **the cancel count returned by 0xC30 accumulates into s4 in
  the `j`'s delay slot** (the shared-delay-slot capture idiom).
- After the walk: `if (TCB+0x10 != base)` (was boosted): TCB+188 = 127,
  TCB+0x10 = base, s4 = 1. Then `if (s4 != 0)` - i.e. **boosted OR any
  cancelled waiters** - ctx+4 = 0 (module+0x44) + `jal 0xFE4(0,0)`
  resched request. Note s4=1 is set ONLY on the boosted path, so a
  non-boosted thread whose mutexes had no waiters skips the resched.

## 3. sub_178C0 - the never-return exit core (Y's chain, word-verified)

(deleteFlag a0, cookie a1, status a2). Acquire; if ctx+0x0C != 0 →
panic-string 0x29A60 + force-clear ctx+0x0C and continue (same
force-clear-and-continue as the live-kill). deleteFlag → TCB+0x88 |= 8.
TEH 0x1580(4, current, cookie); **0x11E3C** (KTLS), **0xB48(rec, status)**
(type-9 waiters), **0x60D4, 0x66F0, 0x26CC, 0xE0D4**; then
`deleteFlag |= (TCB+0x88 & 8)` via movn -
- plain exit: insert current into the **dead list ctx+0x4A8** (links
  TCB+0/+4), **state = 16**, ctx+4 = 0, `FE4(1,1)`;
- delete leg: TEH 0x1580(8, current, cookie), state = 0,
  `0x126AC(current->id)` uid free, then the same FE4 tail;
- both legs then run the 0x18724 panic (string 0x29A44) + `break 0x1`
  (raw 0x0000004D) + `j` back to the FE4 site - an infinite loop; the
  caller NEVER resumes. (The break's j back-edge targets 0x179B0.)

## 4. sub_1752C - the deferred-exit teardown (HH's function, full decode)

Takes the rec BASE (HH's `P - 4*hdr21` note confirmed: s0 = base +
4*hdr21(threads) = view computed internally). If ctx+0x0C != 0 → the
**0x177AC block**: force-clear ctx+0x0C, 0x18724 (string 0x29988), rejoin
the main flow. Main flow:
1. Unlink from whatever list holds it (view+0/+4). Special case
   next==prev: if next==self trivial; else **clear the runq bitmap bit**
   (`rotrv 0x80000000,prio` and-store at ctx+136+4*(prio>>5)) before the
   unlink - the ring went empty.
2. **B48(rec, 0x800201AC)** - type-9 waiters completed with TERMINATED,
   then 0x2570, 0x27B4, 0x11E3C (KTLS), 0x60D4, **0x66F0** (mutex
   mass-release), 0x26CC, 0xE0D4 - the 8-callee chain Y saw inline in
   0x178C0, factored out.
3. 0x18724 log with (rec+16, view+8) - the death report.
4. ctxblk = view+28; if ctxblk+36 (runStatus) == 3 → a LARGE multi-call
   0x18724 dump of ctxblk fields 44..192 (the "died while terminating"
   register dump, 0x176A0-0x17774); else a small report (+36 non-zero
   dumps the wait block view+36+12 too).
5. **Dead-thread list insert, state = 32 (0x20!)** - into the ring anchored
   at **module+0x4F8** (a SECOND dead list - distinct from 0x178C0's
   ctx+0x4A8 = module+0x4E8!); exitStatus view+84 = 0x800201A2.
6. If the **dead-thread handler (module+0x838 = ctx+0x7F8)** is
   registered: `jalr handler(view+8 /*uid*/, ctxblk)` then return; else
   `break` (raw 0x0000000D) + epilogue.

This explains DeleteThread's sentinel guard: state 0x20 = "on the
dead-thread-handler list at module+0x4F8" - DeleteThread's 0x10/0x20 state
test accepts BOTH dead lists.

## 5. sub_183EC - SystemStatus builder (28B block)

`(info a0, mode-arg a1)`: zero info+4..+24 (7-word loop), size = 28.
mode (info+4): **cause != 0 → 4** (interrupt); cause == 0 && a1 == 0 →
**3**; cause == 0 && a1 != 0 → **0** if ctx+0x0C == 0 else **1**. The
remaining fields ALWAYS come from the **sentinel thread record at
ctx+0x50C** (Y's sentinel): info+8/+12 = rec+52/+56 (idle-thread CPU-time
accumulator - the 64-bit idle clock), info+16 = rec+68, info+20/+24 =
ctx+0x10 / ctx+0x18. (Caller: II's matched ReferSystemStatus 0x17148,
msgno 164.)

## 6. sub_180AC - ThreadDebugStatus builder (244B block)

`(rec base a0, status a1)`: zero-fill 240 bytes of status, size = 244;
name (rec+16, if non-NULL) copied via 0x18814(status+4, name, 31). Field
map (status = s1, view = rec+4*hdr21(threads)): +36 attr (view+20), +40
state (view+12), +44 view+192, +48 view+44 (kernel stackTop), +52 view+196,
+56 view+48, +60 view+204, +64 view+216 (entry), +68/+72 view+292/+296
(argLen/stackTop), +76 view+184 (s16 runStatus), +80 view+16 (prio), +84 =
TCB+0x58 waitType, or waitType+256 if TCB+0x98 (CB armed), +88 = waitblock
(TCB+0x5C)+12 or 0, +92 TCB+0x50 wakeupCount, +96/+100 = view+52/+56 CPU
time (+ live delta via 0xECE4 - {ctx+0x70,+0x74} when view == current
TCB, the same 64-bit borrow chain as ReferThreadStatus), +104/+108/+112
view+68/+72/+76, +116 TCB+0x9C notifyPending, +120 view+28 (ctxblk),
+124 view+32, +128 = waitqueue(view+36)+12 or 0, +136 view+36, +140 =
*(view+124) when timer handle view+120 > 0, +132 = view+60+16 or 0, +144..
+160 = **wait-type-specific union filled via a `jr` jump table @0x29E88
indexed by TCB+0x58 (lbu, <15 gate)** - cases load view+96/+100 (wanted
count / evf bits), view+104/+112, view+116; +176 view+220 (user frame);
+180..+243 = 64-byte copy via 0x1880C(status+180, view+224, 64) (TCB+0xE0
save area). Quirks: `cfc0 s0,$8` (BADVADDR) read; if non-zero, jal
0x5A4(badvaddr) then `ctc0` restore; and a **halfword→word write-back at
ctxblk+36** (`lhu a1,36(a0); sw a1,36(a0)`) - mutates the ctxblk in place.
Caller: II's matched ReferThreadDebugStatus 0x161EC (cause-gated
0x80020197, msgno 36).

## New knowledge bank entries

- **The live-kill protocol** (the batch deliverable): retarget ctxblk+56 =
  0x178C0, runStatus 3, exit status 0x800201AC; victim runs the exit core
  when dispatched; terminator sleeps as a type-9 waiter on the victim
  (synchronous terminate); interrupt-ctx plain terminate returns
  immediately; self-terminate from interrupt with dispatch suspended sets
  ctx+0x0C = 3 (deferred via ResumeDispatchThread).
- **ctx+0x0C = 3 writer found**: sub_17A14's self-kill-from-interrupt
  path (HH only saw the reader).
- **Second dead list at module+0x4F8** (state 0x20, exitStatus 0x800201A2,
  dead-thread-handler list; module+0x838 = the registered handler
  - matches round-1's "deadThreadHandler +0x838"); distinct from the
  ctx+0x4A8 (module+0x4E8) dead list of the exit core (state 16).
- **sub_1752C = the 0x178C0 chain factored out** (B48 with 0x800201AC +
  the 8 callees), plus the 0x20-state list insert + deadThreadHandler
  invocation; force-clears ctx+0x0C and continues (both it and 0x178C0).
- **Mutex ceiling arithmetic** (exact): effective(+188) = min(effective,
  ceiling) at acquire; 127 at release-with-empty-ring or non-0x400 head;
  head ceiling at 0x400 head; requeue prio = base always except
  0x400-head where it is min(ceiling, base); base is s16. Mass-release
  rescheds iff boosted OR waiters were cancelled (0xC30 return counts
  accumulate in a j delay slot).
- **0x908 from thread context makes Terminate synchronous** - the
  terminator is the waiter (type 9, no timeout, no CB retry loop here).
- New unresolved callee: **0x25FC(view)** - gate in the live-kill's
  inline-teardown decision (KTLS-walker-returned-0 path).
- Wait-specific debug fields land at status+144..+160 via jump table
  @0x29E88 (TCB+0x58 indexed, 15 cases) - third member of the 0x19B2C
  jump-table family region, but in rodata at 0x29E88.
- gen_tm.py cfc0-decode bug (rd field ignored) - badvaddr cfc0/ctc0 must
  be raw .word. tools/ left unmodified per rules.
- Remaining unassigned in the exit region: 0x177C4 (31w) and 0x17844 (31w)
  dead-thread dispatchers (both tail into sub_1752C), 0x18474 (36w
  thread-ring init), 0x18504+ (sub_18504 calls sub_17A14).

# Batch OO - the last blocked export closed by patching GCC 3.3.6's regrename

**Outcome: `_sceKernelReturnFromCallback` @ 0x0253C (13w) now `match (reloc)`
from pure C. Full-corpus regression sweep: 0 regressions (165 ok -> 167 ok;
every other function byte-identical).**

Toolchain variant: `../toolchain-build/prefix336b` (built from the same
`src/gcc-3.3.6` tree, one surgical patch to `gcc/regrename.c`). Harness:
`python3 tools/match_oo.py <file.c>` (match.py clone pointing at prefix336b,
objects in `build/oo/`). `tools/match.py` / `prefix336` untouched.

## 1. Root cause - it was NOT REG_ALLOC_ORDER

A.md's blocked note blamed "old-RA temp ordering" and the task brief pointed
at `REG_ALLOC_ORDER` in `gcc/config/mips/mips.h`. RTL dumps (`-da`, vanilla
prefix336) on the blocker shape show that theory is wrong - the allocator
already produces retail's exact registers:

```
;; tm_oo.c.24.greg (global-alloc, vanilla compiler):
;; Register dispositions:
181 in 5  182 in 4  184 in 2  185 in 3      ; ctx=a1 v=a0 base=v0 inner=v1
(set (reg 2 v0 [184]) (high ...))            ; lui v0     <- RETAIL
(set (reg 5 a1 [181]) (mem (lo_sum 2 ...)))  ; lw a1,0x40(v0) <- RETAIL
(set (reg 3 v1 [185]) (mem (plus a1 0x24)))  ; lw v1,0x24(a1) <- RETAIL
(set (reg 4 a0 [182]) (mem (plus v1 0x18)))  ; lw a0,0x18(v1) <- RETAIL
```

The pass that destroys it is **regrename** (`-O3` sets `flag_rename_registers`,
toplev.c: `optimize >= 3`), dump `28.rnreg`:

```
Register $5 in insn 19, renamed as $6    ; a1 -> a2
Register $3 in insn 17, renamed as $2    ; v1 -> v0
Register $2 in insn 14, renamed as $3    ; v0 -> v1
```

GCC 3.3.6's `regrename_optimize()` **always renames every chain away from its
current register** (`best_new_reg = -1` + first-candidate-wins; the chain's own
reg is "live over its own range" so it is never re-chosen). GCC 3.4.6 fixed
this upstream (`best_new_reg = reg` + displacement only on strictly smaller
tick, message "no available better choice"). Retail firmware shows the
*pre-rename* allocation in this function - i.e. Sony's 3.3.6 fork did not
behave like vanilla 3.3.6 here.

## 2. Retail evidence pins the exact rule (leaf vs has-calls)

A pure "prefer current register" backport (3.4.6 semantics, candidate A of 3
builds) fixed the blocker but regressed 4 functions that retail had MATCHED
under vanilla renaming - all leaf, all single-chain:

| Fn | Retail | Vanilla (3.3.6) rename | Chain kind | Leaf? |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `sceKernelGetSystemStatusFlag` 0x1712C | base a0 | v1->a0 (retail agrees) | terminate_dead | yes |
| `sceKernelRegisterDeadThreadHandler` 0x17138 | base v1 | v0->v1 (retail agrees) | terminate_dead | yes |
| `sceKernelSetThreadmanLogHandler` 0x16BD8 | base v1 | v0->v1 (retail agrees) | terminate_write | yes |
| `ThreadManForKernel_D366D35A` 0x16B1C | const a2 | v0->a2 (retail agrees) | terminate_write | yes |
| `_sceKernelReturnFromCallback` 0x0253C | keep a1/v1/v0 | all renamed (retail disagrees) | terminate_dead x3 | **no (2 jal)** |

So retail = aggressive vanilla renaming in **leaf** functions, unrenamed
allocator output in **functions containing calls**. (A single-rule model is
provably impossible: the 4 leaf cases require always-rename, the call case
requires never-rename under identical tick state; a REG_ALLOC_ORDER-only
explanation is also impossible - RDTH and ReturnFromCallback reach the same
free-register state {v0,a0 taken; a1,v1 free} and retail picks different
registers.) The upstream 3.4.6 fix is used verbatim for the has-calls case;
leaf functions keep stock 3.3.6 behavior, so the 4 leaf matches are preserved
bit-exactly.

## 3. The patch (vs vanilla regrename.c, kept in toolchain-build/oo-regrename.patch)

```diff
--- gcc/regrename.c (vanilla 3.3.6)
+++ gcc/regrename.c (patched)
@@ -196,9 +196,23 @@
   int this_tick = 0;
   basic_block bb;
   char *first_obj;
+  int has_calls = 0;
+  rtx scan;

   memset (tick, 0, sizeof tick);

+  /* PSP firmware-merge finding (batch OO): retail code shows the
+     aggressive always-rename behavior only in leaf functions.  In
+     functions containing calls, retail keeps the raw allocator's
+     register choices (the later upstream "prefer current register"
+     behavior).  Select the rule per function.  */
+  for (scan = get_insns (); scan; scan = NEXT_INSN (scan))
+    if (GET_CODE (scan) == CALL_INSN)
+      {
+       has_calls = 1;
+       break;
+      }
+
   gcc_obstack_init (&rename_obstack);
   first_obj = (char *) obstack_alloc (&rename_obstack, 0);
@@ -236,7 +250,7 @@
       CLEAR_HARD_REG_SET (regs_seen);
       while (all_chains)
        {
-         int new_reg, best_new_reg = -1;
+         int new_reg, best_new_reg;
          int n_uses;
          struct du_chain *this = all_chains;
          struct du_chain *tmp, *last;
@@ -246,6 +260,10 @@

          all_chains = this->next_chain;

+         /* Leaf function: original behavior (best_new_reg = -1 below);
+            function with calls: prefer the current register (3.4-style).  */
+         best_new_reg = has_calls ? reg : -1;
+
 #if 0 /* This just disables optimization opportunities.  */
@@ -340,6 +358,14 @@
                fprintf (rtl_dump_file, " crosses a call");
            }

+         if (has_calls && best_new_reg == reg)
+           {
+             tick[reg] = ++this_tick;
+             if (rtl_dump_file)
+               fprintf (rtl_dump_file, "; no available better choice\n");
+             continue;
+           }
+
          if (best_new_reg == -1)
            {
              if (rtl_dump_file)
@@ -348,7 +374,10 @@
            }

          do_replace (this, best_new_reg);
-         tick[best_new_reg] = this_tick++;
+         if (has_calls)
+            tick[best_new_reg] = ++this_tick;
+         else
+            tick[best_new_reg] = this_tick++;

          if (rtl_dump_file)
```

Semantics: leaf = stock 3.3.6 (always rename to least-recently-ticked, ties to
lowest regno, post-increment tick - bit-identical to vanilla); has-calls =
GCC 3.4.6's prefer-current rule verbatim (`best_new_reg = reg`, displace only
on strictly smaller tick, tick the kept reg, pre-increment). The candidate
condition keeps the original `best_new_reg == -1 || ...` short-circuit so
leaf mode never reads `tick[-1]`.

## 4. Build (prefix336b)

`prefix336` was copied with `cp -a` and never rebuilt or written by the
harness runs (`tools/match_oo.py` recreates only prefix336b symlinks). Build
script `toolchain-build/oo-rebuild.sh` in the etch386 container, separate
build dir `src/b-gcc336oo`, `--prefix=/build/prefix336b`, same configure
flags as build-toolchain.sh (16-byte EABI alignment patch already in the
shared source tree - untouched by this batch). Fresh full build ~6 min;
regrename-only incremental rebuild ~1 min.

Rebuild log tail (`toolchain-build/oo-build2.log`, 83 lines, 0 errors):
