echo timestamp > stmp-multilib
make[1]: Leaving directory `/build/src/b-gcc336oo/gcc'
```
`mipsel-elf-gcc (GCC) 3.3.6` - version string unchanged.

## 5. Blocker result

```
$ python3 tools/match_oo.py match/threadman/tm_oo.c
_sceKernelReturnFromCallback                 0x0253C  13 words  match (reloc)
    2 word(s) differ only in relocated fields (layout-dependent)
```

The 2 reloc words are the `jal sub_186ac` / `jal sub_28c0` targets (extern
declarations - the standard legitimate finish, same class as GetKTLS). Every
register field is byte-exact:

```
27bdfff0 addiu sp,sp,-16      8ca30024 lw v1,36(a1)
afbf0000 sw ra,0(sp)          8c640018 lw a0,24(v1)
0c000000 jal sub_186ac (r)    0c000000 jal sub_28c0 (r)
00000000 nop                  aca40080 sw a0,128(a1)   ; jal delay slot
3c020000 lui v0,0 (reloc)     8fbf0000 lw ra,0(sp)
8c450040 lw a1,64(v0)         03e00008 jr ra / 27bd0010
```

Same result from `tm_a.c` (the original batch-A source, unchanged): its
`_sceKernelReturnFromCallback` row also flipped DIFF -> match (reloc).

## 6. Regression sweep (candidate history + final)

Method: `tools/run_corpus_oo.sh` - all 42 `match/threadman/tm*.c` files under
both compilers; per-function tag comparison (tools/match.py = prefix336
baseline vs tools/match_oo.py = prefix336b), plus whole-file byte comparison
of harness output.

| Build | Blocker 0x0253C | Regressions vs baseline | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| baseline prefix336 | DIFF (5 bad words) | - | 165 ok tags |
| candidate A (3.4 prefer-current everywhere) | match (reloc) | **4**: GetSystemStatusFlag, RegisterDeadThreadHandler, SetThreadmanLogHandler, D366D35A | all leaf single-chain, retail wants vanilla rename |
| **candidate E1 (leaf split, shipped)** | **match (reloc)** | **0** | 167 ok tags; only tm_a.txt/tm_oo.txt outputs differ from baseline at all |

Files whose entire harness output changed vs baseline: `tm_a.txt`,
`tm_oo.txt` (both = the one function flipping to match). All other 40 files
byte-identical, including the transcription batches (tm_b/tm_c/tm_e...).
Final totals: 165 -> 167 ok tags (+2 = the same function matched in two
files), 0 tag degradations.

## 7. Notes for future agents

- Use `python3 tools/match_oo.py <file.c>` for **functions containing calls**
  (jal to externs counts - the split is has-CALL_INSN, not has-Return).
  Leaf functions are unaffected: either harness gives identical bytes.
- `prefix336` remains valid for everything (leaf behavior is identical);
  `prefix336b` additionally fixes non-leaf register placement.
- The remaining A.md puzzle `sceKernelUSec2SysClock` natural-C form is
  unchanged (still 2 words off in leaf mode - vanilla rename output, same as
  baseline; it stays closed via transcription in tm_e.c).
- The shared source tree `src/gcc-3.3.6/gcc/regrename.c` now carries this
  patch (backup of vanilla: `/tmp/regrename.c.orig-vanilla`; canonical copy:
  `toolchain-build/oo-regrename.patch`). Rebuilds are incremental in
  `src/b-gcc336oo`.

# Batch PP - the full-module link pipeline (capstone tooling)

Deliverable: `tools/link/build_prx.py` - compiles EVERY `match/threadman/tm*.c`
in the etch container, links every function at its retail address inside a copy
of the retail segment image, resolves relocations against ONE unified
module-data image, and diffs the result region-by-region against
`orig/threadman.prx`. No `tools/match.py` / `tools/prx.py` changes - both are
imported read-only.

```
python3 tools/link/build_prx.py              # full run (compile + link + diff)
python3 tools/link/build_prx.py --no-compile # reuse build/link/*.o
```

Artifacts (all in `build/link/`): `threadman-linked.prx` (PRX-shaped ELF,
115864 bytes), `threadman-recon-only.bin` (non-reconstructed bytes zeroed -
auditable coverage twin), `data_image.bin` + `data_image.map` (unified
module-data model), `functions.tsv` (per-function verdicts), `gaps.txt`
(wave-11 target list), `report.txt` (full run log).

Snapshot warning: this run raced concurrent batches - `tm_ll.c`, `tm_mm.c`,
`tm_nn.c`, `tm_kk.c` (and `tm_oo.c`) landed or changed WHILE the pipeline was
being built; all numbers below are the state at the final full run (42 sources,
0 compile failures). Rerun the tool for live numbers.

## Headline numbers

| Metric | Value |
|---|---|
| sources compiled (etch container, prefix336 AND patched prefix336b) | 42 -> 84 objects |
| functions linked at retail addresses | 202 (166 export-named + 36 internal-named; all 167 locatable export addresses covered) |
| words exact after link | 18704 / 18704 (0 reloc-pending, 0 diff) |
| **text region (vaddr 0..0x1868C, 99980 B) byte coverage** | **74.83%** |
| whole ph0 (file 0xC0..0x19F84 = vaddr 0..0x19EC4, 106180 B) | 70.46% |
| unmatched exports | **0** (all 167 NIDs the map can locate) |
| unmatched named internals | 33 (list below) |
| unmatched text (the wave-11 grind) | 43 ranges, 25164 B |

The linked ELF is `cmp`-identical to retail - by construction: every
reconstructed word is retail-exact and every unreconstructed region is copied.
The honest coverage measure is the provenance table plus
`threadman-recon-only.bin` (zeroed twin differs from retail ph0 in 23229
non-zero bytes; the other 8135 copied bytes are zero-fill).

## What the link adds over the per-batch grind

- `match (reloc)` functions close to full EXACT: veneer-region `jal`s
  (R_MIPS_26 against `sub_187XX` externs) resolve to the stub addresses, and
  module-data `%hi/%lo` pairs resolve through the unified data image. Spot
  cross-check vs `tools/match.py` on tm.c/tm_f.c: match.py reports
  GetSystemStatusFlag / RegisterDeadThreadHandler / CancelCallback /
  GetCallbackCount as `match (reloc)`; the linker reports all four EXACT.
- **_sceKernelReturnFromCallback @0x253C (the last "Blocked" export) is
  closed**: compiled from agent A's `tm_a.c` with the patched **prefix336b**
  compiler (OO's REG_ALLOC_ORDER patch), 13/13 words retail-exact (verified
  word-by-word; PROGRESS "Blocked (1)" row can be retired). The pick-best
  duplicate resolution preferred it over both prefix336 attempts (3 diff).

## Pipeline design (why a Python linker)

`ld` with per-symbol placement would need one section per function; instead
each function's words are taken from its `.o` and placed directly at the
retail vaddr inside a copy of retail ph0 - same output, deterministic, and
byte-verifiable. The container still does what it must: all compilation, plus
`mipsel-elf-readelf` validation of the output ELF (ET_SCE_RELEXEC 0xFFA0,
entry 0x11F68, phdrs identical: RX 0xC0/0/0x19EC4, RW bss 0x19F00/0x844,
LOPROC+0xA1 reloc table 0x24D8).

Relocation resolution (own ELF reader - match.py's parser drops the symbol
index): R_MIPS_26 to `sub_XXXXX` externs / defined functions; HI16/LO16 per
the MIPS ABI AHL convention (`AHL = (AHI<<16) + (short)ALO`, `hi =
(V - sext(V&0xFFFF)) >> 16`; one HI16 may pair with several LO16s - the
follower-LO16 case cost one iteration); R_MIPS_32 for `.word sym` sites.

## Unified module-data image

Every batch models the module data as one `g_pad*` static chain at offsets
0x0..0x1784C. The link overlays all 84 objects' statics (124 placements) into
one image (`data_image.bin`/`data_image.map`); section-relative relocs are
attributed to the innermost static of their object and rebased on its unified
offset. Manual placements (documented retail offsets the per-.o GCC layout
missed - the grind's own findings):

- `tm.c` `g_systemStatusFlag` 0x818 -> **0x810**, `g_deadThreadHandler`
  0x834 -> **0x838** (LogCtx got 8-aligned in round 1's model),
- `tm_a.c` `g_d366` 0x17848 -> **0x17844** (GCC 8-aligns >=8-byte statics;
  results/A.md documented the retail pair {0x3E00008, 0}).

Cross-check vs `grep module+0x/ctx+0x results/*.md`: 81 documented offsets;
68 appear as immediates in linked code (the byte-exact words carry them);
the other 13 (+0x520, +0x528, +0x530, +0x680, +0x6A8, +0x6C4, +0x6D0,
+0x6E4, +0x6EC, +0x738, +0x73C, +0x81C, +0x41F4) are only touched by
still-unmatched internals (e.g. Tlspl pool table +0x530 by the ring helpers,
+0x820/+0x824 pool/gates by the walkers) - they become live as wave 11 lands.
Note: this model is the *pre-relocation* address space the retail file bytes
encode (lui 0 / lui 1 + offset); at runtime the loader's ph2 relocations map
it into the RW segment at 0x19F00 (size 0x844 - exactly the documented
g_pad field span) plus the initialized tail in ph0.

## Verification evidence

- match.py cross-check (tm.c, tm_f.c): MATCH/reloc verdicts consistent;
  linker EXACT strictly stronger, and every upgrade is a reloc word that my
  resolution proves equals retail.
- _sceKernelReturnFromCallback: 13/13 words dumped retail-vs-mine, all equal
  (prefix336b object).
- 202/202 linked functions classified EXACT (functions.tsv); 0 placement
  overlaps; 0 unresolved relocation symbols.
- readelf on output: valid PSP PRX ELF, phdrs unchanged; `cmp` vs retail:
  identical (expected, see above).
- Stage-by-stage: compile (84 objs, log in build/link/compile.log), inventory
  (167+35), resolve (HI16/LO16/26/32), place (overlap-checked), diff
  (provenance table), artifacts written.

## GOTCHAs for future link work

- MIPS REL HI16 addends: gas writes the hi part of the FINAL offset into the
  HI16 imm (0 for small offsets, 1 for 0x1xxxx) - hi output must be
  recomputed as `(V - sext(V & 0xFFFF)) >> 16`, NOT `V >> 16`.
- A second LO16 can follow the flushed HI16 pair (sw + addiu sharing one
  lui): keep the last AHI around after flushing.
- GCC 3.3.6 EABI 8-aligns pointers inside structs: tm.c's `LogCtx` is 16
  bytes, not 12 - statics after it drifted +4/+8. Per-.o symtab is ground
  truth, not the C comments.
- Concurrent-batch races: compile per-file (one in-flight `tm_xx.c` must not
  kill the run), and expect the file set to change between runs. A transient
  podman `:Z` relabel storm produced `Permission denied` on the toolchain
  once; retrying worked.
- The 0x1A34-style entries in the ph2 reloc table use the PSP-specific
  format; we copy ph2 verbatim (v1 scope) - decoding it is a v2 task if a
  from-scratch .prx is ever wanted.

# Batch QQ - the module_start block (the single largest gap, 0x11F68-0x127D0)

**Verdict: 3 of 3 functions word-exact, 538/538 words, ZERO relocations, FIRST
COMPILE (0 fix iterations).** Source: `match/threadman/tm_qq.c`, generated by
`/tmp/gen_qq.py` (clone of /tmp/gen_kk.py = tools/gen_tm.py decoder + the two
known decoder fixes + NEW mfc0/ctc0 rd-field decode). Compiled with
`tools/match_oo.py` (patched prefix336b - all three functions contain calls),
verified with `build/vdiff.py` (positive control first: `tm_kk.o sub_FE4`
87/87), then confirmed EXACT in the full link (`build/link/functions.tsv`).

| function | addr | words | status | iters |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| sub_11F68 (**module_start**, the ELF entry) | 0x11F68 | 400 | *** 538/538 exact via vdiff; linker EXACT *** | 1 |
| sub_125A8 (thread-2 entry: cleaner/reaper loop) | 0x125A8 | 65 | same | 1 |
| sub_126AC (uid free - deferred release list) | 0x126AC | 73 | same | 1 |

Coverage: `python3 tools/link/build_prx.py` before → gap list line
`0x11F68-0x127D0 2152 bytes uid free @0x126AC`; after → the range is GONE
from `build/link/gaps.txt`, `functions.tsv` shows all three EXACT from
`tm_qq.prefix336.o`, text-region coverage 74.83% → **82.72%** (82688/99980
exact). Honest attribution: 2152 B of that delta is this batch; the remainder
of the jump came from concurrent `tm_rr.c`/`tm_tt.c` batches that landed
between my baseline run and the final run (227 functions linked vs 202 at
PP's snapshot). `threadman-recon-only.bin` non-zero diff 23229 → 17311 bytes.

## Function inventory of the block (boundaries pinned)

- **0x11F68..0x125A4 (400w) = module_start.** Prologue `addiu sp,sp,-160`
  saving ra+s0-s8, epilogue `jr ra` @0x12550 with `move v0,zero` - returns 0.
  The syslib stub table @0x18A00 names it (NID D3744BE0 → 0x11F68, the ELF
  e_entry). The two error/mode tails 0x12558-0x125A4 belong to it (no other
  entry points: the only external branch/jump targets INTO the whole block are
  `jal 0x126AC` from 0xE9FC/0xEDF8/0xEF14/0x179EC - everything else is
  internal flow; verified by scanning every j/jal in threadman.dis).
- **0x125A0..0x125A4 = a 2-word stub `jr ra; li v0,-1`** - NOT part of
  sub_125A8: nothing reaches it by flow; it is passed as an ARG (see below).
- **0x125A8..0x126A8 (65w) = thread 2's entry** ("SceThreadmanCleaner" body:
  the deferred-uid-free reaper loop, never returns; `j 0x125E4` closes it).
- **0x126AC..0x127CC (73w) = uid free** - 4 external jal callers (Y's exit
  core 0x179EC + three 0xE9xx/0xEFxx alarm/cancel paths).
- Note for the brief: **module_bootstart is NOT in this block** - the syslib
  stub table @0x18A10-0x18A14 says module_bootstart = 0x16B84 (inside the
  0x16B40-0x16BD8 gap) and module_reboot_before/phase table words
  (0x1891C/0x19E30) are not code entries here. Only module_start lands in
  0x11F68-0x127D0.

## What module_start actually builds (the deliverable decode)

### 1. Prologue + S-block cache flush (0x11F68-0x11FC8)

`jal 0x186AC` enter-critical (cookie → 92(sp)); **s8 = gp**; saves the
caller's sp to 96(sp) (thread 3 adopts it later); s2 = S = module+0x40;
**`jal 0x18764(S, 0, 2052)`** = cache-range op over the whole 0x804-byte S
block; **`mfc0 a3,$22` → S+0x7C8** (raw word 0x4007B000 - the CP0 $22 value
at module_start entry); `jal 0xC50` (first of the init calls).

### 2. The 13 subsystem init calls (0x11FCC-0x12028)

`0xC50, 0x16B40, 0x283C, 0x4FBC, 0x6120, 0x743C, 0x84B4, 0x92F4, 0xB1B4,
0xC244, 0xD474, 0xE4EC, 0x1121C` - each is the START of (or sits inside) one
of the remaining anonymous gap ranges: these are the per-family static
initializers. Mapping them is the fastest way to name those gaps: 0x6120
(mutex), 0x743C (LwMutex/lwm), 0x84B4 (mbx), 0x92F4 (msgpipe), 0xB1B4
(vpl/vtimer?), 0xC244 (vpl/fpl), 0xD474 (FPL/Tlspl), 0xE4EC (tlspl/KTLS),
0x1121C (alarm/timer block).

### 3. Ring/list self-init + runq anchors (0x1202C-0x120B8)

Five S-block list heads made self-referential: **S+0x498, S+0x4A0, S+0x4A8
(dead list), S+0x4B0 (suspend list), S+0x4B8** (each `*p = p; *(p+4) = p`).
Then the **128 runq ring anchors S+152+8*p** (0x12070 loop, 127→0, both link
fields = anchor) - matches KK/Z's runq model exactly. UID slot-table header @
module+0x500 gets its self-pointer (+0) and empty first link (+4). S+0x20 =
-1, S+0x38/S+0x5C/S+0x1C/S+0x7F8/S+0x7E4 zeroed.

### 4. Boot-mode detection (0x120B4-0x1210C + tails 0x12558-0x125A4)

Imports 0x1872C(23) / (22) / (26) and 0x18734() decide the mode word
**S+0x7E4** (the field II's profiler gate and AA's FPU-ctx check read):

- f23() != 0: `*(0xBC000030) |= 0x300`, print "CPU Profiler enabled: "
  (0x196F4); then f22() != 0 → print "Thread Mode\n" (0x1971C), **mode 1**;
  f22() == 0 → print "Global Mode\n" (0x1970C), **mode 2**.
- f23() == 0: if 0x18734() != 0 or f26() != 0 → **mode 0** (silent); else
  f22() != 0 → **mode 3**, f22() == 0 → **mode 4**.

(The mode is stored via the `j 0x12100`/`j 0x12108` delay slots - the j
lands ON the `jal 0x18724` print. String constants live 0x196F4-0x1970C.)

### 5. THE THREAD POOL - three threads (the answer to the brief)

**Sysmem pool first**: `0x1878C(1, 2048, 1, "SceThreadman")` → handle stored
**S+0x7E0** - the private 2KB pool every 320B ctx block comes from (AA's
"pool ctx+0x7E0").

- **Thread 1 = "SceThreadmanIdle"** (0x1212C-0x12268): UID-create
  `0x1875C(threads tbl S+0x4CC, "SceThreadmanIdle", 0, &rec)`; record =
  rec+4*hdr21; tid = rec+12 → TCB+8; **prio 127** (TCB+0x10 and sh +184/186,
  ceiling 127 at +0xBC), 256B stack block `0x18794(1,
  "stack:SceThreadmanIdle", 0, 256)` → TCB+0x130, data head *(block) = tid →
  TCB+0x2C, **entry TCB+0xC0 = 0x172C8 iff mode != 0 else 0**; state 2
  (READY); ctxblk: `0x187EC(pool,320,{8,64})` + `jal 0x134A8(ctxblk)` (the
  gap's "ctx setup" - poison-fill) → TCB+0x1C; ctxblk+36 = 3, +52 = 3,
  **+0xBC = 0x172DC** (entry trampoline slot - NN's "the slot StartThread
  fills with 0x13E80"), **+56 = EPC = entry**, +0xB8/+0xB4 = sp/fp =
  stackData+256, +176 = gp; then `jal 0x127D0(TCB)` - queued on runq 127.
  **S+0x50C = this TCB (the sentinel slot Y documented).**
- **Thread 2 = "SceThreadmanCleaner"** (0x1226C-0x12388): same skeleton,
  **entry = 0x125A8** (the reaper loop), **prio 1**, 2048B stack
  ("stack:SceThreadmanCleaner"), **state 4 (WAITING)** - parks immediately;
  gp = module_start's gp. **S+0x510 = this TCB** - KK's "fallback/idle thread
  record" is the CLEANER, not the idle thread; S+0x34 (pending-dispatch flag)
  zeroed here.
- **Thread 3 = "SceThreadmanInit" - the CALLER ADOPTED as a thread**
  (0x1238C-0x12440): `0x187F4(saved_sp, sp+84, sp+88)` (new import: derives a
  pool from the module_start CALLER'S stack - the value saved at 96(sp) in
  the prologue), then `0x187DC(pool, desc56)` with desc[0]=56 → data/blockSize
  from the 56-byte stack-block descriptor; TCB+0x2C = data, *(data) = tid
  (user-thread convention), TCB+0xC4 = blockSize, **prio 32** (sh 184/186),
  **state 1 (RUNNING)** - it is the running context; ctxblk via 0x187EC +
  0x134A8; **`*(module+0x40) = TCB3` and `S+4 = TCB3`** - S->current/next
  re-pointed; then the banked COP0 re-point `ctc0 t1,$14` (ctxblk),
  `ctc0 zero,$15`, `ctc0 t9,$16` (= &TCB3+0x24), `k1 = 0` - same register
  set KK found the dispatch loop programming on every switch-in. So the
  "KT head"/idle-thread question: module_start builds BOTH idle-thread
  records (S+0x50C = idle @127, S+0x510 = cleaner @1) AND adopts the caller
  as the current thread (prio 32) that continues executing module_start.

### 6. Dispatcher registration + sysstat plumbing (0x1245C-0x12520)

- `jal 0x1870C(module+0x1100, 0x12828)` - import call; **0x12828 is KK's
  DISPATCH LOOP**; module+0x1100 is a live code entry (reads S+0x0C dispatch
  state - the software-interrupt dispatch trampoline inside the 0x10FAC gap).
- `jal 0xE5EC(...)` with the same live a0/a1 (internal, still unmatched -
  the exception-path installer).
- `0x186D4(0x41, 0, 0x125A0, 0, 0)` - registers the 2-word `return -1` stub
  at 0x125A0 for interrupt 0x41 (the SAME int FE4's defer path raises via
  `0x186BC(0x41)` right after); then `jal 0x186BC(0x41)` (enable).
- `0x1869C(11, module+0x338)` - registers the table at module+0x338 (inside
  the first gap 0x0-0x680; **module_bootstart @0x16B84 later does
  `0x1868C(11, module+0x338)`** - the sysstat handoff between the two entry
  modes).
- `ctc0 zero,$8` (badvaddr clear).
- **Three syscall-template registrations** `0x18714(f)` with results into
  **S+0x7D4 = _sceKernelExitThread (0x16D34), S+0x7D8 =
  _sceKernelReturnFromChangeStack (0x14F10), S+0x7DC =
  _sceKernelReturnFromCallback (0x253C)** - the user-mode re-entry gadgets
  (MM's callback thunk consumes exactly these).
- **`jal 0x5138` = sceKernelCreateEventFlag("SceThreadmanSysStat", 512, 0,
  NULL)** (internal event flag!) → **S+0x7D0**.
- `0x18744(0x172A8, 3, 0)` - registers the 0x172A8 stub (gap 0x172A8-0x172F8)
  = `jal 0x13AC4(0)` (sceKernelExitDeleteThread!) `; move v0,zero` - the
  **init-thread epilogue**: thread 3 ends by exiting+deleting itself.
- `jal 0x186DC(cookie)` release, `jal 0x186A4()`, epilogue, `return 0`.

### 7. sub_125A8 - the cleaner/reaper loop (thread 2 body)

Infinite loop: enter-critical (cookie s4); **walk the deferred-free block
chain at module+0x78** (head block is the static one at module+0x78; blocks:
+0 next, +0x24 count, +4.. uids): for each uid → `0x18784(uid,
*(S+0x4CC)=threads table, &rec)` resolve; fail → `0x155B8(recView, cookie)`
(gap: delete cleanup) else `0x1877C(uid)` (uid free); reload count each
iteration (it can grow); then count=0, advance next, free non-head blocks
`0x187A4(S+0x7E0 pool, block)`; when chain drained: **`*(P+0x0C) = 4`
(WAITING), `S+4 = 0`, `jal 0xFE4(1,1)`** - park until the next deferred
free wakes it (uid free sets S+0x34=1) - then `j 0x125E4` restarts.

### 8. sub_126AC - uid free = DEFERRED release (not an immediate free)

Appends the uid (a0) to the module+0x78 chain: dup-scan of the current
block's slots (match → **0x80020198**), follow +0 next links when count >= 8;
chain end → `0x18774(pool S+0x7E0, 40)` new 44-byte block (**0x80020190**
fail, first word zeroed, count zeroed); slot[count] = uid, count+1; then
**`S+4 = 0; S+0x34 = 1`** - arms the pending flag that makes the dispatch
core requeue the cleaner. The 4 callers (0xE9FC/0xEDF8/0xEF14/0x179EC) all
run in contexts that cannot walk UID tables directly.

## New S-block / module offsets (for the map)

| module+0x78 | deferred-uid-free block chain head (static first block) | 0x125F4, 0x126B8 |
| S+0x7C8 | CP0 $22 at module_start entry | 0x11FC0/0x11FC8 |
| S+0x7D0 | "SceThreadmanSysStat" event flag uid | 0x124F8/0x124FC |

## GOTCHAs / recipe notes

1. **mfc0/ctc0 decode**: gen_tm.py's COP0 decoder only knows cfc0/$13. This
   block has `mfc0 a3,$22` (0x4007B000) and four REAL ctc0 words
   (0x40C97000/0x40C07800/0x40D98000/0x40C04000) - all rs=0/6 with rd in
   bits 15:11, and gas -mips2 assembles `mfc0 $7,$22` / `ctc0 $9,$14` /
   `ctc0 $0,$15` / `ctc0 $25,$16` / `ctc0 $0,$8` natively. NO raw .word
   needed (unlike sub_12828's truly banked rs=3 words). Fix carried in
   /tmp/gen_qq.py.
2. **j landing ON a jal**: path A1's `j 0x12100` targets the `jal 0x18724`
   word itself; the store in the j's delay slot is executed with v0 = the
   mode constant - the label must go on the jal, and the jal's own delay
   slot (sw v0,2020(s0)) serves both paths (KK's shared-delay-slot class).
3. **Computed globals via delay slots**: `addiu s5,v0,64` in 0x1878C's delay
   slot uses the PRE-call v0 (=0 from a preceding `lui v0,0`) to build
   module+0x40 - transcribe operand-for-operand, do not "fix" it.
4. **2-word stub as data**: 0x125A0's `jr ra; li v0,-1` is passed as an
   argument (0x186D4 a2) - flow analysis would call it dead; it is live as
   a function pointer. Check the arg-setup words before pruning.
5. String constants cluster 0x196F4-0x197BC with overlapping tails
   ("SceThreadmanCleaner"/"dmanCleaner"/"stack:..." suffix reuse).

# Batch RR - the timer block 0x10FAC-0x116F4 + 64-bit clock reader 0xECE4

**Verdict: 11 of 11 functions byte-exact (482/482 words, 0 relocation-covered
diffs).** All source in `match/threadman/tm_rr.c`, generated by /tmp/gen_rr.py
(the gen_tm.py batch-II decoder cloned from /tmp/gen_kk.py - it carries the
three documented decoder fixes: cfc0 rd field, rotrv = srlv with sa=1, and
2-operand bnel→bnezl / beql→beqzl; only the bnezl fix fired in this block).
All eleven are INTERNAL functions (not in the export map) - compared with
`build/vdiff.py`, validated first on the known MM match (build/oo/tm_mm.o
sub_EB7C: 78/78). Harness smoke test passed before starting (tm.c: 4 MATCH +
2 reloc). Compiled with `tools/match_oo.py` (prefix336b - the block is full of
jal sites; asm-only functions are toolchain-identical anyway).

|---|---|---|---|---|
| sub_10FAC - delay timer arm | 0x10FAC | 86 | ✅ 86/86 exact | 2 |
| sub_11104 - delay timer cancel | 0x11104 | 70 | ✅ 70/70 exact | 1 |
| sub_1121C - timer UID-class registration | 0x1121C | 49 | ✅ 49/49 exact | 1 |

Iteration-2 slip (1 class, 2 words): my %lo override table typed **$23 for

encoding, so the decoder's raw `.word` fallback is correct for this block,
unlike FreeFpl's 0x000001CD).

Sizing: the block partitions exactly as
86+70+49+4+2+29+33+35+62+96 = 466 words = 1864 bytes (0x10FAC-0x116F4), no

## 1. The UID-class tree of the timer families (sub_1121C, 49w)

sub_1121C is the timer-class initializer (module-start order):

```
0x18754("Timer"@0x196C0, 20, desc@0x19E18, 0, &ctx+0x4FC)   // base class
0x187CC("Timer", "Delay"@0x196C8,  48, desc@0x19DD0, 0, &ctx+0x500)
0x187CC("Timer", "Alarm"@0x196D0,  48, desc@0x19DE8, 0, &ctx+0x504)
0x187CC("Timer", "VTimer"@0x196D8, 48, desc@0x19E00, 0, &ctx+0x508)
```

- **ctx+0x500 = the DELAY-TIMER UID class** - the one class missing from the
  "complete" maps (fills the 0x4FC/0x504 hole; 16 classes total). Its records
  are named **"SceThreadmanDelay"** (string @0x19694 - that is the name
  constant sub_10FAC passes to the 0x1875C allocator; MM.md's "0x296E0" for
  the alarm name was the same miscalculation, actual string "Alarm"@0x196D0).
- The four 0x18754/0x187CC descriptors (0x19DD0/0x19DE8/0x19E00, 48 B each,
  plus the 20 B base at 0x19E18) are built from tag dwords 0x87089863 /
  0xD310D2D9 and FUNCTION POINTERS 0x118A4 / 0x119B8 / 0x11A04 - i.e. the
  class create/delete callbacks live in the NEXT gap 0x11834-0x11A24 (prime
  next pick: three small callbacks + wrappers, 496 B).
- Record sizes: base Timer/link records 20 B (matches the alarm/vtimer LINK
  layout +0/+4 links, +8/+12 64-bit expiry, +16 state); Delay/Alarm/VTimer
  records 48 B (link view = rec + 4*hdr21(0x4FC), family view = rec +
  4*hdr21(0x500/0x504/0x508)).

## 2. Delay timer arm sub_10FAC (86w) + cancel sub_11104 (70w)

```
sub_10FAC(deadline64* a0, arg1 a1, tcb a2):
    expiry = *a0 (or-lane 64-bit copy);  now = 0xECE4()
    if (expiry.hi - now.hi - borrow) < 0:          // already passed
        return without arming (v0 = hi-delta)
    if (--*(ctx+0x24) > 0):                        // DELAY-TIMER FREE POOL
        link = *(ctx+0x28);  unlink from pool list; rec = link - 4*hdr21(0x4FC)
    else:
        if (0x1875C(ctx+0x500, "SceThreadmanDelay", 0, &rec)) -> 0x80020190
    linkView+8/+12 = expiry64;  timerView+0 = a1;  timerView+4 = a2(tcb)
    0xEAE0(linkView);  0xEB7C();  return rec+12    // the timer SceUID
```
- **New module data: ctx+0x24 (module+0x64) = free-pool COUNT, ctx+0x28
  (module+0x68) = free-pool HEAD** - a doubly-linked recycle list of timer
  records, refilled by 0xEEB0 (below). This resolves the module+0x64 "different
  dword" MM could not place: three distinct timer dwords are now pinned -
  module+0x64 pool count, module+0xA4 active-list head-cache (CancelAlarm),
  module+0xA8 active-queue ring anchor.
- The bnezl/j shared-delay-slot error tail (0x110F4-0x11100) is the standard
  create-family shape: `bnezl v0,0x110E4` with the `lui s2,0x8002` in its OWN
  delay slot, and 0x110E4's `j 0x110BC` carrying the `ori v0,s2,0x190`
  (0x80020190) in ITS delay slot.

```
sub_11104(uid a0, remainingUsec* a1):
    0x18784(uid, ctx+0x500) -> rec;  linkView = rec + 4*hdr21(0x4FC)
    if (a1):                                       // caller wants remainder
        now = 0xECE4();  rem64 = link.expiry - now  (or-lane + sltu borrow)
        rem = max(rem, 0)  (sra-31/nor/and sign-mask dance)
        0x10A74(sp=&rem64, NULL, a1)               // SysClock2USec -> *a1 usec
    unlink linkView (self-loop + ctx+0x64 head-cache fixup @100(s2))
    0xEEB0(linkView)                               // back to the free pool
    0xEB7C();  return 0
```
This is the callee behind batch L's delay cores: TCB+0x78 holds the armed
timer uid, TCB+0x7C the remaining-out pointer.

## 3. The hardware programming model (the brief's deliverable)

Full chain, byte-verified end to end:

```
arm (10FAC) / alarm core (116F4) / vtimer Start (GG)
    -> 0xEAE0: sorted insert into the module+0xA8 ring (ascending 64-bit expiry)
    -> 0xEB7C (reprocess, MM): scan ring for soonest deadline, seeded with the
       *(module+0x82C) BIAS added to the head expiry; winner already passed or
       within *(module+0x824) MARGIN -> deadline = now + margin; else winner
       expiry. Store deadline64 -> module+0xC0/+0xC4, mirror LOW word ->
       MMIO 0xBC600004 (lui at,0xbc60; sw s0,4(at)), then `sync`.
```
My block adds the hardware side:

- **sub_1136C (33w) = the hardware timer bring-up / re-program**: writes
  1 → 0xBC60000C (enable), **48 → 0xBC600008 (prescale)**, 0 → 0xBC600010,
  `sync`; seeds the COUNTER **0xBC600000 ← \*(module+0)**, `sync`; programs
  the COMPARE **0xBC600004 ← \*(module+0xC0)** (low word of the stored
  deadline), `sync; sync`; then `0x186BC(*(module+0x80C))` (subinterrupt
  enable - KK saw the same veneer take literal 0x41 in sub_FE4). MMIO map:
  0xBC600000 counter, 0x600004 compare/deadline, 0x600008 prescale 48,
  0x60000C enable, 0x600010 mode/irq-clear.
- **module+0 = the counter SHADOW/SEED** (single dword at the very base of
  module data). ECE4 computes the 64-bit "now" as
  `{*(module+0xB8) lo, *(module+0xBC) hi} + counter(0xBC600000)` - the +0xB8/
  +0xB8/+0xC pair is the 64-bit epoch base, module+0 the raw counter seed.
- **sub_112F8 (29w) = the tick fixup** (ack path): refreshes the shadow
  `*(module+0) = counter` inside the `0x18704(*(module+0x80C))` delay slot
  (subinterrupt release/ack), then checks `delta = *(module+0xC0) - *(module+0)`
  (deadline lo − shadow): if `delta == 0` or `delta >u 0xF0000000` (sltu
  against `lui a1,0xf000` - the wrap window, NOT 0xF000), it forces
  `*(module+0) = *(module+0xC0) - 1` - i.e. the next 0x1136C-style counter
  seed sits one tick below the compare, KICKING an immediate interrupt that
  forces queue reprocessing (0xEB7C) when the programmed deadline can no
  longer fire on time.
- **sub_112E0 (4w) = the SysTimer OVF interrupt handler**: reads
  0xBC600000 (counter read = ack) and returns via raw `eret`
  (0x42000018). **0x112F0 (2w) = `jr ra; nop` empty stub** (vector table
  filler). The names "SceThreadmanSysTimerOVF" (@0x196A8) / "Timer" live in
  the same rodata cluster as the class names.

So: **deadlines reach 0xBC600004 exclusively through module+0xC0** - 0xEB7C
computes (margin/bias from module+0x824/+0x82C) and both stores +0xC0 and
mirrors the MMIO; 0x1136C is the cold-start/full-reprogram path that ALSO
re-seeds the counter from module+0; 0x112F8 keeps the shadow honest per tick.

## 4. sub_113F0 - 64/32 divmod (35w)

Four-iteration digit-loop division: the 64-bit dividend {lo = *(a1), hi =
*(a1+4)} is rotated 8 bits per iteration (`srl t8,lo,24; sll t7,hi,8; or`),
`divu $0,t6,a2` + `break 0x7` (0x0007000D encoding) per digit, quotient
re-assembled by `sll v0,t2,8; or t2,v0,t5`, remainder = `addu a1,t3,t4`.
Out-params: `*a0 = remainder` pair {a1,t2} and `*a3 = v1` (hi half of one
mfhi/mflo pair) when non-NULL (bnezl). This is the callee behind
SysClock2USec/Wide (batch E/G - divisor 1e6) - both 0x11404/0x11430 breaks
are the gas HIGH-field encoding, splice-raw safe.

## 5. The expiry dispatchers sub_1147C (alarm, 62w) / sub_11574 (vtimer, 96w)

Both are INTERRUPT-context dispatchers with the same skeleton (the vtimer one
is the superset):

- Arm the **sentinel uid slot ctx+0x60** with rec+12 (the "busy" marker MM/G
  documented; CancelAlarm checks it).
- Log through the ctx+8 handler: **msgno 5 (alarm)** a1 = alarm view,
  a2 = 3, a3 = rec+12 uid; **msgno 6 (vtimer)** a1 = vtimer view, a2 = 5,
  extras include the remaining-time pair on sp.
- **GP SWAP**: s? = gp; gp = *(view+12) (alarm) / *(view+32) (vtimer) - the
  0x1873C-validated user gp stored at create; restore after.
- Two dispatch flavors, keyed on the handler pointer (view+0 alarm / view+24
  vtimer): handler non-NULL and thread-context → `0x186DC` release, `jalr
  handler(common)` , `0x186AC` re-acquire, clear ctx+0x60, return cookie.
  The interrupt-context variant queries `0x186FC`, and for vtimer BUILDS A
  16-BYTE FRAME at result−16 ({sched, base2, remaining.lo, remaining.hi},
  written with four sw's) passed to `0x186EC(uid, frame, frame+8, common)` -
  the deferred-call import (same veneer batch KK saw in sub_FE4's
  interrupt-legal path).
- vtimer view fields touched: +4/+8 time64 (remaining computed vs 0xECE4),
  +12 sched, +16 base2, +24 handler, +28 common, +32 gp - matching batch J's
  layout and GG's re-arm notes (the re-insert itself happens in the 0x11834+
  callbacks / GG's setters).

## Notes for next agents

1. **0x11834-0x11A24 (496 B) is the natural next pick**: it holds the UID-class
   callbacks 0x118A4/0x119B8/0x11A04 referenced by the 0x18754/0x187CC
   descriptors (plus the 5-11w resolver wrappers MM noted).
2. The 0x18754/0x187CC import pair is a class/subclass registration ABI
   (name, size, desc, flag, &classHandle) - every remaining unknown caller of
   0x187CC elsewhere in the module is another family registration.
3. delay-timer records consume the ctx+0x24/+0x28 pool; 0xEEB0 (0x0EEB0-0xEF24
   gap, 116 B) is the push-back counterpart - cheap match, closes the pool
   cycle.
4. GOTCHA confirmed again: gas `divu $0,rs,rt` is the bare encoding (safe);
   `divu rs,rt` 2-operand was never tested and should not be.
5. Register-field hand-copy class now at 5 recurrences - always re-derive
   rs/rt/rd from the raw hex, not from the .dis annotation.

Source: `match/threadman/tm_ss.c` (only file written; `.set noreorder`
transcription generated by `/tmp/gen_ss.py` - a `tools/gen_tm.py` clone with
the guide's decoder fixes, see GOTCHAs). Smoke test passed before starting
(tm.c: 4 MATCH + 2 reloc) and re-verified after, unchanged.

**14/14 EXACT - 834/834 words, 0 relocations, 0 diffs** (build/vdiff.py per
function; full-link verdict `EXACT` for all 14 in build/link/functions.tsv).
Both assigned gaps CLOSED:

- 0x0000-0x0680: fully tiled by 7 functions + two ZERO-FILL pads
  (0x2F8-0x300 = 2 words, 0x654-0x680 = 11 words - verified all-zero in
  retail, function alignment padding → **copied-at-link**, not transcribed).
- 0x0E424-0x0EAE0: fully tiled by 7 functions, no padding.

Iterations: 3 compiles (1: branch-to-own-start needed a head label + the
`divu` gas macro; 2: div fixed → 13/14, two function-size arithmetic slips
left a 24-word tail; 3: 14/14). Two gas acceptance probes before generating
(eret/cache rejected by -mips2; explicit-rd div verified).

| 1 | `sub_0000` - entry veneer | 0x00000 | 2 | **EXACT** | 1 |
| 2 | `sub_0008` - idle park loop | 0x00008 | 30 | **EXACT** | 1 |
| 3 | `sub_0080` - VFPU/COP2 ctx SAVE | 0x00080 | 78 | **EXACT** | 2 (size slip) |

---

## Deliverable 1 - what lives at the module's very start (0x0-0x680)

**The module head is NOT threadman code - it is the kernel exception
substrate the dispatcher sits on.** Seven pieces:

1. **sub_0000 @0x0 (2w) - the entry veneer.** `jal 0x18694; nop`: the
   module's FIRST word tail-calls import stub 0x18694 (inside the
   load-patched veneer table 0x1868C-0x18830). I.e. vaddr 0 is a
   bootstrap trampoline into an imported function; the ELF entry
   (`module_start`) is 0x11F68 per PP's readelf. Everything about the
   "entry story" is one jal whose target the loader patches.

2. **sub_0008 @0x8 (30w) - the idle park loop.** Loads `*(module+0)` (the
   only module-data ref), `mtc0 v0,$25` (perfcnt), then an INFINITE loop
   (0x40-0x74): `cache 0xa` I/D flushes over two 64B lines (v0 and
   `*(module+0)`), an interrupt-controller dance at **0xBC200000**
   (save old → write 0x1_0100 → `madd` (SPECIAL2 filler) → restore),
   flanked by SPECIAL2 barrier words (0x700a0024/0x700a0026/0x70000026).
   Contains 20 literal zero words at +0x20..+0x3C (executed as nops - a
   literal .space inside the function, transcribed as nops). No jal/j
   xrefs module-wide: this is the IDLE THREAD BODY - the park address the
   core spins at when 128 = no runnable thread.

3. **sub_0080 @0x80 (78w) + sub_01B8 @0x1B8 (68w) - the VFPU context
   save/restore pair.** Save: gate on CP0 Status CU2 bit (mfc0 $12 /
   or 0x4000 / mtc0), `cache 0x18` line ops between groups, then **32
   `sdc2 $N,N*16(a0)`** - the whole 512-byte VFPU register file (COP2 =
   VFPU on Allegrex, 32 x 128-bit) - then 13 COP1 control transfers
   (0x486f0080-family words, raw) storing t7..t3 to ctx+512..572, plus
   two 0xffff0320/0xffff0000 raw VFPU words: **exactly DD/AA's 576-byte
   FPU context block** (512 + 64). Restore is the ldc2/lw mirror.
   Callers: `jal 0x80`/`jal 0x1B8` from **sub_12C04** (the still-open
   switch-in / interrupt-mask reprogram gap) and 0x18224 (inside the
   0x18474-0x1868C gap) - i.e. the context-switch machinery.

4. **sub_02C8 @0x2C8 (12w) - FPU control-register reset** (same Status
   gate; 0xffff0320 / 0xffff040d raw VFPU ctrl words; 0x486000ff).
   Callers: 0x12C64 and 0x185C0 - same switch cluster.

5. **sub_0300 @0x300 (169w) - THE EXCEPTION REENTRY PATH, ending in
   `eret`.** Entered from the kernel with v1 = a wait-lock node:
   - decrements `v1+8`; on zero, unlinks the node from its wait ring
     (`prev->next = next`, `sync` in the delay slot) and falls to the
     eret tail; otherwise tail-dispatches through **`*(module+0x338)`**
     (`jr v0`) - a load-patched dispatch vector in module data.
   - spins on CP0 **Cause bit 0** (software-interrupt-0 pending) via the
     live `ext v1,v1,0,1` (0x7c630f00); Cause != 0 → the 0x4E0 path.
   - **Lazy FPU switch**: P = `*(module+0x40)` (the current thread
     record, CC's structural finding byte-confirmed here); if
     `*(P+20) & 0x2000` (FPU-owning thread) → `*(P+24) = 1`; an
     EntryLo0→Status shuffle (raw `ins` 0x7c62ef44), then for
     BadVAddr >= 0 (bgez) reads the **FPU-owner byte at ctx+36**: if
     owner != 1, saves $f20-$f31 (always) and $f0-$f19 (conditional) via
     `swc1` at ctx+192..316 (the TCB+0x28 exception-frame FPU area),
     `ctc1 $0/$v1,$31` control restore; the 0x4E0 twin does the mirror
     with `sb 3584,37(v0)` (0xE00 = the FPU-attach flag).
   - **eret tail @0x4CC**: cfc0/mtc0 Index→EPC, Context, PageMask reads,
     raw `eret` (0x42000018). This is where every exception/syscall
     return from threadman code exits the core.

6. **sub_05A4 @0x5A4 (44w) - per-thread FPU save (a0 = thread record)**:
   Status |= 0x2000 (CU1), owner byte at a0+36, `swc1 $f20-$f31` +
   conditional `$f0-$f19` at a0+272..316, Status restore, `jr ra` with
   `ctc0 zero,$8` (BadVAddr clear) in the delay slot. Callers 0x12C34 /
   0x18224 - the switch cluster again.

Coverage note: the two remaining slivers 0x2F8-0x300 (8 B) and
0x654-0x680 (44 B) are all-zero in retail - **copied-at-link zero fill**
(inter-function alignment), per the brief's rule.

## Deliverable 2 - the SysMem-adjacent block (0xE424-0xEAE0)

The block is the Tlspl/pool support library PLUS the module's timer
init/interrupt handler. No data/rodata - 100% code, tiled exactly:

1. **sub_E424(pool, block) (32w) - pool-block validate → index** (CC's
   callee, now byte-locked): `block < pool+4 (dataHead)` or
   `block >= dataHead + count*blockSize` (64-bit end check) or
   `(block-dataHead) % blockSize != 0` (div-by-zero break 7 guard via
   `beqzl` + raw-word delay) or the free-index-table entry at the
   computed slot != 0 (movz select) → **0x800201B6**; success returns
   the index. The index stride into pool+12's table is `sll v1,a3,2`
   (x4, plain u32 entries).

2. **sub_E4A4(uid) (18w) - the Tlspl uid resolver**: walks the class-14
   ring from `*(module+0x530)`: view = rec + 4*hdr21(table) (sll-by-0
   Sony no-op present); `*(view+32) == uid` → return `*(rec+12)`;
   empty ring or not found → -1. This is II's IdOwner type-14 resolver.

3. **sub_E4EC (17w) - uid-table CREATE wrapper**: `jal 0x187CC` with
   a0..a3 = 0x1968C / 0x19684 / 48 / 0x19DB8 (rodata name/descriptor
   pointers - copied-at-link rodata) + `$9 = module+0x530`; returns 0.

4. **sub_E530 (10w)**: `jal 0x1876C(a0); return *(a0+12)` - uid-table
   teardown accessor returning the table's head uid/rec.

5. **sub_E558(rec,...) (37w) - Tlspl pool destroy helper**: view =
   rec + 4*hdr21(rec); if the free-index table is NOT the inline one at
   view+36 → `0x187A4(*(module+0x820), table)` (SysMem validate), then
   `0x187B4(view+32)` frees the pool's ONE SysMem block, then
   `0x1876C(orig args)`; returns 0. (Confirms CC: pools own one block,
   freed at delete.)

6. **sub_E5EC (136w) - MODULE INIT** (the module_start helper living in
   this block):
   - Timer config (base = module+0x40): `USec2SysClock(100)` via jal
     0xECB4 → module+0x828 = low word, module+0x82C = same << 1 (MM's
     "scan bias" dword initialized!), module+0x830/0x834 = 1,
     **module+0x840 = &sub_ECE4 (64-bit clock reader) and
     module+0x83C = &sceKernelGetSystemTimeLow 0x10D28** - the module's
     clock-source FUNCTION POINTER table; module+0x80C = 4 or 19
     depending on module+0x808 (a build/config flag read at init).
   - MMIO: 0xBC60000C = 1, 0xBC600008, 0xBC600010 = 0 (+sync) - SysTimer
     enable sequence.
   - `jal 0x186D4(*(module+0x80C), 1, 0xE80C, module+0x40, 0)` -
     **registers sub_E80C as the alarm interrupt handler**; then
     `jal 0x186F4(val, 2)` (second registration).
   - Two empty rings initialized: module+0x68 and **module+0xA8 (the
     timer-queue anchor, MM)**; module+0x64/0x78 zeroed.
   - Enter-critical 0x186AC; `0x1875C(*(module+0x53C), 0x196A8, 0, &rec)`
     creates the FIRST table's sentinel record (uid marker 0xF0000000
     stored at view+8; deadline slots module+0xC0/0xC4 written - MM's
     deadline dword);
   - **A 32-iteration loop**: `0x1875C(*(module+0x540), 0x19694, 0, &rec)`
     (wave-4's "+0x504 alarms" = module+0x540 absolute), then
     `jal 0xEEB0(rec)` per entry - **32 pre-allocated alarm/timer nodes**
     inserted via the still-open helper 0xEEB0 (the 0x0EEB0-0x0EF24 gap;
     also called from sub_E80C).
   - Programs MMIO 0xBC600004 = *(module+0xC0) (first deadline) + sync,
     0xBC600000 = 0 + sync (timer arm).
   - `0x186CC(24, 0x112E0)`, `0x18824(24, 0x112F8, 0)`,
     `0x1882C(24, 0x1136C, 0)` - three exception/vector registrations
     pointing into the delay-timer block (0x10FAC-0x116F4 gap); warning
     0x186BC(val); exit-critical 0x186DC.

7. **sub_E80C (181w) - THE ALARM EXPIRY INTERRUPT HANDLER** (the function
   registered by sub_E5EC; takes the module+0x40 base in a1):
   - copies deadline {module+0xC0/C4} → {module+0xB8/BC}, reads now64
     via `jal 0xECE4`, walks the module+0xA8 ring (links +0/+4, expiry
     +8/+12 - MM's layout byte-confirmed);
   - `64-bit expiry - now > 0` → done; expired → unlink node, recover
     the RECORD view (`rec = node - 4*hdr21(*(module+0x53C))`), class
     test `0x187FC(rec, *(module+0x540))`:
     - **alarm member**: exit-critical, **`jalr *(alarmView+0)` with
       a0 = *(alarmView+4)** - handler(arg) invoked OUTSIDE the critical
       section - re-enter, recycle node via `jal 0xEEB0(rec)`, loop
       (`bnel` against the anchor, head-cache module+0xA4 reloaded);
     - **non-member** (0xE994 block, a branch target from 0xE900 - this
       is why the block is ONE function, not two): second `0x187FC`
       against the vtimer table `*(module+0x548)` (wave-4 "+0x508"):
       delay-timer nodes route through `jal 0x1147C` /
       `jal 0xEAE0` (timer-queue insert, MM) / `jal 0x126AC` (QQ's
       deferred-release append on the module+0x78 chain - zeroed by
       sub_E5EC at init) / `jal 0x11574`, vtimer members
       re-arm; loop back via `j 0xE944`/`j 0xE9E0` into the walker.
   - tail: `jal 0xEB7C` (reprocess - reprograms MMIO 0xBC600004),
     module+0xA4 = 0, exit-critical, **return -1** (interrupt handlers
     report "not handled" so the kernel PIC ack stays generic).

## Structural finds

- **Module+0x338 = exception-reentry dispatch vector** (jr v0 through
  module data) - new module-data offset; +0x808 config flag selects
  module+0x80C ∈ {4, 19} at init.
- **The clock-source pointer pair at module+0x83C/0x840** (GetSystemTimeLow /
  0xECE4) explains how the timer core stays decoupled: init installs the
  pair, MMIO 0xBC600004 programming reads it.
- **Absolute-vs-relative table map reconciled**: sub_E5EC/E80C address the
  UID tables as module+0x40+X - wave-4's "+0x4FC/+0x504/+0x508" are
  module+0x40-relative (absolute +0x53C/+0x544/+0x548), matching FF/CC's
  absolute "+0x530 Tlspl" (= wave-7's "+0x4F0 type-14").
- **sub_E80C is the interrupt handler registered at init** - the alarm
  interrupt story: init pre-creates 32 timer nodes, registers sub_E80C,
  arms the timer; expiry walks the ring, invokes alarm handlers outside
  the critical section, re-arms via 0xEB7C.
- FPU: the module head holds the WHOLE FPU/VFPU story - 576B VFPU blocks
  (sub_0080/01B8), ctrl reset (sub_02C8), lazy owner switch in the
  exception path (sub_0300), per-thread save (sub_05A4) - all called from
  the still-open switch cluster (sub_12C04, 0x18224).

## GOTCHAs (generator/decoder, for future batches)

- **gas -mips2 rejects the `eret` and `cache` MNEMONICS** ("opcode not
  supported on this processor") - raw .word required (0x42000018 /
  0xBCxxxxxx). `sync` IS accepted and emits 0x0000000F.
- **gas macro-expands 2-operand `div`/`divu`** into bne/break/div/mflo -
  write **`divu $0,$7,$3`** (explicit rd=$0) for the raw word. This is a
  NEW gotcha class (C-compiled batches saw only the break-field variant).
- Branches to the function's own first word need the label ON the entry
  symbol (gen_tm's `a != addr` guard suppresses it) - sub_0300 loops to
  0x300 from 0x4C4.
- gen_tm.py's cfc0 hardcode ($13) fixed in the clone: full rd decode for
  mfc0/mtc0/cfc0/ctc0 - this module uses $0/$2/$4/$5/$8/$12/$13/$14/$25.
- `.set noat` needed: block-1 code uses $at explicitly (lui at,0x4000).
- The .dis file has literal `...` ellipsis lines (0x20-0x3C, 0x2F8,
  0x654 ranges here) - always pull words via `--disas`, never the .dis
  text, when a region looks sparse.
- COP1 0x48xx words (VFPU mfv-family), 0xffff0320/0x040d, SPECIAL2
  0x700x0024/26, SPECIAL3 ins/ext - all raw .word (established recipe,
  confirmed again).

**Verdict: 11/11 byte-exact, zero relocations, 951/951 words.** Source:

`/tmp/gen_tt.py` (tools/gen_tm.py lineage = gen_ll/gen_kk decoder with the
documented fixes: cfc0 rd field, rotrv guard, beqzl/bnezl 2-operand forms).

build/oo/tm_tt.o, compared with `build/vdiff.py`. Harness smoke test passed

no regressions; full link 227 functions / 0 diff / linked PRX identical to
retail).

**2 compile iterations.** Run 1: 10/11 clean; the single diff was my %lo
override typo at 0xB170 (typed `$2` for retail `lw a2` = `$6`) - the same
one-slip class as LL's only diff. Run 2: 11/11. No new gas or toolchain
discoveries; the generator method is now 5-for-5 batches (II, KK, LL, NN,
TT - 4 of them first-or-one-fix compile).

## The canonical wait-core shape (the deliverable)

All three cores are one family. Kernel ABI: the export is a 2-word veneer
`j core; li t3,cbFlag`; the core takes (uid a0, family args a1..t0,
timeout-ptr last, cbFlag t3). Every stage below appears in all three, in
this order:

1. **GATE.** k1 pointer-gates every user pointer argument (the pair
   `(p | p+4) & (k1<<11)` sign test) → **0x800200D3**: sema gates the
   timeout ptr; evf gates result-ptr AND timeout; mbx gates out AND out+4
   (out is `void**`). Then `cfc0 Cause` → **0x80020064**. Then argument
   validation: sema `count <= 0` → **0x800201BD** (new), later
   `count > maxCount` → 0x800201AE; evf `mode & ~0x31` → 0x80020195,
   `mode & 0x30 == 0x30` → 0x80020195, `pattern == 0` → 0x800201B1.
2. **ENTER + RESOLVE.** `0x186AC` acquire cookie; cookie==0 OR
   `*(S+0x0C)` dispatch-busy → **0x800201A7** (CAN_NOT_WAIT; S =
   module+0x40). `0x16FA4` kstack snapshot (result discarded). Resolve via
   `0x18784(uid, *(S+classTable), &rec)` → 0x80020199 (sema) / 0x8002019A
   (evf) / 0x8002019B (mbx). User threads need attr16 bit **4**
   (`lhu rec+22 & 4`, movz kernel bypass) → **0x800200D1**.
3. **VIEWS.** objView = rec + 4·hdr21(classTable) (sema 0x4D0, evf 0x4D4,
   mbx 0x4E0 - S-relative), slotView = rec + 4·hdr21(*(S+0x4C8)).
4. **LOG.** handler = `*(S+8)`; if armed, `jalr` with
   (msgno, a1=S, nextra, a3=uid, t0..t2=family args) - plain/CB pairs
   **60/61** (sema), **69/70** (evf), **93/94** (mbx, N.md's values
   byte-confirmed).
5. **DEADLINE.** timeout ptr ≠ 0 → `0x10D34(sp, *timeout)` builds the
   absolute deadline at sp+0 (the a3 handed to 0x908).
6. **CB PUMP RETRY.** CB flag && `TCB+0x9C` notifyPending → `0x2170`
   callback pump, RE-RESOLVE the uid (deleted → release +
   **0x800201B5**), back to the family test. (Evf also refreshes
   `*result = current pattern` on every retry pass.)
7. **FAMILY TEST / FAST PATH** (the only per-family divergence):
   - **sema**: `0x4D68(slotView)` fairness first - if it returns nonzero
     (waiters queued that must be honored) the core enqueues
     UNCONDITIONALLY (no fast-path steal); else `currentCount >= wanted` consumes
     immediately: count -= wanted, return 0 without parking.
   - **evf**: match the LIVE pattern: `cur & pattern`; OR-mode (bit 0)
     matches iff nonzero, AND iff `== pattern`. On match: `*result = cur`
     (PRE-clear value), apply the waiter's own clears (mode 0x10 →
     cur = 0; 0x20 → cur &= ~pattern), unregister from the registrar,
     return 0. Before all that, unless slot attr **0x200** (multi-wait),
     the thread REGISTERS in the per-object waiter registrar (below).
   - **mbx**: numMessages > 0 → k1-REVALIDATE the oldest message and its
     next (D3 on TOCTOU), single-element ring requires count == 1
     (0x800201C9), unlink oldest, count--, `*out = oldest`, second log
     flavor (a0=0, a3=0x19628 data descriptor), return 0.
8. **ENQUEUE.** Park the wait in the TCB - +0x60 wait arg (sema wanted /
   evf pattern / mbx out-slot ADDRESS), +0x64 second arg (evf mode),
   +0x68 out-ptr (evf result), +0x98 CB flag - then
   `0x908(rec, waitType, policyArg, deadline@sp, timeoutPtr)` with
   waitType **3 sema / 4 evf / 5 mbx**. policyArg: sema = slot attr
   (bit 0x100 priority insert), evf = **0 (always FIFO)**, mbx = slot
   attr - R.md's decodes byte-confirmed. 0x680 does the parking (state 4,
   object stamp, timer arm, ring insert, FE4 away).
9. **WAKE + RESULT ROUTING.** `0x908` RETURNS whatever the waker wrote
   into TCB+0x80 - the wait core's return value IS the waker's word
   (0 granted, 0x800201A8 timeout, 0x800201AA released, ...). Only
   special case: result **0x800201AB** (notify-abort) AND CB flag → loop
   back to stage 6 (pump + retry); non-CB returns it. Otherwise release
   `0x186DC(cookie)`, restore k1, return v0.

Sizing corrections vs gaps.txt labels: the sema core region is 728 B not
~880; evf 1072 B; mbx 840 B (total exactly 2640 B as briefed).

## New decode: the evf waiter REGISTRAR (R.md's "single-waiter slot")

What R.md called "evfobj+12 per-object single-waiter slot" is a counted
registrar, not one slot: objView **+12 = first waiter TCB, +16 = count,
+20/+24 = link bookkeeping**; the per-thread link node lives at
**TCB+0xB0** (new TCB field). The wait core inserts on entry (0x58D0-0x58F8)
unless attr bit 0x200, bumps +16; the match path decrements +16 and fully
unlinks when it hits zero (0x5A50-0x5A80). Poll's 0x800201B0 test =
registrar nonempty AND head ≠ current thread; "poll from your own
callback" = head == me.

## The msgpipe ring pair (0xAFC0 / 0xB094) - field map CORRECTION

P.md's "+8 read / +12 write / +16 tail cursors" is wrong. Byte-exact
decode of both primitives:

| pipe view off | meaning (confirmed by both functions) |
|---|---|
| +8 | data BASE (block data head) |
| +12 | **WRITE cursor** (ring write advances it) |
| +16 | **READ cursor** (ring read advances it) |
| +20 | bufSize |
| +24 | usedBytes |

- `sub_0AFC0(pipe, src, n)`: if writeCursor < readCursor → one contiguous
  `0x1880c(cursor, src, n)`; else two segments: `n1 = min(n, bufSize −
  (cursor − base))` at the cursor, wrap the remainder to BASE, usedBytes
  += n, cursor = base + rem. (Callers guarantee n ≤ free.)
- `sub_0B094(pipe, dst, n)`: exact mirror from the READ cursor, wrap to
  base, usedBytes −= n.
- Call sites: write ← send core (0x9A74, 0x9F5C), TrySend (0xA660),
  TryReceive's drain (0xAB2C), the drain walker (0xB380); read ← receive
  core (0xA400), TryReceive (0xA940).

## The msgpipe UID-class cluster (0xB16C-0xB44C, same gap region)

Data table at **0x19D58** (rodata): a 48B class descriptor whose method
slots point at 0xB3C4 (+4), 0xB2A0 (+0xC), 0xB1F8 (+0x14), 0xB220 (+0x1C)
- registered at module init by `sub_0B1B4` (called from 0x12004) via
import `0x187cc(0x19664, 0x1965C, 48, 0x19D58, 0, &module+0x524)`.
module+0x524 = the msgpipe class-table pointer (S+0x4E4), closing the
"ctx+0x4E4" note. Members:

- `sub_0B16C(uid)` - table-free uid resolver: linear walk of the class
  ring, `view+28 == uid` → return rec+12; exhausted → −1. (0x16A20 =
  GetThreadmanIdOwner's msgpipe leg.)
- `sub_0B2A0(rec)` - **queued-SENDER drain**: iff slotView+8 (waiters) > 0
  AND pipe+4 == 1 (senders queued), walk the waiter TCB ring: n =
  min(bufSize − used, waiter rem[TCB+0x64]); move iff (mode==1 && n>0) ||
  (mode==0 && n == rem) - the same ring-write discipline as the send
  core; `0xAFC0(pipe, waiter src[TCB+0x60], n)`, rem update at +0x64,
  `*waiter pSent[TCB+0x6C] = size[TCB+0x68] − rem`, `0x924(waiter)`;
  accumulates 0x924 results. This is the standalone version of the
  receive core's inline sender-drain loop.
- `sub_0B3C4(rec)` - waiter-LEAVE bookkeeping (the 0x1879C notify hook):
  clears protected-head slotView+4, re-derives the partial flag from the
  NEW head waiter (size != rem → slotView+4 = 1) when direction is 1 or
  2, decrements slotView+8, and when it reaches zero resets pipe+4
  (direction) = 0. This is where a released msgpipe waiter cleans up.
- `sub_0B1F8(rec)` - thin: `0x1876c(rec)` result = rec+12 (uid getter).
- `sub_0B220(rec, ...)` - delete: if view+20 nonzero, `0x187b4(*(view+28))`
  frees the block (arg loaded in the jal delay slot), then forwards to
  `0x1876c`; always returns 0.

## Frame maps (for future diffing)

- sema core 80B: uid@20, rec@16, objView@24, slotView@28, deadline@sp.
- evf core 112B: uid@20, cookie@24, rec@16, deadline@sp, saved t4..t1 and
  0x800201AB @28..52.
- mbx core 80B: uid@20, rec@16, mbxView@24, deadline@sp.
- Register double-life (MM's class): s8 = module base at entry, later
  reused for the 0x800201AB constant (evf 0x5944) / return staging -
  transcribe from the LAST write.

## New knowledge-bank entries

- Wait-core log msgno pairs: **60/61** WaitSema/CB (61 was known; 60 new),
  **69/70** WaitEventFlag/CB (new), 93/94 ReceiveMbx (confirmed).
- New sema error: **0x800201BD** count <= 0 at wait entry (same constant
  as LwMutex's bad-count - generic EINVAL-count).
- TCB **+0xB0** = evf registrar link node (next @+0xB0, prev @+0xB4);
  registrar anchor = evf objView +12/+16/+20/+24.
- Sema wait fairness: 0x4D68 nonzero → queue unconditionally even when
  the count would satisfy (no fast-path steal past waiters).
- Ring field map correction (+8 base / +12 write / +16 read / +20 size /
  +24 used); msgpipe class table 0x19D58 + init helper 0xB1B4; the four
  class methods; module+0x524 = msgpipe class-table pointer.
- The three wait cores CONFIRM the LL family pattern byte-for-byte:
  gate → resolve (0x186ac/0x16fa4/0x18784) → log → 0x10d34 deadline →
  CB pump (0x2170 + re-resolve 0x800201B5) → 0x908/0x680 enqueue →
  wake result routed from TCB+0x80 through 0x908's return, 0x800201AB
  retried only when CB-armed.

# Batch UU - the named-internals sweep (90 internals, 3473 words, all EXACT)

All source in `match/threadman/tm_uu.c` (single file, 90 `sub_XXXXX` asm
functions), generated by `/tmp/gen_uu.py` (tools/gen_tm.py method with the
accumulated decoder fixes). Verified per-function with `build/vdiff.py`
(90/90 `N/N words exact, 0 differ`) and end-to-end by
`tools/link/build_prx.py` (90/90 EXACT, 0 diff, 0 reloc-pending).

## Coverage delta (build_prx.py)

| run | functions | text region 0..0x1868C | whole ph0 |
|---|---|---|---|
| baseline (start of batch, tm_qq/rr/ss/tt not yet landed) | 202 | 74.83% (74816/99980) | 70.46% |
| after wave 1+2+3 (all batches) | 327 | 97.93% (97912/99980) | 92.21% |
| **final (UU complete)** | **331** | **99.95% (99928/99980)** | **94.11%** |

The remaining 52 "unmatched" text bytes are TWO all-zero alignment-padding
ranges (`0x2F8-0x300` 2 words, `0x654-0x680` 11 words - verified zero with
`--disas`; inter-function padding before 16-byte-aligned entries), i.e.
**every real instruction word in the text region 0..0x1868C is now
reconstructed byte-exact**. Remaining "unmatched named internals: 2"
(0x177AC, 0x17B68) are internal BLOCK names inside already-matched functions
(sub_1752C / sub_17A14, batch NN) - name-list artifacts, not gaps.

UU's own contribution: 90 functions, 3473 words (13892 bytes). 14 of the 33
formerly-unmatched NAMED internals closed by UU alone (0x28C0, 0x4D68,
0x9338, 0xD2E4, 0xD338, 0xD38C, 0xD3DC, 0x11E3C, 0x134A8, 0x14124, 0x14598,
0x155B8, 0x156BC, 0x177C4) plus everything else inside the swept ranges;
QQ/RR/SS/TT closed the rest concurrently.

## Method (3 compiles for 90 functions - the generator method is now 6-for-6)

`/tmp/gen_uu.py` = gen_tm.py decoder + fixes: mfc0 (COP0 rs=0), cfc0/ctc0
with live rd field ($8 badvaddr / $13 cause both occur), sllv/srlv/srav only
when sa==0 (Allegrex rotrv = srlv-sa1 falls to raw .word), mfhi/mflo/multu/
div/divu/xor/nor, bltzal/bgezal, 2-operand beql/bnel → beqzl/bnezl. Branch/
jump targets from raw offset fields only. 47 module-data %hi/%lo sites
hand-listed in the generator's LO table after verifying each base register
is a `lui $X,0` module anchor. Fix iterations:
1. `bez`/`bezl` mnemonic bug in my 2-operand branch decode (be→beq).
2. One %lo site off-by-one (0x1780C `lw a3,1292(t0)`, not 0x17810).
Boundary lesson: ALWAYS compute word counts programmatically - three
hand-computed sizes were wrong (0x26CC 24w not 48w, 0x2B64/0x2CA4 70w not
116w) and the generator's branch-leaves-function WARN caught all three
before compile.

| addr | words | identity / decode |
|---|---|---|
| 0x156BC | 67 | **reg init**: walks park-list anchors TCB+0xD0/+0xD4 (208/212), frees each parked stack block via 0x187B4/0x187A4 from pool `*(P+0x7E0)` (=module+0x820); kernel vs user list chosen by TCB attr sign |

| 0x2570 | 35 | teardown-prep TEH: refcount-- at uid-view+12 (table module+0x534), unlink ring view+0/+4, jal 0xB48(view,0) |

- **0x1873C-mark helpers**: 0x1873C itself is an import VENEER (load-patched

# Batch VV - source-only certification: `--no-retail` build

Deliverable: `python3 tools/link/build_prx.py --no-retail` regenerates
`build/link/threadman-linked.prx` **100% from source-derived material - no
retail bytes flow into the output** - and it is byte-identical to retail:

```
$ python3 tools/link/build_prx.py --no-retail        # full run incl. container compile
compiled 47 sources -> 94 objects
functions linked: 331  (24982 words exact, 0 reloc-pending, 0 diff)
metadata tail generated from inventory: 0x1868C..0x19EC4 (6200 B, byte-diff vs retail: 0)

The default path (`python3 tools/link/build_prx.py`) is unchanged and still
produces the same SHA1 (regression-verified after the change). New modules,
all in `tools/link/` (tools/match.py, tools/prx.py untouched):

| module | role |
|---|---|
| `meta.py` | module-metadata generator: veneers, export tables (from `orig/threadman-map.json`), import stub tables + NID arrays + var-import cells (from the import inventory), SceModuleInfo, whole-file scaffolding (`assemble_prx`: ELF header + 3 program headers + pads from fields) |
| `rodata.py` | rodata inventory: strings as text, jump tables / {tag,addr} descriptors / u16 LUTs as annotated word lists (the 4,494-B tail minus what meta.py derives) |
| `reltab.py` | ph2 "A1" load-relocation segment: decoder + **from-image encoder** + semantic verifier |

## Full accounting of the previously-copied bytes

Whole file = 115,864 B = scaffolding 0x00..0xC0 (192 B) + ph0 0xC0..0x19F84
(106,180 B) + pad 0x19F84..0x19FC0 (60 B zeros) + ph2 reloc segment
0x19FC0..0x1C498 (9,432 B). Provenance after this batch:

| region | bytes | old status | new generator |
|---|---|---|---|
| text zero-pad gaps 0x2F8-0x300, 0x654-0x680 | 52 | copied zeros | generated zeros (zero-filled image) |
| import veneers 0x1868C..0x18834 | 420 | copied | 53 x (`jr ra; nop`) = one 2-word stub per imported function, count from import inventory |
| ent region 0x18844..0x1887C | 56 | copied | 3 SceLibraryEntryTable records (4w each) + two 0x00040000 entry trailers, fields from map.json + inventory |
| stub tables 0x18884..0x18918 | 148 | copied | 7 entries of 5/6 words (name ptr, flags/ver, size/vcount/fcount, NID-table ptr, stub addr, [var-cell ptr]) |
| SceModuleInfo 0x1891C..0x18950 | 52 | copied | attr 0x1007 / ver 0x0108 / gp 0x7FF0 / name "sceThreadManager" + the four anchors computed from the generated layout |
| rodata 0x18950..0x19EC4 | 5492 | copied | import-library name strings + syslib table (4 NID/addr) + User/Kernel NID+addr arrays (map.json; Kernel = 149 func NIDs sorted + var NID 0x921B7ACD + 149 addrs) + two u16 LUTs + import NID arrays + var-import cells/structs + string zone + descriptors + 4 jump tables |
| ELF header + phdrs + pads | 192 + 60 | copied (inside `bytearray(retail)`) | fields: e_type 0xFFA0, entry 0x11F68 (map.json), e_flags 0x10A23001, ph0 paddr = 0x80000000 \| file-offset(SceModuleInfo), sizes from generated content |
| ph2 reloc segment | 9432 | copied | `reltab.encode(RECORDS, generated_image)` |

The 4,494 "non-reconstructed" bytes of report.txt v1 were the *non-zero*
bytes of the 6,252-B region 0x1868C..0x19EC4 (1,758 B were zeros). ph0 is
now 100.00% covered (94.11% code-exact + 5.89% generated metadata +
generated zeros), and `threadman-recon-only.bin` differs from retail in
**0** bytes.

## Data-image seeds (work item 5) - none exist in the file

ph1 (RW, vaddr 0x19F00) has `p_filesz = 0`: the file carries **no**
initialized data bytes for the module data block. The runtime RW image is
seeded by the loader itself: the 913 addr_seg=1 relocation records (352
HI16 + 559 LO16 with lo16 addends 0..0x838, plus two R_MIPS_32 - the
SceModuleInfo **gp** field and the word at 0x1942C) write data pointers
into the zero-filled block. What the grind modeled as "data image seeds"
is actually (a) those reloc addends - now generated - and (b) ph0-tail
rodata, covered by the inventory. The `g_d366` "+0x17844 pair" of round-1
notes is TEXT (inside matched function `sub_17844`, tm_uu.c).

## The ph2 relocation table (the hard part)

Format decoded by reimplementing the A1 loader (PPSSPP
`ElfReader::LoadRelocations2` semantics; tables include their size byte as
entry 0; commands are u16 LE with LSB-first fields flag(3b)|seg(1b)|type(3b)
|delta(9b signed); type values 2=32, 3=26, 4=HI16, 5=LO16, 6=J26, 7=JAL26;
addr_seg 0 = add ph0 base, 1 = add ph1 base). The retail stream is ONE
set-base + a 4,215-record delta chain (offsets strictly byte-multiplied-by-4,
range -1124..+1320).

What is DERIVED at encode time from the generated image (all verified
byte-equal against retail):
- **flag class** for every record: J/JAL -> zero-form, HI16 -> inline (or
  reuse when chained after an HI16 with the same lo16), LO16/32/26 ->
  reuse-form; **4215/4215 exact**,
- **short vs mid form**: mid iff |delta| > 255; **4215/4215 exact**,
- **HI16 lo16 addend**: the immediate of the partner LO16 record's word
  (partner = first LO16 record following in the *stream*, matching Sony ld's
  grouping, e.g. the dangling `lui` at 0xB638 hoisted into the 0xB550/0xB554
  group); **475/475 exact**.

What is parsed metadata (RECORDS inventory in reltab.py):
- the record *set and order* - Sony ld's emission order is not recoverable
  from the image (the hoisting above is one example). Semantics of every
  record are re-derived and verified against the generated image by
  `reltab.verify` (0 anomalies): every j/jal word in text (2,542) is covered
  exactly once by a J/JAL26 record; typ<->opcode correlation is perfect
  (typ6<->j 1075, typ7<->jal 1467); HI16 records sit only on `lui` words.
  Cross-checks on addr_seg: 468/475 HI16s satisfy "addr_seg=0 iff the pair
  value is a known function start or ph0-rodata (>=0x1868C), else RW
  (<=0x838)"; the 7 exceptions are pointers to *not-yet-matched* internal
  functions (0x338, 0x1100, 0x125A0 etc.) - they stay correct because
  addr_seg is stored per record.
- the 5 R_MIPS_26 records target the var-import stub structs
  (0x19594/0x195A0): the loader rewrites their first words into `j`-stubs
  that route variable imports (Exception var NID 0x6437D22A, Interrupt var
  NID 0x201A1A19) - emitted from the same import inventory.

## Residual honest caveats

1. **Reloc record set/order** (and per-record addr_seg) = parsed from the
   retail A1 stream (same standing as `orig/threadman-map.json`: structure,
   not bytes). All encodable properties are re-derived from our generated
   image; the encoded output never touches retail.
2. **rodata opaque tables**: the two u16 LUTs (0x18EEC/0x1942C), the
   {tag,handler} descriptor records and the 4 jump tables are transcribed
   word lists (labels document their nature; the addresses inside them are
   internal function entry points that the linked code also references).
   No semantic re-derivation was attempted - they are content, like NIDs.
3. **Constant fields without an external source**: syslib NIDs/addresses
   (module_start = ELF entry, plus 0x16B84/0x1891C/0x19E30), import
   NID lists, flags/version words (0x00090011/0x00010011/0x40010011...),
   SceModuleInfo attr/ver/gp, e_flags 0x10A23001, Kernel var NID
   0x921B7ACD (149 function exports + 1 variable export - the one-word
   asymmetry vs ThreadManForUser).
4. The 7 addr_seg exceptions above (they would self-resolve if the
   referenced internals are ever matched and their addresses added to the
   known-starts set used by the verifier).

## Verification evidence

- `reltab.decode(retail_blob)` == `reltab.RECORDS` and
  `reltab.encode(RECORDS, retail_image)` == retail blob (9,432/9,432 B).
- `meta.build_tail(map.json)` == retail 0x1868C..0x19EC4 (6,200/6,200 B);
  `meta.assemble_prx(image, relseg, entry)` == whole retail file.
- Full `--no-retail` run (container compile of all 47 sources, 0 failures):
  output SHA1 = 32e4457c8018af2f168bb931c07ab01b88b836af, "identical to
  retail: yes", 0 reloc anomalies, recon-only twin diff = 0.
- Default-mode regression: same SHA1, region table still reports the v1
  copied/exact split (6,252 copied) - behavior unchanged.

## GOTCHAs for future work

- The A1 tables self-include their size byte as entry 0 (`buf += size` from
  the table start), and commands start at file offset 0x19FC0+20 - an
  off-by-one there parses cleanly for ~1,100 commands and then desyncs.
- Sony ld groups HI16s ahead of their LO16 across address order (112
  backward steps in the record stream); partner lookup MUST follow stream
  order, not address order.
- ph0's p_paddr is 0x80000000 | (0xC0 + modinfo_vaddr) - derivable, not a
  magic constant.
- The Kernel export entry has vcount=1: its table is 150 NIDs (149 funcs
  sorted + var NID appended) but only 149 addresses - prx.py's heuristic
  scan reports "150 nids" for exactly this reason.
